# GLM review 2026-10-07 (glm-5.3): 495 findings, 221 high severity, 15 shard(s)

Revision 86ff029bc4b3. Shards: agentbox, agentbox#2, agentbox#3, dreamlab-ai-website, estate, knowledgegraph, nostr-rust-forum, sidestr-rs, solid-pod-rs, visionclaw, visionclaw#2, visionclaw#3, visionflow, visiongraph, vowl-wasm. Unverified hypotheses: reproduce each with a failing test or check (build-with-quality) before acting.

## agentbox / critical

### F-01 — Agent identity is forgeable on the management API by any holder of the shared API key
- Topics: AB-10.14, AB-10.7, AB-10.10, AB-03.9
- Evidence: `agent-event-auth.js:31-39` (quoted at AB-10.14) states the ADR-2042 "path 2" — trusting `X-Agentbox-Pubkey` when there is no `Nostr` Authorization header — "is sound only while nothing but the proxy reaches port 9090, and that is NOT true today, server.js binds 0.0.0.0 in-container so any holder of MANAGEMENT_API_KEY clears the gate and can then forge the stamp". The auth gate itself never reads the header (`middleware/auth.js:164-199`, AB-10.7), and on the `/mgmt/` hop the proxy *replaces* Authorization with the route bearer (`proxy.mjs:1028-1029`), so the forged stamp is the only identity carrier reaching `POST /v1/agent-events/emit`. MANAGEMENT_API_KEY is handed to hooks and internal consumers (e.g. ontology-monitor's gate at AB-08.11 requires it), and every sidecar on `visionclaw_network` can reach 0.0.0.0:9090.
- Failure: a compromised sidecar, or any prompt-injected process that reads the key (see F-02), publishes agent-events and execution-journal envelopes under an arbitrary `did:nostr:` — poisoning the audit chain, failure taxonomy and governance attribution with no downstream check able to catch it.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, ADR-2042, stated in-source; AB-10.7 also self-corrects an earlier "zero references" claim)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — Every secret in the box is readable by every process, including model-directed code: one uid, PID 1 carries all role keys by default
- Topics: AB-11.16, AB-02.24, AB-11.2, AB-03.12
- Evidence: AB-11.16: "a 0600 file is a same-uid boundary only. Every agentbox process runs as devuser, so co-resident code can read the agent key, the bridge key and the AoE daemon token alike" (`agent-identity.js:164-166`; per-process isolation named future work). AB-02.24 invariant: with `role_isolation` off — the shipped default (`agentbox.toml:2227`, `agentbox.default.toml:1664`) — the env capture/scrub "return at their first line, and PID 1 still carries every `.env` key" (`entrypoint-unified.sh:411`, `:1221`), and `/run/secrets` is chowned to devuser 0700 (`:388-390`). The audit chain is a bare hash chain whose "deletion at the tail is the one tamper mode a bare hash chain cannot see" (`audit-chain.js:14-16`).
- Failure: any malicious skill, MCP server or prompt-injected Bash (agents execute model-chosen commands as devuser, e.g. the tab0-bridge Bash allowlist at AB-12.7) reads the bridge signing key, AoE daemon token, BRIDGE_TOKEN and role secrets, signs as the box, and can truncate the tail of the audit JSONL invisibly. The role-isolation flag that would mitigate this is documented as off pending rehearsal (AB-05.16).
- Confidence: high (facts stated); the agent-code-as-attacker vector is inferred from the system's purpose
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE at AB-11.16; invariants at AB-02.24) — but the compounding with model-directed code is not marked anywhere
- Severity: high

### F-03 — Default boot re-widens the *host* Docker socket to world read-write; nothing inside can narrow it
- Topics: AB-06.13, AB-02.24, AB-06.1
- Evidence: the host socket is bound rw into agentbox (`docker-compose.override.yml:117`); with `role_isolation` off (the default) the entrypoint runs "chmod o+rw on the socket every boot" (`entrypoint-unified.sh:589-592`, AB-02.24 O2); AB-06.13 OPEN R2: "The host socket inode stays world-accessible until the host chmods it. The entrypoint under role_isolation stops widening it but never narrows it, and only a host-side chmod 0660 narrows it" (`docker-compose.override.yml:116-117`).
- Failure: on the shipped default configuration every boot makes the host's `/var/run/docker.sock` world-read-write on the host itself — any local host account gets host-root via the daemon — while in-container devuser (i.e. agent code, per F-02) can drive the host daemon directly.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: partially — R2 is marked Open and the widening is stated as an invariant of the off-branch, but it is presented as designed default behaviour, not as a default-posture defect
- Severity: high

### F-04 — The HP compose overlay still binds the host's `~/.claude` whole, keeping the exact vector ADR-2118 closed
- Topics: AB-06.1, AB-05.14, AB-06.11, AB-07.11
- Evidence: AB-06.1 DEBT: `docker-compose.hp.yml:24-31` "still the legacy whole-directory ~/.claude bind, not yet migrated". AB-05.14: when `~/.claude` is a legacy whole-directory bind the instruction projector sets `global_out = None` — "the host keeps its own CLAUDE.md" (`entrypoint-unified.sh:2659-2663`). The ADR-2118 invariant the primary overlay enforces — "the container reads settings, hooks and agents ONLY from the volume" (`system-manifest.js:226-228`) — therefore does not hold on that host profile.
- Failure: on the HP host, host-side `settings.json`, hooks and agents are loaded into agent sessions inside the container (host config injection into governed sessions, plus host secret exposure through the bind), and the boot-time instruction-tier projection silently skips the global tier — the box's security posture differs per host with no gate or warning.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT at AB-06.1; Q43 residual at AB-07.11)
- Severity: high (for any deployment using the hp overlay)

### F-05 — The credential bind is writable both ways: in-container code can modify the host's `.claude`
- Topics: AB-06.11, AB-07.11
- Evidence: AB-06.11 Open: "the credential bind stays writable both ways, so a compromised in-container tool could still modify the host's .claude; only the configuration-read path was closed" (`docker-compose.override.yml:84-85`); repeated as the Q43 residual (`BASELINE-container.md:213`).
- Failure: given F-02, "compromised in-container tool" is the baseline condition — model-directed shell code overwrites `/var/lib/agentbox/host-claude/.credentials.json` (and anything else under the bind), planting credentials on the operator's host Claude. This is a standing container→host pivot.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, twice)
- Severity: high

### F-06 — The browser sidecar still authenticates as the house key; K_browser was never minted
- Topics: AB-06.12
- Evidence: "k_browser's pubkey is still null, g5-key-split.json:16-19, and the nip98-proxy roster still admits the house key for it, agentbox.toml:1720... Until the owner mints it and the dual-admit step lands, the sidecar's flows still authenticate as the house key." The sidecar runs an internet-facing Chrome whose raw CDP is published on the LAN (`docker-compose.browsercontainer.yml:53`, sanctioned at `check-ports-loopback.mjs:99`) and whose profile volume holds the Podkey vault.
- Failure: any compromise of the browser sidecar — via CDP on 0.0.0.0:9222, VNC 5903, or the automated browser itself — yields the *house* identity, the same key that governs LAN-ingress approvals, collapsing the G-5 key split the custody design depends on. (The material asserts the non-9096 LAN publishes "carry their own auth" at AB-10.1 N1 but describes no auth mechanism for CDP or the VNC ports anywhere.)
- Confidence: high on the key facts; the CDP compromise path is inferred (auth on CDP is never described)
- Marked by authors: yes (Open ×2, including the pinned artefact expiring 2026-12-24)
- Severity: high

### F-07 — `payment_settlement` is declared zero-tolerance but gated nowhere, and the doc contradicts itself about whether the spend-policy middleware is wired at all
- Topics: AB-05.13, AB-03.16
- Evidence: AB-05.13 Debt: "payment_settlement is declared zero-tolerance with its own task properties (`agentbox.toml:1134`, `:1182`) but no route passes that action class to the authority gate; mandate_revoke is the only class any route names (`routes/llm-marketplace.js:473`)". AB-03.16's own diagram says `spend-policy.js` and `consumer-payer.js` have "no require() from any routes/*.js or server.js" and are "consumed only by scripts/agentbox-config-validate.js", while the same diagram's Note1 says they are "wired via routes/chain-payments.js (spend-policy.js:34, consumer-payer.js:35) — POST /v1/chain/pay is the only route carrying them".
- Failure: settlement/receipt flows (`routes/payments.js`, kind-38201 JOB_SETTLEMENT, marketplace receipts) run without the authority gate the manifest promises. If the arrows rather than the note are correct, the spend-policy and consumer-payer middleware enforce nothing at runtime anywhere and exist only for CI validation; if the note is correct, exactly one route is covered. Either way the manifest's declared control and the enforcement disagree.
- Confidence: high that the contradiction exists; medium on production impact
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt) for the unwired action class; **no** for the AB-03.16 self-contradiction
- Severity: high

### F-08 — `agentbox.sh backup` silently omits the memory database whenever Postgres is not running
- Topics: AB-07.8
- Evidence: "else PG not running → WARNING ruvector-postgres not running — memory DB NOT included in this backup (`agentbox.sh:321`)"; the tarball is still produced, with the omission recorded only as a manifest boolean (`agentbox.sh:395-396`). The always-included volume tar of a live datadir is explicitly "not crash-consistent, so the backup carries pg_dump -Fc instead" (`agentbox.sh:308-310`) — i.e. pg_dump is the *only* consistent copy, and it is the part that is conditionally skipped.
- Failure: a backup run during an outage or partial shutdown (exactly when operators reach for backups) succeeds with a warning line and no memory DB; the later restore rebuilds agents over a stale or empty 2M+-row store. Nothing fails the backup or blocks the restore.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no — the conditional omission is described as behaviour, never flagged
- Severity: high

### F-09 — The MCP hub is a single point of failure for eight servers, and parks FATAL with no auto-recovery
- Topics: AB-09.8, AB-02.17, AB-09.7
- Evidence: AB-09.8 Debt: "eight servers are hub-routed (`agentbox.toml:1329-1332`, down from nine at ADR-2104) and all refuse connections while this program is down, so supervisorctl status agentbox-mcp-hub is the first check." AB-02.17: `startsecs=130` exceeds the 120 s wait, `startretries=2`, `autorestart=unexpected` → a hub that exits parks FATAL; nothing restarts a FATAL supervisord program.
- Failure: a transient hub crash or a lost projection after boot takes all eight governed MCP servers dark for every session until a human intervenes — and per AB-09.7 the harness's failure mode is to simply not offer the tools, so sessions continue with silently missing capabilities rather than erroring.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-10 — A degraded boot proceeds with a placeholder DID: `did:nostr:local`
- Topics: AB-11.3, AB-11.2
- Evidence: the mint CLI now exits non-zero on mint or persist failure (`agent-identity.js:208-224`), but "config/entrypoint-unified.sh's `${AGENTBOX_AGENT_DID:-did:nostr:local}` shell fallback is UNCHANGED and still runs unconditionally on that non-zero exit (`|| true`)... the boot-level placeholder-DID gap named in INGRESS-identity.md remains". Related: a persistence failure "yields a valid but unstable identity — the DID changes on next boot" (AB-11.2, SECURITY-profiles custody row).
- Failure: after a disk or permission fault at boot, the box comes up looking healthy; every subsequent event, URN, mandate and mirror message is durably attributed to `did:nostr:local` (or to a different DID after the next restart), corrupting the audit chain and any WAC/mandate bindings minted in that window.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (the remaining gap is stated explicitly; the unstable-identity case is a marked DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: medium

### F-11 — Marketplace Deny and Revocation events are deliberately non-federated: peers never learn a grant was revoked
- Topics: AB-13.16, AB-13.12
- Evidence: "38303 Deny and 38305 Revocation are deliberately NOT federated (point-to-point only), so a peer that federates the other four kinds never learns of a denial or a revocation" (`agentbox.toml:313`; `llm-marketplace.js:28`, `:30`).
- Failure: an operator revokes (or denies) an LLM-compute grant at the origin; every federated peer that already holds the grant keeps honouring it. Revocations are invisible to all peers by design, so stale grants remain usable across the mesh indefinitely.
- Confidence: high on the mechanism; medium on exploitability (depends on peers enforcing grants)
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: medium

### F-12 — A withdrawn experiment tick signs and publishes as JunkieJarvis without consulting its manifest gate, and reads its key from the repo `.env` by default
- Topics: AB-05.13
- Evidence: "the experiment tick itself signs its one forum summary with the JunkieJarvis key (`scripts/experiments/exp-b8-label-log.cjs:528-530`; since custody W2 the key is read as a role secret and, under `[security].role_isolation`, never from the repo `.env`) and publishes it (`:550-563`) without consulting `junkiejarvisEnabled`, so a manifest with `junkiejarvis = false` would still see a JunkieJarvis post if the withdrawn tick fires again." The parenthetical implies that under the shipped default (role_isolation off) the signing key is read from the repo `.env`.
- Failure: a stale scheduler entry or manual rerun posts to the forum under a governed agent identity while the manifest says that agent is off — an ungoverned utterance the catalogue cannot report (junkiejarvis also has no CATALOGUE entry, same section's Debt). Under default config it also demonstrates a signing credential living in a devuser-readable repo file.
- Confidence: high on the described behaviour; the rerun trigger is hypothetical
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — The manifest enables factrail compaction while its own ADR says the feature is staged and has never been built or booted
- Topics: AB-08.15, AB-05.13
- Evidence: `agentbox.toml:663` has `[features.jev_compaction] enabled = true` (AB-05.13 F1); AB-08.15 Open: "ADR-2121 is `activation_status: staged` — no image with factrail has yet been built and booted, and the residency soak that gates `live` has not run" (ADR-2121 `:139-141`). The plugin decides what transcript content survives compaction on every long session.
- Failure: the next `agentbox.sh rebuild` bakes and installs a context-rewriting plugin that has never passed an image-level soak; a residency bug silently drops or mangles mid-session context. Because the gate is on, `/v1/system` reports the feature as on, so no operator reads it as unproven.
- Confidence: medium (whether the image at the verified commit already contains the bake is not stated)
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)
- Severity: medium

### F-14 — Two sections disagree on whether one relay inbox writer or two exist: a security boundary documented as both resolved and live
- Topics: AB-13.6, AB-13.1, AB-13.3
- Evidence: AB-13.6 carries an unqualified DIVERGENCE: "a SECOND, independent JS consumer (`relay-consumer.js:546`) subscribes to the SAME relay and writes to the SAME pods/NPUB/events/inbox/ path with its own allowlist (`AGENTBOX_RELAY_ALLOWED_PUBKEYS`)... independently of BridgeConfig.allowed_pubkeys here." AB-13.1 N1 says "RESOLVED ADR-2065 (2026-09-05): the Rust spawn_consumer is the sole inbox writer when the pod-bridge daemon runs — RelayConsumer takes writeInbox=false via AGENTBOX_POD_INBOX_WRITER".
- Failure: if AB-13.6 is the live truth (projection missing or env not set), the effective inbox allowlist is the *union* of a Nix-baked, rebuild-only list (F-14 below) and a runtime env list — tightening the governed allowlist does not tighten the actual boundary, and unauthorized events reach governance consumers. If AB-13.1 is right, AB-13.6's divergence label is stale and misleads the next reader. Either way the material contradicts itself about a security boundary.
- Confidence: medium — the contradiction between the two sections is certain; which one is true needs the code
- Marked by authors: no — neither section cross-references or reconciles the other
- Severity: medium

### F-15 — The governing baseline document is unpinned and systematically stale, and a mandatory review trigger has fired unnoticed
- Topics: AB-03.6, AB-01.5, AB-02.13, AB-04.4, AB-01.6, AB-05.13
- Evidence: "the governing doc carries an EMPTY verified_commit (BASELINE-container.md:6), so nothing states the revision its route citations were read at" (AB-03.6). Specific staleness the material itself confirms: ruvector pin 0.3.0 vs `flake.nix:420` = 0.3.3 and a stale flake-size claim (AB-01.5); "ADR-2007 is partial and the wrapper host assertion is substring-based" vs full URL parsing closed 2026-09-05, "confirmed still open" (AB-02.13, BASELINE:244); three dispatch layers claimed vs the decided two (AB-04.4); the PROPOSED supervised-set table gates `sidestr-producer` on `[sidechain.signer].enabled` while the code gates on `[sidechain].enabled` and the schema has no `signer` entry — so the documented off-switch cannot even be expressed (AB-05.13). Separately, AB-01.6 records that "ADR-2006's graphics review trigger has been reached" (wrappers now ship GLX/EGL/Vulkan defaults, BASELINE:257-259) with no review recorded anywhere.
- Failure: an operator resolving an incident by the governing doc follows wrong version pins, wrong gate names and wrong partial/implemented statuses; the ADR-2006-mandated graphics-surface review has been triggered by shipped code and missed.
- Confidence: high on every cited item; "no review occurred" for ADR-2006 is inferred from absence
- Marked by authors: yes in pieces (DOC-DRIFT / DIVERGENCE markers on each item); the aggregate pattern — an unpinned primary governing document — is marked only once, at AB-03.6
- Severity: medium

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Authentication on the nine non-9096 sanctioned LAN publishes.** AB-10.1 N1 asserts "the others carry their own auth" for voice 8443/8444, browsercontainer 5903/8931/9222, gui-tools 5905/9876/9877 and xr 5904, but no auth mechanism for VNC, CDP or the MCP SSE port is described anywhere — while the android overlay explicitly warns "never expose it on 0.0.0.0" for a comparable surface. I need the sidecar configs to judge whether CDP/VNC are actually protected.
2. **Post-boot health re-probing.** `/ready` reads `adapterHealth`, which is populated at `connectAdapters` time (`server.js:1358`); only pods have a runtime probe (`probePodHealth`). Whether ruvector-postgres dying an hour after boot leaves `/ready` at 200, and what restart policies the sidecars carry, cannot be determined from the material.
3. **Durability of the audit chain and execution journal.** Where the events JSONL physically lives (pod volume vs ephemeral path), its retention, and whether any backup covers it — given the hash chain's documented tail-deletion blindness, this decides whether the governance evidence is durable at all.
4. **The actual runtime wiring of `spend-policy.js` / `consumer-payer.js` and `routes/chain-payments.js`** (resolves F-07's self-contradiction), and whether any settlement path — payments, kind-38201, marketplace receipts — consults the authority gate under any action class.
5. **The shipped environment's relay configuration**: whether `AGENTBOX_POD_INBOX_WRITER=false` is actually projected (resolves F-14), and what `NOSTR_RELAYS` contains in production — i.e. whether the single free-tier Cloudflare worker relay is the only path for governance approvals and operator commands, making it an external single point of failure for control.

## agentbox / premortem

# Post-mortem — agentbox estate

Scope note: reconstructed entirely from the estate's own diagrams-as-code corpus and the markers it carries (Open, Divergence, Tension, Debt, Drift). Every anchor below is a citation the material itself gives; nothing was re-verified against code beyond those citations. The three root causes are the highest-likelihood candidates the corpus itself documents as unresolved security/custody exposures; the incident is consistent with any one of them, and all three share a common trigger — code execution as `devuser` inside the agentbox container, which the surface census (28 MCP servers, skills, hooks, agent Bash, a devuser-appended `WORKSPACE/.cargo/bin`) makes a routine event rather than an exotic one.

---

### R-1 — Default-off role isolation leaves the host Docker socket drivable by every agent process: container escape to host root
- Topics: AB-06.13, AB-02.24, AB-05.16, AB-06.8
- Chain of events: 1) `[security].role_isolation` ships false (agentbox.toml:2227, agentbox.toml.schema.json:1981-1984). 2) Every flag-off boot runs `chmod o+rw` on the host socket (entrypoint-unified.sh:592) and chowns `/run/secrets` to devuser (entrypoint-unified.sh:388-390). 3) Any process running as devuser — MCP servers, skills, hooks, agent Bash — is compromised or prompt-injected. 4) It speaks to the host docker socket, bound rw into the container (docker-compose.override.yml:117). 5) The daemon is root on the host; the host filesystem is mounted into a new container; the vault corpus, client data and every key on the host are read, modified or destroyed. Every in-container custody control is void at that point.
- Evidence: custody design §0 bypass 1, quoted at AB-06.13 — "The Docker daemon is root on the host, so while devuser can drive it every in-container boundary is void". OPEN R2 (docker-compose.override.yml:116-117): the socket is the HOST inode, earlier boots left it o+rw, only a host-side `chmod 0660` narrows it; the entrypoint under the flag "stops widening it but never narrows it" (AB-02.24). The manifest comment still advises leaving the flag off until the rehearsal passes (agentbox.toml:2220-2226, AB-05.16).
- Warning signs visible today: the OPEN R2 note itself; the shipped-default-OFF branch in AB-02.24; the GET-only `docker-read-proxy` mitigation existing only for flag-on devuser shells.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

### R-2 — All key material is same-uid readable: one devuser compromise yields the sovereign signing key, the bridge key and the AoE token
- Topics: AB-11.16, AB-11.2, AB-02.2, AB-02.24, AB-13.14
- Chain of events: 1) Code executes as devuser (same trigger as R-1, or any of the co-resident MCP/skill processes). 2) It reads the profile key file holding the `did:nostr` private key (0600 under devuser's own home, agent-identity.js:164-166). 3) It reads `/run/secrets/nostr.key` (0400 devuser in the flag-off branch, entrypoint-unified.sh:1082-1091; the secrets directory itself is chowned devuser 0700 flag-off, entrypoint-unified.sh:388-390). 4) It reads the AoE daemon token state file. 5) With isolation off, every supervised program additionally inherits PID 1's env, which "still carries every `.env` key" (entrypoint-unified.sh:411, :1221) — and the flag-off `identity.env` source (entrypoint-unified.sh:1063-1064) is the path the :1066-1069 note implies carries `AGENTBOX_NSEC`/`AGENTBOX_BRIDGE_SK` when isolation is off. 6) The attacker signs NIP-98 as the box's did, signs colloquy events under the sovereign identity via the publish-on-behalf path (AB-13.14), drives AoE with the token, and impersonates the pod to pods WAC and federation peers.
- Evidence: AB-11.16 Divergence, verbatim: "a 0600 file is a same-uid boundary only. Every agentbox process runs as devuser, so co-resident code can read the agent key, the bridge key and the AoE daemon token alike. Per-process isolation is named future work in GOVERNANCE-capabilities item 5."
- Warning signs visible today: that Divergence note; `nostr.key` ownership under flag-off; the identity.env source into PID 1; AB-09.1's census of 28 MCP servers as the devuser attack surface.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The host `~/.claude` credential bind is read-write in-container: host OAuth tokens are stealable and poisonable through claude-cred-sync's merge
- Topics: AB-06.11, AB-06.3, AB-05.15, AB-07.11
- Chain of events: 1) In-container code (any devuser process) reaches `/var/lib/agentbox/host-claude`, mounted read-write (docker-compose.override.yml:89; AB-06.3: host `~/.claude` "read AND written"). 2) It reads the host's `.credentials.json`, exfiltrating live OAuth refresh tokens for the host's Claude account. 3) Optionally it writes a token record with a far-future `expiresAt`; cred-sync's per-token merge takes "later expiresAt wins whole" (cred_sync.rs:32-33) and writes back to whichever side changed within its 2 s poll (cred_sync.rs:104-109, :131-135), converging the HOST's `~/.claude` to attacker-influenced token records.
- Evidence: AB-06.11 Open — "the credential bind stays writable both ways, so a compromised in-container tool could still modify the host's `.claude`; only the configuration-read path was closed (docker-compose.override.yml:84-85)"; the same residual is repeated as Q43 (BASELINE-container.md:213, AB-07.11); merge semantics at AB-05.15.
- Warning signs visible today: the Open note; the design's own framing that the bind exists "solely for credential convergence" while remaining bidirectionally writable.
- Confidence: high (write path stated; the read-side exposure follows from a devuser-readable rw mount — only the material's wording as "modify" makes the exfiltration half inferred)
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Forged X-Agentbox-Pubkey stamps pass as did:nostr identities on non-NIP-98 hops
- Topics: AB-10.14, AB-11.14, AB-03.4, AB-03.5
- Evidence: agent-event-auth.js:31-39 (quoted in AB-10.14/AB-11.14): path 2 trusts the header whenever Authorization is not `Nostr` — nip07-session or break-glass hops, where the proxy replaced Authorization with the route bearer — and "server.js binds 0.0.0.0 in-container so any holder of MANAGEMENT_API_KEY clears the gate and can then forge the stamp". Bearer is also the unconditional operator credential at lib/authz.js:188.
- Failure: any sibling container on visionclaw_network (browsercontainer, gui-tools, voice, xr, openmed, android, cloudflared — AB-06.1) holding MANAGEMENT_API_KEY posts `/v1/agent-events/*` as any did:nostr it names; `reconcileSourceUrn` (agent-event-auth.js:169) passes because the claimed urn matches the forged stamp.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence, stated in-source)

### F-02 — `payment_settlement` is declared zero-tolerance but no route passes it to the authority gate
- Topics: AB-05.13
- Evidence: "payment_settlement is declared zero-tolerance with its own task properties (agentbox.toml:1134, :1182) but no route passes that action class to the authority gate; mandate_revoke is the only class any route names (routes/llm-marketplace.js:473)".
- Failure: a settlement/receipt path executes an irreversible payment action with no signed 31402/31403 approval loop, despite the manifest's declared policy.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-03 — LLM grant Deny and Revocation kinds are never federated: peers keep revoked grants live
- Topics: AB-13.16, AB-13.12
- Evidence: agentbox.toml:313 and the AB-13.16 note — kinds 38303 (Deny) and 38305 (Revocation) are "deliberately NOT federated (point-to-point only), so a peer that federates the other four kinds never learns of a denial or a revocation".
- Failure: the operator revokes a compromised grant; every mesh peer federating 38300/38301/38302/38304 keeps honouring it — unauthorized compute and billing continue off-box after revocation.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

### F-04 — An empty NIP-98 allowlist at the proxy admits ANY valid Nostr identity
- Topics: AB-10.3, AB-10.2
- Evidence: the `pubkeyAllowed` branch (proxy.mjs:729-730, AB-10.3): "allowlist non-empty and pubkey missing → ok false … else ok true" — rejection requires a non-empty list; `FILE_CONFIG` defaults `allowedPubkeys: []` when the config file is absent (proxy.mjs:396), and the config file is written best-effort at boot (entrypoint-unified.sh:2155).
- Failure: a deployment whose projected nip98-proxy-config.json is missing or whose allowlists are empty accepts any schnorr-valid key; the default route then swaps in the AoE daemon token (proxy.mjs:1037-1045) and a stranger drives the session board.
- Confidence: medium — the code branch is stated; whether the live config ever ships empty is not (scenario inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — The AoE daemon token cannot be revoked by deleting its file, and its reader is hand-copied in four places
- Topics: AB-10.6, AB-10.11
- Evidence: `readAoeToken` keeps a last-good cache, "NEVER caches null on error" (proxy.mjs:323-329); the SECURITY-profiles custody row: "deleting the state file alone is not a revocation mechanism, last-good cache persists" (AB-10.6). The reader is "DUPLICATED VERBATIM (modulo fs accessor) across four runtime consumers" under a KEEP IN SYNC comment (aoe-seed-sessions.mjs:494-497, AB-10.11).
- Failure: the token leaks; the operator deletes `serve.url` believing this revokes it; all four consumers keep presenting the cached token until restarted; a future fix lands in one copy only.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (custody row / keep-in-sync debt)

### F-06 — The audit chain cannot detect deletion at its tail
- Topics: AB-03.12
- Evidence: "INVARIANT deletion at the tail is the one tamper mode a bare hash chain cannot see (lib/audit-chain.js:14-16)"; verification walks files sorted by name (routes/system.js:67).
- Failure: after a forged or unwanted mandate/authority record is appended, an attacker with write access to the events directory (same-uid, see R-2) deletes the newest `YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl`; the remaining chain verifies and the tamper is invisible.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Invariant limitation)

### F-07 — Mandates can exist as unsigned registry entries the relay never saw
- Topics: AB-11.7, AB-11.9
- Evidence: "the registry write is never blocked purely by a missing relay signer" (routes/mandate.js:152); `isMandateActive` checks only `revoked` and `expires_at` (lib/mandate.js:191); the Divergence note: local registry and relay "can disagree about what is granted".
- Failure: a registry.json entry — or a tampered one, same-uid writable per R-2 — with no signed kind-30078 behind it is treated locally as an active mandate; relay-side revocation cannot touch it.
- Confidence: high (dual-state stated; the tamper route is inferred via R-2)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

### F-08 — Degraded boots still come up under the placeholder identity `did:nostr:local`
- Topics: AB-11.3, AB-11.2
- Evidence: the CLI now exits 1 on mint or persist failure (agent-identity.js:208-224), but "config/entrypoint-unified.sh's `${AGENTBOX_AGENT_DID:-did:nostr:local}` shell fallback is UNCHANGED and still runs unconditionally on that non-zero exit (`|| true`)" (AB-11.3); AB-11.2: a null loadOrMint leaves the caller on `did:nostr:local`.
- Failure: a key-persistence failure (disk full, permissions) yields a booted box that signs events and pod writes as a placeholder identity; attribution across federation and the audit chain is meaningless for that period.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (noted as the remaining gap)

### F-09 — The browser sidecar's vault still authenticates as the house key; K_browser is minted nowhere; the Podkey pin expires 2026-12-24
- Topics: AB-06.12
- Evidence: g5-key-split.json:16-20 — k_browser's pubkey is still null; agentbox.toml:1720 — the nip98 roster still names the house key for the browsercontainer vault; "Until the owner mints it and the dual-admit step lands, the sidecar's flows still authenticate as the house key". podkey.pin:11 — the pinned CI artefact expires 2026-12-24, "after which a fresh fetch needs a new pin".
- Failure: compromise of the least-trusted component (it renders untrusted web) yields house-key-grade signing; after the pin expiry, rebuilds that re-fetch block or tempt an unpinned fetch.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, twice)

### F-10 — Governance decisions are signed and durable even when the ontology write they authorise silently failed
- Topics: AB-04.13
- Evidence: local-process-manager.js:481-487 — "A write failure is recorded as ontology.applied=false and swallowed, never thrown — the signed 31403 stays durable whatever the corpus write managed to do" (ADR-2109 invariant note).
- Failure: an approved promote/demote lands as a signed, relay-visible decision while the vault page edit failed; peers and consumers act on a decision whose effect never existed, and nothing requeues the write.
- Confidence: high (behaviour stated and framed as intended; the divergence it creates is not separately flagged)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Invariant framing only)

### F-11 — Backups enumerate only ruvector-data and the PG dump; the pod store and claude-home volumes are not covered
- Topics: AB-07.8, AB-06.8, AB-06.11
- Evidence: cmd_backup includes `_volume_tar agentbox-ruvector-data, always included` (agentbox.sh:301-304) and a pg_dump (agentbox.sh:314); the manifest records ruvector flags only (agentbox.sh:395-396). Named volumes include `agentbox-solid-data` (docker-compose.yml:174-181) and the external `agentbox-claude-home` (docker-compose.override.yml:186-188); neither appears anywhere in the backup flow.
- Failure: volume or host loss "restores" a box whose Solid pods, DID documents and OAuth state are gone, while the operator believed backups were complete.
- Confidence: medium — inferred from absence in the enumerated backup path; the diagram may be partial
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — Two consumers write the same event inbox under two different allowlists
- Topics: AB-13.6, AB-13.1
- Evidence: AB-13.6 Divergence: the JS RelayConsumer (relay-consumer.js:546) subscribes to the same relay and writes the same `pods/NPUB/events/inbox/` path "with its own allowlist (AGENTBOX_RELAY_ALLOWED_PUBKEYS) … independently of BridgeConfig.allowed_pubkeys here"; AB-13.1: the Rust writer is sole only when the pod-bridge daemon runs, and the JS consumer is "narrowed, not deleted".
- Failure: the two boundaries disagree on an author — one refuses and audits while the other commits; the "relay OK is not an authorised commit" guarantee holds on only one of the two paths, and `podBridgeEnabled=false` silently re-widens the JS writer.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

### F-13 — Credentials ride query strings on WS upgrades, and the break-glass bearer's expiry/scope bounds default to OFF
- Topics: AB-10.9, AB-10.3
- Evidence: WS upgrades accept `?access_token=` or `?bearer=` (proxy.mjs:1111-1116); "break-glass bearer accepted via ?access_token= or ?bearer= on WS upgrades over the LAN" (INGRESS-identity known divergences bullet 4, AB-10.9); ADR-2027's expiry and scope are "both bounds default-OFF and opt-in" (AB-10.3, AB-11.16).
- Failure: the shared bearer appears in proxy/access logs and browser history on the LAN; while unconfigured for expiry and scope, a single leak is an indefinite, full-surface NIP-98 bypass.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

### F-14 — Flipping role_isolation on (the fix for R-1) breaks signing for JunkieJarvis, the mirror hook and dream-engine
- Topics: AB-05.16, AB-02.24
- Evidence: agentbox.toml:2220-2226 comment: "until the W3b consumer cutover lands, JunkieJarvis, the mirror hook and dream-engine cannot sign under the flag"; the surrounding Drift note warns other statements in that same comment are stale, but presents this condition as the new one the old text lacked.
- Failure: an operator responding to a socket incident enables the flag; three governance/observability producers stop signing while the box otherwise boots green — the forum agent goes quiet and session mirroring stops, with no boot-level failure.
- Confidence: medium — the source is a comment the material itself flags as partly stale; which parts hold at HEAD is unverified
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-15 — Capability scopes are declarative only: nothing routes MCP tools, consultant actions or background jobs through them
- Topics: AB-11.12
- Evidence: note on CapabilityScope: "this classification is declarative. Nothing forces a code-mode sub-call, MCP plugin tool, consultant action or background job through a scope — the acknowledged bypasses" (cross-referenced to AB-14/AB-15).
- Failure: a mandate narrowed by trust class (e.g. network-only) is honoured nowhere in the path an MCP tool or consultant call actually takes; blast-radius narrowing exists on paper only.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

---

Defects noticed but not ranked into the fifteen above, reported for completeness: an explicit `ONTOLOGY_PAGES_DIR` still overrides `VAULT_PAGES` for legacy consumers (AB-02.14, marked Divergence — agents may index a stale corpus tree); `nostr-gateway`'s replay guard rests on a same-uid-writable `executed.json` inside a 50 h since-window where NIP-59 randomises `created_at` by up to 48 h (AB-13.5/AB-13.13 — replay resistance degrades to the durable set alone; inferred); the linked-objects viewer bundle and docs are auth-skip and reachable on the LAN through the voice origin's direct `/lo` and `/docs` routes on published 8444 (AB-12.7 Caddyfile:82-87, server.js:236-272 — bundle-level exposure only, low); JS `stableStringify` content addressing is not byte-parity with VisionClaw byte hashing (AB-11.5, marked DOC-DRIFT — cross-system provenance identifiers can disagree); the tmux rune fallback hint installs the upstream `aka-rider/rune` v1.4.0 rather than the pinned DreamLab fork (AB-02.11, marked Debt — an unpinned binary run in-box; supply-chain risk inferred). Confidence on each: medium or lower, as noted.

## agentbox#2 / critical

### F-01 — The fail-closed approval pipeline is unreachable by almost all real traffic
- Topics: AB-14.1, AB-14.2, AB-14.14
- Evidence: The pipeline implements a fail-closed approval state machine (NEVER_FAIL_OPEN, `agent-action-pipeline.js:36`; contract-tested in `tests/contract/agent-action-pipeline.contract.spec.js`), but AB-14.1's DIVERGENCE1 records GOVERNANCE-capabilities.md:276-281 naming "no single policy decision point" and shows that only `POST /v1/tasks` is wired through `dispatchTaskSpawn` (`action-plane.js:252`); direct tool calls, code-mode, ACI shell, consultants, dream, beads and alternate harnesses reach EXEC "with no interceptor". AB-14.2's own note: the state machine "is never reached by real traffic".
- Failure: any agent-initiated side effect other than task spawn — file mutation via code-mode, shell via the ACI allowlist, paid consultant calls — executes with no approval receipt, no capability token and no journal entry; the kind-31402/31403 approval UI can never gate it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE1, "TOP OPEN RISK")

### F-02 — Shipped default is a same-uid secrets free-for-all
- Topics: AB-16.1, AB-16.7, AB-16.9
- Evidence: AB-16.1 N4: with role_isolation off (the shipped default), "every supervised program, MCP server, skill and agent runs as devuser, so each can read every other's 0600 key file, the AoE daemon token and the mirror key". AB-16.7: with the flag off a co-resident devuser process SUCCEEDS reading the profile key, the AoE `serve.url` token and `/run/secrets/nostr.key` (0400 devuser, `entrypoint-unified.sh:1082-1091`).
- Failure: one compromised or prompt-injected MCP server or skill reads the bridge signing key and the operator-derived mirror child key, signs as the box, and exfiltrates through the one non-LAN egress (nostr-live-mirror, AB-16.11) — with no OS boundary to stop it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-03 — Nightly repo content egresses to a vendor by default with no accepted record
- Topics: AB-23.15, AB-23.2, AB-24.14
- Evidence: `agentbox.toml:2191` (also cited as `:2306` elsewhere) keeps `llm_provider = "zai"` as the default, so "nightly repository content leaves the LAN on every unattended night" (AB-23.15 Debt); the egress register's zai-night row carries marker "no-accepted-record — nothing accepts the corpus leaving" (`config/egress-policy.json:254-268`). The LAN-only Loom path is opt-in.
- Failure: every night, the full content of each nominated repository is POSTed to a third-party API with no accepted record and no redaction step shown; an NDA'd repo or one containing secrets-shaped material egresses unattended and invisibly.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt; egress-register marker)

### F-04 — The break-glass bearer equals BRIDGE_TOKEN, which is handed to the external voice stack
- Topics: AB-16.9, AB-16.6, AB-27.7
- Evidence: AB-16.9 C3: the proxy break-glass bearer (`NIP98_PROXY_ALLOW_BEARER`, `proxy.mjs:131`) is "still the same value as BRIDGE_TOKEN until Q4". AB-27.7: the voice stack passes `KYUTAI_LLM_API_KEY=${BRIDGE_TOKEN}` to the Unmute backend (`agentbox.sh:2445`), and `voice-stack/unmute` is "external, not vendored". AB-16.6: while configured, that single shared secret "bypasses NIP-98 entirely" on port 9096 and via `?access_token=` on WS upgrades, with downstream attribution a sentinel placeholder pubkey.
- Failure: compromise of the external, non-vendored voice stack yields the proxy's break-glass token — a full identity bypass credential whose uses are logged only by fingerprint.
- Confidence: medium; inferred (the material states the value-sameness and the voice substitution separately; the linkage is mine)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the sameness is noted as context in C3, never flagged or connected to the voice stack)

### F-05 — Spend is accounted in three stores that never reconcile, and the zero-tolerance spend class is never gated
- Topics: AB-15.6, AB-14.11, AB-15 closing drift
- Evidence: AB-15.6 DEBT: "spend is accounted in three stores that never reconcile: the flock-guarded cost_cap ledger file (`cost_cap/ledger.rs:75`), the pod-held sats balance (`routes/payments.js:205`), and the marketplace token_budget (`llm-marketplace.js:85`). No surface sums them." AB-14.11 DEBT: the classification table names `payment_settlement` zero-tolerance (`agentbox.toml:1074`) "but no production path passes that actionClass… `routes/payments.js` never reaches `authority.js:226`". Closing drift: sidestr-wallet ships a permissive SpendPolicy, "leaving the authority gate… to the caller, which nothing in this topic yet supplies".
- Failure: a runaway agent spends across three ledgers with no single number to detect it and no approval gate ever firing; sats withdrawals, deposits and token grants each bypass the zero-tolerance class the manifest promises.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT twice, Drift)

### F-06 — Cross-repo identity recovery hangs on one append-only JSONL file with no recovery path
- Topics: AB-17.4, AB-17.6, AB-17.10
- Evidence: for activity/thing crossings the local id is "a FRESH sha256-12 with no algebraic inverse. Recovery is store-dependent, so store loss is identity loss. No recovery receipt exists"; the store is `JsonlUrnMappingStore` at `BC20_URN_MAPPING_PATH` or `/var/lib/agentbox/code-harness/bc20-urn-mappings.jsonl` (`bc20-provenance-bridge.js:346`), last-writer-wins. `roundTrips()` proves the algebra only "against a FRESH IN-MEMORY store… not a durability or recovery proof" (`:307`); PROTOCOL-registry's "Durable translation" row (persistence, replay, round-trip, recovery receipts) is entirely open.
- Failure: corruption or loss of one JSONL file permanently orphans every execution/kg mapping; VisionClaw identifiers can never be resolved back to agentbox URNs and cross-repo provenance continuity breaks with no rebuild path.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-07 — Default skill routing sends operator prompts (and synced skill descriptions) to an external vendor
- Topics: AB-22.2
- Evidence: "router jev is the default at this revision (`agentbox.toml:1022`), so a turn that routes at all and misses the cascade leaves the container. The per-project gate is deferred" (DEBT); the local BM25 cascade stage is off by default (`agentbox.toml:1038`), so prompts escalate to "ONE Choice question over every routable skill description" at TypeSafe System One. OPEN: whether ADR-2090's acceptance of routing-prompt egress "also covers the descriptions of the operator's synced claude.ai skills, now sent as candidate rubrics".
- Failure: by default, every non-slash prompt over `min_prompt_chars` 24 is transmitted off-box to an external LLM along with the operator's synced claude.ai skill rubrics; pasted secrets or private context egress with no per-project gate.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, OPEN)

### F-08 — Enabling the security flag silently stops forum governance, and the planned fix is structurally blocked
- Topics: AB-23.20, AB-14.16
- Evidence: AB-23.20 Open: under role_isolation the key file belongs to ab-identity while dream-engine runs as devuser, and `load_key` "treats the refusal as fail-open and skips" (`governance.rs:645-653`) — "the forum governance round trip stops without an error". Tension: the port's loader refuses kinds 31400-31405 to anyone (`acl.rs:65-73`) while the dream engine publishes 31400/31402 with the JunkieJarvis key (`governance.rs:9-10`) and ADR-2122 still plans the W3b cutover (`ADR-2122:224-225`) — "As written, the port can never sign what AB-23.17 sends". AB-14.16 OPEN1: under the flag, JunkieJarvis, forum suggestions, colloquy publish and the gateway "fail closed until the W3b cutover" (`ADR-2122:218-219`).
- Failure: the boot rehearsal flips role_isolation; from that night the forum panel and cases are no longer published or withdrawn, governance posts signed as JunkieJarvis stop, the gateway exits keyless and the mirror falls back to a throwaway sender — all silently — and the W3b remedy cannot work without breaking the port's own ACL invariant.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, Tension — the combined silent-stop is spelled out in the Open; the W3b contradiction in the Tension)

### F-09 — No proven secret backup or recovery path exists
- Topics: AB-16.8, AB-16.12
- Evidence: the BackedUp state note: "PARTIAL and unverified. The backup script invokes ordinary zip and unzip integrity testing with NO encryption flags and no explicit umask or chmod… The script was not run and no backup contents were inspected." The replacement age tool is packaged but "The active container has not been rebuilt with this source change" (AB-16.12 N2) and SelfTest proves restore "on SYNTHETIC data only, never on a real secret" (N4).
- Failure: host or volume loss leaves the sovereign operator key, bridge key and role keys unrecoverable — and because the relay allowlist is baked at nix build (AB-16.8: publisher revocation needs a full rebuild), compromised identities cannot be promptly re-established or revoked either.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (PARTIAL/unverified; N2/N4)

### F-10 — Three failed posts silently disable the dream-night audit journal
- Topics: AB-23.2, AB-23.19
- Evidence: "Fail-open: three consecutive failed posts open a breaker and the rest of the session runs unjournalled (`journal.rs:30, 227-231`)" — while ADR-2071's premise is that every turn and side effect of the unattended night is recorded via `POST /v1/exec/record`, and AB-23.19 shows nights are expected to run with the management API deliberately stopped, i.e. the very dependency the journal posts to. No alert path for breaker-open is described.
- Failure: transient management-API instability during the 01:00-05:00 UTC window (or the sanctioned api-down night) turns off the audit trail for the remainder of the night; unattended agent actions execute with no per-side-effect record and nothing notifies the operator.
- Confidence: high (behavior stated; absence of alerting inferred from its absence in the material)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (described as designed fail-open, unflagged)

### F-11 — The Loom serves a known-stale corpus and promotion is blocked by the baked binary
- Topics: AB-24.13, AB-24.6
- Evidence: the next generation `visionGraph@015ca2c1f` "is built, promoted and verified, but the door still serves `visionGraph@ae913f93…`, and the vault 0.1.0 baked into the agentbox image predates the portable-records file, so it cannot produce a promotable bundle" (`ADR-141:138-147`); the fix reaches a running vault "only at the next image rebuild". Invariant ADR-141 D2: a stale generation "is STILL SERVED, honestly labelled — staleness is carried, never enforced"; ADR-140 §1.3 measured a bundle stale by a month.
- Failure: every grounded surface — [ONTOLOGY] breadcrumbs, condense, loom-selected dream prompts, email-gateway reasoning — answers from a corpus known to lag the vault, silently degrading grounded correctness until someone rebuilds the image.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt; D2 invariant)

### F-12 — Shared knowledge can never be retired: terminal lifecycle states are unreachable
- Topics: AB-22.7, AB-22.8
- Evidence: OPEN note: "no code path in this crate or colloquy-mcp's six verbs currently WRITES UnitStatus::Retired or Superseded; only tests set them directly. The 'signed decision' retirement door ADR-2085/ADR-2086 describe is not yet wired to an MCP verb" (`graduation.rs:400` test only). Disputed units "are still servable and are ranked down, never hidden" (`unit.rs:269-271`).
- Failure: wrong or superseded knowledge confirmed into the shared tier has no production removal path; `query` keeps serving disputed content indefinitely and the ADR-2086 principal-collapse guarantee comes with no way to undo an admission.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN)

### F-13 — The audit hash chain cannot see tail deletion, and receipt minting fails open
- Topics: AB-14.9
- Evidence: "reorder is equivalent to a splice at the first moved record — deletion at the tail is the ONE mode a bare chain cannot see (`audit-chain.js:13-16`)". Receipt minting: on failure it returns `urn:agentbox:receipt:error:mint-failed` and "never throws" (`receipt-minter.js:58-60`); a crossOutbound failure "is logged to stderr and never blocks the caller" (`:103-112`), so the durable UrnMapping is silently never written.
- Failure: truncating the newest JSONL file after a spend leaves the chain verifying clean while records are gone; a mint outage mints error-URN "receipts" that downstream consumers may treat as valid identifiers, and skipped crossOutbound writes quietly break the F-06 recovery path.
- Confidence: high (properties stated; "treated as valid" is inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated as notes, no marker)

### F-14 — /v1/system always reports the execution plane "declared", even when it is live
- Topics: AB-14.8
- Evidence: DRIFTNOTE: `buildLiveExecutionCoverage()` now pulls a real singleton from `getCoverageSnapshot()`, "but `routes/system.js:26,61` still calls the OLD `buildExecutionCoverage(live)` with `live={}` — `routes/system.js:48-49` reads `options.execution.snapshot` and server.js never supplies one — so GET /v1/system still reports status: declared always, even though a live instance now genuinely exists".
- Failure: the single dashboard for governance-plane liveness shows "declared" regardless; a silent regression to `plane.ready=false` (events adapter off, /v1/tasks answering 503 per AB-14.14) is invisible on the one surface built to show it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT, "partially resolved")

### F-15 — The custom seccomp profile replaces Docker's default with a narrower denylist
- Topics: AB-16.2, AB-16.3
- Evidence: the profile is `defaultAction SCMP_ACT_ALLOW` with one AF_ALG rule plus 46 unconditional syscall names; "explicit Docker security_opt selects it IN PLACE OF the default profile… Docker's default is not automatically stacked". `check-seccomp.sh` enforces exactly that posture — it asserts the profile "STAYS allow-by-default" and verifies the 46 names, locking the narrowing in as a CI invariant.
- Failure: any syscall Docker's stock profile denies but this 46-name list omits (newer sandbox-escape primitives) is permitted in this container; a control that reads as hardening is a regression against the default posture, and the gate prevents reverting to a stronger profile.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — the material references Docker's default profile but does not enumerate it, so the weaker-than-default comparison needs the default denylist to confirm
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the override-not-stacked fact is marked DIVERGENCE/audit-qualified; the weakening consequence is not assessed)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Image vs. source drift.** Several findings turn on whether the running container matches the verified commits — AB-16.12 says the active container "has not been rebuilt" with the backup tool; ADR-2081 fixes are "staged"; ADR statuses range proposed→live. I would need the running image's provenance (which commit it was built from, which gates are actually set) before signing off on anything.
2. **Durability behind the events adapter.** AB-14.7 stops at "dispatch" — where `/v1/tasks` journal envelopes persist, their retention, and append failure semantics (what `ExecutionJournal.append` does to an already-executed action when the writer throws) are not shown.
3. **Docker's default seccomp denylist contents**, to confirm or clear F-15.
4. **Deployed secret values and configuration**: whether `NIP98_PROXY_ALLOW_BEARER` and `BRIDGE_TOKEN` are literally the same secret in the running environment (F-04), whether the voice/unmute stack is deployed, and whether relay/egress endpoints match the placeholders or are live addresses.
5. **Concurrency and multi-instance assumptions**: single-writer guarantees for the beads SQLite store, the bc20 JSONL mapping store and the LLM orderbook — and whether any of these stores, the pod balance, or the journal assume exactly one agentbox container.

## agentbox#2 / premortem

# Post-mortem — custody-boundary failure: client-confidential material left the operator's control and could not be reliably deleted, revoked or attributed afterwards

Scope note: everything below is drawn only from the diagram corpus at the cited revisions. Where a diagram does not assert ordering or concurrency, none is claimed here. "Inferred" marks reasoning beyond what the material states.

---

## Most likely root causes

### R-1 — One compromised devuser process yields the entire key estate, because the shipped default puts every secret in one uid's reach and there is no revocation path
- Topics: AB-16.1, AB-16.4, AB-16.5, AB-16.6, AB-16.7, AB-16.8, AB-16.9, AB-16.10, AB-16.12, AB-16.13, AB-16.14, AB-23.20
- Chain of events:
  1. Any single supervised program, MCP server, skill or consultant CLI is subverted (prompt injection into a skill, a malicious npm dependency, a compromised consultant CLI path — vector inferred). It runs as devuser, uid 1000, like every other program.
  2. It reads the bridge key at `/run/secrets/nostr.key` (0400 devuser, entrypoint-unified.sh:1082-1091), every per-agent DID key file (0600, agent-identity.js:165-166), the AoE daemon token, and the mirror child derivation inputs — same-uid reads all succeed (AB-16.7).
  3. It inherits PID 1's environment, so the ROLE variables the flag-off boot leaves ambient — including `JUNKIEJARVIS_PRIVKEY_HEX` (dream-engine reads it from the environment, relay.rs:102-103, with a `.env` fallback on the workspace bind, relay.rs:104-118) — and the 35 DEVUSER_CLASS provider credentials are readable (entrypoint-unified.sh:424, :468; env-classes.json:92, AB-16.14).
  4. With `AGENTBOX_X_ONLY_PUBKEY_HEX` and `AGENTBOX_EMAIL_GATEWAY_TOKEN` from the same environment, it calls `fetch_email_raw` and receives verbatim client mail with real headers (AB-16.10).
  5. It signs as the operator: the gateway's default identity is `'operator'`, so out of the box the gateway signs with the ROOT operator key (gateway.cjs:193, AB-16.5).
  6.containment fails: ADR-2027 rotation/revocation is NOT IMPLEMENTED — no cadence, custodian, incident window or revocation procedure for any register row (AB-16.8); publisher revocation requires a full image rebuild because the relay allowlist is baked at nix build, so the compromise window is one build-deploy cycle (AB-16.8, ADR-2027 Context); and the age-encrypted backup tool is not in the running container (AB-16.12 N2).
- Evidence: AB-16.1 N4 ("role_isolation OFF, the shipped default — a same-uid boundary... each can read every other's 0600 key file, the AoE daemon token and the mirror key"); AB-16.7 (PEER read SUCCEEDS ×3 under the flag-off branch); AB-16.7 DIVERGENCE quoting GOVERNANCE-capabilities item 5 ("a process running as the same devuser can still read the token file"); AB-16.9 (every custody row: custodian UNCONFIRMED, rotation cadence UNCONFIRMED); AB-16.14 DEBT (Q8 pending).
- Warning signs visible today: the entire AB-16.7/16.13 divergence set; a custody register with zero confirmed custodians; a break-glass bearer that is "still the same value as BRIDGE_TOKEN until Q4" (AB-16.9 C3).
- Confidence: high. Every link after the initial compromise is directly evidenced; only the compromise vector is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Client-confidential session content leaves the box unredacted on the live-mirror path, the estate's only non-LAN egress, with fail-open delivery and no retention contract
- Topics: AB-16.5, AB-16.11, AB-13.9 (referenced), AB-23.17
- Chain of events:
  1. An operator works on client material in a session; selected text (commands, snippets, pasted content) is captured by the per-turn `nostr-live-mirror.cjs` hook.
  2. The hook composes UNREDACTED selected text, wraps it NIP-59 to the derived child identity, and publishes to the cloud worker relay `wss://dreamlab-nostr-relay.solitary-paper-764d.workers.dev` (AB-16.11 M1-M3) — the only agentbox egress to a non-LAN destination in this domain (AB-16.11 Z).
  3. Wrap confidentiality does not contain the exposure: the port returns the mirror child `secret_hex` to devuser (port.rs:635-637, identity-port-acl.json:42), so under R-1's same-uid posture any process can unwrap; ADR-2101:110-111 calls a generic sign-this-payload port a bypass (AB-16.5 TENSION).
  4. Publish errors are swallowed (fail-open), so the only control is the env off-switch `AGENTBOX_LIVE_MIRROR=0`; the digest path is a second, separately-signed egress to a configured summarisation provider under a different gate (AB-16.11 OFF/D1/D2).
  5. Nothing on the relay side is governed: the kind-5 withdrawal machinery covers forum governance cases only (governance.rs:304-331, AB-23.17), not mirrored session content. ADR-2026 remains proposed/partial/INACTIVE: "A shared off/redaction/recipient/retention contract remains open" (AB-16.11 X).
  6. The client learns their confidential session content sits on a third-party relay with no deletion mechanism.
- Evidence: AB-16.11 M1 ("composes UNREDACTED selected text"), M3 (the workers.dev URL), OFF ("fail-open on any publish error"), Y ("there is no single answer to 'is session content leaving this box, and redacted how'"), Z (trust boundary), X (ADR-2026 status); AB-16.5 TENSION (child secret to devuser).
- Warning signs visible today: the trust-boundary node itself; two egress paths with two gates and two encryption postures; the fail-open posture.
- Confidence: medium — the mechanism is directly asserted; whether the mirror was enabled in the incident window is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Deletion does not propagate: erasing the pod copy leaves RuVector agent memory live, and already-egressed copies have no withdrawal path
- Topics: AB-20.7, AB-20.12, AB-16.11, AB-23.17, AB-21.4, AB-21.6
- Chain of events:
  1. The engagement ends; the client requests deletion of their data.
  2. The operator deletes the VisionClaw pod copy via `deleteAgentMemory()`.
  3. There is NO reverse tombstone into RuVector: the RuVector-held agent memory — full values in `memory_entries.value` (AB-20.12), plus aggregates and patterns distilled from trajectories that included the client's work (AB-21.4, AB-21.5) — persists and keeps surfacing through `memory_search`, `memory_hybrid_search` and the feed_retrieval re-rank (AB-21.6).
  4. There is no point-in-time RuVector backup (SQLite-only `backup-sqlite.sh`), no cross-store consistent restore, no RPO/RTO for memory (AB-20.7 D5).
  5. Mirror copies from R-2 persist on the cloud relay; the withdrawal sweep does not reach them (AB-23.17).
  6. The client's data is demonstrably still in production memory after a deletion commitment — the relationship-ending failure.
- Evidence: AB-20.7 note, verbatim: "the VisionClaw Solid Pod deleteAgentMemory() has NO REVERSE TOMBSTONE into RuVector, so deleting the pod copy does not revoke the RuVector-held agent memory. No point-in-time RuVector backup exists... no cross-store consistent restore, RPO or RTO for memory today. Cross-reference docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md before designing any right-to-erasure flow."
- Warning signs visible today: the D5 note itself; the LEARNING-memory invariant set contains no erasure invariant; the mirror has no retention contract (AB-16.11 X).
- Confidence: high that the gap exists as described; inferred that it was this incident's trigger.
- Severity: high

## agentbox#3 / critical

### F-01 — The host Docker socket is mounted and world-accessible; the container-side fix only "narrows" it, and the boot continues where the ADR says fail
- Topics: AB-36 (intro), AB-36.7
- Evidence: The material states the socket "only closes under the flag, and only fully once the host narrows the socket's mode" (AB-36 intro). `docker-compose.override.yml:116-117` — "The socket is a bind of the host inode that earlier boots widened, and skipping the chmod under the flag does not narrow it". `config/entrypoint-unified.sh:605` — the flag-on boot can only *detect and record* `degraded:docker-socket`, while `ADR-2122...md:142` *counts* `degraded:docker-socket` as a failure. The host rehearsal half records the mode "without gating on it" (`scripts/activation/role-isolation-rehearsal.host.sh:18-19`).
- Failure: Every process in the container runs as devuser with the flag off (the shipped state); any of them — including any AI agent tool — talks to the Docker daemon, which is root on the host: container escape, host filesystem access, and every role boundary in AB-36 bypassed from outside. Even flag-on, the boot logs "degraded" and continues rather than failing as the ADR requires.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, R2) — but the boot-continues-vs-ADR-says-fail contradiction is not reconciled anywhere.

### F-02 — Role isolation ships off, so in production every secret reaches every process, including `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY`
- Topics: AB-36 (intro), AB-36.3, AB-36.6
- Evidence: `[security].role_isolation` "ships **false**" (`agentbox.toml:2227`). With it off, `/run/secrets` is "chown 1000:1000, chmod 0700 as before… devuser's /run/secrets/nostr.key is written as today" (`entrypoint-unified.sh:388`) and the devuser chown of the secrets volume is skipped *only* under the flag (`entrypoint-unified.sh:548`). 35 DEVUSER_CLASS credentials, "among them provider API keys, `BRIDGE_TOKEN` and `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY`, remain in the agent environment by exception Q8" (`env-classes.json:90`, `ADR-2122...md:158-159`); the scrub is explicitly a denylist, not an allowlist (AB-36.6), so future secrets default to staying ambient.
- Failure: A compromised or merely mistaken agent tool reads the management API key from its own environment and drives the management API (port 9090) — payments routes included — with full authority, plus the bridge token and provider keys. The X-1 custody boundary that AB-36 exists to draw is absent in the shipped configuration.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open on the flag; Debt on Q8) — the shipped-off default is presented as staging, not as the exposure it is.

### F-03 — The isolation boundary is incomplete even when switched on: every legacy twin stays devuser-readable, including a spend-capable key, "until a manual purge"
- Topics: AB-34.3, AB-36.3, AB-36.1
- Evidence: "a spend-capable key stays on the host-visible bind in both modes until a manual purge" (`ADR-2122...md:212-215`); with the flag off — "which is how it ships" — the faucet still defaults to and the live manifest still sets the workspace path (`run-faucet.sh:27`, `agentbox.toml:1638`); `role-accounts.json:78` names the workspace file as the legacy twin; AB-36.3's own invariant concedes the twins "are never deleted and stay devuser-readable by design until a manual purge".
- Failure: The owner flips the flag, the rehearsal passes, and the treasury key that pays DREAM and sats — plus every other file-backed role secret's twin — is still readable by devuser, i.e. by every agent process. One read exfiltrates a spend-capable key across a boundary the organisation believes is closed.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension in AB-34.3; conceded in AB-36.3's invariant prose) — marked, but as a residual rather than as a voiding of the whole control.

### F-04 — The settlement chain's entire trust rests on one signer key, with no parent anchor, and in the shipped mode that key is readable by every process in the container
- Topics: AB-32 (intro, AB-32.4), AB-34.1, AB-34.2, AB-36 (intro)
- Evidence: "one signer" (`sidechain/README.md:10`); the key lives at `/var/lib/agentbox/secrets/sidestr-dreamlab.key` (`sidechain/README.md:25`) and the producer runs as devuser when the flag is off (`flake.nix:2947`), reading that at-rest path (`run-producer.sh:70`) from a volume chowned to devuser in that mode (`entrypoint-unified.sh:548` context, AB-36.3). "Neither chain is anchored: no producer writes checkpoints into its parent" (`config/sidechain/README.md:13-18`). The mirror trust rule accepts anything the signer's key matches and only forbids the mirror running *ahead* (AB-34.2); the mirror program itself runs as devuser (`flake.nix:2777`), holding the Pages git checkout.
- Failure: Compromise of any one devuser process yields the single signing key: it can produce blocks, sign kind-33333 tips and the kind-3500 document, and push the public mirror — a silently substituted chain with no parent checkpoint to contradict it. Conversely, loss of that one key kills the chain outright (the k-of-n federation of AB-35 is unbuilt). The compound is the finding: single key + no anchor + ambient readability.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — the no-anchor drift and the flag-off ambient state are each marked; the concentration of the chain's whole trust in one readable key is nowhere problematised.

### F-05 — The mainnet-containment story is asserted, not enforced: the genesis commits no containment fields, the p21 receipt is existence-only, and a prior record already once claimed this containment was "architecturally enforced" when it was not built
- Topics: AB-32.2, AB-32.3, AB-31.9
- Evidence: ADR-2103 D3 claims parent/headerProfile/currencyPin/cashOut/pegConfirmations/refundBlocks are committed as a `pin:` record in the genesis coinbase (`ADR-2103...md:73-74`); the seal established upstream commits only four things — chain id, pegs, genesisTime, signer's witness — and "every containment field" is NOT committed (`ADR-2103...md:207-208`); the pin is UNBUILT and depends on an unfiled upstream proposal (`:210-214`, Open at `:213`). CI case 3 "enforces only that a mainnet parent carries a `p21Receipt` field. Resolving the receipt is NOT checked", and the level-1-on-mainnet refusal and refusing node are unbuilt (`:229-230`, Debt). The record itself concedes a prior claim of "architecturally enforced" containment was false while unbuilt (`sovereign-settlement.md:60`, corrected at `:329-330`).
- Failure: `cashOut false` and `currencyPin` — the properties that keep real money out — bind only via the git-committed document, not via anything a validator replays from genesis. When value is at stake, a modified document replays to the same genesisHash, and a mainnet parent with an unresolvable receipt passes CI. This estate has already demonstrated it will record this containment as enforced before building it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, Debt, Open, and the correction invariant in AB-31.9)

### F-06 — The isolation boundary fails open at boot: a missing roles config or plan silently downgrades a flag-on boot to the un-isolated shape
- Topics: AB-36.2, AB-36.3
- Evidence: `READY{"ab_role_isolation_ready — roles config and plan present (role-custody.sh:59)"}` → no → "ROLE-ISOLATION-UNAVAILABLE: boot as flag off (`entrypoint-unified.sh:357`)" → `exec supervisord` with today's config. The material frames this as "says so loudly". Delivery likewise "fails open with a counted, grep-able marker" (`role-custody.sh:11-14`). Nothing refuses to start.
- Failure: A partially baked image, a corrupted `/etc/agentbox/role-secrets.tsv`, or a lost `/etc/supervisord.roles.conf` boots with the flag *on* but the old shape: role secrets stay in PID 1's environment, `/run/secrets` is chowned to devuser, and every role program runs as devuser — the full pre-X-1 exposure — while the operator believes isolation is active. The only signal is a log line.
- Confidence: medium — the downgrade path is explicit; the likelihood of artefact absence is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — presented as a designed loud fallback, not as a fail-open on a security boundary.

### F-07 — The https-bridge forwards to a plaintext HTTP target whose address is guessed, with a hard-coded LAN fallback IP
- Topics: AB-30.4
- Evidence: "detectGatewayIP() if HOST_IP unset — `ip route | grep default` (`https-proxy.js:21-28`), falls back to 192.168.0.51 on any failure"; the proxy then does `http.request` "forwards method/headers, adds x-forwarded-proto https" (`https-proxy.js:193-203`). The bridge's purpose is browser access to the management API (module doc, `https-proxy.js:3-4`), which authorises with `Authorization: Bearer` (AB-30.3).
- Failure: On any detection failure — changed routing, missing `ip`, minimal images — every forwarded request, headers including any bearer token the browser sends, goes in cleartext HTTP to whatever 192.168.0.51 is on that LAN today. On someone else's network that is a credential exfiltration to an uncontrolled host.
- Confidence: medium — the fallback and header forwarding are stated; that credentials actually traverse the bridge is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — the fallback IP is described without comment.

### F-08 — The flag-on path is not deployable end-to-end: the gateway holds no key and signs nothing, the only activation receipt is STAGED with the flag off and predates rules it would now fail
- Topics: AB-36.4, AB-36.7
- Evidence: "Under the flag today the gateway holds no key and so signs nothing until then [W3b]" (`ADR-2122...md:224-225`, Open consumer cutover; `role-accounts.json:41`). The committed receipt is STAGED with flag false (`x1-rehearsal-20261003T090708Z.json:4`, `:8`), from an earlier commit (`:16`), with a uid table "derived from /etc/agentbox.toml" (`:26`) that the rehearsal at this revision would reject — it "requires the baked table and plan, with no derived numbering" (`role-isolation-rehearsal.sh:41-43`). Activation moves only on a flag-on PASS from the owner's rebuild (`ADR-2122...md:135-143`).
- Failure: The remediation for F-01/F-02/F-03 cannot complete today. An operator who flips the flag breaks the gateway's signing outright, and the estate possesses no rehearsal evidence that would satisfy its own current activation rule — the one receipt on file fails it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, Drift)

### F-09 — The "pinned and validated" upstream-code guarantee has already been violated by its own maintainers, the escape hatch is unbounded, and the business-facing assurance overstates both
- Topics: AB-34.1, AB-34 (business)
- Evidence: `upstream-pins:5-7` — bump a line "only after the chain has been restarted and a block produced on that commit"; `upstream-pins:10` — blaketestnode bumped as an "operator-accepted active development head", acceptance rather than a produced block, and "the bake now carries that same commit, so the acceptance is baked into the image as well" (`lib/sidestr-upstream.nix:57-60`). `SIDESTR_ALLOW_UNPINNED=1` runs a workspace checkout at any commit, logs a warning, "never refuses", and "nothing bounds how long it stays set" (`run-producer.sh:84-93`, Debt). Yet the business prose says the code is "fixed into the container image itself, so nothing running inside the box, an agent included, can change the rules the chain is produced by."
- Failure: Consensus-adjacent code reaches the image without the validation the pin file exists to guarantee — the exact failure mode the rule was written against, already normalised once. With the override set, a dirty devuser-writable checkout produces blocks for as long as nobody notices a log line. The stated assurance to the organisation is stronger than the mechanism.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension on the bump; Debt on the override) — the contradiction with the business prose is not.

### F-10 — Production runs ahead of its own governance: proposed and inactive decision records are partially implemented, and the governing baseline still describes programs that do not exist
- Topics: AB-31 (intro, AB-31.4), AB-32.5, AB-34.1, AB-34.5, AB-36 (intro)
- Evidence: All eight settlement decisions were minted `proposed` and the marked sections "join the compliance surface only on ratification" (`GOVERNANCE-capabilities.md:520-522`) — yet the seal, supervised producer, mirror and faucet run live. The proposed baseline table gates `sidestr-producer` on `[sidechain.signer].enabled` and gives a `sidestr-node` on 9097 (`BASELINE-container.md:356-357`); as built, `[sidechain].enabled` gates the JS producer and "there is no `sidestr-node`" (`flake.nix:2934-2958`), with ADR-2103's amendment approving the interim shape "without amending that table" (`ADR-2103...md:233-237`). ADR-2122 is "`proposed` with `activation_status: inactive`" while W3 code ships (AB-36 intro, `ADR-2122...md:224`). PRD-024 question 19 still calls the live second chain "proposed and blocked" (`sovereign-settlement.md:527-530` vs `agentbox.toml:1649`).
- Failure: The compliance surface — the thing an auditor or a new operator reads to know what is enforced — describes a different system from the one running. Gates written against the baseline validate a fiction; the "no gate may cite a proposed invariant" rule is already moot in practice.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — each individual drift is marked; the pattern (unratified decisions running in production) is not.

### F-11 — A fresh install seeds its config from the shipped default with all learning/hygiene gates OFF
- Topics: AB-30.3
- Evidence: "config file missing (fresh install) → seed from the SHIPPED agentbox.default.toml (all learning/hygiene gates OFF), NOT the live toml — `setup/server/src/main.rs:44`".
- Failure: A new operator walks the setup UI, saves what it shows them, and brings up an agent container whose learning and hygiene gates are all disabled — whatever those gates guard (behavioural constraints, data-handling limits) defaults to permissive at exactly the moment nobody has configured anything.
- Confidence: medium — the seeding is stated; the impact of the gates is not defined in this material.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — The https-bridge emits a CORS combination browsers reject for credentialed requests, so its core function is broken as written
- Topics: AB-30.4
- Evidence: "set Access-Control-Allow-Origin * AND Access-Control-Allow-Credentials true (`https-proxy.js:208-211`)… a combination browsers reject for credentialed requests per the Fetch spec — the two headers as written cannot both take effect for any request that actually carries credentials" (Divergence note).
- Failure: Browser clients cannot make authenticated cross-origin calls through the bridge — the bridge's stated reason to exist (cross-origin access to the management API, `https-proxy.js:3-4`). The predictable operator "fix" is to strip auth or proxy credentials, which converts a broken feature into a security hole.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence note in the diagram)

### F-13 — A hand-rolled node:crypto X.509 builder sits in the TLS path as a fail-open fallback
- Topics: AB-30.4
- Evidence: `ensureCertificates()` — "hand-rolled node:crypto X.509 builder ONLY IF openssl's boot-time generation is somehow absent (`https-proxy.js:48-49`, `:67`) — buildSelfSignedX509 at `:79`"; the boot comment names the trusted path as openssl and the builder as "a fail-open FALLBACK only" (`flake.nix:4064-4069`).
- Failure: On whatever path leaves the openssl-generated cert absent, TLS termination proceeds on a certificate assembled by non-standard, unaudited crypto code rather than failing. Hand-rolled X.509 is exactly where malformed or subtly wrong certificates come from; "fail-open" is the material's own word.
- Confidence: medium — the fallback's existence and fail-open framing are stated; the likelihood and impact of it producing a bad cert are inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — marked as a DESIGN fallback; the risk of hand-rolled certificate construction is not assessed.

### F-14 — Live level-2 co-signing runs over ephemeral Nostr kinds that the estate's own review says cannot carry a safety protocol, and the corrected stored kinds have no allocation
- Topics: AB-35.3, AB-34.5, AB-34.4
- Evidence: "23510 to 23514 are EPHEMERAL under NIP-01: relays are not expected to retain them" and "chain is not a single-letter tag, so it is not a portable indexed filter" (`REVIEW-kofn-consensus-gpt6-astra.md:217`); the registry still allocates that band without recording the rejection (`PROTOCOL-registry.md:167`, Debt), the review's required stored kinds have no allocation (Open), and yet "sidestr-round 0.1.0's cosign co-signs with the JS signers live" (AB-34.5 N6). The shipped round is upstream's "availability-tolerant round (not the reviewed BFT shape)" (AB-35 drift note).
