- Failure: Consensus certificates transit transports that discard them; a signer that needs to prove an authorisation or detect a conflicting certificate after the fact cannot, because nothing durable carries it. Harmless on testnet today; it is the safety-critical path the day the chain carries value, and the round actually shipped is not the reviewed shape.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, Open) — but the fact that cosigning is *live* over the rejected transport is only visible by joining two topics.

### F-15 — The forum wallet reads the chain from a mirror that is permitted to be stale, so payment verification runs on outdated state
- Topics: AB-34.2, AB-34.3
- Evidence: The mirror is "where the forum wallet reads the chain (`mirror-sync.sh:5-6`)"; the trust rule explicitly allows the mirror to be "BEHIND but never AHEAD" (AB-34.2); the sync loop runs every 120 s by default and on producer failure "keep the previous copy, do not fail the loop" (`mirror-sync.sh` loop).
- Failure: The wallet evaluates a payment against a view that can lag the chain by the sync interval or longer during producer outages, and sees spent UTXOs as unspent — a double-spend accepted at face value. With test sats this is noise; with pegged value it is direct loss.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — the staleness-permission and the wallet's read path are stated; the double-accept scenario follows from them but is not demonstrated in the material.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — "behind is allowed" is presented as a property of the rule, not as a consumer hazard.

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **The secret-backup service.** AB-30's title and source list name `services/secret-backup/src/main.rs`, but no section describes it — what it backs up, where to, whether restore has ever been tested. Backup and restore of the estate's keys cannot be assessed at all, and the omission itself is unremarked.
2. **What the learning/hygiene gates actually gate.** F-11 hinges on the default-off seeding; the material never defines these gates or the blast radius of running with them off.
3. **The real deployed environment.** Which of the 35 DEVUSER_CLASS credentials are actually set in production, what the host Docker socket's current mode is, and whether the host rehearsal half has ever been run on the production host — the record lists names and rules, not the live state.
4. **Actual traffic through the https-bridge.** Whether any consumer sends credentials through it (confirming F-07), and what `TARGET_PORT`/`HOST_IP` resolve to in production.
5. **Relay retention behaviour.** Whether the five public relays actually retain kinds 33333 and 3500 long enough for chain discovery and the mirror-trust bootstrap to work — the durability of the whole "relays are the registry" scheme rests on third parties the repository cannot cite.

## agentbox#3 / premortem

# Post-mortem — custody/identity compromise in the agentbox container

Scope note: this reconstruction is written against the record at `6466e39313c3eb4ba0cadfc2efd4e7ffa3ccc296`. Every claim below cites what that record states; where I reason past the record I say so. The three root causes are ranked by how directly the material itself already carries them.

---

### R-1 — The role-isolation boundary was never activated, so every process in the container — including every agent tool — could read every key
- Topics: AB-36, AB-36.1, AB-36.2, AB-36.3, AB-36.6, AB-36.7, AB-34.6
- Chain of events:
  1. The image ships with `[security].role_isolation = false` (`agentbox.toml:2227`); activation requires a flag-on PASS rehearsal receipt from the owner's rebuild (`ADR-2122:135-143`), and the only committed receipt is a STAGED run with the flag false (`docs/estate-closeout/x1-rehearsal-20261003T090708Z.json:4`, `:8`).
  2. At boot the entrypoint picks `/etc/supervisord.conf` (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:1207`), chowns `/run/secrets` to `1000:1000` mode 0700 (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:388`), and performs the devuser chown of the `agentbox-secrets` volume (skipped only under the flag, `config/entrypoint-unified.sh:548`).
  3. Every long-running program runs as `user=devuser` (`flake.nix:2947`, `flake.nix:2982`, `flake.nix:3054`), and supervisord passes its environment to every child.
  4. A client-serving agent tool — buggy, or driven by injected instructions — reads `/var/lib/agentbox/secrets/*` (sovereign identity key, both chains' signer keys, mgmt key) and the ambient environment.
  5. Exfiltration or in-place misuse: impersonation of the estate's Nostr identity, an alternative chain history validly signed with the sealed `sidestr` signer key, management-API calls with full authority.
  6. A client detects impersonation or fraudulent activity attributable to the operator's identity; the relationship ends.
- Evidence: the topic states it flatly: "Until this change every secret in the container reached every process… a 0600 key file protected nothing from a co-resident agent (AB-16.1, AB-16.7)" and "It is switched off today" (AB-36 For the business); the activation gate and STAGED-only receipt (AB-36.7); the flag-off supervisor shape (AB-34.6, `flake.nix:2947`).
- Warning signs visible today: the committed receipt is STAGED with the flag false; the default is false; the flag-off boot path restores every pre-change permission in one line (`entrypoint-unified.sh:388`, `:1207`).
- Confidence: high — the exposure is stated verbatim; the agent-tool vector is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Spend-capable keys survive the boundary even after it is activated: legacy twins on the workspace bind are never deleted
- Topics: AB-34.3, AB-34.6, AB-36.3
- Chain of events:
  1. The operator eventually runs the flag-on rehearsal on a rebuilt image, both halves pass, and the flag is set (the path AB-36.7 describes).
  2. `ab_custody_migrate` (`config/lib/role-custody.sh:447`) copies each canonical secret once from its legacy twin (`:556`) into the role-owned volume copy; role programs read `/run/secrets/<role>/`.
  3. The legacy twins on the workspace bind are never deleted and stay devuser-readable "by design until a manual purge" (`ADR-2122:212-215`).
  4. No purge happens — nothing in the material performs one; it is manual by definition.
  5. A co-resident agent reads, for example, the faucet treasury key at its workspace path — the faucet's live default and the live manifest both still name it (`config/sidechain/run-faucet.sh:27`, `agentbox.toml:1638`), and the role table names the workspace file only as the legacy twin (`config/role-accounts.json:78`).
  6. The treasury is drained or grants are misdirected; on any later mainnet variant of the same shape, real value moves.
- Evidence: `ADR-2122:212-215` ("a spend-capable key stays on the host-visible bind in both modes until a manual purge"); AB-36.3's invariant note ("Those twins are never deleted and stay devuser-readable by design until a manual purge"); AB-34 For the business: "the stored copies of those keys are still readable from inside the box".
- Warning signs visible today: the ADR records the persistence as accepted; the live manifest still points the faucet at the workspace path; the migrate step is copy-once by construction (`role-custody.sh:556`).
- Confidence: high — the persistence is explicit; the incident scenario is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The management plane's only credential is ambient in every agent process, in both modes, and the bridge in front of it emits wildcard CORS
- Topics: AB-36.6, AB-30.3, AB-30.4, AB-31
- Chain of events:
  1. `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY` and `BRIDGE_TOKEN` sit in DEVUSER_CLASS — 35 credentials that "stay in devuser's ambient environment in BOTH modes" (`config/custody/env-classes.json:90`, `:92`), defaulted to "stay" by owner question Q8 (`ADR-2122:158-159`).
  2. Every devuser process therefore holds a valid bearer token for the management API at all times, flag on or off.
  3. The management API carries the payments, broker-bridge and LLM-marketplace surfaces (`management-api/routes/payments.js`, `broker-bridge.js`, `llm-marketplace.js`, AB-31 sources) and the setup server demonstrates the key is the sole authorisation on that plane (`setup/server/src/main.rs:85`, `:166-184`).
  4. A tool, or an injected instruction inside a tool, calls loopback 9090 with the ambient key; there is no second gate.
  5. Contributing exposure: the https-bridge terminates TLS in front of the management API and stamps `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` on every response (`https-proxy.js:208-211`), so unauthenticated responses are readable from any page in the operator's browser.
  6. Unauthorized payment or configuration operations execute; client-visible damage follows.
- Evidence: the Q8 debt (AB-36.6 Debt note, `ADR-2122:158-159`); the CORS divergence the material itself records (AB-30.4 note: the two headers "cannot both take effect for any request that actually carries credentials"); the key-loading order including `~/.agentbox/mgmt-key` and env (`setup/server/src/main.rs:166-184`).
- Warning signs visible today: 35 named credentials including provider keys and the management key are listed as staying; the bridge's CORS combination is flagged as broken-by-spec in its own topic.
- Confidence: medium — the ambient-key exposure is stated with high confidence; its exploitation as *the* incident is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — The https-bridge emits a CORS combination browsers reject for credentialed requests
- Topics: AB-30.4
- Evidence: `https-proxy.js:208-211` sets `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` together with `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true`; the topic's own note says the pair "cannot both take effect for any request that actually carries credentials" per the Fetch spec.
- Failure: either the bridge's legitimate credentialed flows against the management API silently fail, or workarounds strip credentials and unauthenticated management responses become readable from any origin in the operator's browser.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)
- Severity: medium

### F-02 — Bridge binds 0.0.0.0 relying on a compose comment, with a fail-open hand-rolled certificate builder
- Topics: AB-30.4
- Evidence: `HTTPS_HOST` defaults `0.0.0.0` (`https-proxy.js:190`, `:262`), "published loopback-only via compose (R-003 comment, `https-proxy.js:31-33`)"; the hand-rolled `node:crypto` X.509 builder runs "ONLY IF openssl's boot-time generation is somehow absent" (`https-proxy.js:48-49`, `:67`, `:79`) and the boot comment calls it a "fail-open FALLBACK" (`flake.nix:4064-4069`).
- Failure: any compose change or mispublished port exposes the management-API bridge beyond loopback; if the openssl boot step is absent, TLS identity is minted by hand-rolled crypto.
- Confidence: medium (defaults stated; exposure inferred)
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-03 — Setup server seeds from the shipped default when the live config is missing, then overwrites the whole file
- Topics: AB-30.3, AB-30.2
- Evidence: on a missing config the server seeds "from the SHIPPED agentbox.default.toml (all learning/hygiene gates OFF), NOT the live toml" (`setup/server/src/main.rs:44`); `save_config` parses then performs a whole-file overwrite (`setup/server/src/main.rs:60-77`, `:64-66`).
- Failure: a wrong config path or cwd makes the operator see defaults as if they were the live config; one save replaces the live file, turning supervision and gate settings off.
- Confidence: medium (mechanics stated; scenario inferred)
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-04 — The setup server injects the management bearer key for any caller that finds its port
- Topics: AB-30.3
- Evidence: `/api/proxy/{*path}` forwards with `Authorization Bearer <key>` (`setup/server/src/main.rs:85`); the key is loaded from `/var/lib/agentbox/secrets/mgmt-key`, `~/.agentbox/mgmt-key`, then env (`setup/server/src/main.rs:166-184`); the server binds `127.0.0.1:0` ephemeral (`setup/server/src/main.rs:229-232`).
- Failure: while the setup server runs, any local process — or a webpage in the operator's browser that discovers the port — reaches the management API pre-authenticated; the `~/.agentbox` fallback widens where a planted key would be honoured.
- Confidence: medium (inferred)
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-05 — `SIDESTR_ALLOW_UNPINNED=1` restores devuser-writable consensus code with no time bound
- Topics: AB-34.1
- Evidence: the override runs a workspace checkout "at any commit" (`run-producer.sh:85`); it "logs SIDESTR-UNPINNED every start… warns, never refuses" (`run-producer.sh:86-93`); the topic's own debt note: "while the override is set… a dirty checkout runs, and nothing bounds how long it stays set".
- Failure: the override is left set after debugging; the producer executes a modified `siding.mjs` and signs its output with the sealed signer key — a chain history produced under attacker-chosen rules, validly signed.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-06 — `upstream-pins` was bumped on acceptance, not on a produced block
- Topics: AB-34.1
- Evidence: the file's rule is to bump only "after the chain has been restarted and a block produced on that commit" (`upstream-pins:5-7`); the `blaketestnode` line was bumped as an "operator-accepted active development head" (`upstream-pins:10`), and the bake carries the same commit (`lib/sidestr-upstream.nix:57-60`).
- Failure: consensus-affecting upstream code ships into the image without the recorded evidence path; both chains' producers run an untested development head whose rule changes silently alter block validity.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)
- Severity: medium

### F-07 — Genesis commits neither parent nor containment; the closing pin is unbuilt
- Topics: AB-32.2, AB-32.1
- Evidence: upstream's `buildGenesis` commits only id, pegs, genesisTime and the signer's witness; "parent, comment, signers… every containment field" are not committed (`ADR-2103:207-208`); the coinbase pin is unbuilt and would be an upstream proposal, unfiled (`ADR-2103:210-214`); the binding is "the committed document plus its genesisHash" (`config/sidechain/README.md:77`).
- Failure: a served or altered `chain.json` asserting a different parent or containment (e.g. `cashOut` true) still matches the chain's `genesisHash`; consumers that trust the document's containment are enforcing an unverifiable claim.
- Confidence: medium (gap stated; exploitation inferred)
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension + Open)
- Severity: medium

### F-08 — The p21/mainnet gate is a field-existence check, not a gate
- Topics: AB-32.3, AB-31.9
- Evidence: CI case 3 enforces only "that a mainnet parent carries a `p21Receipt` field"; resolving the receipt is not checked (`tests/config/sidechain-genesis.test.sh:86`); the record lists the unrun CI resolution check, the level-1-on-mainnet prohibition and the refusing node as not built (`ADR-2103:229-230`); the corrected invariant says mainnet "cannot begin until the regulatory gate exists as code rather than as an assertion" (`sovereign-settlement.md:329-330`, `:60`).
- Failure: a mainnet-parented chain ships with an unresolvable receipt; nothing at CI or runtime stops real-value operation behind a containment that is asserted in JSON only.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt + Open)
- Severity: high

### F-09 — The record disagrees with itself on whether spend and sign keys derive from the identity key
- Topics: AB-34.4, AB-31.7
- Evidence: PRD-024 still derives `k_spend` and `k_sign` from `k_id` via `derive_subkey` (`sovereign-settlement.md:171-173`); the registry row, since the 2026-10-02 ADR-2097 D3 amendment, says the binding key is "an INDEPENDENT key minted beside k_id… never derived" (`PROTOCOL-registry.md:172`).
- Failure: an implementation following PRD-024 turns any `k_id` disclosure (see R-1) into a full spend/sign key compromise; an implementation following the registry breaks every consumer still coded to PRD-024's derivation.
- Confidence: high (disagreement stated); consequence inferred
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)
- Severity: high

### F-10 — The faucet binary is baked from a git revision, not crates.io
- Topics: AB-33.8
- Evidence: `lib/sidestr-agent.nix:29-30` fetches sidestr-rs from GitHub at a pinned commit (0.6.0 at `a7aadd68`); ADR-2112's rule is "consume from crates.io only, never by path or git dependency" (`ADR-2112:39-41`).
- Failure: the spend-capable faucet program's supply chain is a moving GitHub revision outside the consumption rule that governs every other consumer of the crates.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)
- Severity: medium

### F-11 — Level-2 safety traffic rides ephemeral kinds, and the shipped round is not the reviewed protocol
- Topics: AB-35.3, AB-35.1, AB-35.2
- Evidence: kinds 23510-23514 are ephemeral under NIP-01 and `chain` is not a portable indexed filter (`REVIEW-kofn-consensus-gpt6-astra.md:217`); the corrected wire profile has no registry allocation (`ADR-2101:162-168`; `PROTOCOL-registry.md:163-173` records only the external set and 38420-38425); the drift note records that `sidestr-round` ships "upstream's availability-tolerant round (not the reviewed BFT shape)".
- Failure: when federation runs, round votes and certificates are dropped by relays and cannot be fetched by filter; the availability-tolerant round does not provide the reviewed q-threshold, two-decision safety — stalls or double-spends under faults.
- Confidence: high (defect statement is the review's own)
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt + Open, plus the drift note)
- Severity: high

### F-12 — Each chain runs with exactly one signer and no anchor into its parent
- Topics: AB-34.5, AB-32 (drift note), AB-35.1
- Evidence: `sidestr:dreamlab` has a single 32-byte signer (`dreamlab/chain.json:17`); AB-35.1 records that even three instances is "a CRASH-ONLY research profile, f equals 0" (`ADR-2101:143`) — one signer is below that; `checkpoint_every = 0` (`agentbox.toml:1666`) and "no producer writes checkpoints into its parent" (`config/sidechain/README.md:13-18`).
- Failure: one compromised or lost key (see R-1, R-2) is the entire chain — no quorum exists to catch it; with no parent checkpoints a rewritten sidechain history cannot be detected from the parent; the four-day halt after the 25 September restart is the availability half already observed.
- Confidence: high (facts stated; risk framing inferred)
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — The host Docker socket stays world-accessible, and the rehearsal records it without gating
- Topics: AB-36.7
- Evidence: the socket is a bind of a host inode "that earlier boots widened" (`docker-compose.override.yml:116-117`); the flag-on boot can only "detect and record `degraded:docker-socket`" (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:605`); the host half records the mode "without gating on it" (`scripts/activation/role-isolation-rehearsal.host.sh:18-19`) while ADR-2122 counts it a failure (`ADR-2122:142`).
- Failure: any process in the container reaches the socket, runs containers as root on the host, and mounts the secrets volume; because the gate and the decision rule disagree, a "PASS" activation can coexist with an open socket.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)
- Severity: high

### F-14 — Flag-off builds bake a NOPASSWD wheel grant for devuser, defused only by two runtime guards
- Topics: AB-36.7
- Evidence: built flag-off — how it ships — devuser stays in `wheel` (`flake.nix:3864`) with a `NOPASSWD: ALL` drop-in (`config/bake-devuser-privilege.sh:44-47`, called at `flake.nix:3878`); it is inert only because `no-new-privileges` is set (`docker-compose.yml:129`) and no setuid sudo ships (`flake.nix:3632-3636`); a mismatched boot only records `degraded:devuser-sudo` (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:642`).
- Failure: a compose edit drops `no-new-privileges`, or a sudo binary enters the image, and devuser becomes root — past every role boundary in AB-36 — with no boot-time repair.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)
- Severity: high

### F-15 — The identity port hands the live-mirror child secret to devuser rather than wrapping
- Topics: AB-36.5
- Evidence: the ACL grants devuser `mirror_key` "with secret true" (`config/custody/identity-port-acl.json:42`); the design had specified a `mirror_wrap` operation; the topic notes "That tension is recorded once, in AB-16".
- Failure: even with the flag on and the port live, any devuser process extracts the mirror signing key in the clear — the one operation the port exists to make non-extractive remains extractive.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes
- Severity: medium

## dreamlab-ai-website / critical

### F-01 — A plaintext secp256k1 private key is committed in three root-level scripts
- Topics: DW-07.2, DW-06.9 (D13)
- Evidence: `ADMIN_PRIVKEY_HEX`, "a plaintext secp256k1 private key hardcoded in source", appears at `scripts/seed-forum.mjs:20`, `scripts/seed-semantic.mjs:17`, `scripts/assign-cohorts.mjs:17` — directly contradicting the repo's own rule "NEVER hardcode API keys, secrets, or credentials in source files" (`CLAUDE.md`, Security Rules). The key stays in git history even if the files are cleaned. Its pubkey is not in the `[admin]`/`[[agents]]` rosters (DW-03.3), but whether it is whitelisted or has signed published events on the live relay cannot be determined from this repo — the material says so explicitly.
- Failure: if the key is live in any environment (relay whitelist, D1 members, old test state), anyone who ever cloned or forked the repo can sign as that identity — publish events, authenticate, or act as whatever role it holds. Because it is in history, deleting the files fixes nothing short of a history rewrite plus credential rotation everywhere the identity was used.
- Confidence: high (the key's existence and rule violation are stated; its privileges are not determinable from this material)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes ("INVARIANT VIOLATION", DW-07.2; D13)

### F-02 — One unsplit key is both primary forum admin and governance publisher, and rotation is a 4–5-location manual atomic change including a secret CI cannot touch
- Topics: DW-03.4, DW-03.3, DW-01.8, DW-03.5
- Evidence: `visionclaw-server` (`11ed6422…663c`) is BOTH primary admin AND `[governance].agent_pubkeys` publisher (`dreamlab.toml:275`, DW-03.4 state diagram "Unsplit"). The split is "Open, staged": it requires moving `[admin].static_pubkeys` (`dreamlab.toml`), relay and search `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` `[vars]`, and the auth-worker `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` Cloudflare secret together (`docs/deployment/admin-key-split-runbook.md`, cited `dreamlab.toml:54-55`); `deploy.yml:61-66` extends it to five locations by adding `VITE_ADMIN_PUBKEY`. The CF secret "cannot be rotated from this repo or CI" and the mirror check marks it `verifiable: false` (DW-03.5 M1).
- Failure: compromise of the visionclaw-server key yields full forum admin (whitelist, moderation, promotion via `/api/whitelist/set-admin`) plus the ability to publish governance events. Emergency rotation is a five-site manual change with one site invisible to every automated check — a partial rotation leaves the old key admin somewhere or the new key admin nowhere, silently, because only the readable mirror sites are compared.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes ("Open, staged" DW-03.4; D7)

### F-03 — Public anonymous ingress has no server-side abuse mitigation beyond an IP rate limit, and the LLM-cost throttle is client-side
- Topics: DW-04.5, DW-04.3
- Evidence: threat register states mitigation for spam/DoS is "10 events/s/IP relay limit only — no PoW/CAPTCHA/per-recipient throttle; kind 1059 bypasses content moderation" (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:282`), and LLM-cost abuse is mitigated by "serialised sends (one in-flight/session), client-side throttle, ops kill-switch `JUNKIEJARVIS_ENABLED=0`" (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:284`). Client-side throttles and per-session serialization are bypassable by scripting (new sessions, rotating IPs); the kill switch is a manual operator action.
- Failure: a scripted campaign against the public website's DM ingress floods the operator/admin inbox with unmoderated kind-1059 content and drives junkiejarvis LLM spend until a human notices and flips the env switch; the relay IP limit is the only automated barrier.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (threat register T1/T3, DW-04.5 — though "mitigation" framing understates that the throttles are client-side)

### F-04 — The documented worker endpoints are unprovisioned in DNS; shipping the documented values has already severed every client API call once
- Topics: DW-01.3, DW-06.7 (D4), DW-06.6 (I3)
- Evidence: branded domains (`relay./api./pods./search./preview.dreamlab-ai.com`) are "the documented end-state but are **not provisioned in DNS**"; the client instead uses raw `*.workers.dev` hosts baked into the build env (`deploy.yml:50-54`; verified `ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED` 2026-06-09, "not re-checked as of 2026-08-31"); "shipping the branded domains baked in previously severed every client API call" (`BASELINE-architecture.md:91-97`). Endpoints are baked into static assets at build time (I3, DW-01.2), so any endpoint change requires a full site rebuild and redeploy.
- Failure: an operator "completing" the documented end-state (or re-cutting DNS per Invariant 4 without provisioning the domains first) takes down all client↔Worker communication site-wide — this exact failure has occurred. Separately, the whole backend's reachability hangs on the `workers.dev` hostname scheme, whose status was last verified months before HEAD.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT DW-01.3; D4)

### F-05 — Four different kit-version claims coexist (README beta.6, baseline doc beta.9, comments beta.14, live beta.15), the gate checks SHA only, and pin skew previously "wiped the forum"
- Topics: DW-01.5, DW-06.7 (D9), DW-06.9 (D12), DW-06.6 (I1)
- Evidence: live pins are `72463fbd…` / `1.0.0-beta.15` (`deploy.yml:118`, `workers-deploy.yml:45`, `rust-ci.yml:21`, `forum-config/Cargo.toml:49-52`, `kit-compatibility-record.md:30-31`). But `README.md:298` says "Live pin `2d693ed2…` (beta.6)", `BASELINE-architecture.md:113/:110` cites beta.9/`a754468…`, the three workflow comments say `v1.0.0-beta.14` (`deploy.yml:113`, `rust-ci.yml:19`, `workers-deploy.yml:40`), and the record's own History table marks `931898a` (beta.10) as "Current (canonical — matches `CANONICAL_KIT_SHA` above)" (`kit-compatibility-record.md:347`) — a false claim inside the canonical record. Pin-parity compares only the SHA, "so the stale version annotation does not fail the gate". The material records that client/worker skew "wiped the forum on 2026-06-15" (`BASELINE-architecture.md:117-120`).
- Failure: during an emergency re-pin, an operator reading README.md — identified as the repo's most-read file — targets beta.6, or triage trusts the History table's "Current" row; a consistent-but-wrong pin passes every gate and rolls five production Workers and two WASM clients against live D1 data, the incident class that already destroyed the forum once.
- Confidence: high (all four conflicting values are quoted; the incident linkage is stated history)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT DW-01.5; D9, D12)

### F-06 — The only backup path has no restore script, runs on one cron host, and retains 14 nights
- Topics: DW-07.5
- Evidence: `forum-backup.sh` runs nightly at 03:10 UTC via supercron **on the agentbox** (`crontab:6`), exports raw D1/R2/KV to a NAS (`DEST_ROOT`, `KEEP_NIGHTS=14`), and the material states flatly: "there is no corresponding restore script, only the raw D1/R2/KV export." Backup execution depends on a single machine being up; missing CF credentials exit 2 so the gap is visible, but nothing stated alerts on a silently absent run.
- Failure: a D1 corruption or bad migration lands and recovery is an improvised, never-exercised reconstruction from raw exports; if the agentbox was down or the 14-night window has passed over an unnoticed failure, there is nothing to reconstruct from. Untested backups are indistinguishable from no backups until the incident.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — stated as fact ("no corresponding restore script") but not flagged as a defect

### F-07 — Moderation state (ADMIN_KV: bans/mutes) is excluded from the nightly backup, and deploy tooling silently creates a fresh empty namespace if it can't resolve one
- Topics: DW-03.8, DW-03.9, DW-07.5
- Evidence: the backup script's KV scope enumerates only "SESSIONS/POD_META/SEARCH_CONFIG — low priority, rebuildable" (`forum-backup.sh:31-35`); `ADMIN_KV` is absent. `ADMIN_KV` (auth-worker, read/write — `auth-worker.wrangler.toml:40-41`) and `ADMIN_KV_RO` (pod-worker — `pod-worker.wrangler.toml:22-23`) hold ban/mute state — DW-03.8 says a namespace split would "silently drop bans/mutes". The `provision-kv` pre-job "resolves **or creates** a SINGLE shared `dreamlab-admin-kv` namespace" (`workers-deploy.yml`, DW-03.8).
- Failure: the namespace is deleted, renamed, or fails lookup during a deploy — `provision-kv` creates a new empty one, both workers bind it, and every ban and mute silently vanishes with no backup copy to restore from: banned users regain access and muted pod access revocations lift, with a green pipeline.
- Confidence: high (the backup-list omission and create-if-missing behavior are both stated; the loss scenario is their direct combination)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — both halves are stated; neither is connected or flagged

### F-08 — Data-subject deletion of ingress DMs is cryptographically impossible and the manual purge path competes with 14 nights of plaintext NAS backup copies
- Topics: DW-04.5 (T2), DW-07.5
- Evidence: "erasure is operator-side D1 purge only — self-service NIP-09 deletion is cryptographically impossible (author key is a discarded throwaway)" (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:283`). Meanwhile nightly backups copy relay/auth D1 to `/mnt/dell/shared/backups/…` with `KEEP_NIGHTS=14`, and the script header warns the NAS copy is readable plaintext (`forum-backup.sh:16-18`). No purge runbook for ingress DMs appears anywhere in this material.
- Failure: a user submits PII via the anonymous contact/ingress surface and requests erasure; the operator must hand-surgery D1 across two databases, and the content persists in NAS backup sets for up to two weeks afterward — deletion is neither self-service nor complete-on-demand.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (threat register T2) — the backup-retention interaction is not marked

### F-09 — The encryption model shipped citing "ADR-2016", which was never written, while the normative governing doc still says only zone3 is encrypted
- Topics: DW-03.1, DW-06.6 (I7), DW-06.9 (D15), DW-03.6 (M7)
- Evidence: `dreamlab.toml` sets `encrypted=true` on zone2/3/4 (`:123,137,148`), `agent_keys=true` on zone4 (`:149`), and a master `[encryption].enabled` gate (`:33-37`) — citing 'ADR-2016' (`:34,149`) — but "no ADR-2016-*.md exists in docs/adr/ at HEAD — the record was never written" (I7, "BREACHED at HEAD"). The governing `IDENTITY-zones.md:166-167` invariant still reads "only zone3 is encrypted", and the pack states these docs "are normative". The master gate is itself a 3-site hand-synced mirror (M7: `dreamlab.toml:33,37`, `deploy.yml:93`, `relay-worker.wrangler.toml:16`).
- Failure: an operator handling keys, backups, logs, or an incident per the normative doc operates on a false model of what is encrypted — treating zone2 content or zone4 agent-key grants as outside the E2EE regime — and the demonstrated process failure (config shipped, record never written, governing doc never updated) means the next security-relevant change can land the same way.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT DW-03.1; D15; I7 "BREACHED")

### F-10 — Admins promoted at runtime (D1) are invisible to the search-worker until a redeploy
- Topics: DW-04.6, DW-03.10
- Evidence: admin = static ∪ D1 (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:268-269`, promotion at `:270`), but "the search-worker honours only its own `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` `[vars]` value — D1-promoted admins are not visible to it" (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:272`), because search has "its own separate `[vars]` copy, not read from ADMIN_KV/ADMIN_KV_RO" (`search-worker.wrangler.toml:30,38`, DW-03.10).
- Failure: an operator promotes a moderator via `/api/whitelist/set-admin`; that moderator gets 403s on every search-admin endpoint with no error pointing at the cause, and the authorization answer differs per worker for the same principal until someone redeploys search-worker with updated `[vars]`.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes ("Known gap" / "Gap 2")

### F-11 — The poker configuration is an eighth hand-synced mirror that the completeness sweep structurally cannot see
- Topics: DW-03.6, DW-03.7
- Evidence: `dreamlab.toml` `[poker]` is "mirrored BY HAND into `deploy.yml` `POKER_CONFIG_JSON`" (`dreamlab.toml:305-308`; `deploy.yml:83-89`, injected `:274,346`), but neither site appears in `MIRROR_BEARING_KEYS` (`config-mirrors.mjs:41-47`), and DW-03.7 states the sweep's guarantee holds "only for the six keys it enumerates".
- Failure: a `[poker]` edit (stakes, house-seat behavior for the DREAM table dealt by `poker-citizen`) that misses the deploy mirror ships stale table parameters silently with `MIRRORS-OK` green — the exact silent-stale-ship class ADR-2005's sweep was built to close, reopened one group at a time.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes ("Drift (an eighth, unenumerated mirror)")

### F-12 — Talk-to-AI has no request/reply correlation, an unverified identity hint, and a reply path that dies silently if an external party changes config
- Topics: DW-04.3, DW-06.6 (I8)
- Evidence: "the DM transport carries no protocol-level correlation" — a late answer "can otherwise read as the answer to whatever was asked most recently" (`AIChatFab.tsx:9-19`, closeout comment; `IDENTITY-zones.md:189`). `identityHint` is "EXPLICITLY UNVERIFIED — no proof of possession" (`AIChatFab.tsx:429-430`). Replies are "readable only from open relays" — `relay.damus.io`, `relay.primal.net` (`VITE_REPLY_RELAYS`) — and the invariant "reply-relay set must remain a subset of the agent's own publish fan-out, or replies are never seen" (`IDENTITY-zones.md:168-169`) is a cross-repo property: the agent's fan-out lives in agentbox, external to this repo, with no check stated here.
- Failure: (a) a timed-out question's late reply is displayed against the newest question and the user acts on a mismatched answer; (b) junkiejarvis's upstream fan-out or a third-party open relay's policy changes, and website replies simply stop arriving — no error in this repo, the feature just looks dead; (c) any visitor can assert any pubkey as `identityHint`, so the agent's conversation state can be poisoned under someone else's claimed identity (this last consequence is inferred one step beyond the stated "unverified").
- Confidence: high (facts stated); the impersonation consequence is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — correlation and the fan-out invariant are flagged (closeout comment, I8); the third-party-relay single-path dependency is described but not flagged as a risk

### F-13 — Zone-identity rules have two prior incidents and no stated automated guard
- Topics: DW-03.1, DW-07.3
- Evidence: the dual-accept `required_cohorts` invariant exists because "dropping either arm collapsed legacy members to welcome-only in a 2026-07-20 regression" (`IDENTITY-zones.md:164-165`; `dreamlab.toml:116-119`). The `friends`→`minimoonoir` rename "orphaned `friends-*` channels (kind-40 section tags are immutable), requiring a delete-and-recreate rather than an in-place rename" (`reseed-minimoonoir.mjs:1-6`). The material names no test or gate covering either rule — the `forum-config` Rust tests cover "config parsing, branding, deploy manifests" (DW-02.3).
- Failure: a future cohort-list cleanup or zone rename repeats a member lockout (locked-zone members silently collapsed to welcome-only) or orphans live channels requiring destructive delete-and-recreate — both failure modes have already happened once, and nothing stated would catch a recurrence before deploy.
- Confidence: high (the absence of a stated guard is an absence-of-evidence observation, stated as such)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (INVARIANT DW-03.1; migration note DW-07.3) — the untested status is this pass's observation

### F-14 — Content generators fail soft: a missing directory or YAML typo publishes empty site sections with a fully green pipeline
- Topics: DW-06.1, DW-02.3
- Evidence: "a missing `public/data/workshops/` writes an empty `workshop-list.json` instead of failing the build" (`generate-workshop-list.mjs:97-99`) and "a YAML parse error writes an empty `testimonials.json`" (`generate-testimonials.mjs:22-25`); both run as `predev`/`prebuild` before every build.
- Failure: a content-directory move, a rename, or a one-character YAML error on an unrelated commit republishes the production site with an empty workshops index and/or empty testimonials; every gate passes, and detection is a human noticing the public site — the build system converts content regressions into silent successes.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — described as a design property ("fail soft"), not flagged as a risk

### F-15 — Nothing tests the upstream kit automatically: kit CI is manual with advisory clippy, E2E is unwired, and the product runs yanked-adjacent beta crates
- Topics: DW-05.1, DW-05.2, DW-01.5
- Evidence: `rust-ci.yml` is `workflow_dispatch` only because "kit source lives in a separate repo" (`rust-ci.yml:4-9`); its clippy is advisory, "warnings reported, not enforced" (`rust-ci.yml:40-63`); wasm tests are `--no-run` (`rust-ci.yml:119`). Playwright specs are "deliberately NOT wired into `ci.yml`, `test-and-lint.yml` or any gate job" (`CLAUDE.md:63-64`). The kit is pinned at `1.0.0-beta.15` with beta.13 yanked upstream, and re-pins are routine (multiple since `d852f61`; again 2026-10-05, DW-01.5).
- Failure: a defective beta kit release is re-pinned with all four pin sites consistent — every gate in this repo green — and ships five production Workers plus two WASM clients with zero kit tests having executed and no E2E to catch it; first detection is in production, in a system whose backend state includes the forum's canonical D1.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (documented as deliberate/manual, though not as a risk)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Whether the hardcoded `ADMIN_PRIVKEY_HEX` (F-01) is live**: I need the deployed relay/auth D1 (`whitelist`, `members`) and a search of published events for its derived pubkey to know if it holds any privilege or has ever signed anything reachable in production.
2. **First-user-is-admin bootstrap semantics versus the always-present static `ADMIN_PUBKEYS`**: I need the kit's auth-worker setup logic (`admin.rs`, `/api/setup-status`) to know whether a D1 restore that loses admin rows can ever re-enter `needsSetup` in this deployment, or whether the static set suppresses it (this gates a potential takeover-during-recovery scenario I deliberately did not report as a finding).
3. **Whether the Talk-to-AI reply invariant currently holds**: junkiejarvis's publish fan-out lives in agentbox (external); I need that config and recent publish logs to confirm replies are actually reachable via `relay.damus.io`/`relay.primal.net` today.
4. **ADMIN_KV's true contents and `provision-kv`'s exact resolution logic** (F-07): whether bans/mutes exist solely in KV or are also projected into D1 (`moderation_actions`/`whitelist`), and what the workflow does when a namespace exists under an unexpected name or id.
5. **Current DNS/endpoint reality and the sed injection**: whether the branded worker domains are still unprovisioned (last verified 2026-06-09) and the `workers.dev` route enabled at account level — plus the actual `deploy.yml` sed step, to judge whether a config value containing quotes or `</script>` can break or inject into the `window.__ENV__` block.

## dreamlab-ai-website / premortem

# Post-mortem — dreamlab-ai-website (client-affecting data/custody/security failure)

Scope note: all citations resolve at the pack's `verified_commit: ebaf16c`; the two governing docs carry their own older `verified_commit: d852f61`, which is itself a meta-fact several findings rely on. Every finding below is written to be falsifiable against the cited files. Where the material does not state an ordering or concurrency, I say so rather than assume it.

---

### R-1 — A governed value rotated in the TOML shipped stale at one of its hand-mirrored sites, so clients and workers enforced different access or encryption rules
- Topics: DW-03.1, DW-03.4, DW-03.5, DW-03.6, DW-03.7, DW-06.6 (I7, I9)
- Chain of events: operator makes a governed change in `dreamlab.toml` (admin key rotation, zone cohort change, `[encryption].enabled` flip, poker parameters) → updates the mirror sites they remember → one site is missed. The parity gate only catches misses among the six enumerated `MIRROR_BEARING_KEYS` (`config-mirrors.mjs:41-47`); a novel variable name is ungoverned by definition, the poker mirror is already invisible, and the auth-worker `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` Cloudflare secret is unreadable by CI (`verifiable: false`). Result: relay enforces one zone/admin/encryption view, the client renders another, auth-worker honors a third → a client's members are denied their zone, a revoked principal retains privileges on some workers, or content the client believes encrypted is admitted/stored under a suppressed flag.
- Evidence: before 2026-09-05 CI compared only one mirror, so "a rotation that updated the TOML and missed a mirror shipped stale keys or zones silently" (`config-mirrors.mjs:1-18`, DW-03.5); the dual-accept arm drop already "collapsed legacy members to welcome-only in a 2026-07-20 regression" (`dreamlab.toml:116-119`, DW-03.1); the eighth, unenumerated poker mirror is stated to ship stale parameters silently (DW-03.6); the key split is a four/five-location atomic manual change including the CI-invisible secret (`dreamlab.toml:39-72`, `deploy.yml:61-66`, DW-03.4); the sweep's guarantee "holds only for the six keys it enumerates" (DW-03.6); invariant I9 exists precisely because of this (DW-06.6).
- Warning signs visible today: seven enumerated mirrors plus one known-unenumerated one; a prior access regression in this exact class; the split runbook staged but unexecuted (D7).
- Confidence: High that this class recurs; medium on which specific site. The auth-secret and poker sites are the two the gates provably cannot see.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — A kit re-pin shipped without the kit's own tests having run and without a guaranteed client/worker deploy ordering — the class that "wiped the forum" on 2026-06-15
- Topics: DW-01.5, DW-01.6, DW-03.11, DW-05.2, DW-06.6 (I1)
- Chain of events: operator re-pins `KIT_REF` and the crate rev at all four sites plus the record — pin-parity passes, because it checks SHA agreement, not fitness. `rust-ci.yml` against the kit is `workflow_dispatch`-only with advisory clippy (`rust-ci.yml:4-9, 40-63`), so nothing requires the kit's suite to have run at the new pin. `deploy.yml` and `workers-deploy.yml` both trigger on `forum-config/Cargo.lock` / `KIT_REF` changes, but the material does not state the two workflows are ordered or that one blocks on the other — a failed or lagging workers deploy alongside a successful client deploy recreates the 2026-06-15 client/worker skew. Separately (inferred): the only D1 DDL is this repo's `migrations/001_init.sql` — 24 `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, no later migrations (`001_init.sql:1`; `auth-worker.wrangler.toml:18`) — so a newer kit expecting evolved columns has no migration path; `IF NOT EXISTS` cannot add columns.
- Evidence: "the client/worker skew that wiped the forum on 2026-06-15" (`BASELINE-architecture.md:117-120`, DW-01.5); the trigger design exists "precisely so a kit re-pin never ships a new client against old workers" (DW-01.5); kit CI is manual-only by stated design (DW-05.2); the compatibility record's History marks release `931898a` as "Current (canonical)" two releases behind the actual canonical `72463fbd` (`kit-compatibility-record.md:347` vs `:30`), and workflow comments say `beta.14` against a `beta.15` pin — the human-readable provenance an operator would consult during a rushed re-pin is wrong in three places (DW-01.5).
- Warning signs visible today: one prior total data loss in this exact class; kit tests opt-in; schema ownership split across repos; the kit has been re-pinned repeatedly during the doc's lifetime.
- Confidence: Medium. The precedent and the manual-only kit gate are stated; the workflow-ordering gap and the schema-skew path are inferred — the material is silent on both.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Compromise or misuse of the single unsplit VisionClaw key, which is simultaneously primary admin and governance publisher, with no renderable attribution to detect it
- Topics: DW-03.3, DW-03.4, DW-01.8, DW-04.6
- Chain of events: the `visionclaw-server` pubkey `11ed6422…663c` is both the forum's primary admin and the `[governance].agent_pubkeys` publisher (DW-03.3; `dreamlab.toml:275`, DW-01.8), and the private key lives on an external server this repo does not control (VC-*). Compromise or simply unauthorized use of that one server yields full admin — static-set admin resolution is checked first (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:268`) — plus the ability to publish governance events (kinds 31400-31405) as the trusted publisher. Detection is impaired because `authorised_by` is authored in `[[agents]]` but never rendered by the kit (`IDENTITY-zones.md:154-157`), so no visible attribution trail distinguishes operator acts from VisionClaw acts. Remediation then requires the staged-but-unexecuted four/five-location atomic rotation, one site of which no pipeline can verify.
- Evidence: DW-03.3's diagram marks the "SAME key, two roles" link explicitly; DW-03.4 records the split as open ("Held deliberately: the auth-worker ADMIN_PUBKEYS CF secret cannot be rotated from this repo or CI", `dreamlab.toml:39-72`); D7 registers it; the interim `VITE_ADMIN_PUBKEY` further entangles the operator's working key into the client (`deploy.yml:61-66`).
- Warning signs visible today: the material's own state diagram puts the system in `Unsplit`; the runbook exists but nothing enforces or tracks its execution.
- Confidence: Medium. The key concentration, dual role, external custody, and rotation friction are all stated; the compromise event itself is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Subject data cannot be self-serviced to deletion, and every night it is copied in readable form to a shared NAS for 14 nights, with no restore tooling
- Topics: DW-02.7, DW-04.5, DW-07.5
- Evidence: anonymous-ingress PII (contact-form name/email/message, `Contact.tsx:43-48`, `:395-409`) has "self-service NIP-09 deletion cryptographically impossible (author key is a discarded throwaway)… erasure is operator-side D1 purge only" (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:283`); nightly 03:10 UTC backup (`crontab:6`) copies D1/R2/KV to `/mnt/dell/shared/backups/dreamlab-forum`, `KEEP_NIGHTS=14`, with kind-40/42/0 "readable content — keep it on the trusted segment only" (`forum-backup.sh:16-18, 21`); "there is no corresponding restore script" (DW-07.5).
- Failure: an erasure request is honored in live D1; the data persists in up to 14 nights of plaintext backups on a shared mount, and the improvised restore that follows any incident is untested.
- Confidence: High
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (threat-register T2; backup-header sensitivity note)

### F-02 — A plaintext secp256k1 private key is hardcoded in three committed scripts, violating the repo's own security rule
- Topics: DW-07.2, DW-06.7 (D13)
- Evidence: `ADMIN_PRIVKEY_HEX` hardcoded at `seed-forum.mjs:20`, `seed-semantic.mjs:17`, `assign-cohorts.mjs:17`; CLAUDE.md Security Rules state "NEVER hardcode… credentials in source files"; liveness undeterminable from this repo, and it is not any rostered admin/agent key (DW-07.2).
- Failure: the key is live, or becomes live after someone "fixes" the scripts' broken foreign import path (`/home/devuser/workspace/project2/…`) and runs them against production; it also lives in git history permanently.
- Confidence: High (existence); liveness unknown.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (called an INVARIANT VIOLATION; register D13)

### F-03 — The auth-worker `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` secret is invisible to every parity check, so its drift from the TOML is undetectable until behavior diverges
- Topics: DW-03.5, DW-03.8, DW-04.6
- Evidence: the secret is `verifiable: false`, pushed only by the dispatch-only `set-worker-secrets.yml` (`:12-13`); the mirror set counts it as a site but no check reads it back (DW-03.5 M1); deploy only asserts it is *set*, not that it *matches* (`workers-deploy.yml:288`).
- Failure: a rotation updates TOML + relay + search (all parity-green) but the manual secret push is forgotten — auth-worker keeps honoring a key every other component has revoked.
- Confidence: High (mechanism stated); the miss itself is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DW-03.4 "cannot be rotated from this repo or CI… held deliberately")

### F-04 — No zone-key revocation or re-key path is documented; the master gate suppresses flags but "keys already held still decrypt"
- Topics: DW-03.1, DW-01.5, DW-06.6 (I7)
- Evidence: `[encryption].enabled=false` "suppresses every zone's flag (keys already held still decrypt)" (`dreamlab.toml:33-37`, DW-03.1); keys are "granted on allocation/admin sign-in" per the beta.15 change set (DW-01.5); `zone4` sets `agent_keys = true`, widening holders to ten agents (DW-03.3); no revocation/re-key mechanism appears anywhere, and the design record (ADR-2016) was never written (DW-06.6 I7).
- Failure: a departed member or deactivated agent retains a zone key; any future path that lets them obtain ciphertext (federation, backup exfiltration, re-admission) yields decryption of content the client believes restricted.
- Confidence: Low, inferred — absence of documentation is not proof of absence of a mechanism, and relay cohort-gating currently limits fetch.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — Admin identity resolves differently per worker: search-worker honors only its static copy, so D1-promoted admins are invisible to it
- Topics: DW-04.6, DW-03.10
- Evidence: "D1-promoted admins are not visible to it" (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:272`); search's `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` is its own `[vars]` copy, not read from `ADMIN_KV`/`ADMIN_KV_RO` (`search-worker.wrangler.toml:38`, DW-03.10).
- Failure: promotion/demotion via `/api/whitelist/set-admin` diverges from the static set — an operator demoted only in D1 retains search-side admin, or a promoted one is denied there; enforcement becomes worker-dependent.
- Confidence: High
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes ("Known gap", Gap 2)

### F-06 — The `/data/team` listing guard exists only in the Vite dev server; production serves the directory as plain files with no filter
- Topics: DW-02.4
- Evidence: the middleware (reject `..`/`%2e`, filter to `*.md`, drop dotfiles) "exists only in the Vite dev server (`configureServer`); the production static build serves `public/data/team/` as plain files with no equivalent runtime guard" (DW-02.4, `vite.config.ts:29-36`).
- Failure: a stray non-markdown or dotfile committed under `public/data/team/` ships publicly; the dev-time filter that would have hidden it never runs in the deployed artifact.
- Confidence: High (gap stated); exposure depends on file contents.
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — Kit-pin provenance text is wrong in three places while the gate checks only the SHA, so humans are misled exactly where they'd look during a re-pin
- Topics: DW-01.5, DW-06.6 (I1), DW-06.7 (D9, D12)
- Evidence: workflow comments say `v1.0.0-beta.14` (`deploy.yml:113`, `rust-ci.yml:19`, `workers-deploy.yml:40`) vs crates at `1.0.0-beta.15` (`forum-config/Cargo.toml:49-52`); the record's History marks `931898a` "Current (canonical)" two releases behind (`kit-compatibility-record.md:347` vs `:30`); README states a third, older pin `2d693ed2…` beta.6 (`README.md:298`); "pin-parity compares the SHA, so the stale version annotation does not fail the gate" (DW-01.5).
- Failure: an operator re-pins trusting the History table or README and propagates a wrong-but-internally-consistent pin; parity passes because they updated every site including the canonical field.
- Confidence: High (facts stated); failure chain inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (three Drift entries)

### F-08 — The poker parameter mirror is hand-synced and invisible to the completeness sweep
- Topics: DW-03.6, DW-03.7
- Evidence: `dreamlab.toml [poker]` is "mirrored BY HAND into `deploy.yml POKER_CONFIG_JSON`" (`dreamlab.toml:305-308`; `deploy.yml:83-89`, injected `:274,346`); neither site appears in `MIRROR_BEARING_KEYS` (`config-mirrors.mjs:41-47`), so "a `[poker]` edit that misses the deploy mirror ships stale table parameters silently" (DW-03.6); more broadly the sweep only catches reuse of the six known key names (DW-03.7).
- Failure: table stakes/blinds/house-seat parameters diverge between authored intent and the deployed DREAM table; the same blind spot applies to any future governed var with a novel name.
- Confidence: High
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes ("Drift (an eighth, unenumerated mirror)")

### F-09 — The zone-encryption design has no written record: ADR-2016 is cited by config but does not exist, and the governing doc's invariant is breached
- Topics: DW-03.1, DW-06.6 (I7), DW-06.7 (D15)
- Evidence: `dreamlab.toml:34,149` cite 'ADR-2016' but "no ADR-2016-*.md exists in docs/adr/ at HEAD — the record was never written" (DW-06.6 I7); `IDENTITY-zones.md:166-167` still says only zone3 is encrypted while `dreamlab.toml:123,137,148` mark zone2/3/4 `encrypted = true`.
- Failure: an engineer or operator acting on the governing doc mis-models which zones are confidential and how the master gate behaves — e.g., treats a zone2 flip as inert, or misses that `enabled` is itself a three-site hand mirror.
- Confidence: High
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes ("BREACHED at HEAD"; D15)

### F-10 — Talk-to-AI has no request/reply correlation, an unverified identity hint, and replies only via third-party open relays
- Topics: DW-04.3
- Evidence: "a late answer to a timed-out question can otherwise read as the answer to whatever was asked most recently" (`AIChatFab.tsx:9-19` closeout, `IDENTITY-zones.md:189`); `identityHint` is "EXPLICITLY UNVERIFIED" (`AIChatFab.tsx:429-430`); replies are "readable only from open relays" — `relay.damus.io`, `relay.primal.net` — because the primary relay's whitelist gate rejects wraps addressed to the ephemeral key (DW-04.3).
- Failure: users receive misattributed answers; the conversation also depends on two external public relays remaining in the agent's publish fan-out (invariant I8 is a stated precondition, `IDENTITY-zones.md:168-169`).
- Confidence: High
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (closeout/Open notes)

### F-11 — No automated end-to-end verification of the deployed estate; the only CORS-scoping assertion lives in a manual-only Playwright spec
- Topics: DW-05.1, DW-01.6
- Evidence: Playwright specs are "deliberately NOT wired into `ci.yml`, `test-and-lint.yml` or any gate job"; `npx playwright test` requires a running deployment (`CLAUDE.md:63-64`); `forum-smoke.spec.ts:404-410` is the sole workers-CORS assertion; client config arrives via deploy-time `sed` injection of `window.__ENV__` (DW-01.6), a surface no unit test sees.
- Failure: a deploy-time injection or worker misconfiguration ships green through every gate and is first discovered by users.
- Confidence: High (facts); failure inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (documented as a deliberate choice, not flagged as risk)

### F-12 — Anonymous kind-1059 ingress has only a per-IP rate limit; no per-recipient throttle, no PoW/CAPTCHA, and it bypasses content moderation
- Topics: DW-04.5
- Evidence: "10 events/s/IP relay limit only — no PoW/CAPTCHA/per-recipient throttle; kind 1059 bypasses content moderation" (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:282`); LLM-cost abuse mitigated only by client-side serialization/throttle and a manual kill-switch (`:284`).
- Failure: a scripted flood of gift-wraps saturates the relay or drains the junkiejarvis agent's budget from rotating IPs; unmoderated content lands in agent inboxes.
- Confidence: High
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (threat register T1/T3)

### F-13 — Clients ship with raw `*.workers.dev` hosts baked into build env; the branded custom domains remain unprovisioned and unverified
- Topics: DW-01.3, DW-06.7 (D4)
- Evidence: branded domains "not provisioned in DNS — the client instead talks to raw `*.workers.dev` hosts baked into the build env (`deploy.yml:50-54`; verified 2026-06-09 `ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED`, not re-checked as of 2026-08-31) — shipping the branded domains baked in previously severed every client API call" (DW-01.3).
- Failure: a partial DNS re-cut toward the branded domains (or a workers.dev hostname change on redeploy) severs all client API traffic — a repeat of the documented precedent; the "not re-checked" note means current resolvability is unverified.
- Confidence: High (state and precedent stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (D4)

### F-14 — The deploy pipeline is order-fragile: BBS must build after the forum merge, env is sed-injected, and deep links depend on a 404 shim whose inline-script variant is CSP-blocked
- Topics: DW-01.6, DW-01.7
- Evidence: the BBS "build[s] after the forum merge so `cp kit/dist/*` does not grab the bbs subtree" (DW-01.6); the 404-redirect shim is "load-bearing, not incidental" (`deploy.yml:353-383, 277-286`, `BASELINE-architecture.md:152-155`); an inline pickup script is impossible for the React shell because `index.html`'s CSP has no `'unsafe-inline'` — "exactly the bug class that broke `/workshops` deep links" (DW-01.7).
- Failure: a workflow re-order clobbers the bbs subtree or a pickup-script change reintroduces the inline-script class; deep links and community auth silently break post-deploy with all gates green.
- Confidence: High (fragility stated); specific failure inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes ("load-bearing" callout; documented bug class)

### F-15 — First-user-is-admin bootstrap re-arms whenever no `is_admin=1` row exists — reachable after any improvised D1 restore
- Topics: DW-04.6, DW-07.5
- Evidence: "GET /api/setup-status reports needsSetup when no is_admin=1 row exists" (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:259`); there is no restore tooling — "only the raw D1/R2/KV export" (DW-07.5) — so any recovery from backup is manual and could land an auth D1 without admin rows.
- Failure: following a D1 loss/restore, the system re-enters bootstrap and the first signup — attacker or ordinary member — becomes admin before the operator notices.
- Confidence: Low-to-medium, inferred (mechanism stated; the restore-without-admin-rows path is not).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

## estate / critical

### F-01 — Shipped compose defaults to total auth bypass and can be exposed to the public internet via a tunnel the repo cannot audit
- Topics: ES-10.3, ES-10.6, ES-01.3
- Evidence: The dev compose defaults `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE: ${VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1}` (docker-compose.unified.yml:90), which activates a "BYPASS IS TOTAL — no NIP-98, no token, no peer check" path (ES-10.6, src/utils/auth.rs:99). Simultaneously `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS:-1` and `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS:-1` (docker-compose.unified.yml:106-107) invert the fail-closed code defaults. The standalone cloudflared tunnel (docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:3-8) "fronts WHATEVER profile the backend booted" and its "route is configured as a Cloudflare dashboard Public Hostname — no local config.yml, so the exposed mapping is NOT reviewable from this repo" (docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:5-6). ADR-2119 makes the unified file's tunnel opt-in "but leaves this standalone file unaffected" (ADR-2119:64-66).
- Failure: An operator starts the dev stack (default posture) and the standalone tunnel (perhaps left over from earlier); the tunnel publishes the dev service to the internet with anonymous /api reads and total auth bypass active, and nothing in the repository reveals the mapping.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially (CFT and LANGAP nodes exist; the compound of DEV_MODE=1 default + unauditable tunnel route is not stated as one scenario)

### F-02 — ES-04.4 and ES-08.2 contradict each other about whether a failed pod signer goes unsigned or throws, at the same verified commit
- Topics: ES-04.4, ES-08.2
- Evidence: ES-04.4 diagrams `signer unavailable → S-->>H: none → H-->>CALLER: no header → CALLER->>POD: UNSIGNED write` with the note "DIVERGENCE — pod signing can fall back UNSIGNED." ES-08.2 states "RESOLVED ADR-2064 (2026-09-05): _signedFetch throws typed SigningUnavailable when requireSigned and no header can be built — no request is emitted" (_solid-http-base.js:103-106). Both sections cite the identical agentbox commit `6466e3931`. They cannot both be correct about what the code does at that revision for the case sign_requests=true and the signer unavailable.
- Failure: An engineer reads ES-04.4, concludes unsigned fallback is live, and ships a mitigation; or reads ES-08.2, concludes it is fixed, and skips mitigation. Whichever section is stale describes the wrong runtime behaviour for a credential-bearing write path.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (each section marks its own claim; the contradiction between them is unflagged)

### F-03 — Deleting agent memory from the pod leaves the embedding row live and searchable in RuVector, with no backup or restore either side
- Topics: ES-08.9, ES-07.9 D5, ES-07.1
- Evidence: `agentMemory.ts:196-204 has no reverse-tombstone dispatch to mcp claude-flow memory_delete or equivalent` (ES-08.9). The embedding row persists and "stays semantically searchable" (ES-07.9 D5). "No point-in-time RuVector backup exists, so there is no cross-store consistent restore, RPO or RTO" (ES-07.9 D5). Invariant 6 of DATA-authority-erasure requires a dropped secondary write to be logged; "this call path does not even attempt the secondary write, let alone log its absence" (ES-08.9).
- Failure: A GDPR erasure request or operator-initiated deletion removes the pod resource but the vector embedding remains discoverable by semantic search indefinitely. There is no backup to restore from and no consistent snapshot to reason about what was lost.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, ES-07.9 D5 and ES-08.9)

### F-04 — Hand-rolled Bitcoin Schnorr and TapSighash scalar arithmetic in the released crate
- Topics: ES-08.12
- Evidence: The released crate "HAND-ROLLS it: BIP-341 scripts, the TapSighash and key-path Schnorr on raw k256, with big-endian mod-n scalar arithmetic written out by hand" (ADR-2008:22-26). The port to rust-bitcoin "landed on main, unreleased" (ADR-2008:119-158), so the hand-rolled code is what consumers get today. The crate's own README says "not to carry value through the payment routes until it is fixed" (ADR-2008:72-75).
- Failure: A consensus-relevant bug in the hand-written mod-n arithmetic produces an invalid signature or an incorrect sighash, causing a funds-losing transaction broadcast or a balance miscalculation that no test catches before it hits a chain.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (PROPOSED with precondition, ADR-2008)

### F-05 — Three independent sats ledgers, none synced, with a 501 stub on the host deposit path
- Topics: ES-03.12, ES-01.8
- Evidence: "the estate runs THREE independent did:nostr-keyed sats ledgers and NONE of them is synced with the others" (sovereign-settlement.md:61). The host FsPaymentStore deposit handler returns 501 so "no value ever entered it" (ADR-2111:22-25). The `payment_settlement` authority class "currently GATES NOTHING" (economy-loop.md:270). Version pins skew: "the forum record still reads the host at solid-pod-rs 0.4.0-alpha.15 while the forum resolves 0.5.0-alpha.7" (ADR-2012:28-30).
- Failure: A settlement is recorded in one ledger, a balance is read from another, and the two disagree. There is no reconciliation path and no gate that would detect the divergence before a spending decision is made on stale numbers.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, ES-03.12 FACT node)

### F-06 — Nostr bridge re-signs under its own key: no user attribution through agents
- Topics: ES-04.1
- Evidence: "nostr_bridge.rs re-signs under the BRIDGE key (service-signed, not delegation), so no request can be attributed to a user THROUGH an agent" (ES-04.1 DIVERGENCE note). NIP-26 delegation is NOT wired.
- Failure: A user delegates a task to an agent; the agent's actions on external relays are indistinguishable from the bridge service itself. In a dispute or audit, there is no cryptographic evidence tying a specific action to the user who authorised it. Governance decisions made through this path are attributed to a service key, not a human.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-07 — Chrome DevTools Protocol and VNC published on 0.0.0.0 with no stated authentication
- Topics: ES-01.3
- Evidence: browsercontainer publishes "0.0.0.0 5903 VNC / 8931 MCP SSE, host 9222 to container 9223 CDP" (docker-compose.browsercontainer.yml:51-53). These are on the CI-enforced SANCTIONED list (check-ports-loopback.mjs:97-99). CDP has no built-in authentication mechanism. The material presents these as "DECIDED exposures" with "cited rationale" but does not state any authentication protecting them.
- Failure: Anyone on the LAN connects to port 9222 (CDP), executes arbitrary JavaScript in the browser session, reads page content including authenticated sessions, or takes control of the VNC display on 5903 to interact with the container's desktop.
- Confidence: medium (authentication may exist in the container; the material does not mention it either way)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the ports are "sanctioned" but the security consequence of unauthenticated CDP on the LAN is not flagged)

### F-08 — A literal production IP address survives in the estate tree despite a committed generalisation that claims none remain
- Topics: ES-06.9, ES-01.2
- Evidence: ES-01.2's GEN note states "commit 2899b3b7e generalised every literal estate address out of this public repository, so the hostnames, the rail subnet and the retired IP that earlier revisions of this diagram cited are no longer stated in any cited source." ES-06.9's state diagram note still reads "Reached over ssh john@10.10.10.1" — a literal address in the same corpus.
- Failure: The generalisation commit was supposed to remove operational infrastructure details from the public repo. The estate tree itself (a public artifact) still carries a reachable address. Anyone reading the repo gains a direct target for the connected node.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — Rationale gate reads the DECLARED tier; no VisionClaw writer declares one, so neither gate fires on a VisionClaw case
- Topics: ES-05.12, ES-05.13
- Evidence: "the server rationale gate reads the case's DECLARED tier, and no VisionClaw writer records one" (ADR-2110:132-160). "VisionClaw's 31400 carries only a d tag and a PanelDefinition with no tier or task-property field, and its 31402 carries d, priority, category, subject-kind, subject-id and title" (events.rs:211-217, events.rs:91-101, events.rs:308-323). "so the relay folds every VisionClaw case to the advertised default, medium, and check_rationale only bites at high or critical: NEITHER rationale gate fires on a VisionClaw case" (governance.rs:525, wrangler.toml:48, governance.rs:769).
- Failure: A high-impact irreversible decision is made through a VisionClaw panel without any rationale requirement, because the system has no way to know it is high-impact. The governance invariant "no surface may fabricate a human's rationale" is silently vacuous for this entire class of cases.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN)

### F-10 — SONA learning returns status "learned" but accumulates nothing due to a dimension mismatch
- Topics: ES-07.9 D1
- Evidence: "The prebuilt @ruvector/sona@0.1.5 NAPI binary hardcodes embedding_dim = 256, so 384-dim learns return status:learned but accumulate NOTHING (verified live)" (ES-07.9 D1).
- Failure: The learning system reports success while silently discarding every pattern. An operator believes the system is improving from experience; no error is raised, no warning logged, and no data is retained. The failure is invisible until someone manually inspects the store.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE D1)

### F-11 — No point-in-time backup for the Solid pod store; backup script covers SQLite only
- Topics: ES-08.8
- Evidence: "no point-in-time backup for the pod store, scripts backup-sqlite.sh covers SQLite only" (ES-08.8, DATA-authority-erasure.md Known divergences). "no cross-store consistent restore, no declared RPO or RTO" (ES-08.8).
- Failure: The pod filesystem (agent memories, ontology contributions, preferences, inbox) is corrupted or accidentally deleted. There is no backup to restore from. The ontology can be re-pulled from the release, but user-authored pod content is unrecoverable.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-12 — Under role_isolation=true, the dream engine governance panel has no signing path
- Topics: ES-05.15
- Evidence: The dream engine publishes 31400 and 31402 as JunkieJarvis (governance.rs:9-10) and is listed for the W3b move onto the port (ADR-2122:224-225), but the port's refusal list blocks kinds 31400-31405 for any container caller (acl.rs:71-73). "under the flag the dream panel has no signer at this revision."
- Failure: Enabling role_isolation for security hardening silently breaks the dream engine's ability to raise governance cases. Nightly cycles that should generate panels and requests produce nothing, and the absence is not alerted — the panel simply doesn't appear.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION)

### F-13 — Docker socket widened o+rw for devuser when role_isolation=false (the shipped default)
- Topics: ES-10.12
- Evidence: "the entrypoint widens the host Docker socket o+rw for devuser" (entrypoint-unified.sh:589-593). "every program but bootstrap and tailscale runs as devuser, and PID 1's environment, .env included, reaches all of them" (ES-10.12 O1-O2). role_isolation defaults to false.
- Failure: Any code running as devuser inside the container (agent, MCP tool, skill) can drive the host's Docker daemon without sudo, mount arbitrary host paths into new containers, and escape the container boundary entirely. All secrets in PID 1's environment are readable by every devuser process.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (design documented, residual risk flagged as R2 in AB-36)

### F-14 — MCP client disables an HTTP/SSE server for the entire session on slow startup and never retries
- Topics: ES-06.8
- Evidence: "The client disables an HTTP/SSE server for the ENTIRE session when the startup initialize exceeds MCP_TIMEOUT, and it does NOT retry HTTP servers after boot" (ES-06.8). The durable fix is noted as "in the build, applied on next nix buildout" — meaning it has not yet taken effect on the running image.
- Failure: A cold LAN model takes >30s to warm at session start; the email-search tools are silently absent for the whole session with no error surfaced to the agent, no retry, and no indication that a restart would fix it. Work is degraded without anyone knowing why.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (with fix noted as pending rebuild)

### F-15 — Adapter contract versions frozen at 1.0.0 despite live churn; consumers cannot detect breaking changes
- Topics: ES-03.9
- Evidence: "pods, memory, events and orchestrator all still declare 1.0.0 despite live churn, so a breaking change would need a MAJOR bump that has NOT happened. A consumer cannot tell from the version whether the contract it compiled against still holds" (ES-03.9 DIVERGENCE note, BASELINE-container.md).
- Failure: An adapter implementation is updated with an incompatible wire format; the version still says 1.0.0, so a consumer that checked the version proceeds and fails at runtime with data corruption or a crash, having been told nothing changed.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

---

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Whether the cloudflared standalone tunnel is actually running in production**, and what Cloudflare dashboard Public Hostname routes it exposes — the material says this is not reviewable from the repo, and I would need the operator's Cloudflare account to verify the actual exposure.

2. **Whether browsercontainer's CDP port 9222 and VNC port 5903 have any authentication** (a Chrome flag, socat wrapper, VNC password, or network-level ACL) — the material states the ports are published but says nothing about auth on those listeners.

3. **Whether the fifteen stale ADR records (ES-09.23) include any that make security-relevant claims** — I would need the actual list of which records fail and what governed paths they name, to assess whether any stale claim is load-bearing for a production decision.

4. **The actual runtime value of `AGENTBOX_AGENT_EVENT_AUTH` and `sign_requests` in the deployed agentbox.toml** — the material shows defaults (off and false respectively) but I cannot tell what the production manifest overrides them to.

5. **Whether the hand-rolled crypto in solid-pod-rs (F-04) is reachable from any production code path today** — the material says the payment routes should not carry value, but I would need to verify that no caller actually invokes them with real funds.

## estate / premortem

# Post-mortem — client-loss incident (data / custody / security class)

Written against the estate tree at the verified commits each topic carries. Every claim below is the material's own, with its citation; where I reason past the material I say "inferred".

---

### R-1 — Shipped dev compose boots a total-auth-bypass container, and a standalone Cloudflare tunnel can put it on the public internet invisibly to every audit
- Topics: ES-10.3, ES-10.6, ES-10.1, ES-10.2, ES-01.3, ES-09.5
- Chain of events:
  1. Operator brings the stack up with the shipped defaults: the dev service gets `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS:-1`, `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS:-1`, `RBAC_DEFAULT_ROLE:-editor` and `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1` (docker-compose.unified.yml:106, 107, 114, 90) — net posture demo-open.
  2. The dev container is built with features defaulting to `gpu,ontology,dev-auth` (scripts/rust-backend-wrapper.sh:42), so `enforce_release_env_hygiene` is the no-op stub (src/main.rs:169) and the boot-time profile assertion only logs and continues in a debug build (security_profile.rs:657-661) — nothing refuses this posture.
  3. With `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE=1` in a dev/dev-auth build, `dev_full_bypass_active()` returns true (src/utils/auth.rs:99) and the bypass is TOTAL — no NIP-98, no token, no peer check, across REST `verify_access`, the settings extractor and the WS handshake, peer-agnostic by design.
  4. The standalone cloudflared file (docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:3-8) fronts whatever profile booted, is outbound-initiated so it reaches nginx port 3001 as an ordinary network peer and is invisible to the ADR-2013 compose port audit that only sweeps published ports; its Public Hostname route lives in the Cloudflare dashboard and is not reviewable from this repo (docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:5-6, image pinned :16).
  5. ADR-2119 explicitly leaves this standalone file unaffected (ADR-2119-prod-ingress-is-declared-lan-or-tunnel.md:64-66): declaring `VISIONCLAW_INGRESS=lan` does not stop a hand-started standalone tunnel. An internet caller now has unauthenticated, unrestricted graph API and WS access; client material in the graph is disclosed and writable.
- Evidence: compose defaults and the demo-open net posture (ES-10.3, docker-compose.unified.yml:106-120); the total-bypass semantics (ES-10.6, src/utils/auth.rs:99, ADR-2039-visionclaw-dev-mode-lan-local-bypass.md:5-7); the stubbed hygiene gate in dev-auth builds (ES-10.2, src/main.rs:169); the tunnel's invisibility and non-reviewability (ES-10.3 CFT node, docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:3-16); the ADR-2119 gap (ES-10.3 LANGAP, ADR-2119:64-66); dev ports 3001/4000 published (ES-01.3, docker-compose.unified.yml:174-175).
- Warning signs visible today: the material already states the net shipped posture is demo-open with anonymous `/api` reads ON, that the standalone tunnel carries that to the public internet, and that no repo-visible config describes the tunnel's hostname mapping.
- Confidence: high — every link is stated; only the operator's choice to run the tunnel is assumed.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Erasure is a no-op against RuVector: deleting agent memory leaves the embedding row semantically searchable, so a client's deleted personal data is later served back
- Topics: ES-08.9, ES-07.9 (D5), ES-07.1, ES-07.3
- Chain of events:
  1. Client exercises erasure; operator deletes the agent's memory through the pod UI: `SolidPodService.deleteAgentMemory` → `agentMemory.deleteAgentMemory` → `ldpClient.deleteResource` issues an HTTP DELETE and treats 404 as success (agentMemory.ts:196-204, ldpClient.ts:206-216).
  2. The pod resource is gone (204), but this call graph never reaches RuVector — there is no reverse-tombstone dispatch to any memory delete (agentMemory.ts:196-204).
  3. The corresponding embedding row persists in ruvector-postgres and stays HNSW-searchable; any later `memory_search` on that namespace returns the deleted content, and an agent surfaces it in an answer or beam to the client.
  4. Recovery is impossible even forensically: there is no point-in-time RuVector backup, no cross-store consistent restore, no RPO or RTO.
- Evidence: ES-08.9 ("no reverse-tombstone dispatch… never attempts the secondary write, let alone logs its absence", contra Invariant 6 of docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md); ES-07.9 D5 ("deleteAgentMemory() in the Pod does not revoke the RuVector-held agent memory… Largest erasure hole. No point-in-time RuVector backup… no cross-store consistent restore, RPO or RTO"); the search path that will serve it (ES-07.3, ruvector-mcp.cjs:290).
- Warning signs visible today: the material names this the largest erasure hole and records the governing invariant it violates, today, unremediated.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — With role_isolation off (the shipped default) every secret-bearing process runs as one uid with PID 1's environment, so any agent or MCP child can read the estate's signing keys and the Docker socket
