- Topics: ES-10.12, ES-04.5, ES-05.10, ES-05.15, ES-10.10, ES-07.10
- Chain of events:
  1. `role_isolation = false`: the entrypoint widens the host Docker socket `o+rw` for devuser (agentbox/config/entrypoint-unified.sh:589-593) and every program but bootstrap and tailscale runs as devuser, with PID 1's environment — `.env` included — reaching all of them.
  2. A compromised session or child (e.g., a proxied ruflo child, ES-07.10, or any same-uid agent — "same-UID agents are not isolated by application-level policy", ES-05.10) reads the bridge identity key at `/run/secrets/nostr.key` (ES-04.5 K1), the AoE token file — explicitly "a same-uid (devuser) process can still READ the AoE token file" (ES-04.5 D3) — and every bearer/API key in the inherited environment.
  3. It signs nostr events and pod writes as the estate's bridge identity, uses the AoE token, and — because the socket is `o+rw` — launches arbitrary containers with arbitrary mounts to exfiltrate client data.
  4. Blast radius is amplified because the house key `11ed6422` "stays admitted for every use until its roles are listed as withdrawn, and that list is empty" (agentbox/config/custody/g5-key-split.json:32), and `.env` is plaintext because SOPS was never executed (ES-10.10 D5).
- Evidence: ES-10.12 OFF branch (entrypoint-unified.sh:589-593 and the PID-1-env claim); ES-04.5 D3 and K1; ES-05.10 UID boundary; ES-05.15 OPN (g5-key-split.json:32); ES-10.10 D5 (ADR-109 accepted 2026-05-09, no SOPS artifacts in tree).
- Warning signs visible today: the material states all of the above as present-tense fact, gated only behind a flag that defaults off pending the owner's rehearsal.
- Confidence: high for the mechanism; the initial compromise vector is inferred.
- Severity: high

## Further latent defects

### F-01 — No point-in-time backup for the pod store or RuVector; the only backup script covers SQLite
- Topics: ES-08.8, ES-07.9
- Evidence: "no point-in-time backup for the pod store, scripts/backup-sqlite.sh covers SQLite only… no cross-store consistent restore, no declared RPO or RTO" (ES-08.8); "No point-in-time RuVector backup exists, so there is no cross-store consistent restore, RPO or RTO" (ES-07.9 D5).
- Failure: a corrupt redb file, a bad batch write or a destructive adapter run destroys client pod data and vector memory permanently; there is nothing to restore from.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence, both topics).
- Severity: high

### F-02 — The tree contradicts itself on whether any gate stops a dev-auth release binary
- Topics: ES-09.11, ES-09.5
- Evidence: ES-09.11 lists a blocking `dev-auth-release-gate` job (project/.github/workflows/ci.yml:143, "no --release cargo line may name dev-auth" :163-168); ES-09.5 states "ADR-2037 (proposed, implementation_status none): no CI or image-build assertion yet verifies a shipped release binary omits dev-auth — a mis-targeted pipeline could promote the stubbed-hygiene binary to production undetected" — both at the same verified visionclaw commit.
- Failure: if ES-09.5 is the true state (or the text-level gate is bypassed by a feature alias), a release ships with `SETTINGS_AUTH_BYPASS` compiled in and `enforce_release_env_hygiene` stubbed — total auth bypass in production.
- Confidence: medium — one of the two diagrams is stale; which one is unverified.
- Marked by authors: yes (the risk is marked in ES-09.5; the contradiction is not).
- Severity: high

- Topics: ES-06.3, ES-06.2

- Failure: any process or agent holding the gateway bearer — which is environment-borne under role_isolation off (R-3) — names the operator's publicly-known pubkey and receives raw client mail, filter bypassed.

- Failure: a compromised sidecar reaches the pod on 8484, the relay, ruvector-postgres and every other internal listener with no publish-based gate ever firing.

## knowledgegraph / critical

### F-01 — The publication-safety guarantees (private-ID scan, census accounting, count/identity lockstep, per-export manifests) are enforced only by an archived pipeline, while the invariants register still asserts them as live
- Topics: KG-07.1, KG-07.2, KG-05.3, KG-01.1
- Evidence: KG-07.1 states I6 ("EXPECTED_CLASSES in build.yml must equal the true class count", BASELINE-narrativegoldmine.md:182-214), I8 (input_files == parsed+rejected+excluded), I9 ("visibility.py at every output, release gate re-scans") and I11 (manifest.py, SHA-256 per artefact) — and KG-07's own source list puts all four enforcers under `archive/logseq-era-2026-09-22/pipeline/` (census.py, visibility.py, manifest.py, release_gate.py) plus `archive/github-workflows/build.yml`. KG-05.3 states build.yml is "ARCHIVED and inert" and names the successor pipeline's only gates as vault validate/build (publish.yml:157-190) and a "space and Earth domain contract" gate (publish.yml:296-297). No private-identifier scan, census balance, identity agreement, or manifest gate is named anywhere in the live path.
- Failure: the source corpus demonstrably contained private-flagged material (I1/I9 and `visibility.py:133 is_private_iri` exist because `public:: false` mattered). Every publish to narrativegoldmine.com since 2026-09-23 runs with no stated private-identifier rescan; a private page or IRI that survived the Logseq→vault migration is published publicly and nothing detects it.
- Confidence: medium — the archival is stated; the absence of successor gates is inferred from the material naming none (the vault's full gate set is not shown)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — KG-07.2 marks the pipeline as archived *history*, but KG-07.1 still presents I6/I8/I9/I10/I11 as operating invariants; no marker says their enforcement machinery is gone

### F-02 — No rebuild, hotfix, or rollback path exists except one human: the sole producer runs only on the owner's push or by hand, and this repo's builder is inert with its inputs deleted
- Topics: KG-05.3, KG-05.5, KG-01.1
- Evidence: "vault is the ONLY producer — README.md:11-17" (KG-05.3); "Agents never push to visionGraph main — publish.yml runs only on the owner's push or by hand" (KG-05.5 note2); "this repo no longer authors or builds the corpus … [build.yml] cannot run again even if triggered (its inputs are gone) — README.md:5-17" (KG-01.1). The same KG-05.5 note1 frames "one vault, one build, one human gate" as an INVARIANT (VisionFlow ADR-2013).
- Failure: any production emergency on narrativegoldmine.com — including removing content that leaked under F-01, or reverting a bad corpus build — can only be actioned by one person manually running a pipeline in a different repository. If that person is unavailable, the site serves whatever gh-pages last received, indefinitely, with no alternative producer anywhere in the estate.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — the single-human gate is presented as a design invariant, never as a risk

### F-03 — The tracked corpus `dist/data/` is frozen at the last run of the retired pipeline, yet is still cast as the artefact consumers fetch
- Topics: KG-06.2, KG-01.1, KG-05.3
- Evidence: "dist/data/ — 12 tracked files — last local pipeline build, ODbL-1.0" (KG-01.1); the pipeline was archived 2026-09-22 (README.md:16, KG-05.5). KG-06.2's ingest sequence still casts "this repo — dist/data/ontology.ttl, 258,200 triples" as the fetched artefact (ecosystem.md:87-88) and its own bullet calls the GitHub-fetch "the remaining path from this repo's published dist/data/ontology.ttl" after CorpusSource defaulted to the vault (ADR-2114).
- Failure: any consumer on the remaining path — or anyone following ecosystem.md, which KG-06.2 confirms still describes this fetch — downloads a pre-transition snapshot and reasons over it (VisionClaw writes it into Oxigraph and expands it with WHELK) believing it is the current corpus. Nothing in the repo marks `dist/data/` as frozen; it is also the ODbL "dataset of record" with LICENSE-DATA attached, so the licensed dataset and the deployed corpus silently diverge.

- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — the path's demotion to "secondary" is marked; the fact that the secondary artefact is *permanently frozen* is not

### F-06 — Prose delivery depends on a filename convention the producer and consumer disagree on, with a third-party host as the safety net
- Topics: KG-04.4, KG-05.3
- Evidence: pageService.ts:133 fetches `/api/markdown/${title}.md` (TITLE-form); the marked DIVERGENCE says jsonld_to_page_api.py writes `api/markdown/SLUG.md` — "pageService.ts never requests" it — while the live build still generates a mirror (vault build `--with-markdown-mirror`, publish.yml:182-190). On primary 404, pageService.ts:134 falls back to raw.githubusercontent.com, guarded only by isValidMarkdown content sniffing against "a static host 404-ing to index.html" (rejecting HTML content-type, sessionStorage.setItem bodies, DOCTYPE).
- Failure: for every page whose title ≠ slug — the common case, since the six resolution strategies exist precisely because names differ — the mirror misses and prose is served from raw.githubusercontent.com: rate-limited, ref-dependent, and able to return content version-skewed from the deployed SPA. A GitHub outage or branch rename blanks page bodies site-wide while gh-pages itself is healthy.
- Confidence: high on the mechanism; medium (inferred) on how many pages depend on the fallback
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the SLUG/TITLE divergence is marked; the raw-host dependency and its failure mode are not

### F-08 — The only guard between corpus growth and a thrown RangeError in every visitor's browser is a client-side assert whose server-side counterpart was retired
- Topics: KG-04.2, KG-04.3, KG-07.1, KG-05.3
- Evidence: assertScope throws RangeError above MAX_NODES 1500 / MAX_EDGES 4000 / FOCUS_MAX 300 (explorer/modern/src/types/scope.ts:144-155), and KG-04.3 insists rejection is the contract ("never silently truncated"); T2 ego graphs are "client-derived" (KG-04.2), so the budget depends on live data shape. The invariant that tied corpus counts to a gate — I6, EXPECTED_CLASSES in build.yml (BASELINE:182-214) — lost its enforcer when build.yml was archived (KG-05.3); the only successor gate named is the "space and Earth domain contract" (publish.yml:296-297), with no stated tie to explorer budgets.
- Failure: the vault legitimately grows a domain past 1,500 nodes or a hub class past 300 neighbours; vault validate passes, the domain gate passes, publish succeeds — and the /graph route throws RangeError for every visitor of that tier. A data-driven outage of the site's core view, undetected until users report it.
- Confidence: medium — the throw and the retired gate are stated; the absence of any budget gate in publish.yml is inferred from the material naming none
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — the caps are framed as contract; nobody flags that their lockstep check left with build.yml

### F-09 — Downstream agent grounding updates only via a fire-after-deploy dispatch into a single store, with no stated reconciliation
- Topics: KG-06.2, KG-05.3
- Evidence: publish.yml dispatches corpus-sync to VisionClaw "after the deploy" with the source sha (publish.yml:459-465); KG-06.2 states agentbox's Loom facade and the ontology-bridge MCP tools (ontology_ask/ontology_search/kg_pathfind) ground against VisionClaw's single Oxigraph + WHELK instance, never this repo's Turtle. No retry, idempotency, or drift-detection semantics are stated.
- Failure: the dispatch fails (or VisionClaw is down) after a successful site deploy; every subsequent agentbox session grounds against a stale ontology while the public site shows the new one — agents silently answer from superseded classes with no drift signal. Conversely, that one Oxigraph instance is a single point of failure for all ontology tooling.
- Confidence: low-medium (inferred — the dispatch is stated; its failure handling is not)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — The NGG1 binary format is pinned by a golden fixture across three codebases that no single CI is stated to run, while the deployed reader pin has already diverged
- Topics: KG-04.1, KG-04.6, KG-07.1
- Evidence: I2 pins "writer + both readers" to a 183-byte golden fixture (BASELINE:182-214). The writer is now the external vault; the readers are vowl-wasm 0.1.1 (GitHub release tarball, explorer/modern/package.json:20) and 0.1.2 (npm, shipping tree, WasmVOWL/modern/package.json:20) — the skew is marked DIVERGED (KG-04.6). FORMAT-NGG1.md, the format's home document, lives in the repository that ships neither reader.
- Failure: the vault emits a format tweak; whichever reader's fixture is not run in the same CI as that writer silently misparses the 24-byte node records / u16 category and renders wrong categories or garbage positions in production with no gate failing anywhere.
- Confidence: low-medium (inferred — fixture coverage across the three repos is not shown; only the pin divergence is stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the 0.1.1/0.1.2 divergence is marked; the fixture-governance gap is not

### F-11 — The governing documents for each topic still describe the pre-retirement system as live
- Topics: KG-05.3, KG-04.1, KG-06.2, KG-06.4, KG-07.3
- Evidence: build-and-gates.md:1-3 (the governing doc for KG-05) "still describes build.yml as this repo's active 'reproducible half' running six gates" (marked DIVERGENCE, KG-05.3); BASELINE-narrativegoldmine.md:14 and explorer.md:10 cite a nonexistent in-tree crate (KG-04.1); pipeline.md/explorer.md still write against a 7,874-page corpus that is now 8,138 — "every derived count needs re-verification" (KG-07.3 STALE); ecosystem.md:121-127 asserts VisionFlow's licensing.md:11 "still says" MPL 2.0 when it now reads AGPL — the staleness has inverted onto this repo (KG-06.2); ontocast.md:1-4 presents an archived import as the live seam (KG-06.4).
- Failure: on-call or a new owner acts on the governing docs — expects six gates to have run, expects an in-tree crate, trusts derived counts — and every operational decision starts from a false model. The 2026-09-07 audit qualification in KG-05.5 shows this exact failure already occurred once at whole-topic scale.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — each item individually marked (DIVERGENCE/DOC-DRIFT/STALE), but the governing files are uncorrected and the pattern is systemic rather than isolated

- Failure: the canon other repos treat as authoritative — including licensing statements relied on in corrections — carries no grant at all: nobody outside the estate has defined reproduction rights, and internal reliance on an unlicenced authority record becomes a concrete governance defect the moment the estate changes hands, which is precisely the scenario of taking over production.

Fixing the convention requires coordinated changes in two repositories (see F-06).

- Topics: KG-04.4
- Evidence: resolution is listed as "exact id, ci title, labels[], IRI/suffix, camelCase, hyphen-form" against /api/search-index.json; the material states no precedence rule, collision handling, or ambiguity reporting (contrast KG-06.4, where the OntoCast seam explicitly reports and skips slug collisions rather than overwriting).
- Failure: two pages whose titles, labels, or hyphen/camel forms collide (e.g., one page's label equal to another's hyphen-form) resolve to one of them silently; on a public reference site the wrong canonical page renders with no error logged anywhere.

- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **The vault/publish gate set.** Whether `vault build` or publish.yml reimplements I1/I8/I9/I10/I11 (vc:public boolean check, private-IRI scan, census balance, count/identity agreement, per-artefact manifests). Decisive for F-01 — I need visionGraph's CI and crates/vault source.
2. **gh-pages deploy contents.** Whether the peaceiris deploy preserves/includes the CNAME file, and the exact layout (api/, data/, context/, mirrors) that lands on the served branch — decisive for F-04, F-06 and F-14.
3. **vowl-wasm 0.1.1 vs 0.1.2.** The changelog between them, and which CI (if any) runs the 183-byte golden fixture against both the vault writer and the shipping reader — decisive for F-10.
4. **corpus-sync failure semantics.** Retry, idempotency by source sha, and drift detection between the site deploy and VisionClaw's ingest — decisive for F-09.
5. **Credential and pinning posture.** What token peaceiris/actions-gh-pages uses to write knowledgeGraph's gh-pages (scope, expiry, who holds it), whether actions and dependencies are hash-pinned, and which ref the raw.githubusercontent.com fallback targets — needed before any supply-chain or availability sign-off.

## knowledgegraph / premortem

Post-mortem, reconstructed from the knowledgeGraph topic set (KG-01, KG-04, KG-05, KG-06, KG-07) at verified commits `4ed9ac1` (knowledgeGraph) and `015ca2c` (visionGraph). The incident itself is not described in the material; the three R- items are the failure chains the documented state makes most probable, each written so an engineer can confirm or kill it against the code. Paths are relative to the knowledgeGraph repo root unless prefixed `visionGraph/`. Nothing below relies on ordering or concurrency the diagrams do not assert.

---

### R-1 — The entire baseline enforcement layer (visibility, census, identity, manifest) was archived with the pipeline, and the sole builder documents no equivalent
- Topics: KG-01.1, KG-05.3, KG-05.5, KG-07.1, KG-07.2
- Chain of events: (1) On 2026-09-22 `pipeline/` and `build.yml` were archived and `ontology/pages/` moved (`README.md:14,16-17`; `archive/github-workflows/build.yml:1`). The scripts implementing invariants I1, I8, I9, I10 and I11 — `census.py`, `visibility.py`, `manifest.py`, `release_gate.py` — now exist only under `archive/logseq-era-2026-09-22/pipeline/` (KG-07.2). (2) visionGraph's `publish.yml` became the sole producer: `vault validate`, `vault build` (`publish.yml:157-170,182-190`), a "space and Earth domain contract" gate before Quartz (`publish.yml:296-297`), deploy (`publish.yml:453`), corpus-sync dispatch to VisionClaw (`publish.yml:459-465`). (3) No step in that chain is documented to re-check strict-boolean `vc:public`, scan public artefacts for private identifiers, balance the input census, compare class count against class identity, or emit per-artefact SHA-256 manifests. (4) Content the retired release gate would have rejected ships to the public gh-pages at narrativegoldmine.com and, via corpus-sync, enters VisionClaw's reasoned graph — the graph agentbox's Loom facade and `ontology_ask`/`ontology_search`/`kg_pathfind` ground against (KG-06.2).
- Evidence: I1/I8/I9/I10/I11 texts (`docs/BASELINE-narrativegoldmine.md:182-214`); enforcing lines `census.py:61`, `visibility.py:133`, `manifest.py:137`, `release_gate.py:127` cited only under `archive/logseq-era-2026-09-22/pipeline/` (KG-07.2); KG-05.3 names the domain contract as the only pre-deploy content gate; KG-07.2's own note: "equal counts cannot prove equal identities or intended visibility — release_gate.py check_identity (line 127) was the mitigation, never a closure"; KG-05.5: "nothing here computes it any more".
- Warning signs visible today: `docs/ci-cd/build-and-gates.md:1-3` still asserts six gates run while the workflow sits in `archive/` (KG-05.3 divergence bullet) — assurance currently references a retired control; KG-06.2 shows this repo's cross-repo commentary already going stale (`docs/ecosystem.md:121-127`); KG-07.3's "acknowledged, unfixed drift" posture on derived counts.
- Confidence: high that the enforcing code is archived and absent from the described new path; whether `vault validate` reimplements any of it is unstated — the exposure itself is inferred, not proven.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Two live data surfaces: consumers can still ingest the frozen rdflib-era `dist/data/ontology.ttl` while the site serves current visionGraph output
- Topics: KG-01.1, KG-05.3, KG-05.5, KG-06.2, KG-07.3
- Chain of events: (1) `dist/data/` (12 tracked files, including `ontology.ttl`, 258,200 triples) is labelled "last local pipeline build" (KG-01.1) — frozen at the 2026-09-22 retirement, still tracked and still inside the ODbL licence scope (`LICENSING.md:28-29`). (2) gh-pages is written exclusively by visionGraph's `publish.yml` (KG-05.3) and reflects the current corpus (8,138 classes, KG-05.5). (3) VisionClaw's `CorpusSource` defaults to the visionGraph vault (ADR-2114) but retains the GitHub-fetch path over this repo's published `dist/data/ontology.ttl` — GitHubSyncService → Oxigraph → Whelk → agentbox grounding (`docs/ecosystem.md:87-108`; KG-06.2 secondary-path bullet). (4) Anything anchored to the secondary path consumes a corpus predating every change since the transition, including the 2026-10-01 domain expansion, while derived counts already disagree (7,874 vs 8,138, KG-07.3) and `ontology.ttl` is non-byte-reproducible (fresh blank-node ids per run, KG-07.3), making cross-era diffs fragile. (5) The client receives answers or exports from the frozen corpus — or a mix, depending on path — with no manifest on the new path to identify the era (see F-02).
- Evidence: KG-01.1 dist bullet; KG-06.2 sequence and "Secondary path, not default" bullet; KG-05.5 "published artefact is pure TBox (8,138 classes, zero individuals) served from gh-pages; nothing here computes it any more"; KG-07.3 STALE and TTLNODET entries.
- Warning signs visible today: a documented live consumer of exactly the frozen file; two data surfaces in one repo with the older one still named in the licence scope table; count drift already acknowledged as unverified.
- Confidence: high that `dist/data` is frozen and remains a documented ingest source; inferred that a client deliverable was anchored to it.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The explorer that ships is a diverged twin in another repo: remediation landed in `knowledgeGraph/explorer` never reaches narrativegoldmine.com
- Topics: KG-04.1, KG-04.6, KG-01.1, KG-05.3
- Chain of events: (1) Every governing doc here describes `explorer/modern` as the explorer (`docs/BASELINE-narrativegoldmine.md:14`; `docs/architecture/explorer.md:10`), but this copy "is never built or deployed by anything in this repository" (KG-04.6). (2) The retired `build.yml` "never builds explorer/" (`archive/github-workflows/build.yml:1`); the deployed SPA is built by visionGraph's `publish.yml:376` from `publishing-tools/WasmVOWL/modern` and pushed to this repo's gh-pages (`publish.yml:453`). (3) The trees already diverge on the WASM reader pin (tarball v0.1.1 here, `explorer/modern/package.json:20`; npm 0.1.2 shipping, `WasmVOWL/modern/package.json:20`) and on DOMAIN_SLUGS (six vs eight, since 2026-10-01; `explorer/modern/src/types/scope.ts:73-80` vs `WasmVOWL/modern/src/types/scope.ts:75-84`). (4) During incident response, a fix to the page-service fallback, NGG1 parsing or worker transport is landed and tested in `knowledgeGraph/explorer`; nothing here builds or deploys it, so nothing fails; the defect persists on the public site until someone discovers the second tree.
- Evidence: KG-04.6 in full (diagram plus both notes); KG-04.1 note1 (BASELINE:14 and explorer.md:10 cite an in-tree `explorer/rust-wasm/src/ngg1.rs` that does not exist at this commit; the dependency is fetched as an external tarball).
- Warning signs visible today: two trees, two version pins, two domain lists, divergence dated 2026-10-01 and unowned; the responder-facing documentation points at the non-shipping copy.
- Confidence: high that the divergence and non-deployment are exactly as documented; the wrong-tree remediation scenario is inferred.
- Severity: high

- Topics: KG-05.3, KG-01.1
- Evidence: "this repo's root CNAME is read by Pages, not written by any workflow here" (KG-05.3); the deploy that replaces gh-pages content originates in visionGraph (`publish.yml:453`); whether that deploy preserves a CNAME on the published branch is unstated.
- Failure: a deploy that replaces rather than merges the published branch drops the CNAME; Pages unbinds narrativegoldmine.com — outage, TLS failure, and a window in which the domain is unclaimed.
- Confidence: low — the binding's mechanics are only partially described; the drop scenario is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

## nostr-rust-forum / critical

### F-01 — A by-the-book deployment has no way to create its first admin
- Topics: NF-08.7, NF-02.6, NF-10.6 (DD3), NF-10.7 (S2)
- Evidence: Admin authority is the union of ADMIN_PUBKEYS env, relay-D1 `whitelist.is_admin`, and auth-D1 `members.is_admin` (NF-08.7). But: ADMIN_PUBKEYS is declared in no auth/relay template and named in no SETUP step (NF-08.7 N4, `SETUP.md:122-126`); the relay's `members` branch reads a table no migration or bootstrap creates in its own D1, so it is structurally dead (NF-08.7 N3, `nostr-bbs-relay-worker/src/auth.rs:192` vs `lib.rs:686`); every path that sets admin state — `admins add` (`admins.rs:161`), governance register/provision (`governance_api.rs:362/441`), roles grant (`governance_api.rs:670`) — is itself admin-only (NF-02.6). No non-admin path that writes `whitelist.is_admin` is described anywhere.
- Failure: fresh deployment, SETUP.md followed exactly: zero admins exist on relay and auth. Moderation, governance registration, role grants, welcome config and WoT overrides all return 403 permanently; the only escape is undocumented manual D1 SQL. Meanwhile the only functioning admin anywhere in the default deploy is the shipped search-worker pubkey (F-02).
- Confidence: high; the deadlock itself is inferred from the stated facts (no stated non-admin setter of `is_admin`), the deployment gap is stated verbatim.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — the gap is marked (DOC-DRIFT DD3, S2); the no-first-admin deadlock consequence is not.

### F-02 — The search-worker template ships a real third-party pubkey as its default admin
- Topics: NF-07.2, NF-08.7 (N5)
- Evidence: `nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:33` "ships a REAL 64-hex pubkey"; it is "the only template shipping a non-placeholder value where every other template ships example.com or a zeroed key" (NF-07.2 N3). This worker's admin authority is static-only (`auth.rs:52`, no D1 binding, `auth.rs:8`), and `/ingest` — the only mutating route — is gated solely on it (NF-07.1 N2, `lib.rs:834`, `auth.rs:86`).
- Failure: any deployment that keeps the template value grants that unknown pubkey admin over your search index: it can ingest arbitrary vectors/labels declared public (visibility is fail-closed to `publicLabels`, NF-07.4 N2) into what members see as semantic search results. Whoever holds that key holds an admin credential on every by-the-book deployment of this kit.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — flagged as an anomaly/consequence in N3 and NF-08.7 N5, but no Debt/Drift marker is attached.

### F-03 — `ENCRYPTION_ENABLED` is declared in no wrangler.toml, so zone encryption silently fails off
- Topics: NF-08.2 (N4), NF-08.8 (N7), NF-03.12, NF-05.16
- Evidence: `is_encrypted = gate AND zone.encrypted AND NOT is_public_read` (`zone_config.rs:127`), the gate read from env at `zone_config.rs:86` — but ENCRYPTION_ENABLED is "declared in NO wrangler.toml [vars] block in any worker ... a deploy that forgets it silently leaves encryption OFF" (NF-08.2 N4). The ciphertext-shape write gate at `nip_handlers.rs:1156-1163` runs only when `is_encrypted` is true. Client-side, the same gate also suppresses grant re-sync (`app.rs:796-800`, `zone_crypto/mod.rs:99`).
- Failure: operator deploys without the var (nothing instructs them to set it); zones flagged `encrypted` in ZONE_CONFIG are treated as plaintext by relay and clients; members post believing the zone is end-to-end encrypted and plaintext is durably stored in it. Turning the flag on later leaves permanent plaintext history inside a zone every reader now treats as encrypted, and the ADR-2017 sealed-original path (which requires an encrypted zone, NF-03.12 step 15a) is also disabled.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT, NF-08.2 N4) — but framed as doc drift, not as the plaintext-into-encrypted-zone incident path.

### F-04 — Live delivery of protected kinds 4/13/14 through kindless filters may bypass the per-event read gate
- Topics: NF-03.3, NF-03.14, NF-11.5, NF-11.6
- Evidence: The filter-level protected-read gate "reads only the kinds a filter NAMES, so a kindless filter passes it" (NF-03.3 note, asserted `nip42.rs:350-360`); the per-event `authorize_event`/`protected_read_permitted` gate is described only on the REQ path over events returned by `query_events` (NF-03.14, `nip_handlers.rs:1549/1945`). For live delivery, the stated mechanism is that broadcast "matches against the GATED filter" (NF-11.5 N3, `broadcast.rs:47-113`) — and the only broadcast-time authorisation gate the material states is the kind-1059 authenticated-recipient skip (`broadcast.rs:94`). No per-event check for kinds 4 (DM), 13 (seal), 14 (private DM) at broadcast time is stated anywhere. The material's own history shows this bug class is real: the previous ordering bug "stored the raw filter, so a refused historical read still delivered every later matching event live" (NF-03.14 note).
- Failure: if broadcast does not re-run `protected_read_permitted`, a stored kindless subscription (which passes both filter gates per the asserted test) matches every live kind-4/13/14 event and private DMs are pushed to subscribers who are not parties.
- Confidence: low-medium, inferred — an unstated broadcast-time check may exist; the material does not show it.
- Severity: high (conditional on the unstated check being absent)
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — Moderation gates key on the raw signing pubkey while the write gate resolves device keys, and subkeys are unlimited
- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-4, KF-9), NF-03.4, NF-02.7, NF-12.3, NF-10.2 (K6)
- Evidence: "the write gate resolves a device key to its owner, but the moderation gates that follow key on the RAW signing pubkey" (KF-4); "a derived subkey is RECOVERABLE FROM THE ROOT by anyone holding the root" (`keys.rs:240`) — domain separation, not compromise isolation. Admission step 6 uses `effective_pubkey` (`nip_handlers.rs:878`) while step 9's ban/mute gate compares the literal pubkey (`nip_handlers.rs:928`). Gift wraps additionally have no author to resolve at all (KF-9) and are deliberately excluded from ban gating (NF-03.5 N2).
- Failure: with `DEVICE_KEYS_ENABLED=true`, a banned or suspended member holding their root key derives a fresh device subkey: the whitelist check resolves to the (still-member) owner and passes, the ban/silence tables match nothing, and the event is admitted. Device-key revocation is whack-a-mole because derivation is unbounded. Even with the gate off, banned users can still reach whitelisted members via gift-wrapped DMs (stated as deliberate).
- Confidence: high on the mismatch (stated); the evasion scenario is directly implied.
- Severity: medium as shipped (gate defaults false — NF-02.7, NF-10.7 S4); high the moment device keys are enabled.
- Marked by authors: yes (KF-4, KF-9)

### F-06 — Self-review and original-signer guards compare pubkeys case-sensitively, enabling a governance self-decision bypass
- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-6), NF-06.15, NF-06.6, NF-06.13
- Evidence: KF-6: the guards compare case-sensitively while NIP-98 returns the pubkey verbatim; NF-06.15 N1 establishes that "the SAME key can legitimately present in two casings and both verify". The registry fixed exactly this class (`canonical_agent_pubkey`, `governance_api.rs:108`) and the receipts path matches owners case-insensitively (`governance_api.rs:1181/1189`) — the core aggregate (`record_decision` `governance.rs:1738`, supersession `governance.rs:1835`) was left open. Admin lookups lowercase first (`admin.rs:62`).
- Failure: an agent that is also admin or delegated reviewer re-cases its own pubkey hex in a 31403: the admission checks lowercase and pass; the case-sensitive self-review / original-signer comparison misses; the broker decides or supersedes its own case — precisely what the ADR-2011 integrity rules exist to prevent.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (requires a privileged actor; defeats a stated INVARIANT)
- Marked by authors: yes (KF-6)

### F-07 — Governance read endpoints are effectively anonymous, and the admin-gated reviewer aggregate is reconstructable
- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-2, KF-2a), NF-02.10, NF-06.9
- Evidence: "the governance READ endpoints verify a NIP-98 signature with no membership check, so authenticated is effectively anonymous, and the admin-gated reviewer aggregate can be reconstructed from the un-gated decisions read" (`docs/security/known-findings.md:23-24`), recorded as "an operator decision, taken once for both". Cases/decisions listing is `require_authed` only (NF-06.9 note).
- Failure: anyone with any freshly generated nostr key — no membership, no invite — reads all broker cases, decisions, appeals and their reasoning; reviewer identity and workload are reconstructable by joining the un-gated decisions read, defeating the admin gate on `/api/governance/reviewers` (NF-02.10).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (info disclosure; scales with instance sensitivity)
- Marked by authors: yes (KF-2, KF-2a — accepted risk)

### F-08 — The cross-stack `derive_subkey` payments contract is enforced by a manual script nothing runs
- Topics: NF-09.3 (N1, N2), NF-04.11, NF-04.9, NF-12.3
- Evidence: `derive_subkey` is "a BYTE-FOR-BYTE cross-stack contract with agentbox's JS mirror derivation" (ADR-2003), frozen as the settlement-domain Published Language (ADR-2012 D5, `keys.rs:251-265`), used by the frozen deposit-address derivation (`deposit_address.rs:120`). Yet "neither anti-drift-lint.sh nor identity-vector-parity.mjs is invoked by ci.yml or audit.yml — both are manual" (NF-09.3 N2). Production runs `PAY_ENABLED true` while the template ships false (NF-04.9 note).
- Failure: agentbox's JS mirror drifts (refactor, library swap) with no CI signal on either side; the two stacks derive different subkeys/deposit addresses; in a payments-enabled deployment credits are attributed to addresses one system doesn't recognise — settlement and reconciliation breakage with fund-handling consequences, discovered only when someone remembers to run the script by hand.
- Confidence: high on the unenforced guard; the failure is conditional on drift occurring.
- Severity: medium (high at the moment drift occurs)
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, NF-09.3 N2)

### F-09 — The relay's `events` table exists only as a SETUP.md copy-paste
- Topics: NF-08.6, NF-10.7 (S1), NF-09.5
- Evidence: "No CREATE TABLE for events exists in relay migrations 0001-0008 or in ensure_schema" (`relay lib.rs:686`); it is created by the operator pasting SQL from `SETUP.md:69`. "A deployment that skips SETUP.md:65-80 boots and then fails every event query" (NF-08.6 N2). Deploy steps 4–7 are hand-run SQL generally (NF-09.5).
- Failure: fresh deployment passes health checks and accepts WebSocket connections, then every EVENT save and every REQ read errors — a full publish/read outage discovered only at first use, and a standing drift risk between the SETUP snippet and schema evolution.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, narrowed; debt stated)

### F-10 — The governance "applied" loop is unproven, and the one live run read back `projection-failed`
- Topics: NF-10.8, NF-11.10 (N4, N5), NF-03.10, NF-06.11
- Evidence: "Relay OK establishes acceptance only"; the consumer half (agentbox ledger, VisionClaw journal) is absent — "Still SEPARATE consumer implementations" (NF-11.10 OPEN). The M4 run's system-decider 31403 "read back projection-failed and applied false on the edge" (ADR-2010 `:153`), and "the commit path is not yet witnessed live" (`:157`). "Relay projection receipt is not external application proof" (NF-06.11).
- Failure: a human approves a consequential action on the decision card; the relay returns OK; projection fails or the consumer never applies it — and nothing in the shipped kit closes that loop. The live run already produced an accepted-but-not-applied decision; no human high-tier decision has ever been observed at projection-committed.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (approved-but-inert decisions; governance correctness)
- Marked by authors: yes (Open / ADR-2010 staged-partial)

### F-11 — Kind 31405 is two different things, and three of the six ACS types have no typed validation
- Topics: NF-06.2 (N2), NF-06.1, NF-10.11 (KF-8)
- Evidence: `KIND_GOVERNANCE_AUDIT_LOG` is numerically identical to `KIND_PANEL_RETIRED` (`governance.rs:1226` vs `:32`); the roles are "distinguished only by whether the caller supplies a seen_audit_ids set, so a PanelRetired and an audit entry are indistinguishable on the wire" (NF-06.2 N2). KF-8: a legitimate PanelRetired is validated under append-only audit rules. Additionally, "only THREE have a Rust struct" — PanelState, PanelUpdate and PanelRetired "exist as kind constants and doc rows only" (NF-06.1 note).
- Failure: republishing a PanelRetired for the same d tag is rejected as a duplicate audit entry, breaking NIP-33 replaceable semantics and panel lifecycle operations; consumers cannot tell retirement from audit; the three untyped kinds pass malformed payloads as opaque events. Both `workspace` docs and the README table describe six typed payloads that do not exist.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DD7, KF-8)

### F-12 — The SSRF guard is denylist-only by shipped default and has no time bound
- Topics: NF-07.6 (N3, N4), NF-10.7 (S3)
- Evidence: "there is NO wall-clock timeout — only redirect-count and body-size limits bound a fetch" (N3); "without PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS the guard is denylist-only and DNS-rebinding-vulnerable — acknowledged in code at `ssrf.rs:13`; the allowlist (`ssrf.rs:100/114`) is the real mitigation and the template does not set it (`wrangler.toml:13` declares only ALLOWED_ORIGIN)" (N4, S3).
- Failure: the preview worker fetches user-supplied URLs under a guard the code itself declares rebinding-defeated, with no deadline — slow origins consume worker time budget, and every enumerated range check is bypassable whenever DNS answers differently between check and fetch in the shipped default configuration.
- Confidence: high that this is stated and acknowledged; actual exploitability inside the Cloudflare Workers runtime is not judgeable from this material.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE acknowledged in code; S3)

### F-13 — The NIP-42 relay-tag check is off by default, and the README claims the opposite admission model
- Topics: NF-03.2, NF-03.3 (DD1), NF-10.6 (DD1), NF-11.14
- Evidence: "own_relay_url unset or blank SKIPS the relay-tag check ... fails OPEN on the defence-in-depth check ... RELAY_URL ships blank" (`wrangler.toml:38`) — a deliberate rollout choice, i.e. the shipped default runs without it. Meanwhile README:441 "calls NIP-42 AUTH 'scaffolded' ... gates on a pubkey allowlist (auth_required: false) ... 'not yet the enforced admission path'. The code says the opposite" (nip42 is default `nip42.rs:115`, template ships `AUTH_MODE=nip42` `wrangler.toml:31`, the gate runs first `nip_handlers.rs:845`). NIP-11 cannot lie (NF-11.14), but the README does.
- Failure: production runs with the AUTH-event-to-relay binding unverified (any deployed AUTH signature acceptance is challenge-bound but not relay-bound), and an operator following the README reasons about the wrong admission model — not provisioning RELAY_URL, misjudging what unauthenticated sockets can and cannot do.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes for the README row (DOC-DRIFT DD1); the blank-RELAY_URL fail-open default is stated but not flagged as a finding.

### F-14 — Schema DDL runs before authentication and rate limiting on the request path
- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-3), NF-02.1, NF-08.5
- Evidence: KF-3: "ensure_schema runs its DDL on EVERY request, before the CORS short-circuit and any authentication" (`known-findings.md:26`). The NF-02.1 sequence corroborates the ordering: `handle_request` → `ensure_schema` (`lib.rs:162`) precedes the rate-limit step (`lib.rs:174`). NF-08.5 N1 describes the same bootstrap as "every cold start" — an internal wording tension that itself needs resolving.
- Failure: unauthenticated traffic drives an idempotent DDL batch per request against D1 before any budget check — statement/row amplification (cost, latency, D1 daily limits) usable as an unauthenticated cost-DoS lever; whether it is per-request or per-cold-start changes the magnitude by orders of magnitude.
- Confidence: high that the finding is owned; medium on per-request vs per-cold-start (the two topics disagree).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (KF-3)

### F-15 — The material contradicts itself on whether the trust sweep can write false audit facts
- Topics: NF-03.9 (DemotionDecision note), NF-10.10, NF-11.12 (N1)
- Evidence: NF-03.9, dated 2026-09-07: "Remaining: its unconditional audit INSERT can commit when the optimistic UPDATE changes zero rows; the conflict is detected after the batch." NF-10.10, same date: "The audit INSERT is conditional inside the transaction; both result counts must equal one ... The earlier false-audit probe remains in the audit; it is not a current-code claim," with five exact-SQL tests claimed. NF-11.12 N1 separately declares the governing IDENTITY closeout's OFFSET/ignored-write claims stale and fixed. One of NF-03.9 and NF-10.10 misdescribes the shipped code.
- Failure: if NF-03.9 is right, `admin_log` can record demotions that never committed — a fabricated audit fact for a security-relevant moderation action, and the "auditable: scanned == demoted + held + failed" invariant is unsound. If NF-10.10 is right, a current topic note asserts a live defect that does not exist, and the same staleness disease acknowledged in the IDENTITY closeout has spread into this distillation.
- Confidence: high that the contradiction exists; medium on which side describes the code.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (both notes are author annotations; the contradiction between them is unflagged)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. `relay_do/broadcast.rs` source for the candidate loop (`broadcast.rs:91-113`): whether live delivery re-applies `protected_read_permitted` per event for kinds 4/13/14, or only the kind-1059 gate — this decides F-04 outright.
2. The full `SETUP.md` and any seed/D1 scripts not cited: whether any documented non-admin path sets `whitelist.is_admin`, which decides whether F-01 is a hard deadlock or merely requires an undocumented manual step.
3. The production wrangler configurations of actual deployments (the dreamlab estate): which of `PAY_ENABLED`, `ENCRYPTION_ENABLED`, `RELAY_URL`, `ADMIN_PUBKEYS`, `PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS`, `CALIBRATION_SELECTION_KEY` are really set — this converts several template-level traps (F-03, F-08, F-12, F-13) from dormant to live or vice versa.
4. Test wiring and revision parity: whether the five exact-SQL trust tests, the `pay_credit_guard` scan and the wasm checks run in CI against the deployed revision, and whether KF-1's "mechanism fixed at HEAD" is actually deployed (F-15, NF-10.11 N1).
5. The provenance of the shipped `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` value in the search-worker template (`wrangler.toml:33`): whose key it is, whether it is revocable, and whether any live deployment overrides it (F-02).

## nostr-rust-forum / premortem

# Post-mortem: client-loss incident (data / custody / security class)

**Basis of this document.** The record available is the distilled architecture at kit commit `72463fb` (and `dreamlab-ai-website ebaf16c`). Root causes below are ranked by how directly the material states a live, unmitigated mechanism that produces an incident of this class. Every claim carries the citation the record gives; where I reason past the record I say so. Nothing here is advice; it is defect reporting for verification against code and tests.

---

## Root causes, most likely first

### R-1 — Every by-the-book deployment hands permanent admin on the search-worker to whoever holds a real pubkey baked into the template, while the two boundary workers get no admin bootstrap at all
- Topics: NF-07.2, NF-08.7, NF-10.7 (S2), NF-02.6
- Chain of events:
  1. Operator deploys per `SETUP.md`, which never lists `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` (`SETUP.md:122-126`, NF-08.7 N4).
  2. Neither the auth-worker nor the relay-worker template declares `ADMIN_PUBKEYS`; the relay template even asserts there is no reader for it in `src/` (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:10-13`) while `nostr-bbs-relay-worker/src/auth.rs:183` reads it (DD3).
  3. The only template that declares it — the search-worker — ships a **real 64-hex pubkey**, "the only non-generic value in any template" (`nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:33`, NF-07.2 TPL/N3).
  4. Static admin on the search-worker therefore resolves to an unknown third party in every deployment that did not deliberately override an undocumented variable. `/ingest` is the only mutating route and it is admin-gated (`nostr-bbs-search-worker/src/lib.rs:834`, gate at `auth.rs:63`), reached with a valid NIP-98 token from that key.
  5. That party can insert vectors labelled public into the member-facing semantic index (results are returned whenever the relay's text search finds nothing, NF-05.14) and can retract existing vectors by opting in with `allowPrivate` (`nostr-bbs-search-worker/src/lib.rs:248`) — attacker-controlled or vanished search results for all members.
  6. In parallel, the operator's own admin bootstrap on relay and auth depends on hand-editing `whitelist.is_admin` in D1, because no documented path grants the first admin — an authority split that pushes operators toward ad-hoc D1 edits.
- Evidence: NF-08.7 N4 ("ADMIN_PUBKEYS is DECLARED in exactly one wrangler template — the search worker... SETUP.md:122-126 never lists it either"); NF-08.7 N5 ("a by-the-book deployment has NO static admin bootstrap on relay or auth, and the search worker ships a REAL non-placeholder pubkey as its default admin"); NF-07.2 N2/N3; NF-07.4 N4; DD3 in NF-10.6.
- Warning signs visible today: a non-placeholder secret-adjacent value in a shipped template; the relay template's false claim about its own readers; the SETUP gap flagged as deployment divergence S2.
- Confidence: high that the misconfiguration exists in any deployment that followed the book; the exploitation step (someone actually using that key) is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Moderation gates key on the raw signing pubkey: a banned member keeps DMing members via gift wraps under the default configuration, and keeps publishing via device keys the moment DEVICE_KEYS_ENABLED is enabled
- Topics: NF-03.4, NF-03.5, NF-03.6, NF-10.11 (KF-4/KF-9), NF-02.7, NF-08.8
- Chain of events:
  1. A member is banned (kind-30910 mirror; ModCache 60 s, NF-11.7). Their notes, reactions, deletions, channel writes, reports, calendar, kanban and agent-intent kinds are refused (`is_ban_gated_kind`, `nip_handlers.rs:68-75`).
  2. The member publishes a kind-1059 gift wrap over their still-authenticated socket. Admission step 6a checks only that the **recipient's** p tag is whitelisted (`nip_handlers.rs:858-859`); gift wraps are deliberately excluded from the ban gate because the ephemeral author cannot bind (`nip_handlers.rs:56-65`, exclusion visible at `nip_handlers.rs:69-75`; NF-03.5 N2).
  3. The wrap is admitted and delivered to any session authenticated as the recipient (`relay_do/broadcast.rs:94`, NF-11.5). The banned member DMs members indefinitely; ban, mute, suspension and silence are all author-keyed and there is no author to resolve (KF-9: "a gift wrap has no author to resolve at all").
  4. The device-key variant fires as soon as the shipped-but-dormant feature is enabled: the whitelist check resolves the device key to its owner via `effective_pubkey` (NF-03.4 step 6b), but the suspension/silence check (step 8, `nip_handlers.rs:905`) and the ban/mute gate (step 9, `nip_handlers.rs:928`) compare the **raw** signing pubkey (KF-4) — a banned owner's device subkey passes admission.
  5. Either path: banned or muted member continues to reach members; the targeted member's client walks; the operator loses the client.
- Evidence: NF-10.11 N4 quoting `docs/security/known-findings.md:27` and `:44`; NF-03.5 N2; NF-03.4 numbered steps 6a/6b/8/9 with `nip_handlers.rs` line cites; NF-02.7 (device keys default-off, so the device path requires the operator to have enabled `DEVICE_KEYS_ENABLED`, which both templates ship as `"false"` — NF-08.8 N2).
- Warning signs visible today: KF-4/KF-9 sit open in the security ledger; the gift-wrap exclusion is documented as deliberate with only the recipient whitelist bounding it; the device-key story is shipped and dormant, i.e. one flag away from the second half.
- Confidence: high for the gift-wrap DM channel (default configuration, mechanism stated); high that the device-key gap exists exactly as written; medium, inferred, that device keys were enabled in production within twelve months.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — ENCRYPTION_ENABLED is read by the relay and both clients but declared in no wrangler.toml and named in no setup step: one forgotten variable silently converts every encrypted-flagged zone into a plaintext zone
- Topics: NF-08.2, NF-08.9, NF-05.12, NF-05.16, NF-03.12
- Chain of events:
  1. Operator marks the family/business zones `encrypted = true` in forum.toml. The validator even enforces that such zones are not public (`nostr-bbs-config/src/validate.rs:247`), reinforcing the belief that encryption is enforced.
  2. The deployment never sets `ENCRYPTION_ENABLED`: it is "declared in NO wrangler.toml [vars] block in any worker" and has "no documented deployment default" (NF-08.2 N4; NF-08.8 N7).
  3. Relay-side, `is_encrypted = gate AND zone.encrypted AND NOT is_public_read` (`zone_config.rs:127`) evaluates false, so the NIP-44 v2 shape check that is the only thing preventing readable storage never fires (`nip_handlers.rs:1156-1163`, NF-08.9).
  4. Client-side, the retro BBS client's `board_is_encrypted` gates on the **same flag** and the board's zone (`nostr-bbs-bbs-client/src/screens.rs:906-918`, config at `config.rs:68`, `config.rs:162`) — with the flag off it renders a composer on the encrypted-flagged board and posts **plaintext** kind-42.
  5. The relay accepts it. Plaintext is now stored in a zone the operator configured as encrypted, retrievable over plain REQ by every member of the zone's read cohorts and by admins (kind 42 is not in the protected-read set of 4/13/14/1059/30910-16, NF-03.3), and by any future mesh peer if federation is switched on. Forum-client members holding zone keys see a decrypt-failure placeholder and cannot tell.
  6. The stated invariant "nothing readable is stored in an encrypted zone by ANY author, admins included" (`zone_config.rs:193-201`, NF-08.9 N1) silently ceases to exist. The secondary signal — the client grant-sync is dormant with the gate off, so new members never receive zone keys (NF-05.16 N3, `app.rs:796-800`) — is easy to misdiagnose as a messaging bug.
- Evidence: NF-08.2 N4 (twice flagged as DOC-DRIFT, both times with the word "silently"); NF-08.8 N7; `zone_config.rs:127` formula; NF-08.9 write-gate cites; NF-05.12 N4 BBS composer gating; NF-05.16 N3.
- Warning signs visible today: the contrast with `DEVICE_KEYS_ENABLED`, which is at least declared in both templates; the fact that the zone-crypto feature has an undeclared master switch.
- Confidence: high that the gate can be silently unset and that both the relay check and the BBS composer consult it; high for the BBS-plaintext path (directly stated); the leak-via-REQ consequence is inferred from the protected-read set as cited.
- Severity: high

---

## Further latent defects found during the investigation

### F-01 — preview-worker SSRF guard is denylist-only without PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS, is DNS-rebinding-vulnerable by its own admission, and has no wall-clock fetch timeout
- Topics: NF-07.6, NF-07.5, NF-10.7 (S3)
- Evidence: "without PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS the guard is denylist-only and DNS-rebinding-vulnerable `ssrf.rs:13`. The allowlist `ssrf.rs:100` `ssrf.rs:114` is the real mitigation and the template does not set it — `nostr-bbs-preview-worker/wrangler.toml:13` declares only ALLOWED_ORIGIN"; "there is NO wall-clock timeout — only redirect-count and body-size limits bound a fetch" (NF-07.6 N3/N4; NF-10.7 S3).
- Failure: a user-supplied preview URL whose DNS rebinds after the first check reaches a host the denylist evaluated differently; or a deliberately slow origin pins worker wall-time with no bound. Whether Workers egress can actually reach the denied ranges is not shown in this material.
- Confidence: high that the defect exists as described; medium, inferred, on the exploitable target set.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence; deployment divergence S3)

### F-02 — The self-review and original-signer guards in the core governance aggregate compare pubkeys case-sensitively, and the same key can legitimately present in two casings
- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-6), NF-06.15, NF-06.6
- Evidence: KF-6 — guards at `governance.rs:1738` and `governance.rs:1835` compare case-sensitively while NIP-98 returns `event.pubkey` verbatim and "the SAME key can legitimately present in two casings and both verify" (`governance_api.rs:98-100`); the registry-side twin of this bug was already fixed (`governance_api.rs:108`, NF-06.15).
- Failure: a case signer opens a case under its lowercase encoding and decides it under the uppercase encoding of the same key; the case-sensitivity mismatch defeats the self-review and supersession-authority guards. Whether the relay's `is_admin`/reviewer lookups resolve the uppercase form is not stated in this material (the auth-worker lowercases at `admin.rs:62`); the full chain is inferred.
- Confidence: high that the guard is case-sensitive as recorded; medium, inferred, on the end-to-end bypass.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-03 — Governance read endpoints treat any valid NIP-98 signer as authorised: "authenticated is effectively anonymous"
- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-2/KF-2a), NF-06.9, NF-02.6
- Evidence: "the governance READ endpoints verify a NIP-98 signature with no membership check, so authenticated is effectively anonymous, and the admin-gated reviewer aggregate can be reconstructed from the un-gated decisions read `docs/security/known-findings.md:23` `:24`".
- Failure: an arbitrary internet key-holder enumerates moderation cases and decisions; reviewer activity patterns are reconstructable from the public-ish decisions read, defeating the intent of the admin-gated reviewer telemetry (`governance_api.rs:1407`).
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt — recorded as an operator-accepted risk)

### F-04 — The relay distinguishes a human decision from a system decision by the 31403's own `decided_by` field, and in the live posture the requesting agent itself holds the admin flag
- Topics: NF-06.18 (N1), NF-06.13, NF-11.10
- Evidence: "the requesting agent holds the admin flag, and the relay tells a human from a system by the 31403's own `decided_by` — so the boundary is as strong as the admin list; not exercised live, on purpose `ADR-2011...:195`" (NF-06.18 N1).
- Failure: an agent that is an admin publishes a 31403 claiming a human decider; the relay records and receipts it as a human decision; ADR-2010's acceptance evidence (first human high-tier 31403 at projection-committed, `ADR-2010...:157`) and downstream consumers cannot distinguish. No check in the material binds `decided_by` to anything verifiable.
- Confidence: high that the field is self-reported (stated); medium, inferred, on exploitation.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-05 — The payment ledger's chain-fold recovery view is proposed-not-built while production runs PAY_ENABLED=true: D1 is the only place credit evidence lives
- Topics: NF-04.11, NF-04.9, NF-08.4 (N4)
- Evidence: "D1 demoted to a height-stamped derived view folded from sidestr UTXOs... not yet built `ADR-2012...:48-52`"; "read_balance returns the cached D1 number `pay_ledger.rs:412`"; the journal rows naming chain outpoints live in D1 (`pay_ledger.rs:110`); "production sets PAY_ENABLED true" while the template ships false (`nostr-bbs-pod-worker/wrangler.toml:31`, drift note).
- Failure: a D1 loss or restore-from-backup destroys the credit journal with no rederivation path from the chain; credits are either unrecoverable (users lose funds) or re-granted from a stale balance (operator loses funds). Custody incident.
- Confidence: medium — the gap is stated as proposed-not-built; the restore scenario is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence / "PROPOSED, NOT BUILT")

### F-06 — The admin delete path removes kind-42 rows with no kind-5 tombstone, so deletion never propagates to any client that already ingested the content
- Topics: NF-01.3 (ADMDEL, N3), NF-03.10, NF-05.6
- Evidence: "ADR-2017 POST /api/admin/events/delete — silent kind-42 delete, no kind-5 `admin_events.rs:5`"; rationale at `nip_handlers.rs:343-345`; client tombstones grow only from kind-5 (`channels.rs:93`, `channels.rs:98`).
- Failure: harmful content is deleted server-side; every client that cached the event keeps rendering it forever, because nothing ever tells them it was removed. The rationale (protecting sealed-original restore) addresses the restore path, not the propagation gap, which the record does not flag.
- Confidence: high on the mechanism; the operational consequence is a direct implication.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (documented as deliberate; the non-propagation consequence is unflagged)

### F-07 — ensure_schema runs its DDL on every request, before the CORS short-circuit and any authentication
- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-3), NF-08.5, NF-01.3 (N1)
- Evidence: "`docs/security/known-findings.md:26`"; both fetch entries "bootstrap their schema and the shared replay table on EVERY cold start" (`auth lib.rs:162`, `relay lib.rs:194`).
- Failure: an unauthenticated flood forces a DDL batch per request against D1 — cost amplification and exhaustion, an availability incident triggered by anonymous traffic.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-08 — The auth-worker's KV binding and SESSIONS binding point at the same physical namespace id
- Topics: NF-08.4 (N2), NF-10.3 (O11a)
- Evidence: "`nostr-bbs-auth-worker/wrangler.toml:24` and `:39` — key-collision risk across the two logical uses".
- Failure: a key written through one logical use collides with a key of the other (session storage vs the KV alias), corrupting sessions or the aliased data. The record asserts the collision risk, not a concrete colliding key; the failure mode is inferred.
- Confidence: high that the ids are identical; medium, inferred, on the collision consequence.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Anomaly, live)

### F-09 — The relay's events table exists only as a SETUP.md copy-paste; no migration and no bootstrap creates it
- Topics: NF-08.6, NF-10.7 (S1)
- Evidence: "No CREATE TABLE for events exists in relay migrations 0001-0008 or in ensure_schema `relay lib.rs:686`"; "A deployment that skips SETUP.md:65-80 boots and then fails every event query" (NF-08.6 N2).
- Failure: an operator following a partial runbook gets a relay that accepts connections and then fails every save and query — a launch-day outage, or worse, a "successful" migration to a fresh D1 that silently has no event history path.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

### F-10 — Deleting a kind-40 event destroys the channel-creator lookup, locking a TL2 author out of their own channel's metadata
- Topics: NF-03.7 (N2), NF-10.3 (O5)
- Evidence: "`nip_handlers.rs:970`... deleting the kind-40 destroys the lookup and locks a TL2 author out of their own channel's metadata" (deletion path `nip_handlers.rs:2121`).
- Failure: a channel creator who deletes or loses their kind-40 (e.g. via a kind-5 covering it) can no longer update kind-41 for their own channel; support burden and member-visible breakage.
- Confidence: high (re-verified live in the record).
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Anomaly, live)

### F-11 — The search index is two stores that "must be written together" with no stated atomicity: vectors in R2, the label mapping in KV
- Topics: NF-07.4 (N1)
- Evidence: "the deserialiser parses ONLY the Vec segment `store.rs:185` — the mapping the Meta segment documents is actually read back from KV, so the two stores must be written together".
- Failure: an ingest that lands the R2 object but not the KV mapping (partial failure between writes) desynchronises labels from vectors — semantic hits resolve to the wrong content, with no consistency check between the stores. Atomicity of the pair is unstated; the failure is inferred from the stated coupling.
- Confidence: medium.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — The forum client reads window.__ENV__ inline in relay.rs instead of via the shared helper, and the two resolvers' hardcoded fallback relay URLs diverge
- Topics: NF-05.7 (N2), NF-10.3 (O8)
- Evidence: "`relay.rs:974` instead of calling the shared window_env helper, and the two resolvers' hardcoded fallbacks DIVERGE — `relay.rs:22` versus `utils/relay_url.rs:9`".
- Failure: if the `__ENV__` injection fails (deploy regression, script-load order), part of the client connects to one default relay and part to another — posts and subscriptions routed to a relay outside the operator's access boundary, i.e. content published where the deny-by-default gate does not exist. Whether the fallbacks are live public relays or dead placeholders is not shown in this material; that determines whether this is a leak or an outage.
- Confidence: high on the divergence; medium, inferred, on the consequence.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Anomaly, live)

### F-13 — The ADR-2011 "operator's" escalation boundary is authored by the agent: PanelDefinition 31400 is agent-published, and an unlabelled panel folds to the medium default
- Topics: NF-06.1, NF-06.12, NF-06.14
- Evidence: only 31400/31401/31402/31404/31405 are agent-published (NF-06.1 RegisteredAgent note); the task-property triple is declared on the panel (`governance.rs:331-335`); "an entirely unlabelled request folds to the relay's advertised ESCALATION_DEFAULT_TIER `governance.rs:525`, `wrangler.toml:48`" which is itself a declared SCAFFOLD; only Low is ever member-suppressed (`governance.rs:217`); nothing in the record states the operator signs or curates panels before agents publish them.
- Failure: a registered agent authors its panel with the loosest task properties; its requests land at Low/Medium effective tier — member-suppressed from governance view and exempt from the High/Critical rationale gate — inverting ADR-2011's stated invariant that the operator sets the boundary (`governance.rs:515-534`, NF-06.12 N1).
- Confidence: medium — every component is stated; the absence of an operator-curation step is inferred from the record's silence.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Admin authority is split-brained in practice: the relay's members-table branch is structurally dead, so admins granted through the auth-worker UI never govern relay ingress
- Topics: NF-08.7 (N3), NF-02.6 (N3)
- Evidence: "the relay reads members from its OWN D1 (nostr-bbs-relay) at `auth.rs:192`, but no migration and no bootstrap creates a members table there... That branch is structurally dead; effective relay authority is ADMIN_PUBKEYS union whitelist.is_admin only"; the auth-worker's `admins add` writes the members table in the auth D1 (`schema.rs:116`).
- Failure: an operator grants admin through the auth surface; the grant has no effect at the relay's admission and NIP-42 gates — admin actions fail there, or, conversely, hand-edited whitelist admins act on the relay without holding auth-surface admin, and the two surfaces' audit logs disagree about who was an admin when.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

### F-15 — The printable recovery sheet puts the nsec into a /connect magic link's URL fragment, and fragments live in browser history
- Topics: NF-05.8, NF-05.4
- Evidence: "the sheet is 100 percent client-side — the nsec travels in the fragment (#k=), which browsers do not send to the server" (`recovery_sheet.rs:290`).
- Failure: the record's safety argument covers only what browsers send to servers. URL fragments are retained in browser history, tab sync and screen captures; a user who copies the "magic link" rather than the sheet hands over the full account key — total custody loss from one paste. The record does not address this surface.
- Confidence: medium — the mechanism is a stated fact about the artifact; the leak vector is inferred from browser behaviour, not from this material.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

## sidestr-rs / critical

### F-01 — The chains you would run are produced by an engine none of this material validates
- Topics: SR-01.4, SR-02.4, SR-03.4
- Evidence: SR-01.4 states the producer "runs the upstream JavaScript engine baked at the upstream pins" (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:111`, `:117`), shows `sidestr-rs` as "validated components; native producer not deployed", and both `sidestr:dreamlab` and `sidestr:dreamlab-txbt4` are JS-produced. Yet every behavioural guarantee in SR-02.4 (immature-lock refusal, exact-byte eviction) and SR-03 (coinbase-paid withdrawals) is specified for the Rust workspace; no oracle or fixture ties the running JS producer's mempool or rule behaviour to those descriptions.
- Failure: runbooks, monitoring and incident response written from SR-02/SR-03 semantics describe code that is not running. The real producer's behaviour on those exact paths is unknown and can only diverge silently — e.g. it may not refuse immature sweeps at admission, producing the eviction storms the Rust design exists to avoid.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, SR-01.4 "deploy and prove a native producer…")

### F-02 — A published crate version with a known theft-capable channel flaw must be kept in service to close legacy channels
- Topics: SR-04 intro, SR-04.1, SR-04.3
- Evidence: "the 0.1 key let a commitment's owner spend its own revocation leaf, which is the flaw this release fixes" (SR-04 intro, `sidestr-hitch/CHANGELOG.md:45`, `:52`); "a 0.1 channel cannot be restored by 0.2 … Any channel opened with the earlier version has to be closed with that version" (`sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:29`, `:182`). Hitch is one of the eight published crates (`../sidestr-rs/Cargo.toml` per SR-01.1), so 0.1 remains downloadable.
- Failure: any counterparty still running 0.1 — or who downloads it today — can lose channel funds to a cheating peer; and recovering a legacy 0.1 channel requires redeploying the vulnerable code against a peer who may know about the flaw.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (described in prose, never marker-flagged)

### F-03 — Refund-path redemption pays "half a sat each" on an integer-sat ledger
- Topics: SR-02.3, SR-02.2
- Evidence: the SR-02.3 state machine specifies "Refunding --> Refunding: redeem paired outcomes at half a sat each" (`sidestr-core/src/markets.rs:138`). The base ledger is unchanged integer sats (SR-02 intro; EVM's "1 sat equals 1 gwei", `sidestr-evm/src/lib.rs:5`), and the asset rule enforces input/output conservation (`sidestr-core/src/assets.rs:160`).
- Failure: with odd collateral amounts, half-sat redemptions either round — silent value loss that the conservation rule was supposed to make impossible — or are unrepresentable. On any chain that activates a `rules` field this is a direct value-accounting defect on the refund path.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (the half-sat payout is stated; its conflict with integer conservation is my inference)
- Severity: high (latent: `sidestr:dreamlab` has no `rules` field — SR-02 Open)
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — In-flight forwards live outside the specified recovery snapshot
- Topics: SR-04.3
- Evidence: "The channel snapshot contains the signing key, the revocation basepoint and per-state secrets, and preimages" (`sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:22`) — ForwardRecords are not listed. They are persisted separately: "the host persists the record the Forward action carries" (`sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:7`, `:173`). The atomicity guarantee covers what a call returns (`sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:30`), not a restore path for router records.
- Failure: a crash, or a restore from a channel-snapshot backup without the matching forward-record store, loses the mapping from a downstream HTLC to its upstream channel. The downstream leg settles; the upstream cannot be settled; the hub eats the forwarded amount.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (from what the snapshot fields exclude)
- Severity: high (once any channel is actually hosted; today no host exists)
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — The EVM crate deliberately diverges from the declared compatibility contract, and the fixture suite cannot see it
- Topics: SR-03.2 vs SR-01.2
- Evidence: "The crate deliberately commits only an applied block, unlike the reference's `prepare`" (`sidestr-evm/src/lib.rs:84`), while governing ADR-0001 says "The reference engine is the compatibility contract" (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001…md:27`). CI validates only fixture replay of roots, withdrawals, receipts and refusals (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:186`, `sidestr-evm/tests/oracle.rs:1`) — outputs, not commit-timing semantics.
- Failure: the reference's `prepare` persists something the Rust rule discards on refused or sequencing-only blocks. In any mixed deployment (JS producer with a Rust validator, or a future Rust producer re-tested against the reference), durable EVM state diverges after the first such block and every later state root disagrees — a consensus split the oracle suite is structurally unable to catch.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (the divergence is stated; its state effect is my inference)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated as deliberate; never reconciled with ADR-0001)

### F-06 — Mempool policy makes submitters responsible for retry; eviction is silent
- Topics: SR-02.4
- Evidence: admission "refuse[s] a transaction whose BIP 68 relative lock or `nLockTime` has not matured at the next height … they can be submitted again once their height arrives" (`sidestr-core/src/state.rs:628`, `:721`, `:631`); on admission/validation disagreement the builder "removes transactions that admission accepted but current block state rejects" (`sidestr-core/src/state.rs:874`).
- Failure: a wallet that submits an HTLC refund or channel sweep once — normal behaviour against Bitcoin mempools, which retain timelocked transactions — has it refused outright and never mined; the refund expires and the counterparty takes the funds. Nothing in the kernel resubmits on the submitter's behalf, and eviction gives the submitter no signal.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (semantics stated; the loss depends on host/wallet behaviour)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — The entire Hitch service surface is unimplemented, including every money-bearing timer, and retry budgets are bounded
- Topics: SR-04.5, SR-04 intro
- Evidence: "no estate host supplies the complete service boundary"; the host must supply wallet funding, relay I/O, atomic storage, chain lookups, broadcasting and "the scheduler: retries, proposal, funding and unfunded timeouts … here they are the host's scheduler, with the constants exported" (`sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:70`, `:121`); the router expects the host to retry retryable actions "(Hitch tries twenty times)" (`sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:13`); the machine's tick drives claimable HTLCs to chain at `delay + CLAIM_MARGIN` (`sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/machine.rs:1306`).
- Failure: whoever bridges this gap re-implements the timeout regime from exported constants. One mis-timed unfunded timeout, missed tick, or exhausted twenty-attempt retry budget on a broadcast turns a routine stall into a force-close or a missed claim and direct loss.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, SR-04.5)

### F-08 — Wire divergence: invalid points are dropped where the reference peer sends a reject
- Topics: SR-04.5
- Evidence: "a point off the curve is refused at deserialisation and dropped without a `reject`, where Hitch replies with a reject at the proof-of-possession check; the port records this as a deliberate, tested departure" (`sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:63`).
- Failure: a Rust peer paired with a Hitch JS peer sends a malformed point; the JS peer waits for a response that never arrives; its handshake stalls and, depending on its timers, escalates to a force-close. The departure is "tested" only against the port's own expectation, never against the peer's reaction.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (the divergence is stated; the peer's stall is my inference)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-09 — One supervisor and one producer serve both chains
- Topics: SR-01.4
- Evidence: the diagram shows a single "agentbox supervisor" over the producer, mirror and faucet (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:111`, `:117`) and a single "upstream JavaScript producer" feeding both `sidestr:dreamlab` and `sidestr:dreamlab-txbt4` (`:11`). No second producer, supervisor or failover appears anywhere in the material.
- Failure: one producer or supervisor crash, hang or upgrade halts block production, mirroring and the faucet on both chains simultaneously; recovery time is whatever restarting that single component takes, and both chains share that fate.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (topology as shown; failover is simply never described)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — Governance records the same work as both closed and partial, under decisions that were never accepted
- Topics: SR-01.3, SR-04.5
- Evidence: "VisionFlow records source implementation as partial" (`docs/adr/ADR-2012…md:115`) while "The master board closes source parity in N-9" (`../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:100`), with post-closure releases "recorded as an annotation on the closed N-9 row" (`:114`); ADR-2012 is "proposed" with activation inactive (`:130`) yet titled ecosystem canon and governs every file here; ADR-2111 is "proposed, unimplemented and inactive" (`../project/docs/adr/ADR-2111…md:95`).
- Failure: an audit or handover that treats N-9 as authoritative signs off on source parity the governing ADR records as partial; production authority rests on a "canon" that was never accepted, so nobody can say whose decision an incident actually violates.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — Oracle pins move under already-released crates
- Topics: SR-01.2, SR-01.3
- Evidence: "Reef's BIP 21 parser … is repinned from `648487a` to `2bd3cb8`" (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:25`) and "`sidestr-wallet` 0.5.1 (BIP 21 with the Reef oracle)" was released against the new pin (`../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:114`); ADR-0001 makes pinned references the compatibility contract (`:27`).
- Failure: two published wallet versions were validated against different parser revisions — a payment request accepted by one can be parsed differently by the other — and the material states no cross-version interop guarantee. Every future repin repeats this silently beneath already-published semver.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (repin and release stated; cross-version divergence inferred from the reason to repin)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — Channel snapshots are hot key material protected by a single sentence
- Topics: SR-04.3
- Evidence: the snapshot "contains the signing key, the revocation basepoint and per-state secrets, and preimages" (`sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:22`); the sole protection is the note "Snapshot storage must be protected as wallet key material" (`:24`). No encryption, envelope or access-control requirement is stated anywhere.
- Failure: any host that stores the snapshot as ordinary state — database row, backup, log pipeline, the mirror — hands over every channel's keys and revocation secrets in one file. The compromise is silent and total channel loss.
- Confidence: high (that the requirement is prose-only)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — Channel liveness rides entirely on host-chosen Nostr relays
- Topics: SR-04.5, SR-04.2
- Evidence: "a host carries each peer message as a kind-23600 Nostr event" (`sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:49`, `:105`); relay selection, redundancy and delivery guarantees are wholly host-side, while finality requires the complete update/ack/revoke exchange (`sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:4`).
- Failure: a dropped or delayed update, ack or revoke stalls the handshake; HTLCs time out into protective force-closes — on-chain cost and service interruption — and the relays publicly expose channel message metadata in the meantime.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (transport duty stated; relay behaviour is general knowledge, not in the material)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Both governing documents undercount the estate they govern
- Topics: SR-01.1, SR-01.2
- Evidence: README lists seven published crates with no teller row while the workspace carries eleven members and a publishable teller (`../sidestr-rs/README.md:41` vs `../sidestr-rs/Cargo.toml:43`); ADR-0001 says CI pins "all four reference repositories" while the workflow pins six (`…ADR-0001…md:27` vs `.github/workflows/ci.yml:14`).
- Failure: anyone scoping what runs, or what sits under the compatibility contract, works from counts that are wrong in both directions; the teller — the JCS oracle for signed reserve attestations — is invisible in the README an operator would read first.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, both instances)

### F-15 — This distillation verifies the same repositories at different commits per file
- Topics: SR-01, SR-02 frontmatter, SR-04 frontmatter
- Evidence: `verified_commit` frontmatter: SR-01/SR-03/SR-04/SR-05 give agentbox `9fd49a9…` and visionflow `62d16e0…`; SR-02 gives agentbox `6466e39…` and visionflow `e49fe42…` — yet SR-02 cites the same `dreamlab/chain.json` the others cite. SR-01 lists `ADR-2112` in `adrs:` with no source file and no appearance in any body text.
- Failure: every `path:line` citation your engineers check resolves to different content depending on which file's pin they use; two topic files can disagree about the same document, and ADR-2112's obligations are unknown while it is cited as governing.
- Confidence: high (the hashes and the dangling reference are stated in the material itself)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Reorg handling for committed EVM state.** Commits are keyed by block hash (SR-03.2/3.3) and reorgs are first-class elsewhere (Hitch's `un_spend`), but no rollback or invalidation of committed EVM state on a sidechain reorganisation is described.
