2. **The running JS producer's actual mempool, admission and eviction policy** — the live behaviour an operator inherits — and whether any oracle ties it to `state.rs` at all (see F-01).
3. **The signer federation**: who the signers are, the threshold, key custody and rotation ("the chain's signers still order and validate every block", SR-03 business section, is the only mention).
4. **Market resolver custody**: who holds resolver keys (`markets.rs:202`), what a compromised resolver can resolve before expiry, and how expiry-plus-grace parameters are chosen.
5. **Chain reconstruction and key separation**: what the "mirror" actually persists, whether `sidestr:dreamlab` or `sidestr:dreamlab-txbt4` history can be rebuilt after producer loss, and whether the two chains share keys or infrastructure.

## sidestr-rs / premortem

# Post-mortem — client custody loss in the estate's first live channel service

**Scope and method.** Written twelve months after the distillation above. Every claim below is grounded in that material and carries its citations; where I reason past what it shows, the item says "inferred". Nothing here has yet been re-verified against code; findings are reported for verification, not action.

**Incident (reconstruction).** The client held value in a Hitch channel operated by the estate. A counterparty close on the host chain went unanswered past its timelocks and the client's balance was swept; the client departed. R-1 is the most probable cause. R-2 and R-3 remain live alternatives because each is a cliff the estate's own records show was never closed.

## Root causes (ranked)

### R-1 — The channel kernel ran, but nothing answered the chain
- Topics: SR-04.3, SR-04.4, SR-04.5
- Chain of events: The estate deploys `sidestr-hitch` 0.2.x behind a first-generation host (deployment itself is inferred; the material records that no host existed). The host implements peer transport and snapshot storage, but the scheduler and chain-watch duties stay partial — 0.2.0 had just widened exactly those duties. A counterparty unilaterally closes or breaches with an old state. The kernel performs no IO of its own and reacts only when the host invokes `on_spend`, `after_close`, `watch_outputs` and `tick`. The host's watcher misses or late-delivers the funding spend. The claim windows pass — to_local after delay, HTLC-with-preimage, offered-HTLC-after-expiry, the machine's `delay + CLAIM_MARGIN` pre-expiry trip, republication after six blocks. Timelocks expire, the counterparty sweeps, the client's balance is gone.
- Evidence: The kernel is deliberately sans-IO; a host carries messages, broadcasts, tick and route actions (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/lib.rs:19`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:49`, `:105`). The host owes chain lookups for `on_spend`, broadcasting, and the scheduler for retries, proposal, funding and unfunded timeouts, with the constants exported for it (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:70`, `:121`); the router expects the host to retry retryable actions (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:13`). The money-bearing chain reactions are host-invoked kernel functions: classify and penalise (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/chain.rs:176`, `:364`), republish claims after six blocks (`:578`), undo reorganised spends (`:333`), take a claimable HTLC to chain before expiry (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/machine.rs:1306`). Opening leaves funding broadcast and watch to the wallet (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/opening.rs:178`). The material states plainly: "no estate host supplies the complete service boundary, which 0.2.0 widened by the scheduler and chain-lookup duties"; ADR-2111 is proposed, unimplemented and inactive (`../project/docs/adr/ADR-2111-re-sequence-rgb-for-bridged-assets-and-delete-the-host-payment-store.md:95`); adoption sits under open N-10 (`../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:101`). The business text lists the missing pieces verbatim and notes "Nobody in the estate runs one."
- Warning signs visible today: The SR-04.5 Open itself; a breaking release that *increased* host duties while zero hosts and zero channels existed; no operational channel experience anywhere in the estate when the service was first needed.
- Confidence: high that the duty gap exists and is money-bearing (stated); the specific deployment and missed window are inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — A 0.1-era channel was stranded by the 0.2.0 upgrade, and the old revocation flaw stayed live
- Topics: SR-04.1, SR-04.3
- Chain of events: Channels are opened under 0.1's single-party revocation keys. The operator upgrades the service to 0.2.x. Snapshot restore refuses version 1 outright, so a 0.1 channel becomes unwatchable by the running service — it cannot be restored, and the material states such a channel "has to be closed with that version". The counterparty broadcasts an old 0.1 commitment. Under the 0.1 construction the commitment's owner could spend its own revocation leaf — the flaw 0.2.0 fixed — so the penalty remedy is undermined or becomes a race the cheater can win. The operator has no live 0.1 tooling to derive the penalty or even to cooperatively close. Funds are lost; the client departs.
- Evidence: "a 0.1 channel cannot be restored by 0.2: the 0.1 key let a commitment's owner spend its own revocation leaf, which is the flaw this release fixes" (SR-04 intro, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/CHANGELOG.md:45`, `:52`); "Any channel opened with the earlier version has to be closed with that version" (SR-04 business text); the invariant that only version 2 snapshots restore (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:29`, `:182`). The safe behaviour is asserted by oracle test only for the new construction (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/tests/oracle.rs:465`); nothing in the material describes remediation or migration tooling for 0.1 channels.
- Warning signs visible today: A hard version refusal with no stated migration path; an explicit instruction that old channels require old software; the cliff was created while the estate ran no channels at all, so no operational procedure for version-stranded channels can have existed.
- Confidence: high that the incompatibility and the 0.1 flaw exist (stated); the exploit occurring is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Reserve custody and service-account isolation were still open gates when value moved
- Topics: SR-05.1, SR-05.2, SR-05.3
- Chain of events: The estate proceeds with the ADR-2117 plan (private owner USD bridged through RGB into sidestr); a Liquid reserve is funded and the LWK-backed adapter runs as a service. The master board's open rows — reserve custody and service-account isolation — are not closed. Attestation signing keys and the Liquid wallet live in one service account. An isolation failure (shared credentials, operations misuse of the attestation-signing account) lets credits be attested or reserve coins moved without a second control. Attested credits no longer match held collateral, or reserve value leaves; the client's unit is under-backed; the client is lost.
- Evidence: The adapter's own documentation says it is private, unfunded, unpublished and not live (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-bridge-liquid/src/lib.rs:5`, `:73`); the local ADR states neither crate activates a bridge rule, funds a reserve or deploys a chain (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0003-keep-reserve-attestations-origin-neutral-and-private.md:32`); "A signed data format is only one input to a custody system. Funding, issuance, supply validation and activation require separate estate evidence under ADR-2117" (SR-05.3), and the Open: "the master board keeps reserve custody and service-account isolation open; publishing source does not close those runtime gates" (`../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:97`). The BIP-340 signer is a hook — key custody is entirely outside the crate (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-reserve/src/lib.rs:611`).
- Warning signs visible today: The SR-05.3 state diagram terminates at "Gated: explicit estate decision and evidence required"; supply validation is named as absent; the board row is open.
- Confidence: medium — the open gates are explicit; that the estate funded the reserve within twelve months is inferred.
- Severity: high

## Further latent defects

### F-01 — Remedy and forward durability rest entirely on host persist-before-send discipline
- Topics: SR-04.3
- Evidence: "The host persists the full recovery boundary atomically before sending anything the call returned, and restores the previous snapshot if the save fails" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:30`); "the host persists the record the `Forward` action carries" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:173`, `:108`). Both are host obligations the kernel cannot enforce against an external store.
- Failure: A host that sends first and persists second — the natural implementation — crashes between the two; a revealed revocation secret or a ForwardRecord is lost, so a later old-state broadcast is unpenalisable or a forwarded payment cannot be settled upstream.
- Confidence: medium (the seam is stated; the host behaviour is inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — One lost `revoke` message freezes the channel
- Topics: SR-04.2, SR-04.5
- Evidence: Until the last secret reveal, "nothing is forwarded, settled or announced and no further update is accepted or proposed" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:4`, `:27`); the transport is host-side Nostr kind-23600 relay I/O (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:49`) and no delivery guarantee is stated anywhere in the material.
- Failure: A relay drops the final revoke; the proposer has advanced while the receiver awaits the reveal; the channel deadlocks until a funding or unfunded timeout — itself a host scheduler duty.
- Confidence: medium (protocol rule stated; transport loss inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — `sidestr-evm` deliberately diverges from the reference's prepare-commit semantics
- Topics: SR-03.2
- Evidence: "The crate deliberately commits only an applied block, unlike the reference's `prepare`" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/lib.rs:84`); the check/commit split at `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/rule.rs:62` and `state.rs:468`.
- Failure: In a mixed estate — a JS producer whose `prepare` mutates state used to build the next block, a Rust follower that discards — post-refusal state diverges between implementations; the next block's root is computed from different bases, producing a fork or a stuck validator. The oracle suite checks roots, receipts and refusal verdicts (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/tests/oracle.rs:1`), not the divergent sequencing state.
- Confidence: low (divergence stated; production impact inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — The oracle ladder never tests against the deployed producer, and drift-checking is evidenced only for the EVM corpus
- Topics: SR-01.2, SR-01.4
- Evidence: CI regenerates and drift-checks the EVM fixtures (`../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:186`); no equivalent node is stated for spec, schema, blaketestnode, Hitch, Reef or teller (`ci.yml:14`); the live producer "runs the upstream JavaScript engine baked at the upstream pins" (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:117`); Reef was repinned `648487a`→`2bd3cb8` and shipped in `sidestr-wallet` 0.5.1 (`ci.yml:25`, `../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:114`).
- Failure: A repinned reference changes BIP 21 payment-request behaviour; the released wallet passes CI against the new pin while the deployed producer still embodies the old pin's behaviour; a client payment request is parsed differently on the two sides and a payment is misdirected.
- Confidence: medium (drift-gate scope read from the diagram; occurrence inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — Set-aside states bind HTLCs with no peer-independent exit except a host-driven tick
- Topics: SR-04.2, SR-04.3
- Evidence: An alternative state's HTLCs "stay bound until that state number is revoked or the funding is spent by something else" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:20`, `machine.rs:561`); the router's tick force-closes "when only a set-aside state binds" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:570`, `:578`).
- Failure: The peer vanishes after a collision or reject; revocation can never arrive; the only exit is the tick's protective force close. If ticks lag past HTLC expiry, the bound value is lost to timeout.
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — Claim republication horizon and claim margin are constants the host must out-run
- Topics: SR-04.4, SR-04.5
- Evidence: "claims republished after six blocks" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/chain.rs:578`); the machine trips at `delay + CLAIM_MARGIN` before expiry (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/machine.rs:1306`); the constants are exported for the host scheduler (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:121`); reorg handling exists only when the host calls `un_spend` (`chain.rs:333`).
- Failure: A reorganisation deeper than six blocks after the host stops watching, or a tick cadence slower than CLAIM_MARGIN, leaves a claim or penalty unconfirmable; the counterparty sweeps.
- Confidence: low (inferred host timing)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — Overlay order is consensus, and mint authority crosses rule boundaries
- Topics: SR-02.1, SR-02.2
- Evidence: "overlay order is consensus behaviour and cannot be rearranged as an implementation detail" (SR-02.1 invariant); the asset rule "delegates exceptional minting to a policy supplied by the pool and market views" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/assets.rs:160`, `overlays.rs:34`) inside the fixed assets→pool→markets installation (`overlays.rs:65`).
- Failure: Any refactor, feature-gating or parallelisation of the overlay pass changes which policy authorises which mint at which point; two validator builds derive different asset supplies and the chain splits silently.
- Confidence: medium (fragility stated as an invariant; failure inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — ForwardRecord is keyed by channel and payment hash; invoice reuse collides
- Topics: SR-04.3
- Evidence: Forwards are "keyed by downstream channel and payment hash" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:173`, `:108`); merge-versus-overwrite behaviour for a second record under the same key is unstated.
- Failure: A client pays the same invoice twice (same payment hash, same channel); the second record overwrites the first; one forward's settlement bookkeeping is lost and one leg cannot be resolved.
- Confidence: low (inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — Canonical attestation bytes are anchored to one pinned teller plus serde_jcs
- Topics: SR-05.2, SR-01.2
- Evidence: The bytes are "held to `serde_jcs` and to the JCS of the solidpayorg teller `7c00cea` by `tests/jcs.rs`" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-reserve/src/lib.rs:60`); the teller is pinned in CI (`../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:30`).
- Failure: Both anchors share any RFC 8785 edge-case gap — notably number formatting — that sits outside the cross-check corpus; a verifier using a third JCS implementation recomputes a different digest and rejects a legitimate attestation, or accepts a disputed one.
- Confidence: low (inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — Off-curve points are dropped silently where the reference answers
- Topics: SR-04.5
- Evidence: "a point off the curve is refused at deserialisation and dropped without a `reject`, where Hitch replies with a reject at the proof-of-possession check" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:63`), recorded as a deliberate, tested departure.
- Failure: Against a reference-conforming peer, the Rust side's silence where the peer expects a reject leaves the peer's opening or update state machine waiting; the channel stalls until the host-side funding/unfunded timeouts fire, and the two sides disagree about why.
- Confidence: medium (departure stated; consequence inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-11 — Refund redemption is stated at half a sat each
- Topics: SR-02.3
- Evidence: The lifecycle diagram states "redeem paired outcomes at half a sat each" in the Refunding state (SR-02.3, transitions at `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/markets.rs:260`).
- Failure: If refunds are paid as on-chain satoshis, a half-sat output is unrepresentable; odd-sat collateral leaves a residual sat whose destination is unstated — refunds are either refused for odd collateral or a sat leaks from the conservation tally.
- Confidence: low (inferred; the diagram line is the only statement)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — The fixed 1 sat = 1 gwei mapping has no sub-sat representation
- Topics: SR-03.1
- Evidence: "One sat maps to one gwei inside the overlay" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/lib.rs:5`); the invariant requires the same block's coinbase to pay "the required sidechain satoshis" (SR-03.1).
- Failure: A contract computing in wei produces an obligation below 1 gwei; the coinbase cannot pay a fractional sat, so the block is invalid or the obligation is stuck, stalling withdrawal finality for the client.
- Confidence: low (inferred; sub-gwei handling unstated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — EVM snapshot restore refuses on any configuration or time mismatch
- Topics: SR-03.3
- Evidence: "Restore verifies version, configuration, root and time, then clears transient execution state" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/state.rs:305`).
- Failure: A follower restarted after any configuration change cannot resume from its retained snapshot and must resync; the retained-height resume path — built for exactly this — is unavailable during an incident recovery.
- Confidence: medium (behaviour stated; impact inferred)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Reference bookkeeping has drifted in two places
- Topics: SR-01.1, SR-01.2
- Evidence: The README lists seven published crates with no teller row while the workspace carries eleven members with `webledgers-teller` publishable (`../sidestr-rs/README.md:41`, `../sidestr-rs/Cargo.toml:43`); ADR-0001 says CI pins "all four reference repositories" while the workflow pins six (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001-reference-oracle-is-the-compatibility-contract.md:27`, `../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:14`).
- Failure: An audit or client integration scoped from the README and ADR misses the teller and Reef compatibility surfaces — the teller is the JCS authority for reserve attestations — and signs off on an incomplete compatibility contract.
- Confidence: high (both stated and marked)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-15 — These governing documents were verified against inconsistent revisions
- Topics: SR-01, SR-02, SR-03, SR-04, SR-05 (verified_commit frontmatter)
- Evidence: SR-02 records agentbox `6466e39` and visionflow `e49fe42`; SR-01, SR-03 and SR-05 record agentbox `9fd49a9` and visionflow `62d16e0`; SR-04 records visionclaw `af3dff3` with visionflow `62d16e0` and no agentbox pin.
- Failure: An engineer verifying the findings above checks line citations against different trees; a fix confirmed against one revision may not exist in another, and mixed-vintage facts can silently understate a gap.
- Confidence: high (visible in the frontmatter)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

No corrective actions are proposed here; each item awaits verification against the code and tests at a single agreed revision.

## solid-pod-rs / critical

### F-01 — COPY reads the source with no ACL check: any writer can exfiltrate any resource, including `.acl` sidecars and the provisioned nsec
- Topics: SP-03.8, SP-10.4 (W6), SP-05.19, SP-04.5
- Evidence: SP-03.8 states "COPY authorises Write on the DESTINATION only — it never checks acl:Read on the SOURCE, so a principal with write rights on the destination can copy a resource it could not have read directly" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:2563`, handler `:2556`, sidecar copy `:2598`). SP-05.19 shows provisioning writes the nsec private-key document into Storage behind an owner-only ACL (`solid-pod-rs-idp/src/key_provisioning.rs:45`, `:187`); SP-04.5 N2 says reading a sidecar "discloses … the whole authorization graph of the governed resource".
- Failure: On a multi-tenant pod, an attacker with Write on their own container issues `COPY Destination: /attacker/x Source: /victim/secret.ttl` (and `/victim/.acl`, and the key-provisioning document). The server fetches the source via `storage.get` with no read gate and writes it where the attacker can read it — full confidentiality bypass plus private-key and authorization-graph disclosure.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, SP-03.8; also SP-10.4 W6)

### F-02 — MCP ships with a reproduced CRITICAL "anonymous reads / WAC sidecar bypass" whose only mitigation is a default-off flag, contradicting the per-tool WAC architecture shown
- Topics: SP-08.14, SP-10.8 (F2), SP-10.5, SP-02.3
- Evidence: SP-08.14's own flow asserts "WAC check per tool, per path" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/mcp/tools.rs:83`) while its note records the README's status section: "anonymous MCP reads / WAC sidecar bypass" as a reproduced CRITICAL finding, "keep MCP disabled" — "the endpoint is off by default … that default is the mitigation" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:370`; enablement via `--mcp`/`JSS_MCP`, `main.rs:110`, `:292`). SP-10.8: "The security controls the crate ships do NOT make this checkout production-safe, and the README says so."
- Failure: an operator enables `--mcp` (one flag, also settable via baked-in env) for an agent integration; anonymous callers read private resources and WAC sidecars through the tool surface. The defect is unfixed — the flag is the entire control.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (SP-08.14 DIVERGENCE note; audit F2)

### F-03 — OIDC access-token verification does not validate `aud`, omits `cnf.webid`, and accepts EdDSA that discovery does not advertise
- Topics: SP-05.8, SP-10.6 (D1–D3), SP-10.1
- Evidence: SP-05.8 D1: "aud is NOT validated — only the claim's PRESENCE is enforced" (`solid-pod-rs/src/oidc/mod.rs:812`; `SolidOidcClaims::aud` `:670`) — "a token minted for another audience verifies here". D2: `extract_webid` has "NO cnf.webid branch … the LWS10 C.1 delta is unshipped" (`:864`; `CnfClaim` `:685` unused). D3/invariant 6: discovery advertises only ES256/RS256 (`:184`) while the verifier dispatches EdDSA (`:763`), a stated baseline invariant currently "BROKEN in the shipped direction" (SP-10.1).
- Failure: pod enables `auth.oidc_enabled`; a token issued by the shared issuer for a different audience/resource server is accepted here, granting pod access to a principal the issuer never authorised for this pod. Relying parties built from discovery metadata also disagree with what the server actually accepts.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (ADR-2003 deltas; SP-10.6 D1–D3, "documented and pinned by tests, not fixed")

### F-04 — Glob GET merges children that carry stricter ACLs than the folder
- Topics: SP-03.9, SP-10.4 (W7)
- Evidence: SP-03.9 N2: "the merge gates on the FOLDER only. A child carrying its own, stricter acl sidecar is still included in the merged body" — gate at `solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:2640`, child filter `:2651`, merge `:2667`; repeated as W7.
- Failure: a container is public-read (e.g. a shared inbox) containing one child restricted to the owner by sidecar; `GET /container/*` returns the restricted child's full body to any anonymous caller.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, SP-03.9 N2; SP-10.4 W7)

### F-05 — NIP-98 single-use replay protection is process-local: every restart reopens the window, and each replica accepts the same credential again
- Topics: SP-05.5, SP-05.4, SP-10.4 (W3), SP-05.1
- Evidence: SP-05.5 note: "A process-local store loses every entry on restart, so each restart reopens the window for one TTL"; the seam "has exactly ONE implementor in this repo" (`solid-pod-rs/src/auth/replay_store.rs:90`). W3: "a second replica shares nothing, and a restart reopens the window for one TTL" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:192`). TTL 120 s (`replay.rs:78`), freshness window 60 s (`nip98.rs:28`).
- Failure: a captured Authorization header (proxy log, client compromise) is replayed against a second instance behind a load balancer, or against the same instance within 120 s of a restart/crash — the destructive request (PUT/DELETE) is verified and accepted. The DPoP jti caches (SP-05.7) are the same class.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (SP-05.5 DIVERGENCE note; SP-10.4 W3)

### F-06 — The edge-tier ACL resolver still fails invalid/failed reads open to inheritance — the exact escalation ADR-2005 fixed in-repo is live in a deployed consumer
- Topics: SP-10.6 (D5), SP-04.3, SP-04.4, SP-08.16
- Evidence: SP-10.6 D5: "the edge ACL resolver is out of repo and still treats a failed or invalid read as a miss" (`solid-pod-rs/src/wac/resolver.rs:265` referenced). In-repo, the fix is explicit: "Invalid --> [*]: DENY, never inherit" and `may_inherit()` is true only for `Missing` (`resolver.rs:153`), because "a malformed RESTRICTIVE policy [must not be] silently replaced by a permissive inherited one". SP-08.16: the nostr-rust-forum worker runs this tier on Cloudflare Workers over pod storage.
- Failure: on the edge path, a restrictive child ACL that fails to load or parse is discarded and the permissive ancestor policy is inherited instead — a read bypass triggered by inducing a read failure or malformed policy on the resolution path.
- Confidence: high (the divergence is stated; the trigger precondition — who can induce the failure — is not shown)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (SP-10.6 D5, ADR-2005 open item)

### F-07 — Payment state is non-atomic and the whole `/pay/*` surface is registered unconditionally
- Topics: SP-07.15, SP-07.14, SP-07.18, SP-10.8 (F4)
- Evidence: SP-07.15: "PAYMENT_STATE_LOCK … process-wide" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:200`), "The storage API has NO cross-resource CAS primitive", and "Two replicas serving the same pod storage share no lock, and the README's audit line records 'non-atomic payment state' as a reproduced critical finding". SP-07.14 N2: the whole `/pay/*` family is "always on, with no payments feature flag" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:4625`). SP-07.18: the ledger is still a stored number (ADR-2008 D4/D5 not built) and `.buy`/`.withdraw` still trade the token despite D3 declaring it retired.
- Failure: a crash mid-mutation loses ledger/order/pool state (intents recovered only where an external broadcast was involved); two instances over one pod store race the read-check-mutate-persist sequence — double credit or double withdrawal on routes that are reachable in every default deployment.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (SP-07.15 DIVERGENCE; audit F4; ADR-2008 partial/Tension)

### F-08 — A default server build records zero provenance marks while the README claims every write is a git-mark commit, and nothing warns at startup
- Topics: SP-01.7, SP-07.2, SP-10.7 (R1), SP-10.5, SP-10.3
- Evidence: server default features = `[]` (`crates/solid-pod-rs-server/Cargo.toml:123`) so "git_mark_write compiles to the no-op shim, so a default build records ZERO provenance marks" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:3650`). SP-10.7 R1: README claims "every write to a pod is a git-mark commit" — "true only with --features git". By contrast, the binary does warn when OIDC is disabled (`main.rs:321`); no equivalent provenance warning is shown. Even when enabled, write-then-mark is not atomic and the receipt only observes the gap (SP-10.3).
- Failure: operators run the stock binary believing tamper-evident, anchorable history exists; a dispute or audit finds no marks at all — a custody/integrity claim that is silently false.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT R1; ADR-2004 note in SP-01.7/SP-07.2)

### F-09 — Busy-port shifting can silently run two server processes against one data root, defeating the payment lock and replay store at once
- Topics: SP-02.7, SP-02.1, SP-07.15, SP-05.5
- Evidence: SP-02.7: on `AddrInUse` the server retries up to 11 times and ships a "warn port busy — shifted listener" (`main.rs:155`, `:162`) rather than failing; graceful shutdown takes up to 30 s (`shutdown_timeout(30)`, `main.rs:349`; on non-unix only ctrl_c can trigger it, `main.rs:381`). The material itself warns "Two replicas serving the same pod storage share no lock" (SP-07.15) and "a second replica shares nothing" for replay (SP-10.4 W3) — but never connects the port-shift behaviour as an accidental way to create that condition.
- Failure: a supervisor restart races the 30 s drain; the new process finds the port held, binds port+1, and both processes serve the same `data_root`: per-process payment mutex → concurrent ledger mutations; per-process replay store → captured tokens replayable on the other instance; concurrent writes to the same pod tree and quota sidecars. `base_url` also silently reflects the shifted port.
- Confidence: medium (inferred composite; every component is individually stated)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — No per-resource serialisation is shown on LDP writes: PATCH is an unlocked read-modify-write and POST name-minting is check-then-put, so the "POST never overwrites" invariant is only happy-path
- Topics: SP-03.6, SP-03.4, SP-03.3, SP-02.1
- Evidence: SP-03.6 shows PATCH as `get(path)` → seed → apply → put back with no lock anywhere in the sequence (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:1662`); SP-03.4 shows `mint_unique_target` "probe existence, append a numeric suffix" (`lib.rs:1501`) followed by `put` — a check-then-act — while asserting the invariant "an LDP POST creates, never overwrites" (SP-03.4 note). No per-resource mutex appears in any handler diagram (SP-03.3, SP-03.4, SP-03.5), and the server runs multiple actix workers (`main.rs:327`, "closure per worker").
- Failure: two concurrent PATCHes to one resource interleave get/apply/put and one agent's triples are silently dropped; two concurrent POSTs with the same Slug both observe the target free and the second `put` overwrites the first — data loss the stated invariant claims cannot happen.
- Confidence: medium (inferred from the absence of any lock in the material; the diagrams do not assert atomicity)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — `load_trail` maps every storage error to "no trail exists", so a transient failure can silently fork the Bitcoin anchoring chain
- Topics: SP-07.12, SP-07.7
- Evidence: the marked Debt: "`load_trail` maps every storage read error to `Ok(None)` (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/trail_store.rs:93-99`), so a transient or permissions failure reads identically to 'no trail exists yet' — a durability fault and a genuinely fresh ticker are indistinguishable to the anchorer". The anchorer holds the trail's private key (`trail_store.rs:37`) and anchors the git-commit SHA to a UTXO (SP-07.1, SP-07.12). Additionally, "A failed batch anchor is swallowed — it never fails the write nor the git-mark" (`lib.rs:3626`).
- Failure: a transient read error makes the anchorer proceed as if fresh — it creates a new trail (new key) and anchors under it, stranding the previous trail's UTXO and splitting the commit-to-chain custody chain, with no error surfaced; alternately, commits in a filled epoch are never anchored after a swallowed failure.
- Confidence: medium (the error-mapping is stated; the fork-creation consequence is inferred from "a genuinely fresh ticker" being the anchorer's interpretation)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt bullet, SP-07.12; the swallowed epoch anchor is a note in SP-07.7)

### F-12 — The `acl:origin` gate is off in every default build, so cross-origin writes are gated only by the credential
- Topics: SP-04.8, SP-01.6 (O1), SP-10.5
- Evidence: SP-04.8: "acl-origin is NOT in any default feature set, so a stock build ignores the Origin header entirely and a cross-origin write is gated only by the agent's credential" (`crates/solid-pod-rs/Cargo.toml:168`; `wac/evaluator.rs:352` — pre-F4 behaviour is the default). SP-10.5 lists the origin gate among default-off switches.
- Failure: with browser clients holding ambient credentials (the server-injected pod session used by the forge, SP-08.10, or any signer/session flow), a malicious page drives authenticated pod writes/reads the victim is authorised for — the exact cross-site class the WAC `acl:origin` condition exists to stop.
- Confidence: medium (the default-off is stated; exploitability depends on ambient-credential clients, which the material only partially shows)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, SP-04.8; SP-01.6 O1)

### F-13 — With no `--allowed-origins`, the server echoes every Origin back
- Topics: SP-06.10, SP-02.3
- Evidence: SP-06.10 N2: "an empty allowed_origins list means every Origin is echoed back — a local-dev default, not a production one" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:3087`; the echo logic at `security/cors.rs:236-249`); preflights echo the origin and the policy handles the credentials trap by echoing rather than emitting `*` (`cors.rs:174`).
- Failure: a default deployment reflects any attacker origin (and approves preflights for it); combined with any credential the browser can attach — the forge pod session, dev bearer, or an auto-consenting signer — a malicious site reads and writes the victim's pod cross-origin.
- Confidence: medium (the echo-any-origin default is stated; whether credentials are attachable by default in the server wiring is not shown)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (flagged as "a local-dev default, not a production one", SP-06.10 N2)

### F-14 — Filesystem writes are two renames: a crash leaves a body/.meta pair that does not match, violating the advertised atomic-storage contract
- Topics: SP-06.4, SP-10.8 (F5), SP-10.4 (W2)
- Evidence: SP-06.4: "The rename is atomic per file, but the body and its sidecar are two renames — a crash between them leaves a mismatched pair" (`solid-pod-rs/src/storage/fs.rs:111`); audit F5 records "filesystem writes violate the advertised atomic storage contract"; the etag is a SHA-256 computed at write time (`fs.rs:107`).
- Failure: power loss between the sidecar rename and the body rename leaves the new metadata (etag, content-type) paired with the old bytes — conditional requests and `If-Match` semantics break against real data, and the mismatch persists until the next successful write.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in SP-06.4; audit F5; SP-10.4 W2)

### F-15 — The replay store's fail-closed capacity rule converts a cheap flood into a total authentication outage
- Topics: SP-05.4, SP-05.5, SP-10.10 (B5), SP-05.1
- Evidence: SP-05.4: at capacity the store returns `ReplayError.CapacityExhausted — REFUSE`; "the store refuses the NEW credential instead" (`solid-pod-rs/src/auth/replay.rs:275`); defaults are 10 000 entries (`replay.rs:82`) with a 120 s TTL. SP-10.10 B5 notes it is "the only bound that fails the REQUEST rather than truncating the DATA". SP-05.1 N2: a replay rejection resolves to anonymous, then WAC denies.
- Failure: an attacker with any throwaway nostr key sends ~85 valid, unique kind-27235 events per second for two minutes; the store fills with unexpired ids and, per its own invariant, refuses to evict — from then on every legitimate user's fresh credential is rejected as `CapacityExhausted`, and sustained flooding keeps all authenticated access down while the pod stays up and looks healthy.
- Confidence: medium (the fail-closed behaviour and defaults are stated; the flood economics — server-side Schnorr verification at ~85/s — are inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Per-resource locking in the LDP/storage layer** — whether `handle_patch`, `mint_unique_target` or `FsBackend::put` hold any mutex; F-10 stands or falls on this and no diagram or note shows one.
2. **Whether the modern WebSocket/Webhook notification channels apply any WAC check on subscribe** — only the legacy `solid-0.1` adapter shows a `SubscriptionAuthorizer`; SP-08.2/SP-08.3 show none for the 0.2 channels, so a private-resource change-metadata leak there is possible but not asserted.
3. **How the "WebID URL passed through" branch of `agent_uri` is authenticated** (SP-05.1, `lib.rs:563`) — if any caller-supplied WebID reaches it unverified, that is an identity-spoofing hole larger than everything above; the source of that branch is never shown.
4. **The server-side CORS wiring as deployed** — the default of `allow_credentials`, which headers preflight approves, and whether any real deployment sets `allowed_origins`; the library policy is shown, the `CorsHeaders` middleware's configuration is not.
5. **Durable implementations and real topology** — whether any persistent `UserStore`/`SessionStore`/`ReplayStore` exists beyond the in-memory ones (SP-05.18 shows only `InMemoryUserStore`, implying IdP accounts vanish on restart), and the actual deployment shape (replica count, TLS termination, whether MCP/OIDC/IdP are enabled anywhere) — this decides which of the conditional findings (F-02, F-03, F-05, F-07) are live rather than latent.

## solid-pod-rs / premortem

# Post-mortem — solid-pod-rs production incident

**Basis:** this report is drawn entirely from the diagrams-as-code distillation of solid-pod-rs at `verified_commit 93e2200218fad37927df16a1b7784c93c475670d` (0.5.0-alpha.11 era). Twelve months have elapsed since; every finding below must be re-verified against the code and tests at the incident commit before action. Where I reason beyond what a diagram states, I say so. Nothing here asserts concurrency or ordering a diagram does not show.

---

### R-1 — Glob GET serves private child resources: the merge gates on the folder only
- Topics: SP-03.9, SP-04.2, SP-04.3
- Chain of events: an operator leaves a container readable (public, or granted to a principal) while a child resource inside it carries its own stricter `.acl` sidecar — the supported way, under the inheritance model, to be more restrictive than the parent. A client issues `GET /folder/*`. The handler enforces `acl:Read` on the **folder**, lists its children, keeps the RDF-typed ones, and concatenates their bodies into one response. The private child's body is disclosed in the merged output even though a direct `GET` of that child would have been denied.
- Evidence: SP-03.9 shows the gate is `enforce_read_ctx` on the folder (`crates/solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:2640`), the listing (`:2642`), the RDF filter (`:2651`) and the merge (`:2667`), with the note: "DIVERGENCE: the merge gates on the FOLDER only. A child carrying its own, stricter acl sidecar is still included in the merged body." SP-04.3/SP-04.7 establish that per-resource sidecar resolution is otherwise the norm; nothing in SP-03.9 shows a per-child re-check, and no test pinning child exclusion is cited.
- Warning signs visible today: the divergence note exists verbatim in the living record at this commit; the glob handler is unconditional (no feature gate), so every build carries it.
- Confidence: high — the bypass is explicitly stated, not inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Two server processes over one `data_root`: the payment lock, replay cache and quota guards are all process-local
- Topics: SP-07.15, SP-10.4 (W3, W4), SP-10.8 (F4), SP-02.7, SP-05.5
- Chain of events: a second server instance ends up running against the same `data_root` — a restart race, a duplicated supervisor unit, or deliberate scale-out. `bind_available` does not fail on a busy port; it retries up to 11 times, warns, and binds a **shifted** port (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/main.rs:146–162`, warn at `:155`), so the duplicate stays alive. `PAYMENT_STATE_LOCK` is one process-wide mutex (`crates/solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:200`) held across replay-check, mutation and persist because "the storage API has NO cross-resource CAS primitive". Two processes now interleave read-modify-write cycles over the same ledger/order/pool state (`handlers/pay.rs:169` read, `:198` commit, `:302`/`:306` replay record). A last-writer-wins clobber loses a debit or a replay-guard entry: the same anchor outpoint is credited twice, or a credited balance vanishes; `recover_payment_intents` (`handlers/pay.rs:246`) only compensates external-broadcast intents, not clobbered state.
- Evidence: SP-07.15 note: "DIVERGENCE: the lock is PROCESS-wide. Two replicas serving the same pod storage share no lock, and the README's audit line records 'non-atomic payment state' as a reproduced critical finding." SP-10.4 W4 repeats it; SP-10.8 F4 records it as a README audit finding the process lock "narrows… does not close". SP-05.5: "A process-local store loses every entry on restart… each restart reopens the window for one TTL." SP-02.7 supplies the silent second instance.
- Warning signs visible today: a reproduced **critical** audit finding carried in the README; the README's own guidance not to carry value through `/pay/*`; the routes are registered unconditionally with no feature flag (SP-07.14, `lib.rs:4625`).
- Confidence: high that the defect is real and unmitigated at this commit; the two-process deployment is inferred — but SP-02.7's busy-port shift makes an *accidental* duplicate unusually easy.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — MCP enabled for agent integration re-activates the reproduced "anonymous MCP reads / WAC sidecar bypass"
- Topics: SP-08.14, SP-08.15, SP-10.8 (F2), SP-10.5
- Chain of events: the estate this crate serves is agent-heavy (agentbox forwarding, MCP tooling per SP-08.16). Twelve months on, an operator flips `--mcp` / `JSS_MCP` to serve agent clients. The only mitigation the repo ever had for the audit's reproduced critical finding was the default-off switch; the finding itself — anonymous reads of pod resources and a WAC sidecar bypass — was not fixed at this commit. An unauthenticated `tools/call` against `read_resource` (or the ACL tool family) reads private resources or reads/writes sidecars without the Control elevation the LDP path enforces (SP-04.5). Client data is disclosed.
- Evidence: SP-08.14 note: "the README's status section records 'anonymous MCP reads / WAC sidecar bypass' as a reproduced CRITICAL finding and says to keep MCP disabled. The endpoint is off by default (SP-02.3) — that default is the mitigation." SP-10.8 F2 repeats it. SP-08.15 shows a per-tool `wac_check` exists (`mcp/tools.rs:83`) — the material presents both facts without reconciling them, so the bypass's exact shape (unchecked route, anonymous default, or sidecar path) is unresolved here and must be established in code.
- Warning signs visible today: reproduced critical finding, mitigation-by-default only, in a codebase whose stated consumers want MCP on.
- Confidence: medium — the defect is documented as reproduced; the enabling event is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — COPY never checks `acl:Read` on the source
- Topics: SP-03.8
- Evidence: the handler authorises Write on the destination only (`crates/solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:2565`) then `get(source)` and `put(dest, …)` (`:2591`); the note states: "DIVERGENCE: COPY authorises Write on the destination only — it never checks acl:Read on the SOURCE, so a principal with write rights on the destination can copy a resource it could not have read directly."
- Failure: any authenticated principal with Write anywhere duplicates a private resource it can name into its own writable space, then reads the copy. Always-on, no feature gate.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no (flagged inline as DIVERGENCE, not one of the four markers)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — Access-token `aud` is not validated, only its presence
- Topics: SP-05.8, SP-10.6 (D1)
- Evidence: `validate_aud = false` (`solid-pod-rs/src/oidc/mod.rs:812`); the note: "a token minted for another audience verifies here; a deployment needing audience restriction must enforce it above this API" (ADR-2003).
- Failure: a token issued to a different resource server (another client app, another pod) is accepted as valid here; its `webid`/`sub` drives the WAC principal → cross-service authorisation confusion.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — SP-10.6 governing-doc open-items register)
- Severity: high

### F-03 — `extract_webid` has no `cnf.webid` branch
- Topics: SP-05.8, SP-10.6 (D2), SP-05.16
- Evidence: extract reads only the top-level `webid` claim and a URL-shaped `sub` (`solid-pod-rs/src/oidc/mod.rs:864`); `CnfClaim` exists (`:685`) but is not consulted — "the LWS10 C.1 delta is unshipped". Meanwhile the bundled IdP mints DPoP-bound tokens with a `cnf jkt` claim (`solid-pod-rs-idp/src/tokens.rs:56`).
- Failure: the token's subject identity is not bound to the DPoP key that presented it, so a stolen access token usable without its proof's key (where a deployment relies on the claim) attributes writes to the wrong principal.
- Confidence: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — SP-10.6 register)
- Severity: medium

### F-04 — Invariant 6 shipped broken: the verifier accepts EdDSA, discovery does not advertise it
- Topics: SP-05.8, SP-10.1, SP-10.6 (D3)
- Evidence: discovery advertises only ES256/RS256 (`solid-pod-rs/src/oidc/mod.rs:184`, `:186`) while the verifier dispatches EdDSA (`:549`); SP-10.1 states invariant 6 "is the one currently BROKEN in the shipped direction".
- Failure: an EdDSA-only relying party never learns the pod accepts it (interop failure), and metadata-driven policy allows a weaker set than the crypto path enforces — the class of mismatch the invariant exists to prevent.
- Confidence: high (fact); medium that it causes an incident rather than friction.
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — SP-10.6 register)
- Severity: medium

### F-05 — Crash between the two renames leaves a body/.meta pair that does not match
- Topics: SP-06.4, SP-10.4 (W2), SP-10.8 (F5), SP-03.3
- Evidence: `atomic_write` renames the `.meta.json` sidecar then the body as two separate atomic renames (`solid-pod-rs/src/storage/fs.rs:111`, `:123`, `:138`); the audit record: "the body and its sidecar are two renames — a crash between them leaves a mismatched pair" (2026-08-19). ETags derive from `meta.etag` (`fs.rs:107`, served at `lib.rs:1381`).
- Failure: after a crash, the served ETag does not describe the served bytes. A conditional `PUT` with `If-Match` on the stale ETag succeeds against a body it never saw → silent lost update; container listings can also disagree with `get`.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no (recorded as a README audit finding and inline DIVERGENCE, not one of the four markers)
- Severity: high

### F-06 — Write-then-mark is not atomic; a crash leaves an unmarked resource
- Topics: SP-07.2, SP-07.5, SP-10.3, SP-10.4 (W1)
- Evidence: the git-mark runs after the storage `put` (`crates/solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:1478`); SP-10.3: "The receipt makes the write-then-mark window observable — it does not close it. The three steps are still not atomic."
- Failure: a crash between put and commit (or between commit and `.prov.ttl` write, `lib.rs:3618`/`:3632`) leaves a stored resource with no provenance mark and no anchor; a client sold on "every write is provable" holds data whose history cannot be shown.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no (stated as an INVARIANT/known window, not one of the four markers)
- Severity: medium

### F-07 — NIP-98 replay protection is process-local: replicas share nothing, restart reopens one TTL
- Topics: SP-05.4, SP-05.5, SP-10.4 (W3), SP-10.6 (D6)
- Evidence: the store is process-local (`crates/solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:192`); SP-05.5: "A process-local store loses every entry on restart, so each restart reopens the window for one TTL"; the freshness window alone is a two-sided 60 s gap (`solid-pod-rs/src/auth/nip98.rs:28`).
- Failure: same two-process condition as R-2, or any restart: a captured kind-27235 event replays against the other process (or after the restart) within the TTL and re-executes a state-changing PUT/DELETE/PATCH.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — D6 in the SP-10.6 register)
- Severity: high

### F-08 — Git smart-HTTP auth: method `*`, URL-prefix binding, and a push body that is not hash-bound — suspected replay window
- Topics: SP-05.3, SP-06.12
- Evidence: `GitLenient` accepts method `*` plus URL-prefix matching (`solid-pod-rs/src/auth/nip98.rs:93`); the git bridge passes `body = None`, so "a push body is NOT hash-bound" (`solid-pod-rs-git/src/auth.rs:148`). SP-05.1 shows the single-use replay check on the LDP path (`lib.rs:547`); SP-06.12's git sequence (`BasicNostrExtractor::authorise`, `lib.rs:4306`) shows **no** replay-store consultation — the material never states one exists there.
- Failure: a captured push token, replayed within the timestamp tolerance, pushes a second, attacker-chosen packfile to the same repo prefix. This is a suspected gap, not a demonstrated one — verify whether the git path calls the replay store.
- Confidence: low (inferred — absence in the diagrams is not absence in the code)
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-09 — `load_trail` maps every storage read error to `Ok(None)`
- Topics: SP-07.12
- Evidence: "**Debt:** `load_trail` maps every storage read error to `Ok(None)` (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/trail_store.rs:93-99`), so a transient or permissions failure reads identically to 'no trail exists yet'."
- Failure: a permissions blip or transient I/O error during anchoring makes the anchorer treat the ticker as fresh — it derives/records a new trail state, silently forking the anchored chain and orphaning everything previously anchored under the old key. Provenance and deposit-verification continuity break with no error surfaced.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-10 — agentbox's `{txo_uri, amount_sats}` deposit proxy will hit the unconditional 501 after upgrading past alpha.11
- Topics: SP-07.17, SP-09.7, SP-07.14
- Evidence: non-MRC20 deposits return 501 unconditionally, writing no payment state (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/pay.rs:467`); the record states agentbox "still proxies `{txo_uri, amount_sats}` to this server's `/pay/.deposit` and will surface the new 501 once its build exercises an alpha.11-or-newer binary" (ADR-2008 file, `:151-152`).
- Failure: a user obtains an address, broadcasts real sats, then the credit call 501s — funds are on-chain, the pod records nothing, and there is no stated operator path to credit them manually.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-11 — Default mempool endpoint is the public testnet4 explorer
- Topics: SP-07.11, SP-10.6 (D7)
- Evidence: `DEFAULT_MEMPOOL_URL` is the public mempool.space testnet4 explorer (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/mempool.rs:56`), read from `JSS_PAY_MEMPOOL_URL` (`:52`); "ONE URL, no fallback chain" and the LAN-node cutover "remains deployment config, not a crate default". A startup warning exists when the endpoint defaulted (`:289`).
- Failure: a production deployment with the env var unset anchors to testnet: anchors confirm against the wrong chain, deposit verification against mainnet UTXOs fails or misleads, and a client who paid for mainnet anchoring holds worthless proofs. The only signal is one startup log line.
- Confidence: high (fact); medium (incident requires unset config).
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — D7 in the SP-10.6 register)
- Severity: medium

### F-12 — CORS: an empty `allowed_origins` list echoes every Origin
- Topics: SP-06.10, SP-02.3
- Evidence: the server's `CorsHeaders` middleware with "an empty allowed_origins list means every Origin is echoed back — a local-dev default, not a production one" (`crates/solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:3087`); `SOLID_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`/`--allowed-origins` defaults to none (SP-02.3). The policy itself refuses wildcard+credentials and echoes the concrete Origin with `Vary: Origin` (`solid-pod-rs/src/security/cors.rs:238-240`, `:174`).
- Failure: a default-configured public pod approves any origin's credentialed cross-origin reads; combined with browser-held bearer or dev tokens, a third-party page can drive authenticated reads/writes.
- Confidence: high (fact); medium (exploit requires browser credential flows).
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — An unwired `GroupMembership` resolver silently denies every `acl:agentGroup` clause
- Topics: SP-04.15
- Evidence: `NoGroupMembership` "always false, the server default" (`solid-pod-rs/src/wac/evaluator.rs:28`); the note: "a server that forgets to pass a real resolver into evaluate_access_ctx_with_registry loses group grants without any error."
- Failure: an embedding consumer (the library-first pattern SP-01.12 encourages) evaluates WAC without wiring a resolver; every group-based grant denies silently — mass lockout presenting as "private" data with no diagnostic.
- Confidence: medium (depends on the consumer's wiring)
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-14 — Busy-port shift keeps a duplicate instance alive instead of failing
- Topics: SP-02.7, SP-02.2
- Evidence: on `AddrInUse` with attempts remaining, the server retries and binds a shifted port with a warning (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/main.rs:162`, warn `:155`), re-reading the actual port for `base_url` (`:151`).
- Failure: this is the on-ramp for R-2/F-07: a mis-configured second unit does not crash loudly but serves the same `data_root` on another port — two writers, two process-local locks, no signal beyond one log line. It also strands clients configured against the original port.
- Confidence: medium (behaviour stated; the duplicate-instance scenario is inferred)
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-15 — The `acl:origin` gate is off in every default build
- Topics: SP-04.8, SP-01.6, SP-10.5
- Evidence: `acl-origin` is not in any default feature set (SP-01.6 note O1; `crates/solid-pod-rs/Cargo.toml:168`); with it off, the origin check short-circuits to granted (`solid-pod-rs/src/wac/evaluator.rs:352`) — "a stock build ignores the Origin header entirely and a cross-origin write is gated only by the agent's credential."
- Failure: for browser-carried credentials (OIDC bearer, dev token), a hostile page can mount cross-origin writes with no origin restriction; NIP-98's request binding blunts but does not remove this for other schemes.
- Confidence: medium
- Marked by authors: no (flagged inline as DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: medium

---

**Scope note for verifiers:** R-1, R-3, F-01, F-02, F-05, F-09, F-10 rest on statements the material makes explicitly at commit `93e2200`; F-08 is an absence-of-evidence suspicion and should be checked first. The multi-process trigger behind R-2/F-07/F-14 is inferred from SP-02.7 plus the stated process-local scope of the locks — the material does not assert any deployment topology.

## visionclaw / critical

### F-01 — Release-build WebSocket identity is either never propagated or the two diagrams disagree; drag/pin and visibility hang off it
- Topics: VC-03.2, VC-03.9, VC-13.1, VC-13.2, VC-16.1
- Evidence: VC-03.2 (marked DIVERGENCE) states `ws_server.pubkey/.is_power_user` are set only by a redundant query-string re-extraction that is "hardcoded None in release" (`http_handler.rs:328-341`, `:376-380`), and that the header-token check "never writes its NostrUser into ws_server" — "not yet confirmed whether downstream identity/visibility depends on this field". VC-13.1 asserts the opposite: "signed user, if any, sets pubkey and is_power_user (`http_handler.rs:392-395`)". Both describe the same handler with overlapping line citations.
- Failure: if VC-03.2 is right, every release-build client that authenticates via the Authorization header is treated as anonymous downstream: `PUBKEY_VISIBILITY_FILTER` fail-closed drops all private nodes for authenticated users (VC-03.9), and every `nodeDragStart`/`nodeUnpin` is rejected because `act.pubkey.is_none()` (VC-16.1, VC-13.2) — core XR interaction and private-node visibility break in production while working in dev (where the query-string path sets the field).
- Confidence: high that the material contradicts itself; medium (inferred) on which claim matches the code and on the blast radius
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in VC-03.2) — but the direct contradiction with VC-13.1 is unmarked

### F-02 — NIP-98 tokens are single-use, yet `/api/solid/pods/init-nip98` re-verifies the same token after the RbacGate already consumed it
- Topics: VC-03.4, VC-03 closeout note, VC-03.6, VC-05.15
- Evidence: VC-03.4 makes the replay claim the last, single-use step (`claim_signed_id` under one mutex, `TokenReplayed` on reuse, `nip98.rs:215`), and the 2026-09-07 closeout says "The NIP-98 token is consumed once within the request; a new request presenting the same token still fails" — which is why `settings/auth_extractor.rs` was changed to *reuse* the middleware identity. VC-05.15 still shows `init_pod_nip98` doing a "SECOND, fully independent NIP-98 re-verification" of the same header (`solid_proxy_handler.rs:1331-1338`) after RbacGate's `verify_access` (VC-03.6, `auth.rs:174`) has already claimed the event id.
- Failure: either the second verification hits `TokenReplayed` and every legitimate `POST /api/solid/pods/init-nip98` returns 401 (pod provisioning outage — the exact bug class the settings fix closed), or one of the two paths does not actually consult the replay cache, in which case the "single-use" invariant is false somewhere. The same double-verify pattern is a live trap for any future route under `/api` that re-verifies its own header.
- Confidence: high (inferred from the stated invariants; the closeout implies the bug was real for settings)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — ValidationMiddleware is never wired; the request-size caps and sanitizers the system advertises are inactive on real traffic
- Topics: VC-03.1, VC-04.19
- Evidence: VC-03.1 (marked DOC-DRIFT): ValidationMiddleware is "defined and unit-tested … but NOT in this .wrap() chain — grep across src/main.rs finds no .wrap(ValidateInput::…) call"; its limits (MAX_REQUEST_SIZE 1MiB, MAX_ONTOLOGY_SIZE 10MiB, MAX_STRING_LENGTH 100KiB, `validation.rs:19-25`) are therefore unenforced. VC-04.19 confirms the free-function validators are reachable "only via this explicit dry-run test route, never automatically on the real request path" — while `GET /api/validation/stats` advertises `input_sanitization, schema_validation, rate_limiting, xss/sql-injection/path-traversal prevention` as security features.
- Failure: unbounded request bodies reach handlers (memory-pressure DoS), and unsanitised strings (graph labels, page content) flow into storage and are rendered by clients with no sanitisation anywhere on the live path, despite the API asserting these protections exist.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT in VC-03.1); the contradiction with the advertised security_features list in VC-04.19 is unmarked

### F-04 — The destructive bulk-reload operation is power_user-gated on `/api/graph/update` but reachable by any authenticated user via `/api/files/*`
- Topics: VC-04.3, VC-04.4, VC-03.6, VC-03.7
- Evidence: VC-04.3 justifies `RequireAuth::power_user()` on `/api/graph/update` because it is a "FULL bulk reload (re-fetch, re-process, rebuild). Destructive and expensive" (`graph/mod.rs:1531-1543`). VC-04.4 shows `/api/files/process` running the *same* `fetch_and_process_files` + `AddNodesFromMetadata` pipeline and `/api/files/update_graph` (`files/mod.rs:243`) sending `AddNodesFromMetadata` from freshly disk-loaded metadata, with "no RequireAuth wrap here (only the scope-wide RbacGate mutating classification applies)" — i.e. WriteGraph. The default role is Editor (VC-03.7), which maps to WriteGraph.
- Failure: every authenticated user (default Editor) can repeatedly trigger the full corpus re-fetch/reprocess/rebuild that the S2 escalation reserved for Admins — authenticated DoS via the expensive path, plus wholesale graph mutation at a lower privilege than the design permits.
- Confidence: high on the gate discrepancy; medium (inferred) that files::update_graph is as destructive as graph::update
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — Upgrading past ADR-2115 aborts boot for any deployment whose persisted settings.yaml still keys graphs on `logseq`
- Topics: VC-09.15, VC-09.1, VC-06.2
- Evidence: VC-09.15: `GraphsSettings` has "no serde alias, 'logseq' is an unrecognised field"; "alt persisted document still keys graphs on logseq → deserialisation fails — explicit missing-field error on knowledge" (`visualisation.rs:512-517`). VC-09.1: "alt load fails → Err — boot aborts". Only the *client* migrates localStorage (`settingsHelpers.ts:371`); no server-side YAML migration is described.
- Failure: an existing production instance with a pre-ADR-2041 settings file upgrades the binary and enters a boot loop with a deserialisation error — config-custody/outage incident at the worst possible moment (during upgrade).
- Confidence: high (stated chain), medium (inferred) on how many deployments carry legacy files
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (ADR-2115 is framed as complete; the boot-abort consequence is not drawn)

### F-06 — No cross-store transactions, no actor-reload consistency, and no restore correctness across Oxigraph + SQLite
- Topics: VC-01.1, VC-04.10, VC-04.20
- Evidence: VC-01.1 (marked DIVERGENCE, BASELINE 2026-09-04 persistence closeout): "shared Oxigraph ownership establishes no cross-store transaction, no actor reload consistency, no restore correctness". VC-04.10/VC-04.20 confirm at least four read paths and three write paths onto the same graph with "no single write gateway".
- Failure: a crash mid-write leaves Oxigraph and per-domain SQLite divergent with nothing to reconcile them; when a restore is actually needed, its correctness has never been established — a silent data-loss or corrupt-graph incident during the recovery from a smaller incident.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, BASELINE closeout)

### F-07 — Concurrent settings PUTs lose updates (full-snapshot read-modify-write), and persistence failure is warn-only so settings silently revert on restart
- Topics: VC-06.2, VC-06.3, VC-06.1
- Evidence: VC-06.2: handler fetches one full `GetSettings` snapshot "to avoid a TOCTOU race" (`settings_routes.rs:512`), merges its section, then VC-06.3 sends `UpdateSettings { settings: full_settings }` — a whole-object replace with no version compare. After the actor update, SQLite persistence failure is "warn only — the response still succeeds" (`:648-654`). The hot-reload watcher is disabled because it "was causing database deadlocks" (`app_state.rs:1167-1170`).
- Failure: two users editing different settings sections concurrently: the second `UpdateSettings` overwrites the first's change with its stale snapshot — silent lost update. Any SQLite hiccup after a successful 200 leaves the store stale, so the change evaporates on the next restart. The deadlock history that disabled the watcher suggests the underlying contention is not understood.
- Confidence: high on the mechanism (both flows fully diagrammed); medium (inferred) on frequency
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — the disabled watcher is marked DIVERGENCE in VC-06.1; the lost-update and warn-only-persist consequences are unmarked

### F-08 — GraphServiceSupervisor `Escalate` stops the supervisor permanently; nothing in the process restarts it
- Topics: VC-02.4, VC-02.5, VC-02.6
- Evidence: VC-02.4: after exceeding the restart budget (`max_restarts 5` within 300s, `:479-491`), strategy Escalate — "top of the tree" — logs and calls `ctx.stop()` (`:795-807`). VC-02.6: this is "the only explicit stop … there is no drain timer"; the supervisor was itself started by `AppState::new` (`app_state.rs:814`) and AppState retains its now-dead `Addr`.
- Failure: five child crashes in five minutes (e.g. transient GPU/Oxigraph failures during the exponential-backoff window) permanently stops the supervisor; every actor-mediated graph route then 500s on mailbox errors until a full process restart. A 5-in-300s budget with a 60s backoff cap makes this reachable under ordinary incident conditions.
- Confidence: high on the mechanism; medium (inferred) on exact blast radius (CQRS-read routes may survive)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the state machine is drawn; the unrecoverable-stop consequence is not)

### F-09 — `/ws/client-messages` authenticates on token *presence only* — any non-empty string passes
- Topics: VC-04.25, VC-05.12, VC-05.13
- Evidence: VC-04.25 (marked DOC-DRIFT): "the token's CONTENTS are never verified against NostrService, only its presence, so any non-empty string passes" (`client_messages_handler.rs:131-142`); the in-code comment still claims it only "allows but logs unauthenticated connections". ADR-2090 fixed the identical presence-only defect on `/ws/mcp-relay` (`mcp_relay_handler.rs:449-456`) and `/multi-mcp/ws` (`:831-838`) — client-messages is not in either fix list.
- Failure: an unauthenticated attacker connects to `/ws/client-messages` with `Authorization: Bearer x` and receives the outbound `client_message` stream (content, timestamp, session_id, agent_id) pushed every 100ms — data exposure plus a cheap unauthenticated connection pool.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT) — but the fact that ADR-2090's sweep skipped this handler is unmarked

### F-10 — Pay routes debit the caller's balance and return success without delivering any resource
- Topics: VC-04.27, VC-09.13
- Evidence: VC-04.27: `pay_resource_handler` "confirms and charges but never forwards to the underlying resource handler (`pay_handler.rs:503-505`)" — a stub that returns `{resource, charged, balance}` with `X-Balance`/`X-Cost` headers after `store.debit`. Deposit is a separate unconditional 501 (`:487-492`). ADR-2111, which would delete this store, is explicitly still "proposed / not live".
- Failure: the moment `PAY_ENABLED=true` in production, every caller is charged sats for nothing, with a 200-style receipt asserting delivery — a direct financial-custody and trust incident. Until then the routes are inert, which is why this is medium not high.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (high the instant PAY_ENABLED is set)
- Marked by authors: yes (labelled stub; ADR-2111 proposed) — the charge-without-delivery consequence is unmarked

### F-11 — `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY` is "recommended, warn only" at validation but a missing value panics during boot
- Topics: VC-09.8, VC-09.12
- Evidence: VC-09.8 lists `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY` under "recommended — warn only, src/main.rs:70". VC-09.12: `decide_management_api_credential()` (`agent_monitor_actor.rs:269`) is "called at :301, fail-closed panic at :321-323 (ADR-2094)" — and `AgentMonitorActor::new` runs inside `AppState::new` (`app_state.rs:1188`), i.e. before bind.
- Failure: an operator who trusts the boot warning ("recommended") and continues without the key gets a panic-driven boot crash from deep inside actor construction instead of the clean, named refusal the validation stage implies — misleading failure mode during deployment or config-drift recovery.
- Confidence: medium (panic site stated; the boot-path linkage is inferred from VC-01.2/VC-09.12)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — Single-instance-only state everywhere: replay protection, sessions and in-memory maps do not survive a second replica (and possibly not the 4 actix workers)
- Topics: VC-03.4, VC-01.3, VC-04.11, VC-18.9, VC-07.1
- Evidence: VC-03.4 states the NIP-98 replay cache is "Process-local scope — no cross-replica replay protection". Persistence is per-domain SQLite under DATA_DIR plus one Oxigraph store (VC-07.1) — single-node by construction. Process-global statics include `CLUSTERING_TASKS`, `ANOMALY_STATE`, `FEATURE_FLAGS` (VC-18.9), `BOTS_GRAPH` (VC-04.5), and `GraphExportHandler`'s in-memory `shared_graphs` map (VC-04.11). The server builds its App per worker with `.workers(4)` (VC-01.3), and VC-01.4 places service construction (`main.rs:1044`) inside that per-worker closure.
- Failure: any attempt to run two replicas for availability splits replay protection (a stolen NIP-98 event replayable on the other node), splits share links and task maps, and double-writes the SQLite/Oxigraph stores. Worse, if any `web::Data` handler state (e.g. `shared_graphs`) is constructed per-worker, share creation and retrieval already land on different workers ~75% of the time in the *current* single process.
- Confidence: high on the stated per-process facts; medium (inferred) on the per-worker construction split
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the replay-cache limitation is stated as a known invariant; the worker-splitting and general replica-splitting consequences are unmarked

### F-13 — A misconfigured local corpus source silently falls back to GitHub instead of failing
- Topics: VC-01.2, VC-07.13
- Evidence: VC-01.2: "a misconfigured local source logs an error and falls back to GitHubSource rather than failing boot (`corpus_source/mod.rs:157-171`)". Source selection defaults to Local when `VAULT_ROOT` is set (VC-07.13).
- Failure: a typo'd or unmounted `VAULT_ROOT` in production (volume mount failure after restart is the classic case) makes the sync pipeline silently pull the corpus from the GitHub repository instead — the knowledge graph is rebuilt from the wrong source with only an error log at boot, and downstream reasoning/ontology derivation runs over foreign content.
- Confidence: high on the stated fallback; medium (inferred) on the wrong-corpus scenario (requires GitHub vars to also be set)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (described neutrally as resilience)

### F-14 — `/api/health` is registered by two different handlers; which one serves it is contradictory, and the KG watchdog's verdict depends on the answer
- Topics: VC-01.7, VC-01.10, VC-01.11, VC-01.12, VC-08.2, VC-04.16
- Evidence: VC-01.7: `/api/health` "also collides with api_handler::config's own /health route (`api_handler/mod.rs:121`) — first registration wins". VC-01.11 lists `GET /api/health → health_check (:121)` as api_handler's route; VC-01.12 places the consolidated-health group at `main.rs:1111-1129` while VC-01.10 places `api_handler::config` at `:1131` — the bands conflict, so the material cannot say which registration is first. VC-08.2's watchdog polls `/api/health` and maps "no status field → UP" (`liveness_harness.rs:505-507`).
- Failure: whichever handler is shadowed is dead code; if `api_handler::health_check` wins and its payload lacks a `status` field, the watchdog reports UP through any logical degradation and `CANARY-VC-RESA-KG` fires only on transport loss — the liveness gauge is quietly blind. Operators debugging via the other handler's documented response shape get contradictory data.
- Confidence: medium (collision is stated; which handler wins and the shadowing handler's payload are not determinable from the material)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially (collision noted in VC-01.7; the watchdog-blindness consequence is unmarked)

### F-15 — The nostr `/api-keys` routes cannot succeed as registered
- Topics: VC-05.3
- Evidence: VC-05.3 (marked DIVERGENCE): routes `"/api-keys"` (`:58-59`) have "NO {pubkey} path segment, but update_api_keys/get_api_keys both take `web::Path<String>` as the pubkey parameter (`:262`, `:290`) — actix Path extraction against a pattern with zero dynamic segments fails, these two routes cannot succeed as registered".
- Failure: any client or operator workflow that manages Nostr API keys through `POST/GET /api/auth/nostr/api-keys` gets an extraction failure on every call — the feature is dead in production, and callers may misread the failure as an auth problem.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (functional break of a management surface; no data exposure)
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

---

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **The current `http_handler.rs` identity write path** — whether the release build sets `ws_server.pubkey`/`is_power_user` from the header session (VC-13.1's claim) or never does (VC-03.2's claim), and which consumers read it versus the post-connect `authenticate` result. Needed to settle F-01.
2. **Whether `solid_proxy_handler::extract_user_identity` reuses the RbacGate's identity extension** (as the 2026-09-07 settings closeout did) or still re-verifies the consumed token. Needed to settle F-02.
3. **Construction sites of `NostrService`, `GraphExportHandler`, and the `RateLimit` middleware** — created once outside the worker closure (shared `Arc`) or inside it (per-worker). Needed to settle the worker-splitting half of F-12 and whether session tokens work reliably across connections.
4. **The actual registration order for `/api/health` and the payload of `api_handler::health_check`** (does it carry `status`?) — needed to settle which health handler is live in F-14 and whether the watchdog can detect logical degradation.
5. **Evidence of a rehearsed backup/restore for the Oxigraph store plus the per-domain SQLite files, and the exact semantics of `files::update_graph` versus `graph::update`** — needed to judge F-06's restore exposure and F-04's destructiveness ceiling.

## visionclaw / premortem

# Post-mortem — client-affecting data/custody/security failure, T+12 months

Scope note for the verifying engineer: every citation below is as printed in the verified material (commit `af3dff3f25300cf12bceda5650688ec223270eca`; VC-14 at `58f04f2eb272a2707737f2065f8241b931229e81`). Where a claim rests on joining two diagrams rather than one stated fact, it is labelled inferred. Nothing below has been re-checked against source; treat every line number as material-provided, not independently confirmed.

---

### R-1 — RBAC role removal is not revocation: an offboarded user retains write access
- Topics: VC-03.7, VC-03.8, VC-05.1, VC-09.9
- Chain of events: 1) An operator offboards a user by calling `DELETE /api/users/{pubkey}/role`, reasonably reading "delete the role" as "revoke access". 2) `remove_checked` (`src/services/role_store.rs:500`, called at `src/handlers/admin_rbac_handler.rs:197`) deletes the explicit row and returns 200. 3) The target's authority now resolves through ambient precedence: Admin if they match `POWER_USER_PUBKEYS`, otherwise `configured_default` — which defaults to **Editor** when `RBAC_DEFAULT_ROLE` is unset (`role_store.rs:195`, `parse_default_role`). 4) Editor maps to `AccessLevel::Authenticated` (`rbac.rs:83`) and is documented as "read + WriteGraph" (VC-03.7). 5) Every mutating non-settings `/api` route requires only `WriteGraph` at the RbacGate (VC-03.6: "Some(AccessLevel::WriteGraph) (denies Viewer…)"), so the "revoked" user keeps graph-write with the same Nostr key until an explicit Viewer assignment is made. 6) If the user was a power-user, they remain **Admin** outright (precedence rule 2, `role_store.rs:359`; `POWER_USER_PUBKEYS` at `src/services/nostr_service.rs:132`). 7) The ex-user mutates or exfiltrates the client's graph.
- Evidence: VC-03.8 note: "removal is NOT revocation: deleting the row drops the target to their AMBIENT authority (Admin if power-user, else configured_default), never denies outright. Genuine revocation requires an explicit Viewer assignment." VC-05.1: "ADR-2010 — removal is NOT revocation (:202) — response carries 'note' warning the target may still hold effective_after by ambient default." `RemovalOutcome{authority_reduced}` is the only signal, buried in a 200 response.
- Warning signs visible today: the API's own success response has to carry a warning note because the semantics are inverted from what the verb implies; the material states the trap explicitly in two places.
- Confidence: high (the semantics are stated directly; the incident narrative around them is inferred)
- Severity: high

### R-2 — `/ws/client-messages` accepts any non-empty token string; the client-message stream is consumable unauthenticated
- Topics: VC-04.25, VC-03.2
- Chain of events: 1) The route `GET /ws/client-messages` is registered at root scope (`src/main.rs:1079-1087`, VC-03.2's route list) — outside `/api`, so no RbacGate. 2) The upgrade handler takes a token from the Authorization Bearer header (or, per VC-04.25's diagram, the `?token=` query param) and rejects only an **empty** token with 401 (`client_messages_handler.rs:131-142`). 3) The token's contents are never validated against NostrService — "only its presence, so any non-empty string passes" (VC-04.25 DOC-DRIFT note). 4) `ws::start(ClientMessagesWs::new(app_state))` proceeds; the actor's 100 ms `start_message_stream` drains `app_state.client_message_rx` and pushes `text {type:client_message, content, timestamp, session_id, agent_id}` frames to the socket, kept alive by the 30 s ping / 90 s pong heartbeat. 5) Any party who can reach the port receives the live cross-client message stream indefinitely.
- Evidence: VC-04.25: "else token present (NOT actually verified against NostrService here)"; "the token's CONTENTS are never verified against NostrService, only its presence, so any non-empty string passes"; the in-code comment (`client_messages_handler.rs:114-116`) claims it "allows but logs unauthenticated connections" — the comment and code disagree about which wrong behavior exists.
- Warning signs visible today: the handler's own doc comment describes an auth posture neither implemented nor safe; contrast with `mcp_relay_handler.rs` and `multi_mcp_websocket_handler.rs`, which were both fixed (ADR-2090) to resolve tokens through `get_session` — this socket was not given the same fix.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

### R-3 — the payment handler debits the ledger and never delivers the resource
- Topics: VC-04.27, VC-09.13
- Chain of events: 1) The `/pay/*` routes are mounted unconditionally under the `solid-pod-embed` feature (`src/main.rs:1081` / `src/main.rs:1073-1081`), and that feature is in the **default** Cargo feature set (`Cargo.toml:256`, per VC-01.5) — so production images built with no `--features` flag (`Dockerfile.production:164`) already ship the code. 2) Twelve months in, payments are switched on with `PAY_ENABLED=true` (`pay_handler.rs:96 and :883`). 3) A client calls `GET /pay/{resource}`; the handler computes the per-endpoint cost tier (inference = cost×10, image-gen = ×100, analytics = ×5) and calls `store.debit(&did, cost)`. 4) On success it returns `200 {resource, charged, balance}` — but "resource proxying is a STUB — this confirms and charges but never forwards to the underlying resource handler (`pay_handler.rs:503-505`)". 5) The client's balance is taken, nothing is delivered; deposits were manual all along (`POST /pay/.deposit` → 501 "Contact the server operator for manual funding", `:487-492`), so remediation is a manual ledger adjustment on a host-owned `FsPaymentStore`.
