- Evidence: VC-04.27 stub statements cited above; VC-09.13 N5 confirms unconditional mounting and inert-until-enabled gating; ADR-2111 remains **proposed** — "FsPaymentStore and /pay/* stay host-owned and the record stays proposed" (`ADR-2111…md:83, :95-96`) — i.e. the codebase's own ADR record says this ledger should not exist in this shape.
- Warning signs visible today: a charging path with no delivery path, a 501 deposit endpoint, and an accepted-but-unimplemented ADR proposing to delete the whole store.
- Confidence: high on the defect (explicitly stated); the incident requires `PAY_ENABLED=true` to have been set — inferred
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — ValidationMiddleware is defined, tested, and never wired: no request-size or content-type caps exist on any live route
- Topics: VC-03.1, VC-04.19
- Evidence: "ValidationMiddleware (`src/middleware/validation.rs:124`) is defined and unit-tested (content-length cap, JSON content-type check) but NOT in this .wrap() chain — grep across src/main.rs finds no .wrap(ValidateInput::…) call. Limits it would enforce if wired: MAX_REQUEST_SIZE 1MiB (validation.rs:22), MAX_ONTOLOGY_SIZE 10MiB (:19), MAX_STRING_LENGTH 100KiB (:25)" (VC-03.1). VC-04.19: the sanitizers are "reachable only via this explicit dry-run test route, never automatically on the real request path" — while `GET /api/validation/stats` advertises input_sanitization, schema_validation, xss/sql-injection/path-traversal prevention as live `security_features`.
- Failure: unbounded request bodies on every route → memory-exhaustion DoS; multi-miB payloads reach sync handlers and stores; the stats endpoint reports protections that do not run.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — no cross-store transaction, no restore correctness across Oxigraph + per-domain SQLite
- Topics: VC-01.2, VC-04.10
- Evidence: "DIVERGENCE (BASELINE 2026-09-04 persistence closeout) — shared Oxigraph ownership establishes no cross-store transaction, no actor reload consistency, no restore correctness" (VC-01.2). One shared Oxigraph store plus per-domain SQLite under DATA_DIR is the stated invariant (ADR-2004).
- Failure: a crash between an Oxigraph quad insert and the paired SQLite status update (e.g. mid `governed_commit`/`commit_quads`, or during a bulk `AddNodesFromMetadata` reload) leaves the stores permanently divergent; a later restore-from-backup is itself never validated, so recovery resurrects deleted state or drops committed decisions.
- Confidence: high (defect); inferred (that it has fired)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-03 — three graph-mutation paths and five read paths with no single write gateway
- Topics: VC-04.1, VC-04.4, VC-04.10, VC-04.20
- Evidence: "three distinct graph-mutation paths coexist in this commit with no single write gateway (BASELINE-architecture.md persistence closeout)" — `graph_adapter` CQRS handlers, `graph_query_handlers` reads, and actor `AddNodesFromMetadata` writes (VC-04.10); `schema_handler` adds "a fifth distinct app_data binding onto the same graph" (VC-04.20).
- Failure: a write through `graph_state_handler`'s `AddNodeHandler` is invisible to the actor-held `GraphData` that `/api/graph/data`, the WS position stream, and the GPU layout read (or vice versa) — clients see different graphs depending on which route they hit; lost updates and contradictory exports.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-04 — in a release build, `ws_server.pubkey` is never set by header authentication on `/wss`
- Topics: VC-03.2, VC-03.9, VC-13.2
- Evidence: "ws_server.pubkey/.is_power_user are set ONLY by a SEPARATE, redundant re-extraction of the query string (token_from_qs, http_handler.rs:328-341, dev/dev-auth builds only — hardcoded None in release, :341)… The header-token check just above… never writes its NostrUser into ws_server. Net effect: in a RELEASE build, ws_server.pubkey is NEVER set on this handler, even after a client successfully authenticates via the Authorization header — not yet confirmed whether downstream identity/visibility depends on this field" (VC-03.2).
- Failure: a release client relying on the bearer-header path is treated as anonymous downstream: drag/pin rejected (`position_updates.rs:960-961` requires `act.pubkey`), and the PUBKEY_VISIBILITY_FILTER — fail-closed on `act.pubkey == None` — drops **all** private nodes for that session (VC-03.9). Owners report "my data is gone". Direction is fail-closed (availability), not exposure — unless some consumer sources identity elsewhere, which the material says is unconfirmed.
- Confidence: high on the code fact; impact inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-05 — NIP-98 replay protection is process-local
- Topics: VC-03.4
- Evidence: "Process-local scope — no cross-replica replay protection (SECURITY-profiles.md)" (VC-03.4), with replay claims held in a per-process `REPLAY_CACHE` (`src/utils/nip98.rs:215`) TTL 120 s against a 60 s freshness window.
- Failure: with two or more replicas behind a load balancer, a captured kind-27235 event replayed at a different replica within its freshness window passes freshness, tag-match, and signature checks and finds an empty cache — the authenticated action (a settings write, a decision record) is duplicated.
- Confidence: high on the fact; the multi-replica premise is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — the rationale gate consults the agent's own declared risk tier, and skips cases with no row
- Topics: VC-05.7
- Evidence: "OPEN (ADR-2110 scope note :332-338) — the tier consulted is the tier RECORDED on the case, which today is the proposing agent's own declared risk_tier. PRD FR3's effective_tier… has not landed. A case with no row yet carries no tier and the gate does not fire" (VC-05.7).
- Failure: an agent declares `risk_tier: low` (or triggers the decide path on a case that has no row yet) → the ≥20-character human-rationale requirement never fires → high-impact enrichment is merged and write-backed with no human judgement, despite the rule existing.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN)

### F-07 — REST decisions store no signed-event correlation; the audit trail cannot tie an approval to an approver
- Topics: VC-05.7, VC-05.17
- Evidence: "ADR-2006 — REST decisions carry decision_event_id:None (:569…) — no signed kind-31403 correlation is stored" (VC-05.7); the elevation-actor producer does retain `decision_event_id` from the signed event (`elevation_actor.rs:1749`), so the two producers converge only at the SQLite table and produce indistinguishable rows (VC-05.17).
- Failure: a client disputes a decision; the record cannot show which authority (human panel event vs. agent/operator REST call) produced it — the audit reconstruction fails exactly when it is needed.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-08 — the Nostr bridge re-signs under its own key; no request can be attributed to a user through an agent
- Topics: VC-03.16, VC-09.10
- Evidence: `nostr_bridge.rs:65 Keys::new`, `:169 sign_with_keys` — "re-signs under the BRIDGE's key, not the user's — original authority NOT carried" (VC-03.16); the tag loop at `nip98.rs:438-447` reads only u/method/payload, no delegation tag; "no request can be attributed to a user THROUGH an agent" until NIP-26 lands (VC-03.16 N1).
- Failure: forum events and bridge traffic are attributed to the bridge key; when an agent acts on a user's behalf, provenance (including `/api/trace` joins on `did:nostr`) attributes to the wrong principal or to nobody.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-09 — `/api/health` first-registration collision likely shadows `unified_health_check`, blinding the KG watchdog
- Topics: VC-01.7, VC-01.10, VC-01.12, VC-08.2
- Evidence: "/api/health also collides with api_handler::config's own /health route (src/handlers/api_handler/mod.rs:121) — first registration wins" (VC-01.7); VC-01.10 places `api_handler::config` at registration step 6, before the hexagonal/health group (step 10 → VC-01.12, consolidated health at `:477`); the watchdog polls `GET /api/health` on itself and marks **UP** on "2xx, valid JSON, no status field" (`liveness_harness.rs:500-503`).
- Failure: the shallower `health_check` serves `/api/health`; the Oxigraph-degraded reason surfaced by `unified_health_check` never reaches the poll; `kg_backend_up` stays UP and the `CANARY-VC-RESA-KG` canary (which fires only on transitions, `liveness_harness.rs:421-433`) never fires — an outage runs undetected.
- Confidence: medium — which handler wins is inferred from the stated registration order plus the stated first-wins rule
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — the `/auth/nostr/api-keys` routes cannot succeed as registered
- Topics: VC-05.3, VC-06.6
- Evidence: "route '/api-keys' (:58-59) has NO {pubkey} path segment, but update_api_keys/get_api_keys both take web::Path<String> as the pubkey parameter (:262,:290) — actix Path extraction against a pattern with zero dynamic segments fails, these two routes cannot succeed as registered" (VC-05.3). These are the routes into `ProtectedSettingsActor`'s `UpdateUserApiKeys`/`GetApiKeys` (VC-06.6).
- Failure: API-key reads and updates error on every call; operators cannot rotate or audit the keys held in protected settings through the auth surface — stale external-service credentials persist.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-11 — the settings hot-reload watcher was disabled because of database deadlocks; the underlying contention is unexplained
- Topics: VC-06.1, VC-06.7
- Evidence: "DIVERGENCE — the settings hot-reload watcher is DISABLED, src/app_state.rs:1167-1170, reason recorded in code: it was causing database deadlocks" (VC-06.1). The same SQLite database also carries the corpus-sync SHA1 ledger and batch upserts (`sqlite_settings_repository.rs:578`, `get_file_sha1s :276`, `set_settings_batch :578`, VC-06.7 note).
- Failure: whatever locking pattern deadlocked the watcher remains available to other concurrent writers (batch settings writes, sync ledger upserts); under load a deadlock stalls the settings actor, and `files::process` blocks on `settings_addr.send(GetSettings)` (VC-04.4), cascading ingest and settings-API timeouts.
- Confidence: medium — the disabling is certain; that the deadlock class survives elsewhere is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-12 — nothing prevents the next bare sync hexser call from panicking a worker
- Topics: VC-07.7
- Evidence: "every sync hexser handler enters a nested block_on, so calling one straight from an actix worker panics… execute_in_thread… is what makes the sync handler safe… DEBT — get_owl_property and add_owl_property were the LAST two bare sync calls and were only dispatched off the worker on 2026-09-16 in ad8b83ad4, and the earlier hierarchy endpoint had the same defect fixed on 2026-08-10. Nothing in the type system prevents the next bare call — ontology_handler.rs:424, ontology_handler.rs:489, regression module ontology_handler.rs:1054" (VC-07.7).

### F-13 — a misconfigured local corpus source silently falls back to GitHub and ingests the wrong corpus
- Topics: VC-01.2, VC-07.13, VC-09.11
- Evidence: "a misconfigured local source logs an error and falls back to GitHubSource rather than failing boot (src/services/corpus_source/mod.rs:157-171)" (VC-01.2); source selection keys off `VAULT_ROOT` presence (`corpus_source/mod.rs:223`).
- Failure: the VAULT_ROOT mount is lost or mispathed after a deploy; the server logs one error and starts; the next sync pulls the GitHub repo instead, and the bulk `AddNodesFromMetadata` rebuild replaces/merges the client's graph with foreign content before anyone reads the log.

- Topics: VC-07.12

### F-15 — governance decision broadcasts go to "all WS clients", and anonymous `/wss` sessions are permitted

- Evidence: "BroadcastMessage{enrichment_decision} to client_coordinator (all WS clients)" and `broker_events::broadcast_new_case` / `broadcast_case_decided` to the same coordinator (VC-05.7); unauthenticated upgrade is permitted — "continue unauthenticated (anonymous session — visibility filter drops private nodes…)" (VC-03.2). The material does not show any identity gate on the BroadcastMessage channel.

## visionclaw#2 / critical

### F-01 — Shipped compose inverts the fail-closed RBAC defaults: anonymous read of everything, Editor for any unassigned key
- Topics: VC-22.9, VC-33.6, VC-20.7
- Evidence: VC-22.9 states the code is fail-closed — `public_reads_enabled()` ends `.unwrap_or(false)` (rbac_gate.rs:126-133) and main.rs:795-801 refuses to start owner-less — but the shipped compose sets `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1` and `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS=1` (docker-compose.unified.yml:106-107), so "an unassigned pubkey resolves to Editor" (role_store.rs:359). VC-20.7 confirms all ontology GETs (including provenance, reports, state-at) are public, and ADR-2116 makes the `/api/ontology-agent` POST discover/read/query/traverse routes anonymous (ontology_agent_handler.rs:276-282).
- Failure: Deploy the unified compose as shipped: anyone anonymous reads the full knowledge graph, provenance reports and traversal; anyone who generates a Nostr key gets Editor (write) with no assignment. The authors record this as ADR-2027's "deliberate demo default" that "stays" — i.e. the production artefact ships the demo posture.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (CORRECTED ADR-2070 note; documented as deliberate, not tracked as an open defect)

### F-02 — All credentials in one plaintext .env; SOPS rollout never executed or withdrawn
- Topics: VC-22.1
- Evidence: VC-22.1 puts Credentials under ".env plaintext filesystem", and the DivCreds note records "PROPOSED ADR-2104: execute the SOPS rollout or formally withdraw ADR-109 — plaintext .env is documented as the interim state", with a 43MB gitignored `scripts/sops` binary present but no `.sops.yaml`, `secrets.enc.yaml` or `sops-env.sh` (docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md:112). The same material shows the file holds the agent key (VISIONCLAW_AGENT_KEY, VC-24.3), the governance bridge private key (VISIONCLAW_NOSTR_PRIVKEY, VC-24.8) and the GitHub PAT (PRIVATE_REPO_GITHUB_PAT, VC-28.6).
- Failure: One filesystem read (container compromise, backup leak, mis-mounted volume) yields the key that authenticates agent decisions, the key that signs governance Nostr events, and the token that force-pushes branches in the ontology repo.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (PROPOSED ADR-2104)

### F-03 — An explicit `public: false` is overridden by an owl-class marker: author-marked-private content is published
- Topics: VC-21.12, VC-21.2
- Evidence: VC-21.12's closeout records "an explicit public false plus a class marker remains included by policy — is_kg_included is an OR, so a valid owl-class overrides public false (vault/mod.rs:162-163)". That gate (github_sync_service.rs:2108) is the single inclusion gate for both ingest paths (VC-21.2).
- Failure: An author writes a class/typed page and explicitly sets `public: false` expecting privacy; the page is ingested as a KG node anyway, and under F-01's shipped compose it is anonymously readable via the public graph/provenance reads.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-04 — Erasure is incomplete: deleteAgentMemory leaves the RuVector embedding searchable
- Topics: VC-22.6
- Evidence: VC-22.6: "no reverse tombstone to RuVector — embedding row persists and stays searchable (docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md:87)"; the Pod-side delete "never calls mcp__claude-flow__memory_delete or any RuVector endpoint — no delete-propagation hook exists in this path" (agentMemory.ts:191-198).
- Failure: A data subject (or operator) runs the documented erasure path; the Pod resource disappears, the vector row keeps matching similarity queries, so "deleted" agent memory remains retrievable. This is an erasure/custody incident, not a soft gap.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-05 — Oxigraph has no backup at all; GRAPH_PROVENANCE is explicitly un-re-derivable
- Topics: VC-22.7, VC-22.11
- Evidence: VC-22.11: "Oxigraph still has NO point-in-time backup — that bullet stands." VC-22.7: "PROPOSED ADR-2103: Oxigraph gains a point-in-time snapshot as a REQUIRED backup member… GRAPH_PROVENANCE is restore-only because it cannot be re-derived." backup-sqlite.sh's REQUIRED_DBS (backup-sqlite.sh:75) cover only the four SQLite databases.
- Failure: Oxigraph disk corruption or operator error loses the knowledge graph, the ontology and the entire append-only audit chain, with no recovery path for provenance. The backup tooling will report success because it never looks at Oxigraph.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (PROPOSED ADR-2103; standing bullet in VC-22.11)

### F-06 — A force-full sync silently destroys governed runtime ontology writes that are not in the corpus
- Topics: VC-22.10, VC-20.7
- Evidence: VC-22.10: "this CLEAR wipes the ENTIRE assert graph including runtime OWL classes/axioms added via the governed write door (github_sync_service.rs:931-933)" and "a class added via add_owl_class/add_axiom between full-syncs is NOT itself in GRAPH_PROVENANCE-protected history for the assert graph — only re-derivation from the corpus … restores it". The governed write door is the live power-user POST classes/axioms route family (VC-20.7, api scope lines 1659-1679).
- Failure: A power user legitimately adds classes/axioms through the governed HTTP door; weeks later an operator runs FORCE_FULL_SYNC (the documented recovery action, also run at boot-adjacent workflows); those governed TBox additions vanish with no warning, tombstone, or provenance record, and anything downstream that referenced them dangles.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (stated in two diagram notes as fact; no Drift/Debt/Open marker)

### F-07 — The HITL rationale gate is bypassable: tier is the proposing agent's self-declaration, and first-contact decisions carry no tier
- Topics: VC-24.3
- Evidence: VC-24.3 OPEN note: "the gate reads the tier RECORDED ON THE CASE, which today is the proposing agent's DECLARED risk_tier … and a case with no row yet carries no tier, so first-contact bridge decisions are ungated" (handlers/enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:332, :393; gate at :406/:518).
- Failure: An agent (or an attacker holding VISIONCLAW_AGENT_KEY) submits a first-contact decision on a case the store has never seen — no tier, no rationale required — and the decision is recorded, written back, and broadcast. The ADR-2110 control that refuses unaccountable high-impact judgments does not bind exactly the cases an adversary would create.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN)

### F-08 — /ws/mcp-relay extracts an auth token but is "not yet enforced on all clients"
- Topics: VC-27.3
- Evidence: VC-27.3: 401 only when the token is empty (:474-476), then "SECURITY: logged but not yet enforced on all clients (:447-450)". Behind the handler, MCPRelayActor proxies client text/binary frames to the orchestrator at ws://multi-agent-container:3002/ws (:77-78, :355-372).
- Failure: A WebSocket client that is in the not-enforced class connects and drives the multi-agent orchestrator (task/swarm control) through the relay without a valid token — unauthenticated command execution against the agent estate, scope unknown from this material.
- Confidence: medium (the note is terse; which clients are exempt is not shown)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (SECURITY)

### F-09 — "Attributed" provenance means a pubkey was asserted, never verified: attribution is spoofable
- Topics: VC-27.13, VC-25.3
- Evidence: VC-27.13: "ProvenanceStatus::Attributed means a well-formed pubkey was asserted, NOT that a signature was verified — the wire carries no sig field (provenance.rs:29-38)"; classification is 64-hex → Attributed (:66-72). Those events are persisted to kpi_agent_events (kpi_compute.rs:630/:650) and feed Trust Variance and HITL Precision (kpi_compute.rs:140, :143) and the ADR-2110 verbatim intent record.
- Failure: Any authenticated ingest peer attributes its actions to any victim pubkey; the audit trail, the /api/trace join, and the human-oversight KPIs are silently poisoned — you cannot tell a human's decision record from a forgery.
- Confidence: high (behavior stated; KPI poisoning inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (SECURITY)

### F-10 — Client still calls the /api/inference endpoints ADR-2066 deleted server-side
- Topics: VC-34.4, VC-20.7
- Evidence: VC-20.7 (ADR-2066): "the /api/inference scope is gone … inference_handler.rs, application/inference_service.rs, events/inference_triggers.rs and the main.rs registration were all removed." VC-34.4: useInferenceService (useInferenceService.ts:49) still issues POST /inference/run, POST /inference/validate, GET /inference/classification, GET /inference/consistency and DELETE /inference/cache/{ontologyId}.
- Failure: Every action in the ontology Inference panel 404s in any current build. Worse as a signal than as a bug: a whole client feature was orphaned by a server removal and nothing — type contract, test, or build — caught it.
- Confidence: high (direct textual conflict; note GET /api/ontology/inference exists but is a different path)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — Governance KPIs are fed by a lossy broadcast hub: dropped events silently skew Trust Variance and HITL
- Topics: VC-25.3, VC-27.13
- Evidence: VC-27.13: `hub::publish` is a broadcast::Sender with HUB_CAPACITY=256 that "drops oldest under backpressure" (hub.rs:22, :32-34), and there is no ack. VC-25.3: the KPI tap is "a separate passive tap task … loop rx.recv().await on /wss/agent-events hub" (kpi_compute.rs:630, :650) whose inserts are the sole source for agent_volume, Trust Variance and HITL Precision (kpi_compute.rs:423, :458, :419-420).
- Failure: Under burst load (or if the tap task stalls), agent events are dropped with no error anywhere; the dashboards that justify "humans remain in the loop" (ADR-2110's own metrics) undercount silently. Nobody is alerted because nothing failed loudly.
- Confidence: medium (drop-oldest mechanism stated; production drop rate inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — ElevationActor loses in-flight case state on restart (no boot reconciliation on the class-elevation path)
- Topics: VC-24.5, VC-24.4, VC-24.6
- Evidence: VC-24.5: "STILL OPEN on this path: the ElevationActor poll … persists set_status but has no boot reconciliation, so a case lost between publish and persist is not reloaded on restart"; the elevating map is in-memory (elevation_actor.rs:1572). VC-24.6 shows the sibling DecisionElevationActor got `plan_reconciliation` (decision_elevation_actor.rs:819) — the class-elevation actor did not.
- Failure: Process crash after the 31404 concept_elevated publish but before set_status (or with the case only in the in-memory map): on restart no poll is re-armed, the merged PR is never observed, the case is stranded non-terminal, and the GOV-2 receipt exists only on the relay.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (STILL OPEN)

### F-13 — One bridge secret shared by three concurrent ACSP clients plus the publisher and the Nostr bridge
- Topics: VC-24.11, VC-24.8, VC-24.9
- Evidence: VC-24.11: "three separate relay connections under three different panel identities can exist concurrently, all using the same bridge secret" (AppState boot app_state.rs:1373; ElevationActor elevation_actor.rs:840; DecisionElevationActor). VC-24.8: "bridge bot keypair VISIONCLAW_NOSTR_PRIVKEY shared by both publisher and bridge" (nostr_bead_publisher.rs:18, nostr_bridge.rs:12). The relay accepts kinds 31400-31402 from registered pubkeys (acsp/client.rs:9-13).
- Failure: Compromise of any one component (all reading the same F-02 plaintext env) lets an attacker sign acceptable kind-31402 ActionRequests and inject fabricated governance cases/panel state system-wide; there is no per-component key to rotate or to attribute a leak to.
- Confidence: medium (facts stated; blast radius partly inferred — relay-side registration gates not shown)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — In non-dev builds no real user can ever be a UI power user: the client never learns its server role
- Topics: VC-33.6, VC-33.1, VC-34.1
- Evidence: VC-33.6: isPowerUser is "always false from login()/loginWithPasskey() and true only from devLogin()/dev-mode auto-login" (nostrAuthService.ts:407, :542, :630 vs :332, :674) and "the client never fetches its own resolved role". SettingRow disables every `isPowerUserOnly` control on that flag (SettingRow.tsx:64, :81).
- Failure: In a production build, the legitimate Owner/Admin (valid server-side via POWER_USER_PUBKEYS) sees every governed control permanently lock-iconned; the power-user UI surface — including the write paths the server would happily authorize — is unreachable for everyone except dev builds. Works in dev, dead in production.
- Confidence: high (mechanism stated; lockout consequence follows directly)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially (the binary-flag-vs-role-lattice DIVERGENCE is marked; the production lockout consequence is not)

### F-15 — Whelk reasoning has no timeout or axiom-count cap, and it sits on the post-sync and elevation-gate paths
- Topics: VC-20.3, VC-24.4
- Evidence: VC-20.3: "DOC-DRIFT — module doc calls this bounded EL reasoning but infer() lines 412-467 has no tokio timeout or axiom-count cap." The same engine backs `approve_with_gate`'s GOV-7 consistency check (elevation_actor.rs:1260, `WhelkInferenceEngine.check_axiom_set`, VC-24.4) and is awaited from run_post_sync_reasoning (github_sync_service.rs:1224).
- Failure: One pathological or adversarially large ontology (ingested via the corpus) makes the reasoner run unbounded: every subsequent sync stalls at the reasoning step, and a human's approval of an elevation case hangs the actor's gate — a cheap denial-of-service on the ingest pipeline and the governance loop.
- Confidence: high (absence of a cap is stated; the stall consequence is inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. The actual server-side capability set of the Editor role — which routes/mutations an unassigned-but-authenticated key can perform. F-01's write exposure could range from "annotate" to "rewrite the graph"; the route-to-level mapping in rbac.rs is not in this material.
2. Whether `run_post_sync_reasoning` awaits `infer()` on the sync critical path or is wrapped by any outer timeout — this decides whether F-15 stalls sync entirely or only the reasoning stage.
3. Whether the image-gen pod PUT that forwards the client's `Authorization` header (VC-28.4, image_gen_handler.rs:688-696) can ever pass the pod's NIP-98 URL-match check (VC-26.4, nip98.rs:510): the token's `u` tag is signed for the image-gen URL, not the pod URL, which suggests `pod_image_url` is always null in production — but neither file's code is shown.
4. What the orchestrator behind /ws/mcp-relay actually accepts (task creation, shell exec, swarm control) — this sizes F-08 from "unauthenticated proxy" to a concrete command surface.
5. Whether anything ever retries a decision left at `writeback_committed=false` (VC-22.8's best-effort Oxigraph write): VC-22.10's rematerialise re-derives DecisionRecord quads on full sync, but nothing shown re-derives the GRAPH_ONTOLOGY_SUMMARY triples — need the reconciliation code to know if that half of a committed decision is permanently lost.

## visionclaw#2 / premortem

# Post-mortem — VisionClaw production incident (client-loss class: data / custody / security)

Basis: the VC-20–VC-35 diagram set only, at `verified_commit: af3dff3f25300cf12bceda5650688ec223270eca` (agentbox `6466e39313c3eb4ba0cadfc2efd4e7ffa3ccc296`). No logs, telemetry or incident timeline were provided, so root causes are ranked by how directly the material documents a live, unmitigated exposure. Every claim below carries the citation the material gives; where I reason past the material I say so.

---

### R-1 — The shipped compose inverts every fail-closed RBAC default: anonymous reads across /api and Editor for any unassigned key
- Topics: VC-22.9, VC-33.6, VC-20.7, VC-22.5
- Chain of events:
  1. Deployment uses the shipped `docker-compose.unified.yml`, which sets `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1` and `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS=1` (with `:-1` defaults) and assigns no Owner.
  2. The owner-less boot check passes only because the flag is set; the only remaining Admin path is `POWER_USER_PUBKEYS`.
  3. Any party generates a Nostr keypair; NIP-98 per-request signing works with any key — the material shows no registration or allowlist for general API use (VC-33.1/VC-33.2).
  4. For safe methods with public reads on, `required_level` returns `None` and the gate passes through anonymously (`rbac_gate.rs:248-253`) — the drawn example is `GET /api/graph/data`, but the rule is drawn generically on the `/api` scope (`main.rs:1113`), so `/api/trace` (agent activity and decisions, VC-22.5), `/api/kpi`, `/api/broker/inbox` and the whole knowledge graph are inferred to be anonymously readable too.
  5. For mutating routes whose only guard is the RbacGate role floor, the authenticated-but-unassigned key resolves to Editor (`role_store.rs:359`, `:366`; `rbac.rs:68-70`), which clears the ReadOnly floor shown at `rbac_gate.rs:156-161` — unauthorized writes land.
  6. Independently of the compose flag, the `/api/ontology` scope leaves all GETs public (`api_handler/ontology/mod.rs:1657`, "POST PUT DELETE gated, all GET public"), class-count is unauthenticated by design (`ontology_class_count_handler.rs:24`), and the entire `/api/ontology-agent` scope — POST discover/read/query/traverse/validate/check — is anonymous after ADR-2116 (`ontology_agent_handler.rs:268-286`).
- Evidence: VC-22.9 CORRECTED note — code fails closed (`public_reads_enabled() ... .unwrap_or(false)` `rbac_gate.rs:126-133`; owner-less FATAL `main.rs:795-801`) but "The shipped compose inverts both (`docker-compose.unified.yml:106, :107`, with the `:-1` default), so an unassigned pubkey resolves to Editor (`role_store.rs:359`). The open posture is ADR-2027's deliberate demo default and stays." VC-33.6 confirms the two-posture divergence and that the client has no role model at all (binary `isPowerUser` only). VC-20.7 documents the public/anonymous read surfaces.
- Warning signs visible today: the CORRECTED note itself; ADR-2027 recording the open posture as deliberate; the unauth class-count endpoint; the fully anonymous agent scope; unassigned→Editor as the code default (`role_store.rs:188,196-198`).
- Confidence: high that the exposure ships exactly as described; the link from exposure to this incident is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Erasure is structurally incomplete: a "successful" delete leaves RuVector embeddings searchable and provenance permanent
- Topics: VC-22.6, VC-26.6, VC-26.5, VC-22.4, VC-22.1, VC-22.10
- Chain of events:
  1. A client/subject requests erasure of agent memory.
  2. `deleteAgentMemory` issues a Pod LDP DELETE of the `.jsonld` resource (`agentMemory.ts:191-198`); 404 counts as success ("already-absent is not an error", `ldpClient.ts:210, :215`), so the UI reports deleted-or-absent = true even on a wrong path.
  3. No code path touches RuVector: "this Pod-side delete never calls `mcp__claude-flow__memory_delete` or any RuVector endpoint — RuVector is external Postgres reached only via MCP, no delete-propagation hook exists in this path" (VC-22.6).
  4. The embedding row "persists and stays searchable" (`docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md:87`) — semantic recall keeps surfacing the erased content.
  5. The PROV-O activity trail in `GRAPH_PROVENANCE` is append-only by invariant (no DELETE/DROP/CLEAR ever, `provenance_emitter.rs:32-33`, ADR-2016); retraction only adds `dl:validTo` (`provenance_writer.rs:218,478-502`).
  6. There is no durable erasure record and no partial-failure handling: today's behavior is the silent success ADR-2102 is proposed to replace ("one durable erasure record, five store acknowledgements, and a partial erasure that is recorded and retryable rather than a silent success" — VC-22.10, PROPOSED).
  7. The subject or an auditor demonstrates the data still surfaces; the operator has been reporting erasure complete.
- Evidence: VC-22.6 DIVERGENCE (RuVector tombstone gap, cited twice); VC-26.6 repeats it ("no RuVector counterpart — see VC-22.6"); VC-22.1 names RuVector as a write-master reached only via MCP; VC-22.4 append-only invariant with crypto-shredding only PROPOSED (ADR-2102).
- Warning signs visible today: two independent DIVERGENCE notes; the gap named in the governing doc `DATA-authority-erasure.md:87`; ADR-2102 and ADR-2103 both sitting at PROPOSED; `deleteResource`'s 404-as-success masking bad deletes.
- Confidence: high that the gap is exactly as documented; the incident linkage is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — A force-full-sync CLEAR destroys governed runtime ontology state that exists nowhere else, and Oxigraph has no backup to restore it from
- Topics: VC-22.10, VC-21.1, VC-22.11, VC-20.2, VC-20.7
- Chain of events:
  1. A power user adds a class/axiom at runtime through the governed write door (`add_owl_class`/`add_axiom`, `application/ontology/directives.rs`), writing into `GRAPH_ONTOLOGY` assert — only the elevation path drafts corpus pages via PR; this direct path does not.
  2. An operator runs a corrective full sync (`FORCE_FULL_SYNC=1` or `POST /api/admin/sync?force_full=true`, VC-21.1/VC-22.10).
  3. `rebuild_assert_graph` CLEARs the entire assert graph — "this CLEAR wipes the ENTIRE assert graph including runtime OWL classes/axioms added via the governed write door (`github_sync_service.rs:931-933`)" — then reinserts only corpus-derived ontology-typed nodes and edges (`:1005`).
  4. The runtime additions are gone and were never in `GRAPH_PROVENANCE` either: "a class added via add_owl_class/add_axiom between full-syncs is NOT itself in GRAPH_PROVENANCE-protected history for the assert graph — only re-derivation from the corpus ... restores it" (VC-22.10).
  5. `rematerialise_decisions` re-derives decision quads but is explicitly non-fatal on failure (`github_sync_service.rs:1083, :1021-1027`), so even the recoverable half can silently not land while the sync reports success.
  6. No Oxigraph point-in-time backup exists (VC-22.11), so the lost triples are unrecoverable; the inferred closure rebuilt from assert loses the corresponding edges too.
- Evidence: VC-22.10 full-sync sequence with the wipe note and the provenance-gap note; VC-21.1 force-full triggers and `clear_graph` at `github_sync_service.rs:385-390`; VC-22.11 "Oxigraph still has NO point-in-time backup - that bullet stands"; VC-22.7 ADR-2103 snapshot PROPOSED only.
- Warning signs visible today: the wipe note and the "not in provenance" note in VC-22.10; non-fatal rematerialise; the standing no-backup bullet; sync statistics carry no loss signal for assert-graph content.
- Confidence: medium — the wipe semantics are explicit; whether production actually uses the runtime write door (vs. only the PR/corpus path) is not shown, so the linkage is inferred.
- Severity: high

### F-01 — Oxigraph has no backup at all; provenance is unrestorable by design
- Topics: VC-22.11, VC-22.7, VC-22.2

- Failure: volume or disk loss on the Oxigraph data directory destroys `GRAPH_KNOWLEDGE`, all ontology graphs and the signed PROV-O history with no restore path; the ADR-2016 audit chain ceases to exist.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (standing gap; PROPOSED ADR-2103)

### F-02 — Cross-store dual writes have no 2PC and no retry; a failed writeback leaves a decision permanently half-applied
- Topics: VC-22.8, VC-22.3, VC-25.1
- Evidence: "no cross-store 2PC - the durable decision commits in SQLite first ... the Oxigraph write is best-effort, `writeback_committed` flips only on Ok (`enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:587, :605`)" (VC-22.8); VC-25.1's stage rules then classify such a case as `merged status = pending` forever (decided + triggered-true + no committed timestamp).
- Failure: Oxigraph briefly unavailable during `apply_decision` → SQLite row says decided, `GRAPH_ONTOLOGY_SUMMARY` never written, no retry path appears anywhere in the material → the insight loop shows a loop that never closes and the KG never receives the summary triples.
- Confidence: high (mechanism stated); permanence inferred — absence of a retry path is absence from the material, not a verified absence
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated as a design note)

- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (author labels it SECURITY)

Verification notes for the reviewing engineer: (1) every `path:line` above is quoted from the diagram set, not from source — re-anchor before acting; (2) R-1's route-by-route exposure sweep beyond `/api/graph/data` is inferred from the generic gate rule; (3) F-02's "no retry" and F-11's format divergence are absences in this material, not verified absences in the code; (4) F-03's note (`:447-450`) needs a read of the actual enforcement branches, which the diagram does not draw.

## visionclaw#3 / critical

### F-01 — Dev-mode auth bypass is armed by default and is peer-agnostic
- Topics: VC-36.15, VC-36.3
- Evidence: `docker-compose.unified.yml:90` defaults `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1` (ADR-2108 flipped `:-0` to `:-1`, 2026-09-08), and when on, auth resolve is described as "LAN-local FULL bypass, peer-agnostic: no NIP-98, no token, no peer check" (`src/utils/auth.rs:186-201`), granting every request as `DEV_MODE_PUBKEY = "dev-mode-local-admin"` (`auth.rs:79`, `main.rs:291`). The material itself warns "both services load env_file .env, so the var must never sit in a .env shared with prod — sharper now that the compose default supplies it unasked" (`docker-compose.unified.yml:87-89`). Release builds refuse the var (`project/src/main.rs:109`), but the backend the headset actually talks to (`ws://192.168.2.132:4000`, VC-36.16) is this dev service.
- Failure: any host that can reach the dev backend's port — a browser tab, another workstation, anything on the LAN/VPN — performs drag/pin/physics writes as full admin with zero credentials. "LAN-local" is enforced by network topology only; the code checks no peer. A copied `.env` silently arms any non-release build.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — ADR-2108 records the default flip and the `.env` caveat; the peer-agnostic scope (any reachable peer, not just the headset) carries no risk marker.

### F-02 — The whole credential plane runs over plaintext ws:// and http://
- Topics: VC-36.3, VC-36.11, VC-36.10, VC-36.16
- Evidence: the graph socket path is named `/wss` (`graph_scene.gd:72`) but every shown transport is plaintext: default `ws://localhost:4000`, deployed `XR_BACKEND_WS=ws://192.168.2.132` (VC-36.16); `_http_base` derives `http://` from `ws://` (`graph_scene.gd:1213`), so NIP-98 `Authorization: Nostr` headers (`graph_scene.gd:1114`, `signer.rs:124`) and the ws `authenticate` message (`transport.rs:106-120`) travel unencrypted; presence deliberately bypasses nginx entirely ("nginx :3001 does NOT proxy /ws/presence", VC-36.10), so no proxy-side TLS or controls apply to it either.
- Failure: an on-path observer on the LAN captures kind-27235 Authorization headers and replays them at the same URL for write access (server-side freshness/nonce enforcement is not shown anywhere in the material); captures presence schnorr challenge responses; injects or tampers with position frames. The wss naming shows TLS was intended and is absent from every depicted deployment.
- Confidence: medium (transport facts are stated; the production posture — that no wss endpoint exists — is inferred, since only ws:// examples appear)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — The declared ship target does not exist; the only verified path is one rig and a manual race-prone ceremony
- Topics: VC-36.2, VC-36.16
- Evidence: `project.godot:2` header still calls the Quest 3 APK the ship target, while `graph_scene.gd:25-26` words Quest 3 standalone as PLANNED and names the Vive/SteamVR rig as deployed; the `.mobile` override targets an APK that is UNBUILT with no Android NDK provisioned (`project.godot:49`, `BASELINE-architecture.md:209-210`). The verified path requires: separate kill and launch over two ssh calls ("a single chained call races the compositor"), XAUTHORITY carried per launch, SteamVR runtime verified by hand (VC-36.16), and the NVIDIA 580 driver pinned because 610 "fails the GL multiview second eye" (VC-36.2 C2).
- Failure: there is no shippable artifact for the stated target. The one working rig breaks on an unattended driver update to 610, a single chained restart command, or a SteamVR runtime change; recovery depends entirely on an operator reproducing an undocumented-except-here ceremony. No service unit, no automation is shown.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (operability; certain to bite, not itself a security/data event)
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, TENSION, ADR-2032 consequence notes) — though the composite fragility is not marked as a whole.

### F-04 — The Godot-facing runtime is excluded from the test suites that gate merges
- Topics: VC-36.16, VC-36.18, VC-36.20, VC-36.13
- Evidence: "Godot-facing runtime classes are excluded by cfg(test) (lib.rs:38-45), so no scene/shader test runs" among the 141 headless gdext tests (VC-36.16); "no headset/scene/shader test covers this path… source and helper results do not certify Godot execution" (VC-36.18); the spatial validation boundary is "Observed desktop GL renders and GUT assertions. No fresh headset, live authenticated graph or Quest frame-budget certification" (VC-36.20 Limits).
- Failure: regressions of the class already seen once (the stride-16 divisor bug 63d9bb9b8 that made all edges vanish) pass the entire merge gate and are discovered on the headset, or not at all. Accepting production responsibility means accepting code whose execution surface is untested.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially (DIVERGENCE in VC-36.18, Limits node in VC-36.20; the cfg(test) exclusion itself is stated without a marker)

### F-05 — GDScript/gdext version skew and protocol-version mismatch fail silently
- Topics: VC-36.7, VC-36.6, VC-36.13, VC-36.16
- Evidence: `_update_beam_multimesh` returns with no user-visible signal when `_binary_client` lacks `build_beam_buffer` — annotated "BREAK - older gdext build" (VC-36.7); an unknown frame version yields `DecodeError::BadVersion` → "warn + ignore" (`binary_protocol.rs:715-717`), and a warn is invisible inside a headset; no version handshake between the GDScript layer and the loaded `.so` appears anywhere in the material, and the tests that could catch skew are the ones excluded by cfg(test) (F-04).
- Failure: a partially upgraded client (new scripts, stale extension, or vice versa) or a server protocol bump leaves agent beams absent or the entire graph frozen on a live socket, with only a console warning. Operators see "nothing works" with no diagnostic in the HMD.
- Confidence: medium (the silent guards are stated; the absence of any handshake is inferred from the material's silence)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

- Evidence: "server correctness and the user-visible denied/error states are UNVERIFIED. Query Execute is implemented but runtime acceptance remains open" (`docs/XR-client.md` 'Known divergences' bullet 4); no headset/scene/shader test covers the path. The ADR-2108 write-refusal visibility fix is documented for writes, not for this GET/POST path.

- Topics: VC-36.9, VC-36.22

The 30 s evidence TTL (`render_store.rs:823`) is the only stale exit.

### F-08 — The nostr secret is passed on the godot command line
- Topics: VC-36.16, VC-36.10
- Evidence: the documented launch is `XR_BACKEND_WS=… XR_NOSTR_SECRET=<hex> godot --path xr-client …` (VC-36.16). That same secret is the long-lived identity gating drag/pin and the presence challenge ("XR_NOSTR_SECRET is REQUIRED in practice", VC-36.10).
- Failure: the key lands in the headset host's shell history, ssh session records, and `/proc/<pid>/cmdline`, readable by any local user or agent on that host; a leaked key is a persistent write credential and there is no rotation story in the material.
- Confidence: high (the command line is stated; the exposure channels are standard)

- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — Head-tracking loads code from a third-party CDN at runtime
- Topics: VC-37.6, VC-37.1
- Evidence: `FilesetResolver.forVisionTasks('https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@mediapipe/tasks-vision@0.10.21/wasm')` (`useHeadTracking.ts:27`); only the model asset is local (`/models/face_landmarker.task`).
- Failure: a jsdelivr outage or region block breaks head parallax in production (degrades with an error string); a CDN compromise serves arbitrary code into the app origin — the same origin that holds the `/wss` stream connection and settings store. Supply-chain exposure with no pinned hash shown.
- Confidence: high (the dependency is stated; the compromise scenario is standard)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — NodePositionContext is a module singleton with no invalidation after remount
- Topics: VC-37.7
- Evidence: "NodePositionContext is named a context but is a module-level mutable singleton… GraphManager publishes the live buffer on every frame (GraphManager.tsx:431) and the id map on every node-set change (:75)… Nothing invalidates a stale reader after a remount" (`NodePositionContext.tsx:13`).
- Failure: after a remount the republished buffer and an already-mounted reader's id map disagree — BotsVisualization overlays render at wrong indices/positions until the next node-set change; two publishers (e.g. HMR, two canvases) fight over one singleton.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)

### F-11 — The V5 freshness watermark assumes a single monotonic sequence source
- Topics: VC-36.6, VC-36.4
- Evidence: `admit(Some(seq), …)` drops any frame with `seq <= watermark` as stale (`binary_protocol.rs:551-569`); `awaiting_resync` is named but its trigger and recovery path are not shown; `broadcast_seq` is a server-side counter with no stated persistence across restarts or coordination across instances.
- Failure: a backend restart that resets the counter (or a second backend instance behind the same endpoint with an independent counter) makes every subsequent frame "stale" — the graph freezes on a healthy, connected socket until whatever resync mechanism exists fires, if it does.
- Confidence: low (inferred — the resync path may handle exactly this; the material does not show it)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — The visual gallery still certifies seven tabs; the HUD ships eight
- Topics: VC-36.8, VC-36.20
- Evidence: VC-36.8 states "this HUD now has EIGHT pages, not seven" (Key tab added 2026-09-08, a37433f5d; `hud.gd:161`, `:600`), while VC-36.20's validation fixture is "Seven HUD tabs and radial gallery — xr-client/tests/visual/hud_gallery.gd" — both at the same verified commit af3dff3f.
- Failure: the visual acceptance artifact misrepresents the shipped UI: the Key page — the one whose palette constants are duplicated by hand and can drift (`hud.gd:169`, `:633`) — is absent from visual review, so a colour regression there passes the gallery.
- Confidence: high (the contradiction is stated on both sides); medium on which artifact is stale (fixture vs diagram label)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — Head-parallax "nudge" cameraMode mutates projectionMatrix with no shown restore
- Topics: VC-37.6
- Evidence: the nudge branch does `camera.projectionMatrix.multiply(NUDGE_MATRIX…)` (`HeadTrackedParallaxController.tsx:57-62`); the not-tracking branch restores only via `clearViewOffset()` guarded by "opt camera.view set" (`:66-68`) — but `camera.view` is the asymmetricFrustum mechanism, and no reset of a nudge offset is shown, nor a base-projection recompute before each frame's multiply.
- Failure: tracking drops while cameraMode is the nudge mode → the camera keeps (or accumulates) the last skewed projection, distorting the desktop view until reload or mode switch.
- Confidence: low, inferred (a reset elsewhere in the R3F loop would clear it; not shown in the material)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — The demo's "reserved" id range has no shown enforcement against live agent ids
- Topics: VC-36.23, VC-36.9, VC-36.5
- Evidence: demo provenance is "the reserved id range 0xD001-0xD0FF alone" (`agent_demo_director.gd:25`), and Stop calls `retire_agents(demo_wire_ids())`, removing records outright (`binary_protocol.rs:1206`, `render_store.rs:908`). But real agent wire ids are server-assigned under `NODE_ID_MASK 0x03FF_FFFF` with `AGENT_NODE_FLAG 0x8000_0000` (`binary_protocol.rs:37-38`) — a range that fully contains 0xD001-0xD0FF — and nothing in the material shows the server excludes that window.
- Failure: a live agent whose server-assigned id lands in the window is indistinguishable from a demo agent; pressing Stop retires the real agent's record and despawns its embodiment mid-task.
- Confidence: low, inferred ("reserved" may be enforced server-side; not shown)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — The legacy PHYSICS_BEARER credential path is still constructed by every installed client
- Topics: VC-36.11, VC-36.15
- Evidence: "the legacy dev bearer path still exists and is still constructed by the client. The server refuses it for any non-loopback peer" — `Authorization: PHYSICS_BEARER` + `X-Nostr-Pubkey`, gated only by `_nostr_secret_present` (`graph_scene.gd:94`, `:450`, `:1116`; docs/XR-client.md 'Known divergences' bullet 5).
- Failure: the safety of this path rests entirely on one server-side peer check; a regression or misconfiguration of that check re-opens a credential that all deployed clients already emit. It also means local/loopback development never exercises NIP-98, so signer regressions surface first in production.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Server-side freshness and nonce enforcement** for NIP-98 (`authenticate` frames, Authorization headers) and the presence challenge — whether timestamps are windowed and nonces are single-use. This decides whether the plaintext capture in F-02 is replayable; need `src/utils/auth.rs` verification code and `presence_actor.rs`.
2. **The FreshnessTracker resync path** (`binary_protocol.rs:551-569`) — what triggers `awaiting_resync` and whether a server seq reset actually recovers, which decides F-11.
3. **How live agent wire ids are allocated server-side** — whether 0xD001-0xD0FF is genuinely off-limits, which decides F-14.
4. **Production topology**: whether the backend at 192.168.2.132:4000 is the dev-compose service or a release build, whether any TLS termination exists anywhere, and how the browser client authenticates to `/wss` (VC-37 shows no browser credential at all). This sets the true severity of F-01 and F-02.
5. **CI wiring**: whether the GUT scene tests and the `xr-perf-regression` gate run automatically on merge or exist only as operator-invoked commands (VC-36.16 shows them as manual ceremony steps), and whether `hud_gallery.gd` is among what runs — this decides how much of F-04 and F-12 is mitigated in practice.

## visionclaw#3 / premortem

# Post-mortem — VisionClaw XR backend: unauthenticated graph disclosure and unattributed administrative writes

**Summary.** The material does not describe one bug; it describes a posture. The XR backend serves its read surface without credentials, collapses its write identity to a single pseudo-user whenever `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE` is armed, and carries the whole XR transport — including NIP-98 credential headers — over plaintext `ws://`/`http://` on a shared LAN. Twelve months after ADR-2108 flipped the compose default to armed-by-default, with the material's own warned-about shared `.env` hazard, these three facts are sufficient for a full data disclosure plus unattributed admin writes. Root causes are ranked by how directly the material supports each as the triggering path.

---

### R-1 — `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE` armed by default escapes the dev perimeter and grants peer-agnostic admin
- Topics: VC-36.15, VC-36.3, VC-36.11
- Chain of events: ADR-2108 flips the compose default to `"${VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1}"`, so the backend boots ARMED unless the operator actively opts out → the armed service must be reachable by the headset at `ws://192.168.2.132:4000` (VC-36.16), i.e. it binds beyond loopback → the bypass is peer-agnostic ("no NIP-98, no token, no peer check"), so any routable peer is resolved as `DEV_MODE_PUBKEY = "dev-mode-local-admin"` → outsider performs drag/pin, physics and layout writes as admin with no credential and no individual attribution. Parallel path: the var reaches a release deploy via the shared `.env` the material itself warns about — a release binary then refuses to boot (ADR-06 D11), converting the same misconfiguration into a production outage instead of a breach.
- Evidence: `docker-compose.unified.yml:90` default `:-1`; "boots an ARMED backend and the operator opts OUT with `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE=0`"; "Still not in the \*common-environment anchor and not in the production service"; the caveat "both services load env_file .env, so the var must never sit in a .env shared with prod — sharper now that the compose default supplies it unasked" (`docker-compose.unified.yml:87-89`); peer-agnostic full bypass `src/utils/auth.rs:186-201`; `DEV_MODE_PUBKEY` `auth.rs:79`, grant at `project/src/main.rs:291`; release refusal `project/src/main.rs:109`; the only signal is a log banner `main.rs:288`.
- Warning signs visible today: the material already flags the `.env` vector and the default flip; the bypass is *named* "LAN-local" while the documented implementation has no peer check at all; the friction-free keyless path (`graph_scene.gd:446-451`) means production-shaped traffic patterns develop with zero credentials, so nothing looks wrong until the boundary is crossed.
- Confidence: high — every mechanism is stated; the escape into a shared `.env` or reachable dev service is the material's own flagged hazard, inferred only in which of the two unfolds first.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The graph read stream is served to unauthenticated peers over plaintext ws/http, with sniffable credential headers
- Topics: VC-36.3, VC-36.15, VC-36.11, VC-36.16, VC-36.10
- Chain of events: the client subscribes to the binary stream *before* the optional authenticate step (`GRAPH_SUBSCRIBE` at transport.rs:46 is unconditional; NIP-98 `authenticate` is an `opt nostr_secret_hex non-empty` at transport.rs:106-120) → the material states the consequence for refused peers outright: "read stream still served" (VC-36.15) → the deployed transport is plaintext (`XR_BACKEND_WS=ws://192.168.2.132`, VC-36.16) and REST is derived from it as plain `http://` by `_http_base` (`graph_scene.gd:1213`) → any peer that can reach port 4000 reads the entire graph — the 52-byte records carry community, centrality, anomaly and SSISP parent lineage (VC-36.5, `binary_protocol.rs:682`), and 0x23 frames carry agent task captions → an on-path observer captures `Authorization: Nostr <b64>` headers off plain HTTP and the kind-27235 `authenticate` message off plain WS, and replays them within the signature's validity window to write as the victim.
- Evidence: subscribe-before-authenticate ordering in the VC-36.3 sequence; "401 — drag/pin refused, read stream still served" (VC-36.15); `_http_base` ws→http mapping (`graph_scene.gd:1213`, VC-36.11); deploy env `ws://192.168.2.132` (VC-36.16); "nginx :3001 does NOT proxy /ws/presence — the client points at the LAN backend directly" (VC-36.10 note), so that socket has no edge at all; no TLS is described anywhere on the XR path in any topic.
- Warning signs visible today: full reads already require no credential for any LAN peer; credential headers already cross the wire in cleartext in the documented deploy ceremony.
- Confidence: high for unauthenticated reads and plaintext transport (both stated); the replay step is inferred — server-side signature-freshness or single-use handling for either credential is nowhere described.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Legacy `PHYSICS_BEARER` accepted from loopback peers while an edge proxy shares the deployment
- Topics: VC-36.11, VC-36.15, VC-36.10
- Chain of events: the client still constructs `Authorization: PHYSICS_BEARER` plus a client-chosen `X-Nostr-Pubkey` header whenever no real secret is configured (`graph_scene.gd:94, :450, :1116`) → the server "refuses it for any non-loopback peer" — a refusal boundary stated in terms of loopback-ness, which only has meaning if loopback peers *are* accepted → any process on the backend host (cron, helper, compromised co-tenant) speaking to `localhost` becomes admin and impersonates any pubkey of its choosing via the header → and wherever the nginx edge at :3001 and the backend share a host or network namespace in `docker-compose.unified.yml`, every *remote* caller's proxied request also arrives from loopback, converting the legacy dev path into an unauthenticated remote admin surface.
- Evidence: the VC-36.11 DIVERGENCE ("the legacy dev bearer path still exists and is still constructed by the client. The server refuses it for any non-loopback peer"); client-chosen `X-Nostr-Pubkey: <pubkey_hex>`; nginx edge at :3001 (VC-36.10 note, whose presence-of-exception implies the remaining surface is proxied); both services defined with `env_file .env` in `docker-compose.unified.yml:87-89`.
- Warning signs visible today: the divergence note itself; no rate limit, audit identity, or removal plan for the bearer path is described in any topic.
- Confidence: medium — loopback acceptance is implied, not stated verbatim; the proxy/backend network colocation is inferred (compose topology is not shown).
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Demo provenance is a client-side id-range convention; Stop Demo can retire live agents
- Topics: VC-36.23, VC-36.9, VC-36.5
- Evidence: "Provenance is the reserved id range 0xD001-0xD0FF alone" (`agent_demo_director.gd:25`); `retire_agents(demo_wire_ids())` (`binary_protocol.rs:1206`) "remove record and anchor outright" (`render_store.rs:908`); real agent ids are `AGENT_NODE_FLAG 0x80000000 | node_id` with `NODE_ID_MASK 0x03FF_FFFF` (`binary_protocol.rs:37-38`), so 0xD001–0xD0FF (53,249–53,503) is ordinary legal id space; no server-side reservation of that range appears anywhere in the material.
- Failure: the server's agent-id allocator emits an id in 0xD001–0xD0FF; an operator runs and stops the Agent Demo; Stop retires the live agent's record and anchor, its beam and roster row vanish mid-work, and nothing distinguishes it from a demo artifact.
- Confidence: medium — the collision is inferred; only the client-side reservation is evidenced.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — 0x23 agent actions are fanned to every `/wss` client with no described scope
- Topics: VC-36.9, VC-36.3
- Evidence: the server participant is labelled `broadcast_to_all` and the note reads "Fanned to every /wss client on the SAME binary path"; frames carry task lines and payloads with `{"intent": caption}`; no per-room or per-graph scoping of the fanout is described in any topic.
- Failure: a backend serving more than one room/graph leaks every tenant's agent activity — targets, states, and task captions — to all connected clients.
- Confidence: low — inferred; single-scope deployments would mask it entirely.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — Dev mode collapses all write attribution to one pseudo-identity
- Topics: VC-36.15
- Evidence: "Every request granted as DEV_MODE_PUBKEY = 'dev-mode-local-admin'" (`auth.rs:79`, `main.rs:291`), peer-agnostically (`auth.rs:186-201`); NIP-98, the only per-identity credential, is bypassed entirely in this mode.
- Failure: whenever dev mode is armed (see R-1), all administrative writes — layout, physics, drag/pin — are logged under a single shared identity; after an incident, no write can be attributed to a person or process, destroying non-repudiation and any custody audit.
- Confidence: high — the behaviour is stated; the forensic consequence is direct.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — Work beams are fold-remapped onto representatives, not the real target node
- Topics: VC-36.9, VC-36.7
- Evidence: "beam re-routes to the fold representative when the real target is folded away (`render_store.rs:1954` test)"; the marked DIVERGENCE: "agent endpoints use LOCAL positions directly while beam targets are fold-remapped and drawn-gated — the closeout asks for explicit state precedence, expiry and visible stale/error handling" (docs/XR-client.md 'Estate closeout qualification 2026-09-04').
- Failure: with folding active, an operator sees a beam and ring on node X while the agent actually works on folded-away node Y; the operator pins or intervenes on the wrong node via the signed write paths.
- Confidence: high — the remap is stated and test-asserted.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

### F-05 — No user-visible staleness channel on the data plane; a frozen graph is indistinguishable from a calm one
- Topics: VC-36.6, VC-36.9, VC-36.3
- Evidence: unknown frame versions are "warn + ignore" (`binary_protocol.rs:715-717`); V5 frames below the watermark are dropped with an `awaiting_resync` state (`binary_protocol.rs:551-569`) while the only client→server messages described anywhere are `requestInitialData`, `GRAPH_SUBSCRIBE` and `authenticate` (VC-36.3) — no resync request appears in any described flow; agent evidence expires silently after 30 s (`render_store.rs:823`), parking working agents; signal traffic is engineered to "stay near zero once communities stabilise" (`binary_protocol.rs:120-126`); the only described staleness signal is the library probe `last_agent_action_age_ms` (`binary_protocol.rs:1122`), labelled P1, with no described consumer.
- Failure: after a server protocol bump or a stream stall, clients render a frozen graph and a parked swarm with no in-HMD or in-browser indication; operators act on stale data for as long as the illusion holds.
- Confidence: medium — each behaviour is stated; the absence of any indicator is an inference from the described flows.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the agent-expiry half overlaps VC-36.9's marked divergence; the frame-path finding is unmarked)

### F-06 — The Nostr secret is handed to the headset process as an env prefix on an ssh command line
- Topics: VC-36.16
- Evidence: the launch line is `XR_BACKEND_WS=ws://192.168.2.132 (port 4000) XR_NOSTR_SECRET=<hex> godot --path xr-client ...`, invoked over `ssh john@10.10.10.1` as one of the documented two calls.
- Failure: the key material lands in the headset account's shell history, in `/proc/<pid>/environ` for the lifetime of the Godot process, and in any ssh-command logging the operator's tooling keeps — a custody leak of the write-credential key reachable by any local user or log reader.
- Confidence: high — the ceremony is stated; the exposure mechanics are standard-inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — The keyless default is designed to feel fully functional
- Topics: VC-36.10, VC-36.15
- Evidence: `NostrAuth.create()` silently returns an ephemeral signer when the secret is empty (`graph_scene.gd:435-437`, `signer.rs:50`); docs/XR-client.md INVARIANT 6 says `XR_NOSTR_SECRET` is "REQUIRED in practice"; under dev mode "XR_NOSTR_SECRET can be empty and the ephemeral signer still works" (`graph_scene.gd:446-451`).
- Failure: an environment runs credential-less for months with no visible symptom because dev mode papers over every gate; the day dev mode is off or the backend is replaced, every write 401s — or worse, staff habituation means nobody questions the bypass.
- Confidence: high — all pieces stated.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — `NodePositionContext` is an uninvalidated module singleton; stale readers misattribute node data
- Topics: VC-37.7
- Evidence: the marked DEBT: "NodePositionContext is named a context but is a module-level mutable singleton... GraphManager publishes the live buffer on every frame (`GraphManager.tsx:431`) and the id map on every node-set change (`GraphManager.tsx:75`)... Nothing invalidates a stale reader after a remount" (`NodePositionContext.tsx:13`); `BotsVisualization.tsx:29` reads the buffer directly.
- Failure: after a graph switch or remount ordering change, the agent overlay resolves agent activity through a stale id→index map into the new position buffer, displaying an agent acting on the wrong node to a desktop user.
- Confidence: high — the debt is stated; the scenario is direct.
- Severity: medium

### F-09 — MediaPipe wasm is fetched at runtime from a third-party CDN into the desktop client
- Topics: VC-37.6
- Evidence: `FilesetResolver.forVisionTasks('https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/@mediapipe/tasks-vision@0.10.21/wasm')` (`useHeadTracking.ts:27`); the landmarker then processes live webcam frames (`detectForVideo`); no integrity pinning, hash, or vendoring is mentioned in any topic.
- Failure: a compromised or tampered CDN artifact (or an origin-level substitution) executes arbitrary wasm inside the client origin for every desktop user with head parallax enabled — session and credential theft from the browser client, with webcam frames flowing through the substituted code.
- Confidence: medium — the runtime third-party fetch is stated; the absence of integrity pinning is inferred.

### F-10 — Presence room join appears to be authorised by knowledge of the room URN alone
- Topics: VC-36.10
- Evidence: the client supplies `room_id` itself in the auth message (`connect(base + "/ws/presence", room_urn, display_name)`; `{"type":"auth","did":...,"signature":...,"room_id":<urn>,...}`); the handshake challenge proves key possession, not room membership; no membership check appears in the described flow.
- Failure: any holder of a valid key joins any room URN it learns, receiving the sibling pose stream (0x43), roster, and agent co-presence (0x44) — location and attention telemetry of other users.
- Confidence: low — inferred; server-side membership enforcement is simply not described.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — The `?token=` query auth fallback survives in dev-auth builds
- Topics: VC-36.3
- Evidence: ADR-2058 residue as stated: "the server's own ?token= query fallback is now compiled out of release entirely and survives only dev-auth-gated with a SECURITY: warning".
- Failure: a dev-auth build is reachable (same class as R-1/R-3); a query-string token transits proxies and lands in access logs and browser history, and is replayed by anyone who reads one.
- Confidence: high that the residue exists; the exploitation scenario is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — Agent-evidence timestamps are u32 compared monotonically; units unstated
- Topics: VC-36.5, VC-36.9, VC-36.23
- Evidence: the 0x23 record carries `ts u32`; freshness gates are `ts_is_newer` (`render_store.rs:469`) enforced at `render_store.rs:745` and `:801` with a 30 s TTL (`render_store.rs:823`); timestamps are stamped from the `server_clock_ms()` anchor (`binary_protocol.rs:1217`, `agent_demo_director.gd:211`), i.e. a millisecond clock adjacent to a u32 field whose unit is nowhere stated.
- Failure: if `ts` is milliseconds in u32, it wraps at 2^32 ms ≈ 49.7 days of server uptime and the monotonic gates invert — fresh evidence rejected (every agent parks) or stale evidence accepted; if backends are ever load-balanced, comparisons across clock domains break the same gates.
- Confidence: low — inferred; the unit and any wrap handling are unstated.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

- Topics: VC-36.18, VC-36.16, VC-36.11
- Evidence: the marked DIVERGENCE: "server correctness and the user-visible denied/error states are UNVERIFIED. Query Execute is implemented but runtime acceptance remains open" (docs/XR-client.md 'Known divergences' bullet 4); "no headset/scene/shader test covers this path"; the GUT inventory in VC-36.16 lists no query test, and the only denial-notice cover is `test_write_denied_notice.gd` for the settings path.
- Failure: a 401/403/5xx on `/api/graph/query/pattern` reaches no `flash_notice` on this surface — the exact invisible-failure class ADR-2108 fixed for settings writes — and an operator reads a silent failure as "no matches", acting on a false-empty result set.
- Confidence: high — stated.

- Evidence: the marked DRIFT: "head-parallax settings are read-only from this controller's side — sensitivity and cameraMode have no equivalent updateConfig-style writer here" (note1, VC-37.10), while the controller reads them every render (`HeadTrackedParallaxController.tsx:17-19`).

- Topics: VC-36.2
- Evidence: D2 "the .mobile override targets the Quest 3 APK, which is UNBUILT — no Android NDK is provisioned. 90fps was measured only on VIVE Pro + dual RTX 6000" (BASELINE-architecture.md:209-210); D3 TENSION "project.godot:2 header still calls the Quest 3 APK the ship target, while graph_scene.gd:25-26 reworded Quest 3 standalone as a PLANNED target"; D1 records the editor-managed `config/features` drift (4.3 declared vs 4.6.1 Compatibility reality).

## visionflow / critical

### F-01 — Replay protection for signed NIP-98 auth is process-local and wiped by restart
- Topics: VF-08.2
- Evidence: "VisionClaw process-local single-use cache — the event id is recorded atomically after validation (nip98.rs:164)"; "Restart, replicas, URL semantics and caller wiring require separate proof"; "the cache is declared single-process by invariant in VisionClaw itself (nip98.rs:210)". No marker attaches to this limitation.
- Failure: VisionClaw restarts (deploy, crash, OOM) and loses the consumed-event-id set, or is scaled to a second replica that does not share it — a captured signed NIP-98 request that was already accepted replays successfully. The single-use guarantee holds for exactly one long-lived process and nothing else.
- Confidence: high (facts stated; the replay consequence is inferred, but directly, from "single-use" plus "process-local")
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — The copyright guard scans only root-level *.txt; every other path reaches the public repo
- Topics: VF-05.9
- Evidence: guard enumerates "every tracked ROOT-LEVEL *.txt" (copyright-guard.yml:36) against a two-name allowlist (:34); ".gitignore alone does not stop a git add -f" (:3); "A copyrighted .txt committed one directory down passes, and texput.log at the repo root is a .log, outside the pattern entirely" (DIVERGENCE). Separately, the README badge advertises AGPL-3.0 while no root LICENSE exists (LICENSES/README.md:5).
- Failure: the stated threat — copyrighted book sources — is committed as a nested .txt, any other extension, or force-added; the only automated control never sees it and copyrighted text lands in a public repository. Legal/leak incident on the production surface.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-03 — ADR-2013 deletes agentbox's ontology-bridge.js while the drift gate still treats that file as its axis source of truth
- Topics: VF-01.15, VF-05.2, VF-05.3, VF-02.8, VF-08.1
- Evidence: ADR-2013's deletion list includes "agentbox ontology-bridge.js/ontology-propose.js + hub entry" (ADR-2013-sovereign-corpus-is-ecosystem-canon.md:82-88; D5 at :67-71); VF-08.1 confirms "Agentbox retired ontology-bridge.js (deleted; see AB-25)". Meanwhile resolveAgentbox picks "first candidate holding mcp/servers/ontology-bridge.js" (drift-counter.mjs:77), the mcp-ontology-tools axis scans its TOOLS array (drift-counter.mjs:165) with policed sites (allowlist.json:41-43), and deploy gate 7 enforces against a checkout pinned to the allowlist source_pin (deploy.yml:62, VF-02.8).
- Failure: with the pin predating deletion, publication enforces public "N MCP tools" figures against a revision containing a subsystem the estate has deleted — policing a ghost; once the pin moves past deletion, resolveAgentbox fails, the axis becomes unavailable and fail-opens to reported (drift-counter.mjs:266), so four policed sites silently lose enforcement. Either branch voids "one queryable source" (BASELINE-visionflow.md:296), and the known 7-vs-12 README divergence (README.md:158/165) already escapes the regex by word order (VF-05.3 DIVERGENCE).
- Confidence: high on the textual contradiction; which runtime branch is live is inferred (depends on pin age)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the 7-vs-12 regex escape is marked; the deletion-vs-axis-source conflict is not)

### F-04 — Estate-health can report OK or "no CI by design" while repositories are actually failing
- Topics: VF-03.4, VF-03.6, VF-03.11, VF-03.1
- Evidence: the collector reads "one UNFILTERED /actions/runs page of 100" (estate-health.mjs:603, :608) — marked Debt: PR runs can push default-branch runs off so "the row then reads none rather than its real state"; none + ci_by_design becomes exempt (estate-health.mjs:553); knowledgeGraph's "last Build and verify run failed on an older commit, so the HEAD rule reports none and ci_by_design turns that into exempt" (estate-health.json:464-484), although roster.json:12 annotates it "a publish target whose only workflow is the Pages build" — it demonstrably has a workflow that failed. check() branches only on red, amber/unreadable, and down surfaces (estate-health.mjs:959-981); none rows move no verdict.
- Failure: a repo's CI goes red amid pull-request noise, its row flips to none→exempt and the public pill reads "no CI by design"; or a collector-level regression turns every row none and check() still returns ESTATE-HEALTH-OK. The nightly verdict, the deployed page and the dream cycle's hypotheses all run on the wrong answer.
- Confidence: high (mechanics stated; the all-none scenario is inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partly — the one-page Debt is marked; the exempt-masks-a-failed-run case and check()'s none blind spot are not

### F-05 — The corpus-count equality invariant has no detector anywhere in the estate
- Topics: VF-01.15 (also VF-08)
- Evidence: Invariant 8 requires Loom /health, VisionClaw /api/ontology/classes and vault build --stats to agree — "a divergence is a defect" (BASELINE-visionflow.md:314-315). ADR-2013 records the defect it exists to fix — four doors reporting 8,433 / 8,146 / 4,167 (ADR-2013...md:22-26) — at implementation partial, activation only staged (:5-7). The record's own review_trigger names the first divergence between the three counts as forcing event, "but nothing in this canon repo currently polls the three counts to detect one" (Open).
- Failure: the invariant regresses (stale Loom bundle, parser skew after a vault change) and no signal fires; consumers of Loom and VisionClaw are served silently wrong ontology counts — the exact defect that motivated the decision — and the designated forcing event never occurs.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-06 — Governance decisions cannot be proven applied, and egress bypasses the journal
- Topics: VF-08.4
- Evidence: fan-out is a planner gated by MESH_FEDERATED_KINDS (mesh.rs:185, mesh.rs:59) with "Deferred outbound connector and accept-path joins"; "Relay acknowledgement does not prove external application"; "Complete correlated applied-decision proof remains open"; the agentbox journal "Does not mediate all tools or nightly egress". These are drawn as current state, with no marker.
- Failure: an operator treats a decision as enacted on fan-out/relay-ack evidence when it was never applied remotely (or was applied differently); tool invocations and nightly egress outside the journal leave no audit record. The governance plane runs unauditable in production.
- Confidence: high (stated as implemented seams, not plans)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — Fixture parity is unenforceable in CI, and deletion does not count as drift
- Topics: VF-05.5, VF-05.6, VF-07.3
- Evidence: the canonical sibling checkout is continue-on-error because "no deploy key or PAT on this runner" (fixture-drift.yml:81); without it the gate exits 1 "nothing was compared" unless waived (fixture-drift.yml:149) and "every fixture-touching PR fails this gate deliberately" (fixture-drift.yml:19, DIVERGENCE); the script's default canonical path "was REMOVED... Unqualified runs therefore exit 2, never 0" (check-fixture-drift.sh:82, :93); MISSING alone exits 0 (:337) — deleting a fixture file is not drift; the release generator runs the comparison but "consumers is always an empty array" (generate-release-manifest.sh:196).
- Failure: the parity that release qualification claims is never actually compared in CI; teams habitually waive or avoid the path; a consumer that deletes rather than edits a fixture never trips any gate; a "compared" manifest cannot say which consumers matched.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: mostly yes (DIVERGENCEs); the MISSING-exits-0 blind spot is unmarked

### F-08 — The gates that certify the gates never run in CI

Yet BASELINE-visionflow.md:192 states the graded table "is machine-checked".
- Failure: a regression in any gate script ships behind a green CI badge; the eighteen cited cells of the graded augmentation matrix and all seven fail-proof suites are verified only by whoever remembers to run them locally. The governing document's "machine-checked" claim is false on the hosted path.

- Severity: medium

The publication path has no browser gate at all — every blocking gate in VF-02.6 is browser-free by design" (VF-02.11 DRIFT); "on a runner without the checkout this axis is never actually measured on the publication path, yet the page still ships" (VF-02.8 DIVERGENCE); "no browser evaluator on either path. The mesh module is read by nothing — verified by grep" (ADR-2009...md:84).

- Failure: an engine fault or discarded working tree silently deletes governance history; an agent output can again bypass the human gate (direct push, non-draft PR) with nothing rejecting it; closeout decisions are made on a ledger whose completeness is unverifiable.
