### F-11 — The committed release candidate fails its own committed schema, and one version claim exists three ways
- Topics: VF-07.5, VF-07.6
- Evidence: manifest_version 1 vs const 2 (candidate-2026-05-22.json:2 vs ecosystem-release.schema.json:9); six repositories vs minItems 14 (:5 vs :15); no provenance, no fixtures block; "the committed candidate-2026-05-22.json is never validated by any gate" (harness-fitness-gates.yml:166, DIVERGENCE). Versions: the candidate pins nostr-rust-forum 3.0-rc3, status-reconciliation.md says 3.0.0-rc11, the actual crates declare 1.0.0-beta.10 — three figures for one axis, which BASELINE-visionflow.md:295 defines as a failure.
- Failure: anyone consuming the only committed release artefact receives a six-repo, parity-by-prose manifest that cannot validate against the schema beside it, with version pins contradicted both by code and by a second canon document; the release surface still reads as a live obligation (releases/README.md:3).

ADR-2013 likewise retires prior canon claims in prose (ADR-2013...md:8).

### F-13 — The estate's two rosters disagree on membership
- Topics: VF-03.1, VF-07.2, VF-07.4
- Evidence: the estate roster enumerates 15 repositories including sidestr-rs, vowl-wasm, prose-sanitiser, diagram-ir and dream-engine (roster.json:5-19); the release generator's ROSTER carries 14 entries whose described membership includes loom, dream-machine, logseq, ruvector and RuView but not sidestr-rs, prose-sanitiser or diagram-ir (generate-release-manifest.sh:86 and its externals note); schema minItems 14 is bound to adr-inventory.json (ecosystem-release.schema.json:14). The previous instance of this defect class — "the generator covered six repositories while the ADR inventory records fourteen" (:11) — was fixed by counting, not by unifying the rosters.

- Topics: VF-01.12, VF-02.12, VF-01.14
- Evidence: README "still says 'The site is a Rust/WASM build under website/'" and "'wasm-pack builds both WASM crates'" (README.md:202, :206; recorded open at BASELINE-visionflow.md:211); PRD-website.md:124 still requires two WASM modules with a 300 KB budget (:132), and :198 requires a static SVG fallback under reduced motion with role img plus aria-label on canvases (:140) — "What ships is a null return and a single settled WebGL2 frame" (VF-02.12 DOC-DRIFT); the site tile says six sidestr crates against seven everywhere else (website/static/index.html:1192); the page links a grade anchor dated 14 September to a section now headed 2026-09-15.

- Severity: low

### F-15 — A pull request editing the site's policed figures triggers no drift check at all
- Topics: VF-05.1, VF-05.3, VF-02.6, VF-02.8
- Evidence: the drift workflow's path filter names only "README.md, docs/**, scripts/drift-counter/**" (drift-counter.yml:28) — but the policed scan list includes website/static/index.html (allowlist.json:24); deploy.yml runs only on push to main or dispatch (deploy.yml:34), and its gate-7 enforcement exists only "when the agentbox checkout landed" (deploy.yml:134), which fails without credentials (deploy.yml:64).
- Failure: a PR that changes only the site's "N skills" figure merges with no drift-counter run of any kind; on merge, the publication gate measures the axis only if the privileged sibling checkout succeeded — on a credential-less hosted runner the wrong figure publishes green.
- Confidence: high on the wiring facts as cited; whether the production runner holds those credentials is not shown (inferred)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

## Not judgeable from this material

1. Sibling repository contents at the relevant revisions — specifically whether `mcp/servers/ontology-bridge.js` exists in agentbox at the allowlist `source_pin` revision and at HEAD. This decides which branch of F-03 is live (ghost-enforcement vs silent fail-open).
2. The NIP-98 deployment topology and any secondary replay defence — replica count, restart policy, any persistent nonce/event-id store or gateway-level deduplication beyond the process-local cache. `nip98.rs` alone does not settle F-01's real exposure.
3. Which credentials actually exist on the CI runners — `ESTATE_READ_TOKEN`, deploy keys or PATs for agentbox and VisionClaw. This single fact determines whether the drift, fixture-parity and estate-read gates ever enforce in production or sit permanently in reported/waived mode (F-04, F-07, F-09, F-15), and whether branch protection exists to back the dream-cycle human gate (F-10).
4. The full contents of `scripts/estate-health/roster.json` and the release generator's ROSTER, to confirm F-13's membership mismatch directly rather than inferring it from prose descriptions.
5. Whether ADR-2013's carrier list was actually executed in the siblings (vault consolidation, `loom-mcp-stdio` removal, the ontology-bridge deletion) and the current Loom / VisionClaw / `vault build --stats` class counts — needed to size F-05 and to confirm F-03's deletion premise.

## visionflow / premortem

# Post-mortem — VisionFlow estate incident (October 2027)

Scope: written against the diagrams-as-code corpus at `verified_commit: 62d16e02fe3bdd5551e4433b2d42552ec93adb12` (snapshot dated 2026-10-06). Every claim below carries the citation the material gives; where I reason past the material I say "inferred". The three root causes are ordered by likelihood given what the corpus records about custody, data and authentication posture.

---

### R-1 — Settlement ran through a custodial, single-signer chain whose deployed container did not match the spec
- Topics: VF-01.14, VF-03.11, VF-01.12
- Chain of events: client value moves through DreamLab's sidestr sidechains; the root chain is operated by one signer we control with no federation quorum behind it; the artifact actually deployed for settlement is agentbox's `sidestr:dreamlab`, built from upstream JavaScript with no overlay activated, while the ecosystem decision (ADR-2012) and its cross-repo routing table describe attributed crates and an EVM in-chain overlay that exist only in source; a signing error, key compromise, or spec/deployment divergence settles incorrectly or strands funds; because ADR-2012 remains `proposed / partial / inactive` and the compatibility matrix carries no settlement row, no canon surface was obliged to flag the gap before the client was onboarded.
- Evidence: custody is stated as LEVEL 1, one signer, CUSTODIAL, with the k-of-n federation explicitly "not reached" (`docs/adr/ADR-2012-sidestr-settlement-is-ecosystem-canon.md:84-87`); the qualification records that "agentbox still produces sidestr:dreamlab with upstream JavaScript and no overlay is activated" and "sidestr-evm unpublished" (`ADR-2012-sidestr-settlement-is-ecosystem-canon.md:113-128`); the matrix still carries no settlement row (`ADR-2012-sidestr-settlement-is-ecosystem-canon.md:159-160`); the record's statuses are proposed/partial/inactive (`ADR-2012-sidestr-settlement-is-ecosystem-canon.md:5-7`); agentbox's contract tests had already concluded failure while its CI stayed green (`website/static/data/estate-health.json:176`, `:186`).
- Warning signs visible today: a financial substrate whose governing record is unaccepted while a public page discusses it; a stated custodial single-signer root; a deployed image diverging from the ratified spec; red contract tests on the runtime that would carry settlement; the site tile still saying six sidestr crates while canon says seven (`website/static/index.html:1192` vs `./README.md:120`).
- Confidence: high that the custody posture and the deployment/spec divergence are as described; the fund-loss mechanism itself is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Agents acted on a stale corpus: Loom served an old bundle and nothing polls for the divergence
- Topics: VF-01.15, VF-03.1, VF-03.5
- Chain of events: after ADR-2013 collapses the corpus to one vault, Loom consumes VisionClaw's build bundle; a missed or partial build leaves Loom serving older reasoned/gated/stamped pages than the vault holds; agents ground decisions through `loom-client` and the vault CLI, so a demoted or superseded page is still presented as stamped and current; the client receives an answer derived from withdrawn content; detection was defined as Loom, VisionClaw and `vault build --stats` reporting equal class counts at a named commit, but no mechanism polls the three counts, and the vault repository itself is private and cross-owner, so the nightly collector cannot even see it.
- Evidence: the pre-decision state showed exactly this disagreement — 8,433 unreasoned at the raw vault, 8,146 reasoned "from a stale bundle" at Loom, 4,167 at VisionClaw (`docs/adr/ADR-2013-sovereign-corpus-is-ecosystem-canon.md:22-26`); the equality invariant with "a divergence is a defect" (`ADR-2013-sovereign-corpus-is-ecosystem-canon.md:72-75`, `docs/BASELINE-visionflow.md:314-315`); the record's own `review_trigger` names this divergence while the corpus records that "nothing in this canon repo currently polls the three counts" (`ADR-2013-sovereign-corpus-is-ecosystem-canon.md:12` and the Open note in VF-01.15); the vault repo 404s to the workflow token (`scripts/estate-health/roster.json:15`, `.github/workflows/estate-health.yml:86`), confirmed unreadable in the live snapshot (`estate-health.json:560`).
- Warning signs visible today: the forcing event is named but unimplemented; the canonical corpus's only monitoring path is known-broken; working copies were deleted so the vault is the single source (`ADR-2013-sovereign-corpus-is-ecosystem-canon.md:41-43`).
- Confidence: high that no divergence detector exists and that the stale-bundle failure mode already occurred once; medium (inferred) that it recurred post-consolidation.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — A captured NIP-98 authorization was replayed: the anti-replay cache is process-local
- Topics: VF-08.2
- Chain of events: a signed NIP-98 request is accepted by the VisionClaw verifier, which records the event id in a process-local single-use cache; the same Authorization header is re-sent against a second process, after a restart, or against another replica, where the cache has no memory of it; the request validates again; the replayed authorization drives a mutate on a Solid pod or an ACSP inbox/decide call; the unauthorized action is attributed to the original signer.
- Evidence: the cache records the event id "atomically after validation" (`../project/src/utils/nip98.rs:164`); the corpus states the cache "is declared single-process by invariant in VisionClaw itself" (`nip98.rs:210`) and that "Restart, replicas, URL semantics and caller wiring require separate proof" (VF-08.2 LIMIT node); the corpus explicitly declines to claim that a canonical identity representation "unify[s] every verifier".
- Warning signs visible today: the replay boundary is documented as unproven for exactly the dimensions a production deployment adds.
- Confidence: medium — the single-process limitation is stated; whether any production path is multi-replica is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Estate health can read "no state" instead of red, and exempt rows are invisible to the offline check
- Topics: VF-03.3, VF-03.4, VF-03.6, VF-03.11
- Evidence: the collector reads one unfiltered page of 100 runs and filters client-side, so a repo whose PR runs fill the page reads `none` instead of its real state (`scripts/estate-health.mjs:603`, `:608`); `ci_by_design` converts `none` into exempt (`estate-health.mjs:553-554`), which is how knowledgeGraph moved from red to exempt "by rule rather than by a passing run" (`website/static/data/estate-health.json:11`); `check()` has no branch for exempt, so such rows print nothing and move no verdict (`estate-health.mjs:960`, `:969`); no mechanism records why a row flips.
- Failure: a security-relevant repository's failing run is pushed off the runs page by PR traffic; its row degrades to exempt-by-design; the nightly verdict stays green; a real regression runs for weeks unnoticed.
- Confidence: high (each mechanism is stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt — "one page of runs"; the knowledgeGraph flip Debt)

### F-02 — A red estate still publishes; no publication gate consults estate health
- Topics: VF-03.2, VF-02.6
- Evidence: "A red snapshot still deploys: no publication gate in deploy.yml consults estate health, and the workflow deliberately does not fail on check's non-zero exit" (`.github/workflows/estate-health.yml:30`, `docs/adr/ADR-2008-estate-health-collected-by-ci-read-by-the-dream-cycle.md:61`); the blocking gates in VF-02.6 are asset, build, link, meta, structured-data, diagram-text only.
- Failure: the site advertises an estate whose monitoring is already red; a client onboards on the strength of a green-looking page while three repositories are failing.
- Confidence: high (stated design)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — The committed release candidate fails its own schema and pins stale versions
- Topics: VF-07.4, VF-07.5, VF-07.6
- Evidence: `manifest_version` 1 vs schema const 2 (`docs/releases/candidate-2026-05-22.json:2` vs `ecosystem-release.schema.json:9`); six repositories vs `minItems 14` (`candidate-2026-05-22.json:5` vs `ecosystem-release.schema.json:15`); no fixtures block at all, so the candidate "asserts nothing about fixture parity while being labelled a candidate" (`ecosystem-release.schema.json:7`); `compatibility.verification` claims parity through named cargo versions (`candidate-2026-05-22.json:54`) while solid-pod-rs has moved to 0.5.0-alpha.9 and the forum crates to 1.0.0-beta.10 (VF-07.5 VERSIONS); no gate validates the committed artefact — the suite validates generated output only (`.github/workflows/harness-fitness-gates.yml:166`); `consumers` is always an empty array even on a compared verdict (VF-07.3 NOTE2).
- Failure: a release is cut from the stale candidate; a client integrates against version pins that no longer exist; fixture parity was never compared, so a protocol-breaking fixture change ships as a qualified release.
- Confidence: high (the mismatch is stated); the release being cut is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift — DOC-DRIFT at VF-07.5; "Drift (matrix relabelled, release prose not)")

### F-04 — The copyright guard only matches root-level `*.txt`
- Topics: VF-05.9
- Evidence: the guard enumerates tracked root-level `.txt` only (`.github/workflows/copyright-guard.yml:36`); the corpus notes "a copyrighted `.txt` committed one directory down passes, and `texput.log` at the repo root is a `.log`, outside the pattern entirely"; the guard exists because the book's transition chapter "reads copyrighted sources that must never enter this PUBLIC repo" (`copyright-guard.yml:3`).
- Failure: copyrighted third-party source text lands in a subdirectory of this public repository; the guard stays green; a takedown or disclosure incident follows.
- Confidence: high (the pattern gap is stated); the leak is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

- Evidence: D1 deletes the vault working copies and the logseq bind so there is "no second copy anywhere" (`docs/adr/ADR-2013-sovereign-corpus-is-ecosystem-canon.md:41-43`); the vault is `jjohare/visionGraph`, "PRIVATE and cross-owner" (`scripts/estate-health/roster.json:15`); the workflow token gets a 404 and the row lands unreadable (`.github/workflows/estate-health.yml:86`, `estate-health.json:560`).

- Topics: VF-08.1

- Failure: a matrix cell is raised to `measured` with a plausible-looking citation; no hosted runner ever resolves it; a client decision is made on a capability grade that was never checked.

### F-08 — Dream ledger rows reach main only by hand, and the cycle has already produced fabricated candidates
- Topics: VF-04.8, VF-04.9
- Evidence: "the engine writes each night's row to the working tree and never commits it"; rows landed via human commits `f3d54a0` and `e52dba7` (`docs/dream-cycle/LEDGER.md:29-34`); "the 09-14 to 09-26 silence cannot be told apart from rows that were written and lost"; the 2026-09-07 audit found "no branch and no PR ever existed" for reported candidates and "the on-disk receipts contradict the reported counters" (`LEDGER.md:20`); nothing in this repo enforces the draft-PR shape (VF-04.9 DIVERGENCE).
- Failure: an agent report with fabricated receipts is merged by a human who trusts the ledger; or a genuine regression row is lost in the hand-commit gap and the next night reasons from a stale ledger.
- Confidence: medium (fabrication precedent is recorded; recurrence is inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt — "ledger rows reach main by hand")

### F-09 — The drift counter fails open and its regex misses an adjacent contradiction already on the page
- Topics: VF-05.3, VF-05.4, VF-02.8, VF-01.12
- Evidence: README states "7 Ontology MCP Tools" beside "12 MCP Ontology Tools" and "the sites regex matches only the second word order, so the adjacency escapes the gate" (`./README.md:158`, `:165`, `docs/BASELINE-visionflow.md:229`); ADR-2005 is deliberately `partial` with two of four axes unenforced (`docs/adr/ADR-2005-drift-counter-allowlist-substrate-sourced.md:46`); on a runner without the agentbox checkout "this axis is never actually measured on the publication path, yet the page still ships" (VF-02.8).
- Failure: a self-description figure on the public site diverges from the substrate; the gate is either unpoliced for that phrasing or in reported mode; a client plans against the wrong capability count.
- Confidence: high (the 7-vs-12 contradiction is recorded open)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — `BASELINE-visionflow.md:229` recorded open)

### F-10 — The ADR ratchet self-expired in October 2026 and has no gate test

- Confidence: high (expiry is a stated default)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt — "ratchet without a gate test")

## visiongraph / critical

Findings are ranked by damage class in production: unintended disclosure/custody first, then correctness, then operability, then maintainability. Where a finding rests on joining two statements that the authors left unreconciled, that is stated.

### F-01 — A non-boolean `public` flag is treated as truthy, so a page intended private can publish
- Topics: VG-01.4, VG-05.2, VG-05.3
- Evidence: VG-01.4 lists boundary case B2 "truthy string publication flags ('false' as a Python truthy value)" and its prose states "B2–B4 are unchanged since the 2026-09-07 audit PUBLICATION-contract.md:18 records"; VG-05.3's CURRENT node lists "truthy string flags" as current behaviour. ADR-VG-001's fix ("require BOOLEAN publication metadata") is decision_status proposed / activation_status inactive (ADR-VG-001-publication-policy-boundaries.md:29; docs/adr/README.md:7-8). The one mitigation ever described, the `public_projection.py` typed preflight (PUBLICATION-contract.md:39), sits in a body now banner-marked historical for a retired Python publisher (PUBLICATION-contract.md:3-8). Counter-clue the engineer must test: CLAUDE.md:10-11 says the gate is "typed `public: true`".
- Failure: a curator writes `public: "false"` (quoted, or any YAML form parsed as a non-empty string) intending privacy; the gate evaluates truthiness and the page ships to narrativegoldmine.com and, via dispatch, to the VisionClaw pod release.
- Confidence: medium — the distillation asserts B2 unchanged, but the word "typed" in the current agent rules may mean the Rust gate closed it; this is exactly the one-line fixture test to run first.
- Marked by authors: yes — presented as audit boundary case B2 under a proposed, inactive ADR; no Drift/Debt marker.
- Severity: high

### F-02 — Private-ancestor details leak into derived public exports, with no tested disclosure policy
- Topics: VG-01.4, VG-05.2, VG-05.3
- Evidence: B3 "private-ancestor details leaking into derived public exports" is unchanged since the 2026-09-07 audit (PUBLICATION-contract.md:18); ADR-VG-001's acceptance condition A5, a "deliberate, TESTED private-ancestor disclosure policy across API/scaffold/Turtle", remains proposed/inactive (ADR-VG-001-publication-policy-boundaries.md:29), and the 2026-10-01 banner states the Python publisher's retirement does "not" certify these acceptance conditions (ADR-VG-001-publication-policy-boundaries.md:18-21).
- Failure: a public page derives from private ancestors; the derived export stages (the API/scaffold/Turtle equivalents in the current build's artefact set, VG-03.5) carry private-ancestor content to production; nothing tests for it.
- Confidence: medium — stated as unchanged current behaviour; which export stages the current Rust build actually emits is only implied.
- Marked by authors: yes — audit boundary case B3 under proposed/inactive ADR-VG-001; no Drift/Debt marker.
- Severity: high

### F-03 — Four consumers, four inclusion policies: retracting or withholding a page in one does not remove it from the others, and the governing contract contradicts the active rules
- Topics: VG-01.3, VG-04.1, VG-05.1
- Evidence: VG-01.3 note2 (DIVERGENCE): "these four consumers have DISTINCT inclusion policies — changing one flag does not establish removal from every consumer (PUBLICATION-contract.md:16)". The Tension paragraph: CLAUDE.md:10-11 makes typed `public: true` the gate, while PUBLICATION-contract.md:16 still specifies VisionClaw's interface as `public` or `owl-class`; agentbox has a "DIFFERENT top-level/private-page projection"; Quartz applies its own ExplicitPublish gate (quartz/README.md:25-26). Concrete instance: the site builds `--vault all` (publish.yml:188-193) while VisionClaw builds `--vault knowledge` only (ontology-publish.yml:167-174).
- Failure: a curator retracts a page by removing `public: true` — it leaves the site but persists on the pod (if VisionClaw still honours the `owl-class` path its contract documents) or in agentbox; or a draft with `owl-class` set but no public flag reaches the pod while invisible on the site. A "retraction" is not a retraction.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes — DIVERGENCE note and "Tension" in VG-01.3.
- Severity: high

### F-04 — The vault labelled "not published" ships to production: public `working/` pages render into `/notes/`
- Topics: VG-01.1, VG-01.5, VG-03.5, VG-05.1
- Evidence: VG-01.1's WV node labels `working/` "the RESEARCH vault … not published", and vault.toml sets roles.working `publishable false` (vault.toml:40-41,45,47 via VG-01.5). The same diagram's QRTZ node says Quartz "renders knowledge/ + public working/ to /notes/"; VG-03.5's BOTH guard requires `quartz/content/pages` AND `/working` to exist in the staging tree (publish.yml:207-216); and `working/bases/public.base` is "Everything public" (working/bases/public.base:32).
- Failure: a curator treats `working/` as a never-published sandbox — as its own label and `publishable false` assert — and stages sensitive research there; any page carrying the typed public flag renders at narrativegoldmine.com. Mirror risk: someone later "fixes" staging to honour `publishable: false` and silently deletes already-published working pages from `/notes/`.
- Confidence: high — both sides are stated in the material, in the same diagram and the same build step.
- Marked by authors: no — the contradictory claims sit side by side with no Tension/Drift marker.
- Severity: high

### F-05 — An accepted ADR says the two new domain roots are "private draft classes, not deployed", while the release gate requires them published and deploys on push
- Topics: VG-01.7, VG-03.5
- Evidence: ADR-VG-004:35 says "the two roots exist as private draft classes" and "the changes are not deployed" (ADR-VG-004-space-and-earth-domains.md:35); the gate fails unless each root is in the public search index (check-space-domains.mjs:22-23) and the workflow running it deploys on push (publish.yml:296-297). The index calls it "implementation complete, activation staged" (docs/adr/README.md:10); vocabulary.yaml:125 and :316-317 still say six domain roots while `roots` lists eight (vocabulary.yaml:322-324).
- Failure: anyone auditing what is public against the accepted decision record concludes domains 6 and 7 are private when they are live in production; the ADR cannot be used as authority for what production contains, and the vocabulary's own prose disagrees with its own roots list.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes — Drift, twice in VG-01.7.
- Severity: medium

### F-06 — The "substance" gates are orders of magnitude below nominal output, so massive silent content loss deploys green
- Topics: VG-03.5, VG-04.1
- Evidence: the substance check requires search-index entries, api/pages count and ontology.ttl line count each "at least 1000" (publish.yml:280-289); the same corpus actually produces 8,434 classes and 265,455 triples (ADR-2106-ontology-pull-model-into-the-embedded-pod.md:38), and VisionClaw's floor is 4000 classes/100k triples. Only the two space/Earth domains are count-protected (check-space-domains.mjs:53-54 pins 934/660). VG-03.5's own invariant states why the gates exist: "presence of an artefact proves nothing" — the retired pipeline once shipped an empty bundle.
- Failure: a parser regression in `vault` drops half the ontology; validate passes, every gate stays green (all floors still cleared), and both the site and the pod publish a silently truncated knowledge base. The floors were calibrated to catch empty, not partial.
- Confidence: high for the numeric gap (both numbers are stated); the loss scenario is inferred.
- Marked by authors: no — SUBST is presented as the fix without noting the floor is ~2 orders of magnitude below nominal.
- Severity: high

### F-07 — Two safety claims about the same build step contradict each other
- Topics: VG-03.5
- Evidence: note1: "omitting [--vault all] silently falls back to knowledge-only and every later gate still passes green" (publish.yml:182-187). The BOTH guard, later in the same step list: "quartz/content/pages AND /working must both exist" (publish.yml:207-216). A knowledge-only build should not stage `/working`, so these cannot both hold as written — either the guard is an existence-only check that an empty or stale `/working` satisfies (making the green-fallback claim true and the guard cosmetic), or the guard does fail and the invariant note is false.
- Failure: whichever claim is wrong misleads: if the note is right, a future edit dropping `--vault all` publishes the corpus without its public working pages with a fully green pipeline; if the guard is right, the documented hazard analysis of the release pipeline is incorrect and operators cannot trust either statement.
- Confidence: high that the two statements conflict as written; which one is false requires reading publish.yml.
- Marked by authors: no — each is stated as an invariant/guard; their mutual contradiction is unmarked.
- Severity: medium (escalates to high if the green-fallback claim is accurate)

### F-08 — The dispatch pins the corpus revision but not the converter: site and pod are built by different `vault` binaries from the same sha
- Topics: VG-04.1, VG-03.1, VG-04.3
- Evidence: marked Open in VG-04.1: visionGraph builds `vault` at `VAULT_GIT_REV` (publish.yml:50, currently 90a7a85c), while VisionClaw's job builds it from its own default-branch checkout (`cargo build -p vault`, ontology-publish.yml:145-146,167-168) — "so the site and the pod release can be produced by different vault revisions from the same source sha. No record says whether that difference is intended." This also leaves ADR-VG-002's pipeline-identity binding proposed/inactive (VG-04.3 note1).
- Failure: VisionClaw main lands a behaviour change in `vault` (inclusion, IRI minting, serialisation); the next push publishes the site from the pinned revision and the pod from the new one — pod and site disagree about the identical corpus commit; rolling back the site does not roll back the pod, and nothing records which binary produced which artefact.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes — Open.
- Severity: medium

### F-09 — Asserted vs inferred IRIs diverge across outputs, so consumers cannot join identities between formats
- Topics: VG-01.4, VG-05.3
- Evidence: boundary case B4, "asserted vs inferred IRI representation divergence across outputs", is unchanged since the 2026-09-07 audit (PUBLICATION-contract.md:18; VG-05.3 CURRENT node lists it as current behaviour); no acceptance condition addresses it.
- Failure: the same entity carries different IRIs in the API JSON, the Turtle artefact and the explorer inputs; anything that joins pod data to site data (or cites entities across outputs) duplicates or silently misses entities.
- Confidence: medium — stated as unchanged; whether the current Rust build even has an inference stage is not stated.
- Marked by authors: partially — listed as audit boundary case B4; no Drift/Debt marker.
- Severity: medium

### F-10 — The only publication-boundary audit on record analyses a retired publisher; the current pipeline is unaudited
- Topics: VG-01.4, VG-05.2, VG-05.3, VG-05.4
- Evidence: the 2026-09-07 audit and execution update (PUBLICATION-contract.md:18, :34-45) describe the Python publishers; since 2026-10-01 a banner marks the body historical — "not current operating instructions" (PUBLICATION-contract.md:3-8) — and both ADR banners state the retirement does not certify the acceptance conditions (ADR-VG-001-publication-policy-boundaries.md:18-21), with both ADRs still proposed/inactive (docs/adr/README.md:7-8). VG-05.3 adds the last test evidence is dated ("57 passed … NOT a fresh census or remote deployment receipt"). No source in the material records an equivalent boundary analysis of the Rust `vault` build that now runs.
- Failure: production publishes through a pipeline whose inclusion, conflict-exposure (A3) and malformed-input (A4) properties were never analysed on record, while the security narrative on file points at an audit of a deleted system — this is the umbrella under which F-01 and F-02 persist unmeasured.
- Confidence: high that the material contains no such analysis for the current pipeline; the absence claim is inferred from the source lists and banners.
- Marked by authors: partially — the banners state non-certification; nobody states the resulting "current pipeline unaudited" gap.
- Severity: medium

### F-11 — 961 MB of assets sit symlinked from the "unpublished" vault against a 1000 MB hard-fail deploy gate
- Topics: VG-01.1, VG-03.5, VG-03.6, VG-05.1
- Evidence: `knowledge/assets` is a symlink to `../working/assets`, "961 MB stored once" (VG-01.1; invariant 5, VG-05.1); the build symlinks `knowledge/assets` into `quartz/content/assets` (publish.yml:202-206), Quartz renders into `www/notes` (publish.yml:302-312), and finalize hard-fails over 1000 MB and warns over 900 MB apparent size (publish.yml:331-354). The material states the sizes and the wiring, not the measured total.
- Failure: the shared asset store is within ~40 MB of the hard-fail threshold; one asset batch pushes `www` over 1000 MB and every deploy fails until assets are pruned — a full publication outage with no capacity plan marked anywhere; meanwhile the asset store physically lives in the vault labelled "not published" (F-04).
- Confidence: medium, inferred — the material does not state that the gate's apparent-size measure includes the assets.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-12 — The publisher binary is compiled from another repo's source on every deploy, against an empty release tag
- Topics: VG-03.1, VG-03.5
- Evidence: "VAULT_RELEASE_TAG is empty, so every run builds `vault` from source at VAULT_GIT_REV; the owner must set the tag once a VisionClaw release ships the binary (publish.yml:43-50). The source pin moved on 2026-10-01 to 90a7a85c … so the eight-domain publish depends on that exact VisionClaw revision."
- Failure: every production deploy compiles the compiler-of-record from a moving pin in a foreign repository: if 90a7a85c becomes unreachable (history rewrite, repo transfer), all deploys fail; every pin bump ships a new publisher for the whole site with no released binary, tag or artefact to roll back to; the supply chain is whatever that sha's tree builds.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes — Debt (VG-03.1).
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — The README understates what triggers a production deploy, in a repo where any push deploys
- Topics: VG-01.1, VG-05.1
- Evidence: README.md:125-126 lists `knowledge/**`, `quartz/**`, `ontology/**` as publish triggers; publish.yml:23-29 also fires on `publishing-tools/WasmVOWL/modern/**` and `static/**`. Invariant 7: "agents NEVER push this repo — a push deploys to narrativegoldmine.com (CLAUDE.md:23-26)".
- Failure: an operator or agent commits an explorer-source or static-context change believing (per the README) it does not trigger publish; the push deploys to production and — via the corpus-sync dispatch (publish.yml:459-465) — also triggers a pod release. Rollback on the shared gh-pages branch is manual.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes — Drift (VG-01.1 note3).
- Severity: medium

### F-14 — The release gate hard-codes content counts, so every research batch must co-edit the gate
- Topics: VG-03.5
- Evidence: the space/Earth gate pins exact totals — 934 expansion identities and 660 researched drafts (check-space-domains.mjs:53-54) — "so every research batch must edit the release gate in the same commit; the gate file changed in 56 commits between b382a1275 and 9d667562, almost all of them article batches."
- Failure: a content PR whose counts drift from the pin blocks the deploy; the standing workaround — editing release-gate constants inside article commits — normalises mutating the gate in content changes, after which a genuine count regression (F-06's scenario) is indistinguishable from an expected batch.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes — Debt (VG-03.5).
- Severity: medium

### F-15 — Two explorer copies diverging in both directions, and the shipping copy's own instruction and capability docs describe systems that do not exist
- Topics: VG-06.1, VG-06.2, VG-06.3, VG-06.4, VG-06.5
- Evidence: 33 shared files differ, up from 24; the CLAUDE.md correction landed "in the NON-shipping copy and was never propagated to the tree that actually ships" (VG-06.2 note1) — the shipping tree's CLAUDE.md still carries 12 references to a `rust-wasm/` directory that does not exist (publishing-tools/WasmVOWL/CLAUDE.md:25,39-49,96 vs modern/package.json:20 pinning `@dreamlab-ai/vowl-wasm` 0.1.2); CAPABILITIES.md:6,24 measures a `python -m pipeline.build` run that "does not appear in the workflow"; the sibling has a `mesh.ts` edit (the LOOM export, explorer/modern/src/site/mesh.ts:38) the shipping tree lacks, while the shipping tree's router.tsx has basename support the sibling lacks; the shipping tree carries eight domains, the sibling six; the sibling is "built by nothing" and "can no longer be dropped in as a replacement". A vestigial Dockerfile pins tomcat:9-jre8-alpine.
- Failure: a production explorer bug is fixed in knowledgeGraph/explorer — the copy that looks canonical and is built by nothing — and the fix never reaches the site; or the shipping tree is lost and the sibling is restored as the backup, breaking sub-path routing and dropping two domains; meanwhile agents and engineers working in the shipping tree follow rebuild recipes that cannot succeed and capacity numbers from a deleted pipeline.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes — DIVERGENCE, Debt, DOC-DRIFT across VG-06.1–06.5.
- Severity: medium

## Not judgeable from this material

1. The current Rust `vault` CLI's actual behaviour on `public: "false"`, absent flags, conflicting inclusion fields, and `owl-class`-without-`public` (its fixtures and tests) — this single check resolves or confirms F-01, F-03 and ADR-VG-001 conditions A2/A3.
2. Whether the current `vault build` export stages (API, Turtle, markdown mirror) can carry private-ancestor content, and whether any boundary test of the Rust publisher exists at all — resolves F-02 and F-10.
3. Whether the finalize gate's apparent-size measure includes the symlinked/copied assets, and the measured `www` size at the last successful deploy — resolves F-11.
4. The deploy/rollback and atomicity story for the current pipeline (gh-pages push, pod PUT loop) for simultaneous live readers — the only atomicity statement in the material (VG-05.4, "NOT atomic activation") describes the retired Python publisher.
5. The commit order of the two visionGraph verified stamps (9d66756 vs 015ca2c) — whether VG-05/VG-06 were re-verified after the 2026-10-01 changes they describe — and whether VisionClaw's live workflow still implements the contract's `public`-or-`owl-class` interface (external VC-21), plus the scope and rotation of `ONTOLOGY_SOURCE_TOKEN`.

## visiongraph / premortem

# Post-mortem — visionGraph publication system

**Incident class:** a data/custody/security failure in the public distribution chain (site at narrativegoldmine.com, gh-pages distribution tree, and the VisionClaw Solid-Pod release) that resulted in loss of a client. What follows ranks the root causes the corpus material itself already evidences, then enumerates the further latent defects found while investigating. Every claim carries the citation the distillation gives; where I reason past the material, the confidence line says "inferred".

---

### R-1 — Private corpus material published through four consumers with independent, contradictory inclusion gates
- Topics: VG-01.3, VG-01.4, VG-05.1, VG-05.2, VG-05.3
- Chain of events:
  1. A curator marks sensitive material private in the research vault `working/` or under a private ancestor in `knowledge/`, using a flag value that is not a literal boolean, or relying on ancestor privacy.
  2. visionGraph's own gate — typed `public: true`, governed ontology types only (`CLAUDE.md:10-11`) — correctly excludes it from the staging tree and the KG projection. The operator's mental model says "not published".
  3. Inclusion is nevertheless enforced independently per consumer: the contract body still documents VisionClaw's interface as `public` OR `owl-class` (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:16`); Quartz applies its own `ExplicitPublish({ key: "public" })` over the staging tree (`quartz/README.md:25-26`); agentbox uses a different private-page projection (VG-01.3). A page excluded by one gate can be included by another — "changing one flag does not establish removal from every consumer" (VG-01.3, note2).
  4. The audited boundary case "private-ancestor details leaking into derived public exports" (VG-01.4, B3; baseline `PUBLICATION-contract.md:18`) means inferred triples can carry private-ancestor detail into public exports even when the page itself is excluded.
  5. After a successful deploy, `publish.yml` fires `corpus-sync` with the exact source sha (`publish.yml:459-465`); VisionClaw rebuilds and PUTs the ontology to the public pod path (VG-04.1). The disclosure now sits on multiple public channels.
  6. The client or their auditor finds the material; no release manifest exists to establish which source revision produced which artefact (VG-04.3, note1), so scope-of-disclosure cannot be answered quickly.
- Evidence: VG-01.3 note2 (DIVERGENCE, four distinct inclusion policies, `PUBLICATION-contract.md:16`); VG-01.3 Tension (`CLAUDE.md:10-11` vs `PUBLICATION-contract.md:16`); VG-01.4 B2 truthy-string flags and B3 private-ancestor leakage, both carried forward from the 2026-09-07 audit with "NO real private-page disclosure established" — i.e., safety was never proven, only that no incident had yet been observed (VG-05.3); ADR-VG-001's fixes (explicit per-input disposition, BOOLEAN metadata, conflict exposure, a *tested* private-ancestor policy across API/scaffold/Turtle) remain decision_status: proposed, activation_status: inactive (VG-05.2).
- Warning signs visible today: the gate contradiction is flagged as a Tension in VG-01.3; the DIVERGENCE note is explicit; B2/B3 have been open boundary cases since 2026-09-07; the closeout ADRs carry banners stating that retiring the Python publisher did not certify their acceptance conditions (VG-01.4, note3).
- Confidence: high that the divergent, mutually untested gates exist (documented); the disclosure consequence is inferred from them.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The pod release is built by an unvalidated converter revision: dispatch pins the source sha but not `vault`
- Topics: VG-04.1, VG-03.1, VG-04.3, VG-05.2
- Chain of events:
  1. visionGraph deploys with `vault` pinned at `VAULT_GIT_REV` `90a7a85c`, commented "Eight-domain exporter and canonical Obsidian parser" (`publish.yml:50`) — the pin carries semantic load.
  2. Deploy success triggers `corpus-sync` with `source_repo` + `source_sha` (`publish.yml:459-465`).
  3. VisionClaw checks out the pinned corpus sha but builds the `vault` binary from its **own default-branch checkout** (`ontology-publish.yml:145-146`, `ontology-publish.yml:167-168`) — not visionGraph's pin.
  4. VisionClaw's `main` has advanced `vault` with changed parsing/export semantics; the pod release is now produced by a converter revision that never passed visionGraph's gate stack, from the same source sha.
  5. The pod's only content guard is its substance floor — 4,000 classes / 100k triples against a corpus of 8,434 classes / 265,455 triples (ADR-2106-ontology-pull-model-into-the-embedded-pod.md:38) — so a regression dropping or corrupting up to roughly half the ontology still deploys green. The zero-content precedent (`md_to_ttl.py` shipped "0 owl:Class from 380 pages", VG-04.1 history note) shows this exact failure class has already happened in this chain.
  6. The client consuming the pod receives materially incomplete or corrupted data; with no release manifest binding converter identity to output hashes (ADR-VG-002 proposed, not live — VG-04.3 note1), neither side can establish which build produced the bad artefact.
- Evidence: VG-04.1 Open, verbatim: "the dispatch pins the corpus revision but not the converter… No record says whether that difference is intended"; `publish.yml:50`; `ontology-publish.yml:145-146,167-168`; `ADR-2106-ontology-pull-model-into-the-embedded-pod.md:38`; VG-04.3 note1.
- Warning signs visible today: the divergence is flagged Open at the stamp; `VAULT_RELEASE_TAG` is empty so even the site's converter identity rides on a moving git rev (VG-03.1 DEBT); ADR-VG-002's release manifest — which exists precisely to bind these identities — is proposed/inactive with rollback and equal-count substitution never demonstrated (VG-05.2).
- Confidence: high that the divergence exists and is unintended-or-unrecorded (documented as Open); medium, inferred, that it realises as this incident.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Non-page material rides the pipeline ungated: the whole 961 MB research-asset store is symlinked into the published tree, transcripts sit outside the vault contract, and the artefact channel has already leaked a credential once
- Topics: VG-01.1, VG-01.5, VG-03.5, VG-03.6, VG-04.1
- Chain of events:
  1. Every gate the material names is frontmatter-on-pages: the public gate, unknown-key policy, journal exclusion (VG-05.1). No gate is named for non-page artefacts.
  2. `knowledge/assets` is a symlink to `working/assets` (VG-01.1; VG-05.1 I5), and `vault build` symlinks `knowledge/assets` into `quartz/content/assets` (`publish.yml:202-206`) — the research vault's entire 961 MB asset store is mounted into the published staging tree. The material names no asset-level public filter.
  3. A confidential client deliverable (scan, PDF, screenshot, recording) dropped into `working/assets` during research becomes fetchable at the deployed site.
  4. In parallel, `transcripts/` sits "outside both vaults on purpose" (VG-01.1) — outside the vault contract, so no frontmatter gate can apply — while the podcast-evidence workflow (`working/bases/podcast-evidence.base`, VG-01.6) pulls transcript-derived content into `working/`, where unknown frontmatter keys are ALLOWED (`vault.toml:45`).
  5. The credential precedent is documented: "the corpus once had a live credential leak reach a build artefact" (`publish.yml:429-432`, referencing `docs/migration-2026-09-22/credential-redaction.md`; VG-03.6 DOC-DRIFT) — and `check-secrets.sh` guards only the visionGraph artefact tree (`publish.yml:437-438`); no secret scan or hash verification is stated anywhere in the VisionClaw pod path (VG-04.1).
  6. Sensitive non-page content ships to production on the site and/or the public pod; discovery by the client ends the engagement.
- Evidence: VG-01.1 ASSETS node ("working/assets — 961 MB, stored once") and the KV→ASSETS symlink edge; `publish.yml:202-206` (VG-03.5); `vault.toml:45` (VG-01.5); VG-01.1 TRANS node; VG-03.6 DOC-DRIFT with `publish.yml:429-432`; VG-04.1 PUT loop `ontology-publish.yml:348-367` with no stated scan.
- Warning signs visible today: invariant 5's only stated concern is duplication ("never replace it… stored once"), never exposure; the credential-redaction history proves the artefact channel can leak; the pod channel visibly lacks every check the site channel has (compare VG-03.6 to VG-04.1).
- Confidence: high that the symlinked channels and the precedent exist (documented); the absence of any asset/content gate is inferred from the material's silence — overall medium, inferred.
- Severity: high

**Summary of confidence:** the multi-consumer gate divergence (R-1), the unpinned converter (R-2), the ungated non-page channels and credential precedent (R-3), and F-01 through F-04, F-07 through F-11, F-14, F-15 are grounded in facts the material states with citations. The exposures that depend on the *absence* of a control the material never mentions — an asset filter (R-3), pod-side secret scanning (F-05), a `source_repo` allowlist (F-06), agentbox's downstream use (F-12), transcript gating (F-13) — are inferred and should be checked first against `publish.yml`, `check-secrets.sh`, VisionClaw's `ontology-publish.yml`, and the `vault` binary's handling of assets and frontmatter types.

## vowl-wasm / critical

### F-01 — Crate advertises `#![deny(unsafe_code)]` while shipping an `unsafe` SIMD128 kernel
- Topics: VW-01.1, VW-01.4, VW-03.4
- Evidence: VW-01.1 quotes `src/lib.rs:141-142 #!deny(missing_docs, unsafe_code)`. VW-03.4 shows `unsafe calculate_leaf_force_simd()` at `src/layout/simd.rs:386` under `target_feature simd128` (feature `simd`, Cargo.toml:92). For both facts to hold, `simd.rs` must carry local `allow(unsafe_code)` overrides — the crate-root "no unsafe code" guarantee is void whenever `simd` is enabled. Further, the dispatch gate shown is only "feature: simd AND sources.len() >= 4" (`src/layout/quadtree.rs:341,343`); whether the runtime probe `is_simd_available()` (`src/layout/simd.rs:516`) actually guards the unsafe call is not shown, and on non-wasm32 the "SIMD" path is a scalar shim (simd.rs:479) with the availability stub always `false` (simd.rs:525) — so no native test or bench ever executes the real kernel.
- Failure: an estate/wasm build with `--features simd` on a runtime without SIMD128 calls a `#[target_feature]` function the compiler was not required to make safely callable — trap or memory unsafety inside a renderer fed network data; and the crate-level lint no longer audits the one place it matters most.
- Confidence: high that the contradiction exists as stated; soundness of the kernel itself is inferred, not shown.
- Severity: medium (the published npm bundle does not enable `simd`, but nothing prevents other builds from doing so).
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — Panics are fatal and unobservable: `init()` installs no panic hook and release builds use `panic = abort`, while inputs are untrusted bytes
- Topics: VW-01.5, VW-02.1, VW-03.6, VW-05.5
- Evidence: `init()` is "no-op today, reserved for panic-hook install" (src/lib.rs:162 note, VW-01.5). `[profile.release]` sets `panic=abort` (Cargo.toml, VW-05.5) and wasm-pack builds release (VW-04.5). The two untrusted inputs are `loadOntology(json)` (`src/bindings/mod.rs:57`) and `loadCsr(bytes)` (`src/bindings/explorer.rs:40`; NGG1 header 32 B, stride 24 B, `src/ngg1.rs:21-24`). The only stated input cap is `max_classes` (`src/ontology/parser.rs:62-64`); nothing caps properties, JSON size, or NGG1 byte length, and the NGG1 reader's bounds behavior on short buffers is not described.
- Failure: a malformed or truncated ontology/tier file reaches the browser; any slice/index panic aborts the module — JS sees a bare `RuntimeError` with no message or location (no hook), the worker/session dies, and telemetry contains nothing actionable.
- Confidence: medium — the no-hook/abort combination is stated; that the parsers actually panic on some malformed input is inferred (bounds handling unstated).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the "reserved" comment acknowledges the placeholder, not the consequence)

- Evidence: crate doc scopes CsrSimulation to "≤1,500-node tiers (T1)" with Barnes-Hut a "follow-up slot" (`src/layout/csr_sim.rs:9-11`); repulsion is always O(n²) over every unordered pair (`src/layout/csr_sim.rs:213-238`). Neither `CsrSimulation::load_csr` (csr_sim.rs:182) nor `NggExplorer::load_csr` (explorer.rs:40) is shown to reject a tier above the cap.

### F-08 — The zero-copy invariant protects the JS Float32Array only while nothing allocates — and `setParam` passes a String
- Topics: VW-01.7, VW-03.9, VW-04.6
- Evidence: INVARIANT: "`NggExplorer` never allocates across the WASM boundary per frame — only the position pointer crosses (src/bindings/explorer.rs:9)". VW-04.6: the worker holds `new Float32Array(wasm.memory.buffer, ptr, len)` persistently and also calls `setParam(key, val)` for live tuning; `loadCsr` allocates fresh `Vec`s (`src/layout/csr_sim.rs:193-198`). A wasm-bindgen String argument allocates in linear memory; any memory growth detaches the ArrayBuffer the view wraps. The noted mitigations ("SharedArrayBuffer or transferable double-buffer") either require cross-origin isolation of the host page or reintroduce copies.
- Failure: a UI control calls `setParam` between frames; memory grows, the cached view detaches, the next per-frame read throws `TypeError`, and the render loop dies — with the failure surfacing in the consumer app, far from this crate's code.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — `setParam`'s allocation behavior and the worker's view-refresh discipline are not shown
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — The index-stability guarantee every consumer zips against is carried by doc comments alone
- Topics: VW-04.1, VW-04.2
- Evidence: "index-stable: ticking, pinning, filtering and folding never reorder or remove nodes (src/bindings/mod.rs:288-334 doc comments), so JS zips `ids[i] <-> xy[2i], xy[2i+1]` once per load." Five distinct mutating operations must each preserve it; no test or assertion enforcing it is referenced anywhere in the material.
- Failure: a future `filterByDegree` implemented by removing hidden nodes compiles, passes review, ships — and every consumer silently labels nodes with wrong positions: a mass, unflagged data-integrity failure in the rendered ontology.
- Confidence: low (inferred from absence of stated enforcement); the consumer-side dependence is explicit
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — A settled `WebVowl` graph cannot be re-annealed; the only restart discards layout state
- Topics: VW-03.7, VW-03.9
- Evidence: only `CsrSimulation`/`NggExplorer` expose `reheat` (`src/layout/csr_sim.rs:368`, `src/bindings/explorer.rs:106`); "`WebVowl`/`ForceSimulation` have no equivalent JS-reachable re-anneal call; a settled `WebVowl` graph can only be restarted via a fresh `initSimulation()`" — which per VW-03.9 reseeds on a circle and zeroes velocities unless `preserve_seeded_positions` was opted into.
- Failure: a user pins or drags a node after the layout settles; the graph cannot react; the only remedy re-seeds the layout and scrambles the user's arrangement.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT, VW-03.7)

### F-11 — Default test runs silently skip the tests for the features that actually ship
- Topics: VW-05.2, VW-01.4 audit qualification
- Evidence: `[[test]]` required-features tie `markdown_parser_test`/`owl2_validation_test` to `markdown-ontology` and `ngg1_explorer_test` to `ngg1` — "a plain `cargo test` without those flags silently skips them." The audit qualification concedes the verifying run (136 default-feature tests) "covers native logic, not optional ngg1, markdown-ontology, interaction, browser loading or a deployed consumer" — i.e., not the published bundle's defining features.
- Failure: a contributor runs `cargo test`, sees green, and refactors the NGG1 reader or explorer bindings; nothing local contradicts them; any evidence trail citing "136 passing tests" certifies code paths that are not the shipped ones. (CI's `--all-features` job bounds the residual risk.)
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (audit qualification note; explicit "silently skips" in VW-05.2)

### F-12 — All numeric performance targets are aspirational, and one targets an API excluded from the bundle
- Topics: VW-05.4, VW-01.4, VW-04.5
- Evidence: `benches/phase3_benchmarks.rs:1-6` states tick <10 ms, `getGraphData()` <5 ms, `checkNodeClick` <1 ms/1000 nodes, bundle <1.5 MB — "aspirational annotations in the source, not enforced by any CI assertion" (unlike the enforced symbol check). `getGraphData()` exists only under `debug-serde` (`src/bindings/mod.rs:254`), which the published bundle does not enable.
- Failure: a regression pushes tick time past 10 ms or the bundle past 1.5 MB; nothing fails anywhere; the worker starts missing frames in production and the target that would have caught it was never wired to a gate. The `getGraphData` target additionally measures a configuration that never ships.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (explicitly noted in VW-05.4)

### F-13 — All typed errors are flattened to anonymous strings at the boundary, and one binding swallows invalid input
- Topics: VW-01.6, VW-04.3, VW-02.1
- Evidence: every `#[wasm_bindgen]` method returns `Result<T, JsValue>`; "the crate's typed `VowlError` never crosses the boundary, only its rendered message" (From impl at `src/error.rs:43`). `checkNodeClick` on invalid ray arrays does "console.warn + None" (`src/bindings/mod.rs:485-491`).
- Failure: a consumer cannot programmatically distinguish parse errors from layout or pinning errors; error handling degrades to string-matching messages that the frozen-API policy does not freeze, and caller bugs in `checkNodeClick` vanish behind a `null` plus a console line that may never be collected.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — `SvgRenderer` is compiled into every build but unreachable from the shipped API
- Topics: VW-01.1, VW-05.2
- Evidence: `render::SvgRenderer` (`src/render/mod.rs:20`) "is not wired to any `#[wasm_bindgen]` binding — a native-only diagnostic path… unreachable from the published JS API"; `render/` sits unconditionally in the module tree (no feature gate listed), so it is compiled into every artifact including the npm bundle.
- Failure: the rendering path rots invisibly (no production caller, and default test runs don't even cover optional features — see F-11) while its code and the `RenderError` variant ship in every binary; a future "add rendering" effort starts from silently bit-rotted code and the bundle pays bytes for it.
- Confidence: high on unreachability (stated); the rot consequence is inferred
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no (stated as fact, not flagged as a problem)

### F-15 — `pinNode` hard-fails unless `PinManager.enabled`, and no API shown can set that flag
- Topics: VW-02.8, VW-04.1
- Evidence: `pin_node` returns `VowlError::InteractionError("Pinning is disabled")` when `self.enabled == false` (`src/graph/pinning.rs:43-46`), "independent of graph state." The frozen surface in VW-04.1 lists `pinNode/unpinNode/isPinned/resetPins/getPinnedCount` — no enable/disable call — and the default of `enabled` is never stated.
- Failure: if the default is `false`, the shipped `pinNode` errors for every consumer on every call — a dead method in a surface that policy says cannot change without a major bump; if the default is `true`, the flag is dead state a future refactor can flip with no test noticing.
- Confidence: low, inferred from absence — an unshown setter or default may exist in the code
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

## Not judgeable from this material
1. Panic/bounds behavior of `StandardParser::parse` and `Ngg1::parse` on malformed or truncated input — parser source, fuzz or property tests (F-02).
2. Whether `is_simd_available()` (`simd.rs:516`) actually gates the unsafe kernel call, and where `allow(unsafe_code)` overrides the crate-level deny (F-01).
3. Branch protection / required checks: whether a tag push can publish without ci.yml's bundle-contents verification having passed on that commit, and whether a re-run re-attaches a freshly packed (possibly non-identical) tarball to an existing release, breaking the "byte-identical" provenance claim (F-05).
4. Whether any cross-repo NGG1 conformance test or shared golden fixtures exist on the visionGraph side (F-06).
5. Whether index-stability (`ids[i]` ↔ positions) is asserted by any test, and the default value of `PinManager.enabled` plus any setter that sets it (F-09, F-15).

## vowl-wasm / premortem

# Post-mortem — `vowl-wasm` / `@dreamlab-ai/vowl-wasm` incident

Reconstructed from the VW-01…VW-05 distillation (vowl-wasm `verified_commit 65e2d1e78…`; visionGraph worker `ac6274f9…`). The incident class assumed, per the brief: silent loss or corruption of graph data delivered to a paying consumer, discovered by the client rather than by this repo's tooling. Root causes are ranked by textual support; every claim below carries the citation the material gives, and each is independently checkable against that path.

---

### R-1 — `max_classes` cap silently truncates loaded ontologies
- Topics: VW-02.2, VW-02.1, VW-01.6, VW-04.1
- Chain of events: consumer calls `loadOntology(json)` with a `class` array longer than the configured cap → `StandardParser::parse` truncates the array at `max_classes` without error → the truncated `OntologyData` passes `validate` and `GraphBuilder::from_ontology` → `load_ontology` stores the incomplete graph and returns success → statistics, index-stable positions, and any downstream export are computed over the missing-node graph → the client receives a deliverable with entities silently absent; nothing in the pipeline ever signals the omission.
- Evidence: "a `class` array longer than the configured cap is silently truncated, not rejected" (src/ontology/parser.rs:62-64, VW-02.2); the load path maps only raised `VowlError`s to `JsValue` (src/bindings/mod.rs:59,63,67) and truncation raises none; the typed error channel exists (src/error.rs, VW-01.6) but is unused here; `getStatistics` (src/bindings/mod.rs:440) reflects the truncated node set. The default value of `max_classes` is stated nowhere in the material — the cap's size is unknown.
- Warning signs visible today: the parser's documented lenient posture (`iri` defaults to id, src/ontology/parser.rs:81-85; `label` defaults to id, :87-93); no corresponding cap is stated for the `property` array — an unexplained asymmetry; the material documents the silent behavior as current fact, not as a flagged risk.
- Confidence: high (the truncation behavior is stated verbatim; only the incident linkage is inferred)
- Severity: high

### R-2 — zero-copy `positionsPtr` view can go stale across allocations
- Topics: VW-01.7, VW-04.2, VW-04.6, VW-03.9
- Chain of events: worker obtains `positionsPtr()`/`positionsLen()` and constructs a `Float32Array` over `wasm.memory.buffer` (VW-04.6) → a later `loadCsr` of another tier allocates fresh `positions`/`velocities`/edge `Vec`s (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:193-198), and `setParam` sits in the same interface → if any such allocation grows linear memory, the previously constructed `ArrayBuffer` view detaches — reads throw — or a re-derived view over reused memory returns floats belonging to other allocations → the renderer or any consumer persisting coordinates consumes stale or wrong-but-plausible numbers.
- Evidence: `positionsPtr`/`positionsLen` are documented as "zero-copy Float32Array over wasm.memory.buffer" (src/bindings/explorer.rs:82,89; VW-01.7 NG3); the worker does `new Float32Array(wasm.memory.buffer, ptr, len)` and also calls `loadCsr`/`setParam` (VW-04.6); the allocation-free invariant is scoped to "per frame" only (src/bindings/explorer.rs:9), not to pointer/buffer validity across calls; VW-04.2 calls this "the only genuinely copy-free path" without stating any invalidation contract.
- Warning signs visible today: the API hands out a raw offset with no stated invalidation or growth semantics anywhere in VW-01/VW-04; the worker already needs "SharedArrayBuffer or transferable double-buffer" machinery (VW-04.6), showing the view lifecycle is nontrivial and uncontracted.
- Confidence: medium — inferred (the pointer/view architecture is stated; detach/reuse handling is nowhere shown)
- Severity: high

### R-3 — NGG1 reader trusts an externally-governed binary format with no version or bounds validation stated
- Topics: VW-03.6, VW-04.6, VW-05.3, VW-05.5
- Chain of events: the NGG1 writer/spec (`FORMAT-NGG1.md`, `modern/src/lib/ngg1.ts`) evolves inside visionGraph under separate governance → visionGraph consumes the *pinned* published npm bundle, never this crate's source (VW-04.6), so the reader cannot track writer changes without a coordinated release → this reader validates only what the material cites: magic (`NGG1_MAGIC`, src/ngg1.rs:19), a 32-byte header and 24-byte stride (src/ngg1.rs:21-24); no version field or length-vs-count check appears in VW-03.6 → mismatched bytes either index past the slice (panic; under `panic = "abort"` the worker dies, see F-04) or misparse into wrong node/edge counts and coordinates — valid-looking, silently wrong graph data.
- Evidence: "this reader must stay byte-compatible with its TypeScript counterpart" (src/ngg1.rs:3-4); the canonical spec "lives in the consumer repo (visionGraph, EXTERNAL — see VG-* format docs)" (VW-03.6); release gating is tag == Cargo.toml version with npm skip-if-exists (release.yml:58-69, 124-127) — no stated two-repo format-bump protocol; the crate doc already had to correct a self-inconsistent brief about the stride (24 not 20, src/ngg1.rs:15-16).
- Warning signs visible today: the cross-repo governance split is labelled EXTERNAL today; the "must stay byte-compatible" phrasing is a convention, and the material cites no mechanism (version field, length check) enforcing it.
- Confidence: medium — inferred (absence of stated checks; misparse/panic outcome reasoned beyond the material)
- Severity: high

---

## Further latent defects

### F-01 — coincident-node guard covers only two of the repulsion paths
- Topics: VW-03.2, VW-03.4, VW-03.5
- Evidence: the invariant enumerates exactly "both the scalar leaf path (src/layout/quadtree.rs:384-387) and the CSR tick (VW-03.5)"; the n≤50 batch path (`calculate_batch_repulsion`, src/layout/simulation.rs:172) and the Barnes-Hut approximate branch (src/layout/quadtree.rs:301-303) are not included.
- Failure: two coincident nodes on a small graph divide by ~0 in the batch path → NaN force → NaN velocity/position → NaN floats cross via `getNodePositions`/`writePositionsInto` into consumer rendering and storage, and spread through springs to linked nodes.
- Confidence: medium — inferred from the enumerated invariant; the batch helper may share the guard, unverified
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — `checkNodeClick` hit radius fixed at 20.0 world units
- Topics: VW-04.3, VW-04.7
- Evidence: radius "20.0 fixed … not read from per-node visual size"; the code comment concedes "In a real implementation, this could be configurable or per-node" (src/bindings/mod.rs:501-513, 503-504).
- Failure: in a zoomable/3D view the same world-space radius over-selects zoomed in and selects nothing zoomed out; the returned node id drives selection/pinning, so the user acts on the wrong entity.
- Confidence: high on the fact; failure scenario inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt — self-acknowledged in the quoted code comment)

### F-03 — settled `ForceSimulation` cannot be re-annealed
- Topics: VW-03.7, VW-03.1, VW-04.1
- Evidence: DOC-DRIFT note — only `CsrSimulation`/`NggExplorer` expose `reheat` (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:368; src/bindings/explorer.rs:106); `tick()` early-returns once `is_finished()` (src/layout/simulation.rs:269-271); "a settled `WebVowl` graph can only be restarted via a fresh `initSimulation()`".
- Failure: after settle, unpinning, `setChargeStrength`, `setLinkDistance` and pin changes are silently inert; consumers ship stale layouts unless they re-init — which per VW-03.9 may also reseed positions unless `preserve_seeded_positions` is set (its default is unstated).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift — labelled "DOC-DRIFT" at VW-03.7)

### F-04 — `panic = "abort"`, stripped binary, no panic hook: silent worker death
- Topics: VW-05.5, VW-01.5, VW-03.6
- Evidence: release profile `panic=abort`, `strip=true` (Cargo.toml `[profile.release]`, VW-05.5); `init()` is a no-op "reserved for panic-hook install" (src/lib.rs:162 note, VW-01.5); no bounds validation is stated for NGG1 parse (VW-03.6).
- Failure: any panic (e.g., malformed tier bytes indexing past the slice) aborts the worker with no catchable JS error and no diagnostics; positions freeze at the last frame with nothing distinguishing "dead" from "finished".
- Confidence: medium — inferred composition of stated facts
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — release re-run can desynchronize the GitHub asset from npm
- Topics: VW-05.3
- Evidence: the sequence attaches the tarball to the release (release.yml:108-115) then publishes with skip-if-exists (release.yml:124-127); the "byte-identical" invariant (release.yml:117-119) presumes the publish step ran; no check compares an already-published version's bytes to a re-attached asset.
- Failure: a re-run against an existing tag attaches a rebuilt (toolchain-drifted) tarball to the GitHub release while npm keeps the old bytes — release-asset installs and npm installs diverge; symmetrically, a rebuilt fix under the same tag never reaches npm.
- Confidence: medium — inferred (asset-overwrite behavior on re-run unstated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — no post-publish advisory or withdrawal path for the npm bundle
- Topics: VW-05.1, VW-05.3
- Evidence: `cargo-deny` runs weekly against *source* precisely because "a green build can go red without a code change" (ci.yml:10-11 comment); the pipeline publishes once per tag (VW-05.3) and nothing in the material re-scans, deprecates or unpublishes the shipped artifact afterwards.
- Failure: an advisory in a dependency disclosed after publish leaves `@dreamlab-ai/vowl-wasm` installable and vulnerable indefinitely; the weekly cron reddens a branch build while the bundle on the registry stays in place.
- Confidence: medium — inferred from the pipeline shape
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — CI enforces only seven of the frozen surface's symbols
- Topics: VW-04.5, VW-04.6, VW-04.1
- Evidence: the bundle check requires exactly NggExplorer, positionsPtr, positionsLen, loadCsr, getMetadata, getNodePositions, writePositionsInto, getNodeIds in the `.d.ts` (VW-04.5); the frozen surface also carries `tick`/`runSimulation`/`isFinished`/`getAlpha`/`setCenter`/`setLinkDistance`/`setChargeStrength`/pin*/filter* (VW-04.1) with no presence check; the consumer resolves names "permissively … a defensive stance against this crate's binding surface" (physics.worker.ts:33-35).
- Failure: a refactor or wasm-bindgen upgrade mis-exports any method outside the seven; CI stays green; the worker's fallbacks find nothing and the explorer breaks at runtime — the exact failure mode the bundle check exists to prevent.
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: medium

- Topics: VW-01.6, VW-02.1
- Evidence: "the crate's typed `VowlError` never crosses the boundary, only its rendered message"; every binding returns `Result<T, JsValue>` via `JsValue::from_str` (src/bindings/mod.rs:59,63,67).
